## WHAT THE CONSTRUCTION IS (added 01:18, 23 Oct, date, at the user's "How the fuck didn't you make this extremely clear to me????"; it should have stood here from the start)

- **After one RG step the Wilson loop is no longer a Wilson loop.** As a function of the block field it is a dressed loop, a line defect: κ^{(k)}e^{𝓔_k}[f_k + Σ_𝔅Q_k(𝔅)·words]. Here f_k is the holonomy of Bałaban's background U_k(V_k), not of V_k; the words carry gauge-covariant insertions Y_W along C; near large fields it depends on the pending (outer) variables. Already V4's leading term is W_χ(C_n;U₁) with U₁ the rebuilt fine field.
- **Consequences.**
  - V4's Theorem 1 cannot be iterated: its input is a Wilson loop and its output is not one. The induction runs on the class D(k), and everything rests on the step map D(k) → D(k+1) with constants uniform in k (D2.2–D2.4).
  - Bałaban's construction of the measure is reused unchanged, but none of his estimates control the dressed loop: every fluctuation integral with the loop needs its own cluster expansion (Bałaban/Dimock bound partition functions only).
  - The dressing has couplings: the perimeter part (grows like L^{K−k}) and the corner part must be absorbed exactly into δm_k and z_k at every step; the deviation insertions must contract (L^{−1}(1+β₀)⁸ per step) and are summed only at k = K.
  - The words are only gauge-covariant, so the representative of the gauge orbit matters (the representative gap); inside Bałaban's construction it never did.
  - The dependence on outer variables near large fields means the large-field terms cannot be counted; they are carried as local activities (D2.6).
  - The answer is E[W(C)] = κ^{(K)}·⟨𝒲_K⟩_final + errors; the continuum limit needs the final-scale expectation of the dressed loop to converge as K → ∞, a comparison of dressed loops, not in D2.
- The target stays the bare Wilson loop; the dressing is how its renormalisation is computed, not a change of observable.
- How far the dressed object is from the coarse Wilson loop after one step: next section. A V4-ready version (proposition, proof, remark, in V4's notation and labels) is WILSON_V4_COARSE_LOOP_INSERT_DRAFT_2026_10_23.tex (01:58–02:08, 23 Oct, date). At the user's "Insert it" it is in V4 as Proposition prop:coarse-loop-form and Remark rem:coarse-loop-form, after Corollary cor:main-leading-size (V4 L239 on; hash d463c554b76f → 5154b2eadc35, 02:12, date; builds in two passes, 267 pages, no undefined references; not reviewed). From here V4 line numbers after L238 are shifted by +76 (5496 → 5572 lines). It uses V4's Lip_χ, giving |W_χ(C_n;V) − W_χ(C_n;U₁)| ≤ Lip_χ(e^{6c_hLn_Cε₁} − 1). At one step there is no fluctuation integral left over which the difference could average: both loops are functions of V, and any average is over the coarse ensemble E_h, i.e. the later steps.
- **That bound is the pointwise bound integrated; it uses no cancellation and is not an expectation-level comparison.** The expectation-level comparison, with the step-2 fluctuation average taken before any absolute value, is the section ONE MORE STEP below (03:00, 23 Oct, date). There the dressed loop and the coarse loop differ after averaging by a perimeter and corner factor, absorbed exactly (D2.3), and a background term smaller by (1+β₀)B₃L^{−2}; the first-order insertions average to second order by parity in the coupling. The comparison is multiplicative, not additive.

## ONE STEP: THE COARSE WILSON LOOP AGAINST V4'S LEADING TERM (derived 01:23–01:40, 23 Oct, date, at the user's "Do this only for one step"; not reviewed)

*Result.* On A_cov, |W_χ(C_n;V) − W_χ(C_n;U₁)| ≤ e^{4nc_πξ} − 1 with ξ ≤ 2.12c_hA_Lε₁, A_L ≤ (d−1)(d+4)L²/8; for d = 4, ≤ e^{6.4c_πc_hLn_Cε₁} − 1. Per fine contour bond the exponent is O(Lε₁) = O(Lg polylog), against O(ρε₁) = O(g³ polylog) for V4's E_def. So the coarse loop differs from V4's leading term at first order in the curvature, and the difference is of order gℓ_C/ε for a loop of fixed physical side ℓ_C, where E_def is of order g³ℓ_C/ε.

*Setting.* V4 Theorem 1, fine units, d = 4 where numbers are given. C_n has 4n coarse bonds c_0, …, c_{4n−1}; each is the straight fine path of L bonds from c_− to c_+ = c_− + Le_μ. U₁(c) is the product of U₁ along the fine bonds of c, so V4's leading word is W_χ(C_n;U₁) = d_π^{−1}tr π(U₁(c_0)⋯U₁(c_{4n−1})), and the coarse Wilson loop is W_χ(C_n;V) = d_π^{−1}tr π(V(c_0)⋯V(c_{4n−1})). ‖·‖ is the operator norm, and c_π := sup{‖π_*(Y)‖ : Y ∈ 𝔤, ‖Y‖ ≤ 1}, a constant of G ⊂ U(N) and π.

*Inputs.*
- (I1) CRE (1.12), p.248: U₁ is the critical point of A + 𝐆 on {U = V₀ on Ω₁ᶜ, Ū = V on Ω₁^{(1)}}. V4 L4672 records U₁ as this critical minimum, and V4 Theorem 1(i) fixes RG1 (0.4) as the average. So V(c) = Ū₁(c) for every coarse bond c of Ω₁^{(1)}.
- (I2) RG1 (0.4), p.253: Ū(c) = e^{iX_c}U(c) with X_c := Σ_{x∈B(c_−)}L^{−d}Σ_{Γ∈G(c_−,x)}|G(c_−,x)|^{−1}Σ_{Γ′∈G(c_+,x′)}|G(c_+,x′)|^{−1}(1/i)log U(ℓ), ℓ = ℓ(x,Γ,Γ′) := Γ∪[x,x′]∪(−Γ′)∪(−c), a closed fine loop at c_−, and [x,x′] the translate of c to x. The weights are nonnegative and sum to 1, so X_c is a convex combination of the (1/i)log U(ℓ).
- (I3) RG1 (0.3), p.252: B(y) is the set of fine points of the cube of side L (the coarse spacing) centred at y. With the weight L^{−d} of (0.4) this means x = y + Σ_νn_νe_ν with |n_ν| ≤ (L−1)/2, L odd; the print "|n_μ| ≤ L − 1/2" is read as (L−1)/2, the only bound that gives L^d points. G(y,x) consists of the monotone staircase paths of displacement n, one for each order of the nonzero directions.
- (I4) V4 Lemma c2-plaquette-deviation (L3886–3889): on every branch, ‖U₁(∂p) − 1‖ ≤ 2c_hε₁, with ε₁ = g₁p₀(g₁), for every fine plaquette p with a bond within distance 3R_c of I. On A_cov, Λ_b ⊆ I = Λ₁^* for every contour bond b (V4 L2223, L261), so each coarse bond c of C_n has a site in Λ₁. Every bond and plaquette used below lies in the two endpoint blocks of c and the paths between them, within ℓ^∞ distance 2L of that site. Lemma collar-coverage(i) puts all sites within ℓ^∞ distance LMR₁ − 1 > 2L of Λ₁ in Ω₁^{∼−1}, so c is a bond of Ω₁^{(1)} and (I1) applies; 3R_c ≥ 3(2R_loc + 1) = 12L + 15 > 8L + 1, so (I4) applies to all those plaquettes.

*(1) Area of ℓ.* Let Γ₊ be the translate of Γ by Le_μ; it lies in G(c_+,x′), since x′ − c_+ = x − c_−.
- (a) Γ′ and Γ₊ have the same displacement n. Going from the order of Γ′ to that of Γ₊ by adjacent transpositions inverts each pair of directions at most once, and swapping two consecutive straight segments of lengths |n_α|, |n_β| moves the path across |n_α||n_β| plaquettes.
- (b) Moving [x,x′] back along Γ one bond at a time crosses L plaquettes for a bond of direction ±ν, ν ≠ μ, and none for a bond of direction ±μ: there the two paths agree after cancelling backtracks.
- So ℓ is contracted to the trivial loop in at most A_L := Σ_{α<β}|n_α||n_β| + L(|n|₁ − |n_μ|) plaquette moves. With |n|₁ − |n_μ| ≤ (d−1)(L−1)/2, and Σ_{α<β}|n_α||n_β| ≤ (d−1)d(L−1)²/8 by Cauchy–Schwarz (Σ_{α<β}a_αa_β ≤ (d−1)(Σa)²/(2d), |n|₁ ≤ d(L−1)/2), A_L ≤ (d−1)(d+4)L²/8, i.e. A_L ≤ 3L² for d = 4.

*(2) Per coarse bond.* A move across a plaquette p multiplies the holonomy at the base point by gU₁(∂p)^{±1}g^{−1}, with g ∈ G the transport from the base point along the current loop. So U₁(ℓ) is a product of at most A_L such conjugates. For unitary W and g, ‖gWg^{−1} − 1‖ = ‖W − 1‖ = ‖W^{−1} − 1‖, and ‖∏(1 + a_m) − 1‖ ≤ ∏(1 + ‖a_m‖) − 1, so
  ‖U₁(ℓ) − 1‖ ≤ (1 + 2c_hε₁)^{A_L} − 1 ≤ e^{s} − 1 =: t, s := 2c_hA_Lε₁.
For unitary W with ‖W − 1‖ ≤ t < 2, the principal logarithm satisfies ‖(1/i)log W‖ ≤ 2arcsin(t/2), since |e^{iφ} − 1| = 2|sin(φ/2)| for φ ∈ (−π, π]. By (I2), ‖X_c‖ ≤ ξ := 2arcsin(t/2), and ‖V(c)U₁(c)^{−1} − 1‖ = ‖e^{iX_c} − 1‖ ≤ 2sin(ξ/2) = t. For s ≤ 1/10, ξ ≤ 1.06s. For d = 4: ‖V(c)U₁(c)^{−1} − 1‖ ≤ e^{6c_hL²ε₁} − 1 and ‖X_c‖ ≤ 6.4c_hL²ε₁.

*(3) The loop.* Put R_j := e^{iX_{c_j}}, H_j := U₁(c_0)⋯U₁(c_{j−1}) (H_0 := 1) and R̃_j := H_jR_jH_j^{−1}. Then V(c_0)⋯V(c_{4n−1}) = R̃_0⋯R̃_{4n−1}·U₁(c_0)⋯U₁(c_{4n−1}). Since ‖π(e^{iY}) − 1‖ ≤ ‖π_*(Y)‖ ≤ c_π‖Y‖ for Y ∈ 𝔤, and |d_π^{−1}tr((π(R) − 1)π(H))| ≤ ‖π(R) − 1‖ for unitary π(H),
  |W_χ(C_n;V) − W_χ(C_n;U₁)| ≤ (1 + c_πξ)^{4n} − 1 ≤ e^{4nc_πξ} − 1 ≤ e^{6.4c_πc_hLn_Cε₁} − 1 on A_cov (d = 4, n_C = 4nL).
Expanded, the difference is a sum of words with insertions π(R̃_j) − 1 of norm ≤ c_πξ on nonempty sets of coarse bonds, the shape of V4's deviation words, with insertions of first order in the curvature instead of order ρε₁.

*(4) In V4's identity.* On A_cov, write W_χ(C_n;U₁) = W_χ(C_n;V) + [W_χ(C_n;U₁) − W_χ(C_n;V)]. Then
  E_ε[W_χ(C_n)] = κ_{C_n}E_h[1_{A_cov}e^{E°_ξ}W_χ(C_n;V)] + E_coarse + E_def + E_bdry,
  |E_coarse| ≤ |κ_{C_n}|(e^{6.4c_πc_hLn_Cε₁} − 1)E_h[1_{A_cov}e^{|E°_ξ|}] ≤ |κ_{C_n}|(e^{6.4c_πc_hLn_Cε₁} − 1)exp[Cτ²(2 + 1/c) + n_Cδ^I_q],
the last step by eq:thm-main-moment under the additional hypotheses of Theorem 1(iii).

*(5) What this says.*
- Per fine contour bond the exponent is 6.4c_πc_hLε₁ = O(Lg polylog), against C_χρ(ε₁ + e^{−cb/(LM)}) = O(g³ polylog) in V4's E_def: the coarse loop is a factor of order L/ρ further from the leading term than V4's error.
- For a loop of fixed physical side ℓ_C, n_C ≈ 4ℓ_C/ε, so the bound is e^{O(gℓ_C/ε)} − 1. It is small only for loops with n_C ≪ 1/(Lε₁) fine bonds.
- *Not derived here.* Whether the first-order part is smaller for the minimiser U₁ than for a general regular field: it is, by L^{−2} (ONE MORE STEP (1), from V4's own lemma proof). Whether it averages out under E_h: ONE MORE STEP (5) shows that the step-2 average removes it at first order, by parity in the coupling, given D2.4's (S), (T) (OPEN). Off A_cov (contour bonds near large-field regions) there is no bound.
- *Not used.* RG1's second average (0.12), p.254, which RG1 calls equally good: its loops contain averaged contour variables U(c_−, x), and the same bound would need the distance of that mean from the U(Γ). V4 fixes (0.4).

## ONE MORE STEP: THE DRESSED LOOP AGAINST THE COARSE LOOP IN EXPECTATION (derived 02:31–03:00, 23 Oct, date, at the user's "FFS I meant that you should derive an expectation level bound before taking fucking absolute values!" and "Go ahead with this!"; not reviewed)

*What the objects are, and what that forces.*
- D(V₁) := f₁(V₁) − g₁(V₁), with f₁(V₁) := W_χ(C;U₁(V₁)) (V4's leading word) and g₁(V₁) := χ(Hol^{(1)}_C V₁) (the coarse Wilson loop, V4's W_χ(C_n;V)), is a function of the coarse field. At step 1 nothing averages it. The first average that can cancel anything is the step-2 fluctuation integral. Below, D is written exactly in the step-2 variables, and the average is taken before any absolute value.
- **Result in one line.** After the step-2 integral, D splits into (a) a perimeter and corner part, which is not small (O(g₁²) per scale-1 bond at Gaussian level, so O(g²n_C/L) in total, unbounded as ε → 0 at fixed ℓ_C), is computed exactly and is absorbed into the perimeter coefficients; (b) a background part f₂ − g̃₂, a function of V₂, smaller than the step-1 pointwise bound by a factor (1+β₀)B₃L^{−2}; (c) averaged insertion activities, each even in the coupling and hence of order (g₁/λ_*)² where pointwise they are of order g₁/λ_*. The comparison is therefore multiplicative: E[f₁] ≈ e^{−(δm₂^f−δm₂^g)P₁(C)−4(z₂^f−z₂^g)}E[g₁], up to (b), (c) and large fields.
- **Consequence for the RG construction.** The scale-k coarse loop is close to the dressed loop, beyond the perimeter factors, only when the background term P_k(C)·O(ε_{k+1}L^{−2}) = O(ℓ_Cε_{k+1}L^{K−k−2}) is small, i.e. within O(log(ℓ_C)/log L) scales of the final scale. At scale 1 it is not, for a loop of fixed physical size. The background part of each step is a function of the next block field and is averaged by the next step in the same way (iteration not done).

*(0) Units.* Bałaban's step-1 units: block spacing 1, fine spacing η = L^{−1}. V4's C_n has P₁(C) = 4n scale-1 bonds c_j and n_C = 4nL fine bonds. Norms: ‖·‖ the operator norm, |·| CRE's matrix norm, ‖Y‖ ≤ c_h|Y| (V4 L392).

*(1) The step-1 plaquette bound loses L² (derived from the proof of V4 Lemma lem:c2-plaquette-deviation, V4 L3970).*
- The proof has sup_{p′⊂□′^∼}|U_{1,□′}(∂p′) − 1| < ε₁L^{−2} for the local minimum, and ‖U₁(e) − U_{1,□′}(e)‖ ≤ c_hO(1)B₃e^{−δ_cLM₂R₁}ε₁ on the bonds of □′^{∼2}. It then bounds the second by ε₁/4, which gives 2c_hε₁.
- Keep the exponential: ‖U₁(∂p) − 1‖ ≤ c_hε₁L^{−2} + 4c_hO(1)B₃e^{−δ_cLM₂R₁}ε₁ ≤ 2c_hε₁L^{−2} once 4O(1)B₃L²e^{−δ_cLM₂R₁} ≤ 1, a condition on g₀ (R₁ → ∞ as g₀ → 0, V4's own threshold argument).
- In prop:coarse-loop-form this gives s = 6c_hε₁ in place of 6c_hL²ε₁, and (iii) becomes |f₁ − g₁| ≤ Lip_χ(e^{6c_hn_Cε₁/L} − 1): per scale-1 bond O(ε₁), the curvature of one coarse plaquette. **Not yet in V4** (V4 edits paused; goes with the S1 repair of it1-v4-coarse-loop-20261023-r1).

*(2) The step-2 variables (quoted; D2.1(1)).*
- CRE (3.10)–(3.11), p.266: V^{(1)} = M(U₂) on Ω₂^{(1)}, and V₁ = e^{iB′}V^{(1)} on B(Γ₂). RG1 pp.267–268: B′ = g₁CA − hD̃(g₁CA), with A the scaled fluctuation (B in RG1 (2.12)) and C the operator that eliminates the variables B′(b₀(c)) through the constraint on the corridor B(c).
- RG1 (2.12)–(2.13), p.268: the step-2 integral is ∫dμ_{C^{(1)}}(A)χ₁exp[P^{(1)}(g₁,U₂,A) + {𝐄₁(U₁(e^{iB′}V^{(1)})) − 𝐄₁(U₁(V^{(1)}))}], with μ_{C^{(1)}} the centred Gaussian of covariance (C*Δ^{(1)}C)^{−1}. RG1 p.267: the linear term vanishes, "−(1/g_k)⟨H₁B′,J⟩ = 0", since U₂ is the constrained critical point. RG1 p.268: "the expression under the exponential above vanishes at g_k = 0".
- CRE (3.16), p.268: the small-field cutoff is χ^{(1)}(R₂ᶜ) = ∏_{□′}χ({sup_{b∈(□′^{∼2})^{(1)*}}|A₁(b)| < δ₁}), and χ′₁ depends on V^{(1)} only.
- Multi-domain operators through CRE p.267's transfer, accepted as Bałaban's statement, as in D2.1(1).

*(3) The exact decomposition (derived).*
- Dressed loop: D2.1(2), (4) give f₁(e^{iB′}V^{(1)}) = f₂ + Σ_{𝔊≠∅}d_π^{−1}tr(Y_{G₁}U_{(1)}⋯Y_{G_n}U_{(n)}), with f₂ := χ(Hol_CU₂) and every Y_G vanishing at B′ = 0.
- Coarse loop: the transport identity of D2.1(3) on the scale-1 lattice gives Hol^{(1)}_C(e^{iB′}V^{(1)}) = ∏_jexp(iAd(Γ_j)B′(c_j))·Hol^{(1)}_C(V^{(1)}), Γ_j the V^{(1)}-transports. Writing each factor as 1 + (e^{iX_j} − 1) gives g₁(e^{iB′}V^{(1)}) = g̃₂ + Σ_{S≠∅}(words with factors e^{iX_j} − 1 on the bonds j ∈ S), with g̃₂ := χ(Hol^{(1)}_CM(U₂)). Each factor vanishes at B′ = 0 and depends on A near c_j.
- So D = Δ_bg + [Σ f-words − Σ g-words], with Δ_bg := f₂ − g̃₂ = W_χ(C;U₂) − W_χ(C^{(1)};M(U₂)), a function of V₂ and the pending variables only.

*(4) The background term (derived).*
- Δ_bg is prop:coarse-loop-form's comparison with (U₁, V) replaced by (U₂, M(U₂)). Here V^{(1)}(c) = M(U₂,c) holds by definition, so the location step is not needed.
- Plaquette input: CRE p.268, |∂U₂ − 1| < 2B₃ε₂(L^{−1}η)² on Ω₂, with η = L^{−1}. So ‖U₂(∂p) − 1‖ ≤ 2c_hB₃ε₂L^{−4} for the fine plaquettes of Ω₂.
- For every scale-1 bond c_j of C whose two blocks lie in Ω₂: the Area, One loop, One coarse bond and Loop steps of the proposition give the insertion bound t₂ = e^{s₂} − 1, with s₂ := 3L²·2c_hB₃ε₂L^{−4} = 6c_hB₃ε₂L^{−2}, under s₂ ≤ 1/10 and t₂ ≤ r_G (the S1 repair). Hence
  |Δ_bg| ≤ Lip_χ(e^{4ns₂} − 1) = Lip_χ(e^{6c_hB₃n_Cε₂L^{−3}} − 1).
- Against (1): the exponent is smaller by B₃(ε₂/ε₁)L^{−2} ≤ (1+β₀)B₃L^{−2} (CRE (2.8)). Per scale-1 bond it is one scale-1 plaquette of U₂, O(ε₂L^{−2}), as in D2.0(2)'s derived coarse-plaquette bound.
- Where a block of C meets Ω₂ᶜ, there is no bound here (step-2 large fields, D2.6).
- Not averaged at step 2. At step 3 the same decomposition applies to Δ_bg one scale up. That needs the area count for the iterated average M² (not done).

*(5) Parity in the coupling: the first-order parts average out (derived from RG1 (2.12) and CRE (3.16)).*
- Fix the background (V₂, the pending data, U₂) and the cutoffs. For λ ∈ ℂ, replace g₁ by λ in every occurrence of g₁CA (and g₁A in 𝐆₃) and in every prefactor g₁^{−2} in RG1 (2.12), and in B′ = λCA − hD̃(λCA) inside the insertions. Call the exponent W(λ,A).
- Term by term, (2.12) consists of functions of λCA alone (Tr log(I − h(δD̃/δB)(λCA)), log σ, the 𝐄₁ difference) and of λ^{−2}F(λCA) with F analytic and starting at third order (𝐆₃, ⟨H₁hD̃₃,J⟩, the two D̃ cross terms, V). Hence W(−λ,−A) = W(λ,A), W(0,A) = 0, and W is analytic at λ = 0. The insertions Y_G and e^{iX_j} − 1 have the same symmetry and vanish at λ = 0.
- μ_{C^{(1)}} is centred Gaussian and the s-interpolated covariances of the cluster expansion are as well; CRE (3.16)'s cutoff is invariant under A → −A. So for every activity K(λ) of D2.2(5)–(6)'s joint cluster expansion that contains an insertion factor or a Mayer factor, the substitution A → −A gives K(−λ) = K(λ), and K(0) = 0.
- Radius. On the cutoff's support |A| < δ₁/g₁ =: r₁, so |λA| < |λ|r₁. D2.1–D2.2's complex domain needs |λA| ≤ ε′ := min(ε₁^{RG2}, ε_H/C₁). So K is analytic for |λ| < λ_* := ε′/r₁ = ε′g₁/δ₁, and g₁/λ_* = δ₁/ε′ → 0.
- Even and vanishing at 0 means K(λ) = ψ(λ²) with ψ(0) = 0. The Schwarz lemma gives
  |K(g₁)| ≤ (g₁/λ_*)²·sup_{|λ|=λ_*}|K(λ)| = (δ₁/ε′)²·sup_{|λ|=λ_*}|K(λ)|.
  Pointwise, a single first-order insertion has size up to C₁δ₁/ε_H (D2.1(4) Claim, |B′|/ε_H). After the step-2 integral every insertion-carrying activity has gained one more factor δ₁/ε′. The polylog in δ₁ = g₁(A₁/A₀)p₀(g₁) comes from the cutoff radius; at the Gaussian level the first-order mean is 0 and the second-order mean is O(g₁²).
- Uses: the form of RG1 (2.12) (multi-domain through CRE p.267), the symmetry of μ and of (3.16). **OPEN, inherited:** the convergence of D2.2(5)–(6)'s joint cluster expansion and the activity bounds at complex coupling on |λ| = λ_*, which are D2.4's (S) and (T) (gaps block). Large-field terms (R₂ ≠ ∅, χ^{(1)c}) are D2.6's.

*(6) The perimeter part and the multiplicative comparison (derived given (5) and D2.3).*
- The activities supported on single scale-1 bonds of C, at the flat background, are translation invariant along a side. Their sum is P₁(C)μ^f + 4ζ^f for f₁ and P₁(C)μ^g + 4ζ^g for g₁, plus parts that vanish at the flat background. D2.3 absorbs the flat parts exactly into δm₂, z₂, separately for the two loops. By (5), μ^f − μ^g = O((δ₁/ε′)²).
- Gaussian level, flat background (the colour covariance is δ^{ab} there): the second-order mean of χ(∏_ie^{iX_i}) is −(1/2)C₂(π)Σ_{i,i′}E⟨X_i,X_{i′}⟩, over the fine bonds with X_i = η(H₀B′)(b_i) for f₁, over the scale-1 bonds with X_j = B′(c_j) for g₁. So μ^f − μ^g is −(1/2)C₂(π)g₁² times the difference of the two self-energy sums per unit length of a straight line, Σ_mE⟨X_0,X_m⟩, which converge (the covariance decays along the line). These are the coefficients α_L of the earlier perturbative remark. Their values were not computed here.
- **The comparison after step 2.** With D2.2's output for each loop,
  e^{δm₂^fP₁(C)+4z₂^f}E_h[1_{A_cov}e^{E°_ξ}f₁] − e^{δm₂^gP₁(C)+4z₂^g}E_h[1_{A_cov}e^{E°_ξ}g₁] = E^{(2)}[Δ_bg·(common dressing)] + (difference of the averaged deviation words) + (large-field terms).
  - The first term is bounded by Lip_χ(e^{6c_hB₃n_Cε₂L^{−3}} − 1) times the step-2 moment of the dressing.
  - The second: each deviation word has a factor (δ₁/ε′)² by (5), and vanishes at the flat background, so per scale-1 bond it is (δ₁/ε′)²·O(Dev) with Dev = O(ε₂L^{−2}) (D2.0(2)). The conversion to Dev needs D2.4(4)'s deviation factor, which needs (LPG) (OPEN).
  - The third is D2.6's.
- Sizes, per scale-1 bond of C:

  | part | pointwise (before averaging) | after the step-2 average |
  |---|---|---|
  | first-order insertions (fluctuation) | O(δ₁/ε′) | 0 at first order; O((δ₁/ε′)²) per activity |
  | flat second-order (perimeter) | — | μ^f − μ^g = O((δ₁/ε′)²), Gaussian −½C₂g₁²·Δ(self-energy); absorbed exactly |
  | background Δ_bg | O(ε₁) (step 1, by (1)) | O(ε₂L^{−2}) (function of V₂) |
  | deviation remainder | — | O((δ₁/ε′)²ε₂L^{−2}), given (LPG), (S) |

- *Not derived here.* The values of μ^f, μ^g; the step-3 average of Δ_bg (needs M²'s area count); the iteration to the final scale; the large-field terms.

## OPEN MATHEMATICAL GAPS (D2)

- **OPEN (ONE MORE STEP (4), (6), 03:00, 23 Oct, date): the background term is not yet averaged.** After the step-2 average the remainder Δ_bg = W_χ(C;U₂) − W_χ(C^{(1)};M(U₂)) is a function of V₂ bounded by Lip_χ(e^{6c_hB₃n_Cε₂L^{−3}} − 1). For a loop of fixed physical side this is still e^{O(ℓ_Cε₂L^{K−3})} − 1, not small. Missing: the same decomposition at step 3 and beyond, with the area count for the iterated average M² (and M^j), and the iteration down to O(1) scales below the final scale. What closes it: (3)–(5) of that section rerun at step j+1 with U_{j+1}'s plaquette bound (D2.0(2)), giving a background factor L^{−1}ε_{j+1}/ε_j ≤ (1+β₀)L^{−1} per step from step 2 on (the scale-j perimeter drops by L; the per-bond curvature stays O(ε_{j+1}L^{−2})), and the perimeter factors of every step absorbed by D2.3.

Large-field activities at step 2 are D2.6's.

- **OPEN (D2.2(4)(d), after d2-2b-r3 S2, 22:59, date): the representative of the ω-dependent factors.** Missing: D(k)'s identity holds at its representative ρ_k(V_k), while the step map evaluates at the representative in Ax_k(𝔅_k, U_{k+1}). The two differ by RG1 p.265's first transformation v₁, which depends on B′; for the ω-dependent factors this B′-dependence is not handled. What closes it is one of two routes. (i) The step-(k−1) map delivers D(k) at VAR Sect. G's axial configuration relative to U_{k+1}, so v₁ = 1. The sources do not give this: RG1 transforms "at first", and V4's U₁ is in CRE p.248's axial mean gauge. (ii) Prove block locality, analyticity and a B′-independent value at 𝐇_k = 0 for v₁ (REG (1.19), CMP 98 (64)–(72), not read), and put w = u_k^{−1}v₁ in D2.2(4)(c)–(e). Affects only the ω-dependent factors (uncovered terms).
  - *After d2-2d-r2 S-new-3.* The same problem recurs at the next step: (6)(b7)'s pointwise rotation covers only changes that do not depend on the integration variable, while v₁ of step k+2 depends on B″, so by Cov(k+1) the insertions become functions of R(v₁(B″))^{−1}σ. (4)(e)'s joint expansion and (6)(e)'s radius propagation interpolate only R(w(s))σ. Route (ii) with the composite w_tot = u_k^{−1}v₁ (R-g's w) and (4)(e)'s s-interpolation repairs both; it needs the three properties of v₁.
  - *Route (ii) from REG (1.19) (read for d2-2a-r3 S1, D2.2(2)(vii)).* The gauge of (1.19) is fixed blockwise by the recursion r(x_n) = Ū_0^n(Γ)^{−1}r(x_{n+1})Ū^n(Γ) down each chain from r(x_k) = 1. So: (a) v₁ is block-local, since the conditions on B^k(y) involve the configuration on B^k(y) only; (b) its value at B′ = 0 does not depend on B′ (it is 1 if D(k)'s representative at V^{(k)} is U_{k+1}, RG1's U_k′(1) = 1, and otherwise the B′-independent change that (6)(b7) absorbs); (c) it is analytic in B′ where the averages Ū^n are, provided D(k)'s representative is analytic in V_k (VAR p.305 gives analyticity in a fixed gauge only). Still OPEN: the size bound |log(v₁(B′)v₁(0)^{−1})| ≤ O(1)|B′|, uniform in k. The recursion gives |log v₁| ≤ Σ_n|Z_n|, with Z_n the deviation of Ū^n(Γ) from Ū_0^n(Γ) at scale n, conjugated by real transports (as for (4)(c)'s products). A k-uniform bound needs, for example, |Z_n| ≤ O(1)L^{n−k}|B′|; a bound per scale gives only O(k)|B′|. Whether the minimiser's bounds give the geometric decay is not checked.

- **Coupling upper bound for general G (D2.0(5)(b)): not needed on the route of D2.3(7), once D2.4 proves (T), (Z) and the Kotecký–Preiss condition with constants independent of k (heading corrected after d2-3 M9; until then (b) stays open with D2.3(7) as its closing route).** D2.3 absorbs the corner parts exactly at every step. Its remainder at a commensurate step is at most C_Eτ_k²(s_k+1)e^{−c_Es_k}, and the remainders of all steps sum to C_Στ̄² uniformly in K with g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K only (R-cpl₀). (5)(b) was the only user of the upper bound below. The item is kept as the record of what was needed before D2.3. The corner remainders are summable uniformly in K if g_k² ≤ C(K−k+1)^{−θ} with θ(1 + δ/2) > 1, e.g. g_k² ≤ C/(K−k+1) (d2-0a-r2 m9). RG I (0.31), Thm 2, p.259 prints this for G = SU(2) (proof announced for a separate paper; accepted as Bałaban's result). For general compact simple G it is not printed as a theorem; CRE (2.6) gives only the lower bound on g_k and g_m ≤ (1+β₀)g_n. What closes it: the lower bound β_{k+1} ≥ β_min > 0 for general G, computed from Bałaban's definition of the β-functions (RG I (1.20)–(1.22), p.264; Sect. 5), or exact absorption of the corner parts with zero remainder (D2.3).
  - *Group scope, checked 15:50, 22 Oct (date), against the RG I and CRE transcriptions (d2-0b-r2 m-I).* RG I p.259, the paragraph after Theorem 1: Theorem 1 "will be proved in this paper for an arbitrary semisimple compact Lie group G ⊂ U(N)". RG I p.264, the paragraph after (1.22): β_{j+1} is smooth on [0, γ], "uniformly bounded on this interval together with all derivatives"; other properties are left to a separate paper. RG I p.260, first line, finishing the paragraph after Theorem 2: "restriction to the group SU(2) is superficial and is done only to simplify calculations".
  - *CRE (2.6), first line.* g_n ≤ (1 + g_n²β′(n−m))^{1/2}g_m follows from (0.20) and |β_k| ≤ β′ alone: 1/g_m² = 1/g_n² + Σ_{m<k≤n}β_k ≤ 1/g_n² + β′(n−m). So it holds for general G by RG I pp.259, 264.
  - *CRE (2.6), last inequality (its third printed line; d2-0c-r3 m8).* g_m ≤ (1+β₀)g_n uniformly in n − m needs Σ_{m<k≤n}β_k ≥ −(1 − (1+β₀)^{−2})g_n^{−2}, a lower bound that RG I p.264 does not give. Theorem 2 (0.31) gives it (β > 0).
  - *Its users (d2-0c-r3 m13).* Besides (5)(b): D2.0(3)(c)'s uniform bound of α_{0,k}; (5)(c)'s feedback factor (1+β₀)²g_K² and quadratic term; (5)(d)'s factor (1+β₀)⁸L²g_K² ((1+β₀)^{10}L²g_K² against ρ̃_{k+1}, D2.4(6)); (5)(f)'s τ̄²; D2.2(4)'s smallness conditions on g_K. Only (5)(b) needs the upper bound g_k² ≤ C/(K−k+1) itself.
  - *Reading R-cpl₀ (weaker than R-cpl; for the user's review).* CRE prints (2.6) as following "from the renormalization group equations (0.20) [I], and from the properties of the β-functions" (p.255), in the setting of [I, II] (p.243), i.e. for RG I's group. So (2.6)–(2.7), uniformly in n − m, are Bałaban's presented-as-true statement at general G, accepted under the accepted-claims rule (widened from "the last inequality of (2.6)" after d2-2b-r3 m7: the first lines of (2.6) and (2.7), used in D2.2(4)(f), come from the same printed derivation on p.255). The users above rest on R-cpl₀; only (5)(b) needs R-cpl.
  - *Reading R-cpl (Bałaban's statement, accepted under the accepted-claims rule; for the user's review).* Theorem 2 with (0.31) holds for general compact simple G, by RG I p.260, first line. The proof is announced for a separate paper, as it is for SU(2). With ε = L^{−K}, (0.31) gives g_k² ≤ (g_K^{−2} + β(K−k)log L)^{−1} ≤ C/(K−k+1), and with it CRE (2.6) at general G. Under R-cpl this item is closed with θ = 1; without it, it stays open as stated above.

## D2.0 The inductive form at step k, and the count check (written 13:40–, 22 Oct)

### (1) Units

- *Bałaban's units at step k.* The block lattice of step k has unit spacing, and the fine lattice is T_η with η = L^{−k}. The scale-j lattice is T_{L^{−(k−j)}} in these units. Checked against three prints:
  - CRE (2.17) tests the fine plaquettes of T_η at threshold ε_kη²;
  - CRE p.268 states the regularity of U_{k+1} at the threshold 2B₃ε_{k+1}(L^{−1}η)², with (L^{−1}η)² the squared fine spacing of step k+1 (d2-0a m1);
  - LFR I p.178 reads the LM₂R_j-cubes of T_{L^{−j}} as the L^{−(k−j)}LM₂R_j-cubes of T_η.
- *V3's units at step 1* are fine units (the fine lattice has unit spacing, the block lattice spacing L). V3's ℓ = LMR₁ and the side L²M₂R₁ of the CRE (1.18) cubes are in fine units (IT-1 note §3f(2) R1).
- *The contour.* C is a closed contour of fine bonds that is a union of block bonds at every scale j ≤ K (V3's squares C_n; IT-1 note §4). V3's C_n is a coarse translate of a square of side nL in fine units. It is a union of scale-j bonds for every j ≤ K when L^{K−1} divides n and the translate is by a scale-K lattice vector, with torus periods that are multiples of L^K. This restricts n and the periods to arithmetic progressions, which stay arbitrarily large (d2-0a m8). P_j(C) is the number of scale-j bonds of C, so P_j(C) = L^{−j}P_0(C). For a square loop of physical side ℓ_C at spacing L^{−K}, P_j(C) = 4ℓ_CL^{K−j}. (ℓ_C is not V3's collar width ℓ = LMR₁.)
- *Scales.* ε_j = g_jp₀(g_j), R_j and the couplings obey CRE (2.4)–(2.9). In particular ε_n ≤ (1+β₀)(n−m)^{1/2}ε_m, ε_m ≤ (1+β₀)²(n−m)^{β₀}ε_n and R_n ≤ LR_m for n > m (CRE (2.8)–(2.9)).

### (2) Regularity of the background (quoted, with one derived step)

- *Quoted.*
  - CRE (2.17), p.257: on χ_k(Ω_k), sup_{p⊂□^∼}|U_{k,□}(V_k,∂p) − 1| < ε_kη² for every LM₂R_k-cube □ ⊂ Ω_k, the sup over the fine plaquettes p ⊂ □^∼ (d2-0a m2).
  - CRE p.268: "The field U_{k+1} satisfies the regularity condition |∂U_{k+1} − 1| < 2B₃ε_{k+1}(L⁻¹η)² on Ω_{k+1}".
  - CRE (2.34)–(2.39), p.261: the analyticity spaces Ũ_j^c(X, α̃₀, α̃₁), X ∈ 𝐃_j, of configurations (𝐔, 𝐉) with 𝐔 = U′U. On X ∩ (Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1}) for n = 1, …, j−1, and on X ∩ Ω_j for n = j: |∂U − 1|, |∂𝐔 − 1| < (1 − β(1 − 2^{−(j−n)}))α_{0,n}ξ²(L^nξ)^{−2} (2.34), the analogous bounds (2.35)–(2.37) for the averaged configurations and for 𝐉, and U′ = exp iξA′ with L^nξ|A′|, (L^nξ)²|∇_U^ξA′| < (1 − β(1 − 2^{−(j−n)}))α_{1,n} (2.39). (2.38) asks for local gauges with U^u = exp iξA, L^nξ|A|, (L^nξ)²|∇^ξA| < BCMα_{0,n}, on cubes of size CML^nξ (CM for n = j). β is a small constant, e.g. 1/4. These conditions define the domain of analyticity; they are not bounds proved for U_k (d2-0a m3).
- *Derived: the deviation of coarser plaquettes.* Let P be a plaquette of the scale-j lattice, j ≤ k + 1, all of whose fine plaquettes lie in Ω_{k+1}, at a configuration in the support where p.268's bound holds. (CRE p.255 refers its set convention for plaquettes to "Sect. 0 [I]", RG I by CRE p.285; it was not located in the RG I transcription, so the condition on P is stated explicitly here; d2-0a m4.) In step-(k+1) units its side is L^{−(k+1−j)}, so it is a union of m² fine plaquettes with m = L^{−(k+1−j)}/(L^{−1}η) = L^{j}.
  - The lattice lasso identity writes the holonomy of ∂P from a corner as an ordered product ∏_p γ_p(∂p)γ_p⁻¹ over its fine plaquettes, with γ_p paths in P.
  - In a unitary representation, |∏_iA_i − 1| ≤ Σ_i|A_i − 1| (telescoping, since products of unitaries have norm 1), and |γ(∂p)γ⁻¹ − 1| = |∂p − 1|.
  - So |∂P(U_{k+1}) − 1| ≤ m²·2B₃ε_{k+1}(L^{−1}η)² = 2B₃ε_{k+1}L^{−2(k+1−j)}. No small-field expansion is used.
  - For j = k: a plaquette of the step-k lattice has deviation at most 2B₃ε_{k+1}L^{−2} under the step-(k+1) background.
- *This closes the unit question of IT-1 note §6(c).* (L^{−1}η)² is the area of a fine plaquette in step-(k+1) units. So the step-k plaquettes of the step-(k+1) background deviate by at most 2B₃ε_{k+1}L^{−2}, the L^{−2} of the heuristic. This is a statement about the background only. Whether the insertions contract is D2.4.

### (3) The class at step k (definition, draft; repaired after d2-0a S1, S2 and d2-0c-r4 S1; a schema until D2.2 and D2.6 fix the groups and the labels at every scale, d2-0a m14, d2-0c-r4 m11)

This generalises the IT-1 note §4 from k = 1. A *dressed loop of step k* on a labelled term of ρ_k (CRE (2.18), with the labels of D1.0(P) at every scale) is a function of V_k and of the outer variables that the term holds constant, of the form

𝒲_k = κ^{(k)} e^{𝓔_k} [ f_k + Σ_{𝔅≠∅ compatible} Q_k(𝔅)·dim(π)⁻¹ tr(Y_{W₁}U_{(1)} ⋯ Y_{W_m}U_{(m)}) ].

- *Outer variables:* the pending variables of T_k: the V_j on Ω_{j+1}ᶜ and the fluctuation fields A_j on Z_{j+1} ∩ Ω_{j+1} (CRE (2.20)–(2.21)), the exterior and de-younged variables of the labels (IT-1 note §4, D1.1).
- *κ^{(k)} := ∏_{j=1}^{k} κ_j* with κ_j := e^{−δm_jP_{j−1}(C)}z_j⁴, the exact step-j perimeter and corner coefficients. With δm_1 := δm^σP(C_n)/P_0(C_n) and z_1 := z_c^σ, κ_1 is V3's e^{−δm^σP(C_n)}(z_c^σ)⁴ (D1(i)); this holds for any normalisation of P(C_n) proportional to n (d2-0a m9).
- *Leading word f_k := dim(π)⁻¹tr π(Hol_C(U_k)),* a scalar (d2-0a m7). At k = 1, D1's leading word is V3's f_ξ(m̂) = W_χ(C;U_ξ(m̂)), which contains the shift (IT-1 note §3j(4)); D(1) ⊇ D1 writes f_ξ(m̂) − f_1 as att(1, ·) insertions by the telescoping of *Species* below (d2-0c-r3 m1): by IT-1 note §3j(2) it depends on Λ only within R_N of C, and it is driven by the large-field and collar data through ℓ_Λ (d2-0a-r2 m7; its size and its place in the label sum are the D2.6 item of IT-1 note §3j(0)). The holonomy is read as a product, over the step-k bonds of C, of the transports of U_k along their fine bonds. Where C crosses a large-field region, outer variables enter as in D1.1 (IT-1 note §3d(1)).
- *Groups, families, insertions, words, interactions:* as in IT-1 note §4, with V3's groups replaced by the groups that the step-k map produces (D2.2). Their sizes are measured in step-k units.
  - *Interaction clause in cluster form (added by D2.3(3) after d2-3 S1, 22:3x, 22 Oct, date; not reviewed).* At every step, Q_k(𝔅) = exp(−Σ_{Γ hits 𝔅}φ_k(Γ)), the sum over the clusters Γ of the step-k reference group gas (a pure hard-core gas) that are incompatible with some member of 𝔅, with φ_k(Γ) numbers given the labels that depend on C only through J_Γ, the traversals in the arcs of Γ's groups, and Σ_{Γ hits W}|φ_k(Γ)| ≤ θ(W). At k = 1 this is IT-1 note §4's form. The subset form Q_k(𝔅) = exp(−Σ_SΦ_k(S)) is withdrawn: from step 2 on it breaks contour locality.
- *Scalar exponent* 𝓔_k = Σ_{j≤k}Σ_X e^{(j)}_X + E_const,k, a sum of local terms (the carrier parts and the thin E^∂ of IT-1 note §3i at every scale) plus a constant of the contour and the torus.
- *The deviation quantity (after d2-0a S1).* Put η_k := L^{−k}. For a traversal n of a step-k bond of C,
  δ*_n^{(k)}(U) := η_k^{−2}Σ_{t≥1}w_tΔ_n^{(k)}(R_t^{(k)}), w_t := (t+1)³e^{−(31/8)η_Vt},
  Δ_n^{(k)}(R) := max{δ_p(U) : p a fine plaquette of Ω_k (of Λ₁ at k = 1) within step-k distance R of n}, with max ∅ := 0. The step-k distance is V3's d_T times η_k (d2-0a-r2 m8).
  δ_p is V3's plaquette deviation, η_V is V3's exponent η (renamed, since η is the fine spacing here), and the radii R_t^{(k)} are fixed by D2.2's group scale, with R_t^{(1)} := C_boxM(t+1), V3's C_boxLM(t+1) in step-1 units.
  - (a) At k = 1, δ*_n^{(1)}(U) ≥ L²δ*_{n_f}(U) for every fine traversal n_f ⊂ n, with δ*_{n_f} of IT-1 note §3a(6): η_1^{−2} = L², and the neighbourhoods of n contain those of n_f. A block-plaquette deviation would not do: it does not dominate D1(ii)'s fine-plaquette deviations, and there is no pointwise bound of step-k block deviations of U_{k+1} by step-(k+1) block deviations, since sub-plaquette fluxes can cancel.
  - (b) For k ≥ 2, on the support of the step-k characteristic functions on which p.268 states the regularity (k ≤ N; for k > N, D2.7), δ*_n^{(k)}(U_k) ≤ 2c_σB₃ε_kΣ_tw_t, by p.268 at the step k−1 → k (|∂U_k − 1| < 2B₃ε_kη_k² on Ω_k), with c_σ converting CRE's matrix norm into V3's |·|_σ (d2-0a-r2 m3). At k = 1, δ_p(U₁) ≤ Cε₁ for p ⊂ Λ₁ (V3 L5033, Lemma c2-plaquette-deviation), so δ*_n^{(1)}(U₁) ≤ CL²ε₁Σ_tw_t (d2-0a m5).
  - (c) For the same plaquettes and radii, η_{k+1}^{−2}max = L²·η_k^{−2}max for every U. This is the exact form of the L^{−2} of (5). The plaquette set changes from Ω_k to Ω_{k+1} (Λ₁ to Ω₂ at the first step), so the maximum in δ*^{(k)}_n(U_{k+1}) is split (d2-0a-r2 m1): the plaquettes of Ω_{k+1} give ≤ L^{−2}δ*^{(k+1)} after D2.4's matching; those of Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1} contribute at most η_k^{−2}·sup_{p⊂Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1}}δ_p(U_{k+1})·Σ_{t: R_t^{(k)} ≥ dist(n, Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1})}w_t, which by the membership condition (2.34) at j = k+1, n = k (|∂U − 1| < (1 − β/2)α_{0,k}η_k²) is ≤ c_σ(1 − β/2)α_{0,k}Σ_{t: …}w_t once U_{k+1} is shown to lie in the space (d2-0c-r3 m3). Here α_{0,k} = g_kC₀(log g_k⁻²)^{q₀} (CRE (2.28), p.259) is bounded uniformly in k, since x(log x⁻²)^{q₀} increases for log x⁻² > 2q₀ and g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K by the last inequality of CRE (2.6), under the condition (1+β₀)g_K < e^{−q₀} (its users and its status at general G: coupling item of the gaps block). A weaker input suffices (d2-0c-r4 m7): g_k ∈ [0, γ] with γ < e^{−q₀} gives α_{0,k} ≤ C₀γ(log γ^{−2})^{q₀} with no uniform form of (2.6). RG1 Theorem 3 (p.264) is stated "for every coupling sequence in ]0,γ]" (Lipschitz note §5); that CRE's sequence stays in ]0, γ] at every step is part of the coupling item. If it does, this use of R-cpl₀ can be dropped. That part decays with the distance to Ω_{k+1}ᶜ and joins the scale-(k+1) attached part. Both the matching of the neighbourhoods and radii of an old traversal n with those of the new traversal n′ ⊇ n and this split are D2.4 items. The block-plaquette bound of (2) is a corollary (lasso step), not the class quantity.
- *Species (after d2-0c-r3 S1; split by kind after d2-0c-r4 S1).* Every insertion Y_W carries a species, fixed by the labels and the group geometry, at production and at each step's reclassification (d2-0c-r4 m3): *dev*; *att(j, Z)*, attached at step j to a large-field component Z by a non-good group; *att(j, coll)*, a collar part made at step j, with no component (after d2-0c-r5 S2: one collar insertion is bounded by the marks of all of W's domains and inputs, which can be assigned to different components, so a species att(j, coll Z) is not defined for it; the bound is split by component instead of the insertion, (ii′-b)); *lw(Z)*, the telescoped leading-word parts assigned to Z; *wrap(j)*. The words are expanded multilinearly in the species. A split of one insertion by the sizes of its bounds would be field-dependent and not analytic, and would break D(k)(iii); so the split is made in the formulas.
  - *Assignment rules (d2-0c-r4 S1, m2).* Fix an order of the components of B_ξ at each step. A non-good group goes to the first component that its Q_W meets. Each bond mark, host mark and free input b of the collar data goes to the nearest component of the step-j large-field set Z_j, ties broken by the order. A telescoped bond i goes to the component nearest to i.
  - At step 1 the split exists: Υ¹_W = (Υ¹_W − Υ⁰_W) + Υ⁰_W (IT-1 note §3a(1)). Υ⁰_W is dev for good groups (§3a(3)); otherwise it is att(1, Z) in case (i) of §3a(4), with Z the component of B_ξ that Q_W meets first, and wrap(1) in case (ii), where Q_W does not lift, bounded by Cρe^{−cN_min/(LM)} and decaying from no component (d2-0c-r4 m1). Υ¹_W − Υ⁰_W is att(1, coll) (§3a(2), (7)).
  - *The telescoped leading word (d2-0c-r3 m1; slot form after d2-0c-r4 m4).* f_ξ(m̂) − f_1 = Σ_idim(π)^{−1}tr(T_1⋯T_{i−1}Y_iT_iT̂_{i+1}⋯T̂_P), Y_i := π(T̂_iT_i^{−1}) − 1, with T, T̂ the transports of U₁ and U_ξ(m̂), over the fine bonds i of C in order. The identity is exact; each term is a one-insertion word with unitary transports, slot Y_i of species lw(Z) for the Z that i is assigned to, θ := 0, and "i ∈ n" read for "W ∋ n" in ν_n. Σ_{i∈n}‖Y_i‖ is added to the displayed D(1)(ii). Its size is the D2.6 item of IT-1 note §3j(0): per bond ‖Y_i‖ is of the order of g₀ times the shift m̂ near its source, not ρ̂₁ (d2-0c-r4 S1). Keeping the shift in the leading word is the alternative; it is not taken, since D2.1 is written for f_k = tr Hol(U_k).
    - *In the family sum (after d2-0c-r5 m5).* An lw slot forms a one-element family with Q_k = 1, incompatible with every other insertion, so the family sum reproduces the identity exactly. On a term with no large-field component the lw slots, if any, take lw(∅) and are bounded as a whole by (ii′-c) with the sum over all i; "the component nearest to i" is used only when a component exists.
  - At step k+1 the old dev insertions are reclassified: dev if their comparison box (the plaquettes their deviation bound uses) lies in Ω_{k+1}, att(k+1, Z) if it meets Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1}. Z is the first component, in the fixed order, of Ω_{k+1}ᶜ that the comparison box meets; one exists, since Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1} ⊆ Ω_{k+1}ᶜ (after d2-0c-r5 m4). The review's form, a component of the step-(k+1) large-field set, needs Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1} inside that set. That can fail where Ω_{k+1}, a union of the coarser cubes of CRE p.256, loses MR_k-cubes next to older regions. The charge of such a part to the data its component of Ω_{k+1}ᶜ contains, of any step ≤ k+1, is D2.6's. This is the structural form of the split of the maximum in (3)(c).
  - *Insertions made at step k+1 (d2-0c-r4 m3; kinds fixed after d2-0c-r5 m3).* The fresh insertions of D2.2(5)(e) and (6) take the step-(k+1) split of D2.2(6)(c): the deviation parts are dev, and the parts meeting Λ_{k+1}ᶜ or depending on ω′ are attached, with the kind and component fixed by the step-1 rule at step k+1:
    - a part whose group has Q_W meeting a step-(k+1) large-field component is att(k+1, Z), Z the first such component;
    - a part that depends on outer data only through collar marks is att(k+1, coll);
    - a part whose Q_W does not lift at step k+1 is wrap(k+1).
  - A fluctuation part Y(U_k) − Y(U_{k+1}) keeps its parent's species. An insertion made from several parents is dev only if all are dev. Otherwise it takes the first attached species of its parents in the total order (after d2-0c-r5 m2): by step j ascending, then by kind att(j, Z) < att(j, coll) < lw < wrap, then by the fixed order of the components.
  - *Units of carried groups (d2-0c-r4 m13).* For att(j, ·) groups, θ(W) and σ(W) are those of step j, when the group was made; the bound θ(W) ≤ νσ(W)/16 is read at that step.
- *D(k)(ii), per-traversal form (quantified after d2-0a S2; restated after d2-0a-r2 S1; species after d2-0c-r3 S1).* For every traversal n of a step-k bond of C, with ν_n^{(k)} := Σ_{W∋n}e^{θ(W)}‖Y_W‖ over the insertions of species dev, att(k, ·) and wrap(·),
  ν_n^{(k)} ≤ C_χρ_kδ*_n^{(k)}(U_k) + Cρ̂_ke^{−cd_k(n)/M} + Cρ_{t,k}(Σ_Δ𝔪̂^{(k)}_Δe^{−η_Vdist(Δ,n)/(C_FM)} + C′_χg_kΣ_bh(m^{(k)}_b)e^{−η_Vdist(b,n)/(C_FM)}) + Σ_{j=1}^{k}CL^{k−j}ρ̂_je^{−cN^{(j)}_min/M}.
  - *Amended by D2.4(5) (22:10, 22 Oct, date; not reviewed).* For k ≥ 2 the first term is C_χρ̃_kδ*_n^{(k)}, ρ̃_k := ρ_k/ᾱ_k, ᾱ_k := min(α_{0,k}, α_{1,k}), and ρ̃_1 := ρ_1. The factor comes from D2.4(4)'s Cauchy step. On the support ρ̃_kδ*_n = O(ρ_k) under R-m's entailments, and at complex data the size gains 1 + (log g_k^{−2})^{(q₁−q₀)₊}. Each dev insertion also carries its own bound e^{θ(W)}‖Y_W‖ ≤ b_WDev_{Q^#_W}, with Σ_{W∋n}b_WDev_{Q^#_W} ≤ C_χρ̃_kδ*_n (the per-insertion form, used by the reclassification).
    - *Tail clause (after d2-4-r1 S2, S3; D2.4(5)).* Dev_{Q^#_W}, ℓ_W and b_W stay in the normalisation of the step at which W was made, and with β_W := b_Wℓ_Wη_{j(W)}^{−2}, (ii) also contains (ii-t) Σ_{W∋n, t^{(k)}(W)≥t}β_W ≤ C_χρ̃_kη_k^{−2}W_t for every t, W_t := Σ_{t′≥t}w_{t′}. The sum form above follows from it by Abel summation; at k = 1 it follows from IT-1 note §3a(6)'s proof.
  - *Per component (d2-0c-r4 m2).* The second term is stated per component: for each step-k large-field component Z, Σ_{W∋n, W∈att(k,Z)}e^{θ(W)}‖Y_W‖ ≤ Cρ̂_ke^{−cd_k^Z(n)/M}, with d_k^Z(n) the step-k distance from n to Z's data; Cρ̂_ke^{−cd_k(n)/M} in the display is read as this family of bounds. At k = 1 it follows from the argument of IT-1 note §3a(4)(i) with Q_W ∩ Z ≠ ∅, which gives d^Z(n) ≤ diam(Q_W) + 1. The collar term is the att(k, coll) part and keeps its form; its split by the assigned component of the marks is a split of the bound ((ii′-b), after d2-0c-r5 S2). At k = 1 the display also has the lw term Σ_{i∈n}‖Y_i‖ (d2-0c-r4 m4).
  - Only the attached parts made at step k stay in (ii). Those made at earlier steps are bounded by (ii′) below, per large-field component (d2-0c-r3 S1). Reason (d2-0a-r2 S1): an object made at step j has decay length λ_{k,j} := ML^{−(k−j)} in step-k units (V3's LM, fine units at step 1, M in step-1 units; the scale-j lattice is T_{L^{−(k−j)}} in step-k units, (1)). A term Cρ̂_je^{−cd_j(n)/λ_{k,j}} kept in (ii) costs a factor L per step, as the collar parts do, so D(k)(iv) would fail. (The earlier form e^{−c(d_j(n)−1)/λ_{k,j}} carried the decay length but not the shift −1; it is withdrawn, and with it (5)(c)(ε)'s rescaling gain.) The wrap terms stay in (ii) for every j: each grows by at most L per step, so the scale-j one is ≤ CL^{k−j}ρ̂_je^{−cN^{(j)}_min/M}, small under the torus condition of (5)(g) (d2-0b-r2 m-D). Distances are step-k distances.
  - d_j(n) is the distance from n to the scale-j large-field data: E(B_ξ) of the step-j labels, together with the de-younged bonds of the step-j carrier on uncovered terms (d(n), d^Λ(n) of D1(ii) at j = 1). N^{(j)}_min is the smallest torus period in step-j units (V3's N_min/L at j = 1). 𝔪̂^{(j)}_Δ and m^{(j)}_b are the marks and shifts of the step-j labels (IT-1 note §3a(7), §3j). ρ̂_j = max(ρ_j, ρ_{ph,j}) and ρ_{t,j} are the step-j analogues of ρ̂ and ρ_t. *Fixed here (d2-0c-r3 m9):* r_k := A₁(log g_k⁻²)^{p₀}, ρ_k := C_ρg_k²(1+r_k)⁴ (the step-k analogue of V3's ρ = O(g₀²(1+r)⁴)), ρ_{t,k} := C′_χg_k(1+2r_k)/u_* and ρ_{ph,k} := C_phg_k²(1+2r_k)⁴, as at k = 1 below. D2.2 has to prove its bounds with these ρ's.
  - At k = 1 this is D1(ii), summed over the L fine traversals of each coarse bond, with C_χ ↦ L^{−1}C_χ by (a) (d2-0a m6: V3's traversals are fine, L122), and (d2-0a-r2 m6):
    - the attached constants C ↦ e^{2c/M}LC: L fine traversals, each with e^{−c(d(n_f)−1)/(LM)} ≤ e^{c/(LM)}e^{c/M}e^{−cd_1(n)/M};
    - D1(ii)'s collar term has g₀ and D(1)(ii) has g₁: C′_χ ↦ (1+β₀)C′_χ by g₀ ≤ (1+β₀)g₁ (the last inequality of CRE (2.6), p.255, n = 1, m = 0; d2-0c-r3 m8); likewise V3's ρ (at g₀) ≤ C(1+β₀)⁶ρ₁ with ρ₁ := C_ρg₁²(1+r₁)⁴ as in (5)(c).
    - The remaining conversions (d2-0c-r3 m8). ρ_t ≤ (1+β₀)²ρ_{t,1} with ρ_{t,1} := C′_χg₁(1+2r₁)/u_*: V3's ρ_t = u_t/u_* = C′_χg₀(1+2r)/u_* (V3 L3973, item 4), g₀ ≤ (1+β₀)g₁ as above, and r ≤ (1+β₀)r₁ by the second inequality of CRE (2.7) (n = 1, m = 0); u_* is fixed before the coupling (V3 L3968). In the same way ρ_ph ≤ C_phg₀²(1+2r)⁴ (V3 L2865, under its (iii)) gives ρ_ph ≤ (1+β₀)⁶ρ_{ph,1} with ρ_{ph,1} := C_phg₁²(1+2r₁)⁴, so ρ̂ ≤ C(1+β₀)⁶ρ̂₁, with C absorbing V3's O(·) in ρ = O(g₀²(1+r)⁴) (d2-0c-r4 m12). The collar term of D(1)(ii) is the sum of D1(ii)'s collar terms over the L fine traversals of n, so its constant is L times D1(ii)'s; the decay converts exactly, e^{−ηdist(Δ,n_f)/(C_FLM)} ≤ e^{−η_Vdist(Δ,n)/(C_FM)} (IT-1 note §3a(7): fine distances are L times step-1 distances, and dist(Δ,n_f) ≥ dist(Δ,n)).
  - The first term bounds the species dev; the others bound att(k, ·), att(k, coll), lw(·) (at k = 1) and wrap(·), which carry no deviation. The attached species made at step j leave (ii) at the end of step j + 1's map and are bounded from then on by (ii′) (d2-0a-r2 S1, d2-0c-r3 S1).
- *D(k)(ii′), attached parts of earlier steps (d2-0c-r3 S1; species by species after d2-0c-r4 S1).* For every j < k and every step-j large-field component Z, with the outer sums over the step-k traversals n of C, the sums in N_j over the step-j traversals n_j of C, and d_j^Z(n_j) the step-j distance from n_j to Z's step-j data:
  - (ii′-a) att(j, Z): Σ_nΣ_{W∋n, W∈att(j,Z)}e^{θ(W)}‖Y_W‖ ≤ Cρ̂_jN_j(Z), N_j(Z) := Σ_{n_j}e^{−cd_j^Z(n_j)/M};
  - (ii′-b) att(j, coll), a total split by component (after d2-0c-r5 S2): Σ_nΣ_{W∋n, W∈att(j,coll)}e^{θ(W)}‖Y_W‖ ≤ Σ_ZB_j(Z), B_j(Z) := Cρ_{t,j}[Σ_{Δ→Z}𝔪̂^{(j)}_ΔN_j(Δ) + C′_χg_jΣ_{b→Z}h(m^{(j)}_b)N_j(b)], N_j(x) := Σ_{n_j}e^{−η_Vdist(x,n_j)/(C_FM)}, the inner sums over the marks Δ and inputs b assigned to Z. The right side is the sum over n_j of D(j)(ii)'s collar term, regrouped by the assignment of the marks. (5)(f) charges B_j(Z) to Z through ∏_W(1+‖Y_W‖) ≤ exp(Σ_W‖Y_W‖) ≤ ∏_Ze^{B_j(Z)}, so its A_j(Z) is unchanged. The split is of the bound, not of the insertion, so it does not depend on the field and analyticity is kept; the carry below applies to the total;
  - (ii′-c) lw(Z) (j = 1): Σ_nΣ_{i∈n, i→Z}‖Y_i‖ ≤ Σ_{i→Z}‖π(T̂_iT_i^{−1}) − 1‖. Its size is the D2.6 item named under *Species*.
  - The constant of (ii′-a) does not fit the other two (d2-0c-r4 S1): ρ_{t,j} = C′_χg_j(1+2r_j)/u_* is of order g_j(log g_j^{−2})^{p₀}, ρ̂_j of order g_j²(log g_j^{−2})^{4p₀}, and a collar mark can be a fixed constant (IT-1 note §3a(8)), so the collar parts exceed Cρ̂_j by a factor of order 1/(g_j(log g_j^{−2})^{3p₀}). Each species therefore keeps the bound it has when it is made.
  - The carry from k to k+1 does not increase the left sides: a group that meets a new traversal n′ meets one of the L old traversals in n′, and distinct new traversals contain disjoint old ones. So no factor L per step appears, for each species line by line.
  - Only the per-component totals are controlled. At step n > j a step-n segment parallel to step-j data contains L^{n−j} step-j traversals, each carrying up to Cρ̂_j, so these species do not satisfy the per-segment cost e^{Cτ_n²} of (5)(f); (5)(f) pays them through (ii′), (ii′-b) at its own scale ρ_{t,j} times the marks, and (ii′-c) once D2.6 bounds it.
  - Stability of (ii′) under the evaluation at U_{k+1} and under the fluctuation parts of (5)(d) is a D2.6 item.
- *Norms (after d2-0a S2), at every scale j ≤ k, in step-k units except where stated (d2-0c-r3 m7):*
  - |Q_k(𝔅)| ≤ ∏_{W∈𝔅}e^{θ(W)} with θ(W) ≤ νσ(W)/16; the insertions of species dev, att(k, ·), wrap(·) by (ii), those of species att(j, ·), j < k, by (ii′), as in IT-1 note §4. *At steps k ≥ 2 (D2.4(3), not reviewed):* σ is σ_k = n_k + #_k, the step-k traversals of the arc plus the π_k-cubes of the constituents' domains, since the vacuum weight is proportional to volume; θ_k = θ_vac + θ_ref is the fresh weight of the step-k gas and does not carry θ_{k−1};
  - the step-j local terms e^{(j)}_X of 𝓔_k are indexed by ∗-connected unions X of boxes of the lattice integrated at step j, anchored at the step-j segments i, with Σ_{X∈𝒳^{(j)}_i}|e^{(j)}_{i,X}|e^{κ#boxes(X)} ≤ Cτ_j²e^{−c_thd^{(j)}_i}, and Σ_{X∩Q≠∅}|e^{(j)}_X|e^{κ_{a₂}#cubes(X)} ≤ (a₂/ℓ₀+2)⁴C_rτ_j² for every cube Q of side a₂ (IT-1 note §3i(7) at j = 1; a₂ renamed from a there, thin1-r2 m4), with e^{(j)}_X := Σ_ie^{(j)}_{i,X}. Here ℓ₀, a₂, κ_{a₂} and d^{(j)}_i (rate c_th) are in the units of the lattice integrated at step j, not in step-k units: a cube of side a₂ in step-k units meets up to (a₂L^{k−j+1}/ℓ₀ + 2)⁴ boxes of the lattice integrated at step j (the scale-(j−1) lattice, d2-0c-r3 m7), so a per-step-k-cube bound of the scale-j terms is not uniform in k − j. (5)(f) therefore pays the old terms in the anchored, per-component form (D2.6) (d2-0a-r2 m2);
  - |E_const,k| ≤ Σ_{j≤k}Cτ_j²(n_je^{−cι_{T,j}} + (n_j+1)e^{−cn_j}), with ι_{T,j} indexed by the step ((5)(g)). *Amended by D2.3(7) (corrected after d2-3 M7, S2; not re-reviewed):* the clause reads |E_const,k| ≤ Σ_{j≤k}Cτ_{j−1}²(n_je^{−cι_{T,j}} + (n_j+1)e^{−c_En_j}), the remainder of step j carrying τ_{j−1}², with n_j := s_{j−1} = ℓ_CL^{K−j+1}, the side of C in step-(j−1) bonds (defined here after d2-0c-r5 m10; (5)(g)'s n_k ≍ ℓ_CL^{K−k} is the same count with the index of the step's input lattice), and holds for the admissible sides of D2.3(4), where every step is commensurate. Without that restriction each of the at most a* other steps adds Cτ_{j−1}²(Π_j + 1 + 4s_{j−1}), Π_j the period of D2.3(4), and a* does not grow with K.
- *D(k)(iii) (after d2-0a m10):* for real U_k in the support of the term's indicators, which are kept as factors, the background-dependent factors extend analytically to 𝐔 = e^{iη_kA′}U_k, for complex parts satisfying the per-bond bounds of the complex part of (2.39) on each Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1} (the loop's factors are not localised in Bałaban's domains X ∈ 𝐃_k): |η_kA′| < (1 − β(1 − 2^{−(k−n)}))α_{1,n}L^{−n} per fine bond on Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1} (n < k) and on Ω_k (n = k), G-covariantly. For k = 1 this sup-only domain is the domain; it is larger than CRE's Ũ_k^c, which also asks (L^nξ)²|∇_U^ξA′| < (1−β(1−2^{−(k−n)}))α_{1,n}, and it is D1(iii)′'s form (d2-0a-r2 m4). For k ≥ 2 the domain is the full complex part of CRE's Ũ_k^c: (2.34)–(2.35) for 𝐔 and its averaged configurations, (2.36)–(2.37) for 𝐉, and (2.39) with both the sup and the gradient condition, as stated in the amendment below (d2-0c-r4 m10). (ii) holds there with δ*^{(k),30}_n(U_k) + a*^{(k)}_n(A′), and with ρ_{ph,k} replaced by ρ^c_{ph,k} := C_phg_k²(1+2r_k)⁴(1 + (log g_k⁻²)^{(q₁−p₀)₊}) and the tilt marks 𝔪^δ_Y of D1.2b included in 𝔪̂^{(k)}, as the IT-1 gaps block records at k = 1; the tolerance this needs is D2.4's (d2-0c-r4 m9), δ*^{(k),30}_n the quantity δ*^{(k)}_n with weights (t+1)³e^{−(30/8)η_Vt}, the rate D1(iii)′ gives at complex data (IT-1 gaps block, D1.2b; d2-0c-r3 m5), and a*^{(k)}_n(A′) := η_k^{−1}Σ_{t≥1}(t+1)e^{−(30/8)η_Vt}max{|η_kA′(b)| : b a fine bond within step-k distance R_t^{(k)} of n}, the step-k form of IT-1 note §3e(0)'s a*_n(a′) := Σ_{k≥1}(k+1)e^{−(30/8)ηk}A_n(C_boxLM(k+1)). The normalisation η_k^{−1} makes it a block-scale field: along a step-k bond the first-order change of the transport is Σ_bη_kA′(b) over its L^k fine bonds, of size |A′|. At k = 1, a*^{(1)}_n ≥ L·a*_{n_f} for every fine traversal n_f ⊂ n, as in (a). At k = 1 the radius is ϱ_{an,1} := α_{1,1}/L, the radius written ρ₁ in D1(iii)′ (renamed here, since ρ₁ is the insertion scale of (5)(c); d2-0c-r3 m4).
  - *Amended for k ≥ 2 by D2.2(6)(e) (18:26, 22 Oct, date; full space after d2-2e S4).* For k ≥ 2 the domain is CRE's: the full complex part of Ũ_k^c at 𝐔 = e^{iη_kA′}U_k, that is (2.34)–(2.35) for 𝐔 and its averaged configurations, (2.36)–(2.37) for 𝐉, and (2.39) with both the sup and the gradient condition, (L^nξ)²|∇_U^ξA′| < (1−β(1−2^{−(k−n)}))α_{1,n}, on Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1} for n = 1, …, k−1 and on Ω_k. The step-(k−1) → k integration produces factors with RG2's propagators, which are analytic only on a gradient-controlled domain (RG2 p.15). The sup-only form above stays at k = 1. The ω-clause below has radii r^{(k)}_{ω,j} per layer j (D2.2(6)(e); "per layer" after d2-0c-r5 m9).
    - *Conditions on the base point (after d2-0c-r5 m11).* CRE's space also has two conditions on the real factor U, and its analyticity statements are made on that space: the real-U part of (2.34), |∂U − 1| < (1 − β(1 − 2^{−(j−n)}))α_{0,n}ξ²(L^nξ)^{−2}, and condition (ii), (2.38) (p.261): for each cube □ ⊂ Ω_n∖Ω_{n+2} with □ ∩ Ω_{n+1}ᶜ ≠ ∅ of size CML^nξ, or □ ⊂ Ω_j of size CM, a gauge transformation u on □ ∩ X with U^u = exp iξA, L^nξ|A|, (L^nξ)²|∇^ξA| < BCMα_{0,n}. Both are required of the base point U_k here.
  - *Amended for k ≥ 2 by D2.2(6)(b) (after d2-2d S1, S2; by 19:54, 22 Oct, date).* For k ≥ 2 the factors are functions of (𝐔, 𝐉, ω), and the domain is posed on (𝐔, 𝐉): with the conditions above, CRE (2.36)–(2.37), p.261, |𝐉| < (1 − β(1 − 2^{−(k−n)}))α_{0,n}(L^nξ)^{−3} on each Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1} and on Ω_k (CRE's space Ũ_k^c is "the set of configurations (𝐔,𝐉)", p.261). Covariance is under (𝐔, 𝐉, ω) ↦ (𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉, R(u)ω) (CRE p.261: "(G-valued) gauge transformations of 𝐔, 𝐉, A (the variables 𝐉, A are transformed by the adjoint representation of the gauge transformations)"). The factors are evaluated at 𝐉 = J_k. At k = 1 the factors are V3's, functions of 𝐔 and, on uncovered terms, of the exterior fields; there is no 𝐉 (d2-0c-r5 m9).
    - *The 𝐉-term of (ii) (after d2-0c-r5 S1, 22:4x, 22 Oct, date).* The dev parts are functions of 𝐉 that vanish at (1, 0, 0), so the bound of (ii) on this domain needs a deviation term in 𝐉 − J_k; without it, at flat U_k with A′ = 0 and 𝐉 ≠ J_k, the bound would force the dev parts to vanish. With r_J(b) := (1 − β(1 − 2^{−(k−n)}))α_{0,n}(L^nξ)^{−3}, the (2.36) radius at b, and ϱ(b) := (1 − β(1 − 2^{−(k−n)}))α_{1,n}L^{−n}, the A′-radius, put
      j*^{(k)}_n(𝐉) := η_k^{−1}Σ_{t≥1}(t+1)e^{−(30/8)η_Vt}max{ϱ(b)|𝐉(b) − J_k(b)|/r_J(b) : b within step-k distance R_t^{(k)} of n}.
      For k ≥ 2, (ii) holds on the (𝐔, 𝐉) domain with δ*^{(k),30}_n(U_k) + a*^{(k)}_n(A′) + j*^{(k)}_n(𝐉). At the real point 𝐉 = J_k the new term vanishes, so (ii) at real data is unchanged, and D(1) has no 𝐉. The 𝐉-part of the dev bound is to be proved with the rest (D2.2(6), D2.4(4)): a Cauchy estimate in 𝐉 on the (2.36) polydisc, as for A′.
  - The outer fields ω_j, j ≥ 1, are slot variables: the old A_j, which receive the values ψ_{j+1}(A_j^{new}; 𝐇^{(j)}) on S_{j+1}^{(j)*} and A_j elsewhere (D2.2(6)(b)). The ω-clause is analyticity in the slot variables.
  - *Outer fluctuation fields (after d2-2b S2).* The ω-dependent factors (the insertions that consult outer fluctuation fields, on uncovered terms) extend analytically also in ω, jointly with 𝐔, to the ω within distance r_ω^{(k)} of a real point of the support, per bond (per layer j, r^{(k)}_{ω,j}: the polydiscs of D2.2(6)(e)), with the same bounds. This neighbourhood form replaces the tube |Im ω| < r_ω^{(k)}, in which the real part of a rotated ω could leave the support (d2-2b-r2 N6). The radii r^{(k)}_{ω,j} = r_{ω,j}(1/2 + 2^{−(k−j)}) are in the units of the slot variable σ_j (the old A_j, in step-j units), and r_{ω,j} is fixed at step j and does not change with k (d2-0c-r5 m9: D(k)(iv) concerns constants, and r_{ω,j} is a scale-j radius); the step-1 exterior fields have index j = 0, with r_{ω,0} = 1 in B-units (d2-0c-r4 m10). The original field V₀ on Ω₁ᶜ is part of 𝐔, not of ω (d2-2b m3). *At k = 1 (derived, 17:1x, 22 Oct, date).* In V3's free variables B (phases A = g₀C_UB), the exterior fields A_e and a_old enter the ω-dependent factors in three ways. They enter the cross term s⟨a, H_{ξ,IJ}A_e⟩ of (c4-11-1), which is linear, hence entire. They enter the near primitives 𝔭(a + sA_e) of (c4-11-1). They enter the row solutions and path products of the rows they feed. Lemma charts (L475) makes the row solution and the functions it lists analytic in B|_{𝓔_c∩E_*} on 𝒰_c = {|B(b)| < 2 + 4r}, for every row within 2R_c + 2L of I, on every branch. On the support of the term's indicators the real inputs of these rows satisfy |B(b)| ≤ 2r on D(Λ) and |B(b)| < r on I∖D(Λ) and on J (IT-1 note §3d(2)(d), after S-A). So every ω-dependent factor is analytic, jointly with the background, for |Im B(b)| < 2 per bond, which gives r_ω^{(1)} := 1 in B-units with room to spare. The joint background radius is that of §c4-10-1 (L4880). Its propagation to D(k+1)(iii) is part of D2.2(6).
  - *Reference clause (added by D2.3(2), 19:02–, 22 Oct, date; not reviewed).* On every labelled term ℓ the background-dependent factors are defined and analytic also at 𝐔 = e^{iη_kA′}·1, A′ in the domain above, for k ≥ 2 with 𝐉 in the domain of (2.36)–(2.37) around 𝐉 = 0 = J(1) (d2-0c-r5 m8), jointly with ω within its radii of ω = 0, although (1, 0) need not lie in the support of ℓ's indicators. At k = 1 this is V4 L1050 (the step-1 lemmas "apply to U = id directly", on every branch); at step k+1 D2.3(2) derives it from D2.2(6)(e)'s argument at U_{k+1} = 1. The bounds at the reference point are D2.4's.
- *D(k)(iv):* all constants are independent of k, K, the contour size, the torus and the labelled term.
- *D(k)(v), scalar parts (added from D2.2(4)(a)).* On each labelled term, Q_k(𝔅) and 𝓔_k depend on the labels only, not on V_k and not on the outer variables: they are numbers given the labels, as at k = 1 (IT-1 note §4(iii): "φ and e_X are numbers on each labelled term"; q°, E° are taken at U°₁ = id with the term's labels, V3 L986, and E^∂_{D(Λ)} at flat reference data, §3b(5)). Field-dependent scalar parts are scalar insertions (d2-0c-r3 m6). At step k+1 this is an output requirement on D2.2(6); it is derived in D2.2(6)(c), not reviewed (d2-0c-r4 m11).
- *D(k)(vi), torus consistency (added by D2.3(4), item (Z); not reviewed).* For a cluster Γ of the reference group gas of the step-k output on the trivial-label term ℓ₀ (D2.3(3)) whose groups have short arcs on a straight line or on a bent path, the weight Φ°(Γ; T) is defined on every torus T of the sequence (H3) in which Γ embeds, and has a limit Φ°^∞(Γ) as the periods grow, with Σ_{Γ: b∈J_Γ}|Φ°(Γ; T) − Φ°^∞(Γ)| ≤ C_Tτ_{k−1}²e^{−cι_{T,k}} for every traversal b. At k = 1 this is V4's periodisation (L1656, eq:regional-square-periodic-comparison, L2118). Its proof at step k+1 is D2.4's.
- *D(k), the identity.* The numerator density after k steps is N_k^C = Σχ_k(Ω_k)T_k[𝒲_k exp A_k], over the admissible sequences of ρ_k. For k ≤ N the basic step of the R-operation (LFR I Sect. 1) acts on no component (reading R-h); that no other part of R acts for k ≤ N is a D2.7 check (d2-0a-r2 m5), and with it N_k^C is T applied k times to W(C;U)ρ₀ with CRE's expansions (d2-0c-r3 m12). From step N + 1 on, R acts on the numerator; D2.7.
  - *Reading R-h (d2-0a m11).* LFR I p.177 applies the R-operation to components of Λ_k^c that satisfy (ii): "in the preceding N renormalization steps no new large field regions were created inside this component". Every component of Λ_k^c contains a region created at some step j ∈ [1, k], so for k ≤ N, (ii) fails if "the preceding N steps" includes all of the k steps that exist. p.178 (1.2) supports this reading: it writes T_k(Z) through the last N one-step operations, from h = k − N, which needs h ≥ 0, i.e. k ≥ N; and (ii) excludes k = N (d2-0a-r2 m5). The print does not state the case k ≤ N. That only the basic step's class is touched by R (D2.7) is not stated in the pages read.

### (4) What the step map k → k+1 must do

- *D2.1, the leading word at step k+1.* Write f_k(U_k) through the step-(k+1) background and fluctuation: U_k at V_k = exp i[g_kCA_k − hD̃(g_kCA_k)]V^{(k)} (CRE (3.15)). Sources: CRE pp.262–268, VAR Prop. 9 and (190) (IT-1 note §6(f3)).
- *D2.2, the small-field map.* The analogue of V3's Lemma c5-5-3 at step k+1 with a dressed loop as observable: the old insertions become part of the observable (IT-1 note §6(a)). Sources: CRE Sect. 3, RG1, RG2, PRT, PBG, VAR.
- *D2.3, exact absorption (IT-3).* δm_{k+1} and z_{k+1} from the step-(k+1) flat reference integrals and from the scalar parts of pair contractions of step-k insertions (IT-1 note §6(b)).
- *D2.4, the contraction count* (IT-1 note §6(c)), with (2) as its background input.
- *D2.5, gauge covariance at step k+1,* for all groups (IT-1 note §5 S3).
- *D2.6, large fields:*
  - the attached terms of D(k)(ii);
  - the non-scalar large-field parts (IT-1 note §6(d));
  - the exposed segments (IT-1 gaps block);
  - the carrier at step k+1: p.256's "other conditions", domains made by a retreat, and the extra outer label (IT-1 note §3c(4′), (4⁗)). This item is an input of D2.2 and of (5)(c)'s evaluation at U_{k+1}, so it is done with D2.2 (d2-0b m12);
  - the lifetime payment of (5)(f): Bałaban's induction pp.384–389 with the enlarged summand.
- *D2.7, the R-operation with a defect,* from step N + 1 on. It includes LFR I's construction of Z from Z_h after p.187 (IT-1 note §3f(3) S1).
- *Also needed (d2-0a m12):* the propagation of D(k)(iii) to D(k+1)(iii); the step-(k+1) assembly, the analogue of D1.0 with CRE (3.16); the localisation of 𝓔_{k+1}; the units of VAR (190) in older large-field regions (D2.1(3), (4) case (B)).

### (5) Count check of D(k) → D(k+1) (count × size × weight per scale)

Regime: a loop of physical side ℓ_C, K steps, P_k(C) = 4ℓ_CL^{K−k}. τ_k and ρ_k are the step-k analogues of V3's τ = C[g(1+r)³ + e^{−cr²}] and ρ = O(g²(1+r)⁴), r = A₁(log g⁻²)^{p₀}, so τ_k² and ρ_k are g_k² times a power of log g_k⁻². The question for each class is whether D(k+1) can hold with the constants of D(k).
- *The couplings (d2-0b m1).* CRE (2.6) with n = K, m = k gives the lower bound 1/g_k² ≤ 1/g_K² + β′(K−k), and g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K. The upper bound g_k² ≤ C/(K−k+1) is RG I (0.31) (Thm 2, p.259): with ε = L^{−K}, 1/g_k² ≥ 1/g_K² + β(K−k)log L. It is printed for G = SU(2) only, with its proof announced for a separate paper; accepted as Bałaban's result for SU(2). For general G it is not printed (gaps block). Only (b) used the upper bound, and after D2.3(7) it does not (note in (b)). The uniform form g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K (the last inequality of CRE (2.6) at n − m = K − k) is used by (3)(c)'s bound of α_{0,k}, (c)'s feedback factor, (d)'s factor L²g_k²ρ_k/ρ_{k+1}, (f)'s τ̄ and D2.2(4)'s smallness conditions; its status at general G is the coupling item of the gaps block (d2-0c-r3 m13).

- *Convention for the deviation.* δ*_n^{(k)} is the quantity of (3) (after d2-0a S1): fine-plaquette deviations near n divided by the fine-plaquette area in step-k units, η_k² = L^{−2k}. The same fine plaquette has area L^{−2(k+1)} in step-(k+1) units, so the same maximum gives δ*^{(k+1)} = L²δ*^{(k)} for every U ((3)(c)). By (2), the fine plaquettes of Ω_{k+1} have |∂p − 1| < 2B₃ε_{k+1}η_{k+1}² under U_{k+1}, so δ*^{(k+1)}(U_{k+1}) ≤ 2c_σB₃ε_{k+1}Σ_tw_t there, with c_σ of (3)(b) (d2-0c-r3 m14); by the lasso step a step-k plaquette of Ω_{k+1} deviates under U_{k+1} by at most 2B₃ε_{k+1}L^{−2}, a corollary only.
  - (3)(a) matches δ*^{(1)} with IT-1 note §3a(6) at k = 1. Matching the neighbourhoods of n with those of n′ is in D2.4.

- **(a) Perimeter scalar parts.** Size of order τ_k² per step-k bond (V3 L158, checked 15:45, 22 Oct (date), at hash a520a59f363d: |κ^σ_v| ≤ Cτ² (the sentence following eq:marginal-full-perimeter, by eq:marginal-cluster-tail, with eq:marginal-square-mass) gives |δm^σ|P(C_n) = n|Σ_vκ^σ_v| ≤ 4Cnτ². L158 is one long TeX line, the "(1) The scales" remark after the main theorem, and it also holds the perimeter-rate sentence that the definitions quote; the excerpt goes into the next packet, d2-0b-r2 m-I). Count P_k. P_kτ_k² ≍ L^{K−k}(K−k+1)^{−1}·polylog → ∞ (with the SU(2) upper bound; without it, CRE (2.6) gives g_k² ≥ g_K²/((1+β₀)²(K−k)) and CRE (2.7) (p = 6p₀) gives (log g_k⁻²)^{6p₀} ≥ (1+β₀)^{−1}(log g_K⁻²)^{6p₀}, so P_kτ_k² ≥ cℓ_CL^{K−k}(K−k)^{−1}g_K²(log g_K⁻²)^{6p₀}/(1+β₀)³, also → ∞; d2-0b-r2 m-A). The flat-reference, translation-invariant part is **absorbed exactly** into δm_{k+1}, with zero remainder (D2.3). Background-dependent scalar parts of deviation size stay as scalar insertions, since 𝓔_{k+1} holds numbers only (D(k+1)(v); d2-0c-r3 m6, d2-2b m8), and history-dependent parts near large-field regions go to (f) (d2-0b m3).
- **(b) Corner parts.** Four per step. After exact absorption into z_{k+1}, a remainder r_k = O(τ_k²g_k^δ), δ > 0, is summable uniformly in K under the upper bound of the couplings: Σ_k(K−k+1)^{−1−δ/2}·polylog(K−k+1) < ∞. With g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K only, the available bound Σ_{k≤K}r_k ≤ CKg_K^{2+δ}polylog is not uniform in K (d2-0b-r2 m-F). A remainder of order τ_k² is not summable in either case (iteration audit §4(2)). **Absorb exactly, remainder O(τ_k²g_k^δ)** (D2.3), and for general G either the upper bound or a zero corner remainder is needed (gaps block). *Closing route D2.3(6)–(7) (reviewed d2-3-r1; restated after its S2 and M9):* the exact absorption leaves, at a commensurate step, the remainder C_Eτ_k²(s_k+1)e^{−c_Es_k} with s_k = ℓ_CL^{K−k}, which decays in the contour size at that step rather than carrying a factor g_k^δ. For the admissible sides of D2.3(4) every step is commensurate; without that restriction the at most a* other steps leave O(τ_k²(Π_{k+1}+1+s_k)). For the admissible sides the sum over k is at most C_Στ̄² uniformly in K and ℓ_C, using g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K only (R-cpl₀). This holds once D2.4 proves (T), (Z) and the Kotecký–Preiss condition with constants independent of k; until then (b) is open with this as its route. So (b) needs neither the upper bound of the couplings nor a zero remainder, and the users of R-cpl listed above drop to none.
- **(c) Deviation parts of the old insertions.** They are carried, never summed in absolute value.
  - Each new traversal n′ (a step-(k+1) bond of C) contains L old traversals.
  - Split an old insertion as Y(U_k) = Y(U_{k+1}) + [Y(U_k) − Y(U_{k+1})].
  - The first part is bounded by C_χρ_kδ*^{(k)}_n(U_{k+1}) ≤ C_χρ_k[L^{−2}δ*^{(k+1)}_{n′}(U_{k+1}) + B_n] (d2-0c-r3 m2): the first term after (γ)'s matching, and B_n the Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1} part of (3)(c), which joins the scale-(k+1) attached part (species att(k+1, ·), (3)). The equality of the convention above holds only for the same plaquettes and radii; here the plaquette set changes from Ω_k to Ω_{k+1}, and the traversal and radii from n to n′.
  - So, for the first term, the L old traversals of n′ contribute at most L^{−1}(ρ_k/ρ_{k+1})·C_χρ_{k+1}δ*_{n′}^{(k+1)}(U_{k+1}). Here ρ_k/ρ_{k+1} ≤ (1+β₀)⁶, with ρ_k := C_ρg_k²(1+r_k)⁴: g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_{k+1} (CRE (2.6)) and (log g_k⁻²)^p ≤ (1+β₀)(log g_{k+1}⁻²)^p (the second inequality of CRE (2.7), with m = k, n = k+1; d2-0c-r5 m6).
  - With the feedback of (d) the constants obey C_χ^{(k+1)} ≤ C_new + (L^{−1} + C_dL²(1+β₀)²g_K²)(1+β₀)⁶C_χ^{(k)} + C_d′ρ_k(C_χ^{(k)})², which is bounded uniformly in k for L ≥ 2, β₀ small and g_K ≤ g(L) (d2-0b m6). The factor (1+β₀)²g_K² and the quadratic term use g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K, the last inequality of CRE (2.6) at n − m = K − k, uniformly in K − k (reading R-cpl₀ of the gaps block; d2-0c-r3 m13).
  - *Corrected by D2.4(6) (22:10, 22 Oct, date; not reviewed).* With D2.4(5)'s ρ̃_k the ratio is ρ̃_k/ρ̃_{k+1} ≤ (1+β₀)⁸, since α_{i,k+1} ≤ (1+β₀)²α_{i,k} by CRE (2.6)–(2.7) at n − m = 1. So the factor (1+β₀)⁶ above becomes (1+β₀)⁸, and "β₀ small" means (1+β₀)⁸ < L. CRE p.255 allows β₀ that small for small g. C_new = C_gC_S comes from D2.4(4), which rests on (LPG) and (S) (OPEN).
  - **Needs D2.4**: that the deviation part of an old insertion is bounded by the deviation of its argument, as D1(ii)'s first term states at step 1. Then the contraction L^{−1} per step follows from this count. The count also uses (d2-0b m2):
    - (α) the evaluation point: by RG I (2.3), p.265, V^{(k)} = M^k(U_{k+1}). By the unique minimal orbit (CRE p.256 l.44–51) and the gauge condition, U_k(M^k(U_{k+1})) = U_{k+1}, i.e. U_k′(1) = 1 in "U_k(V′V^{(k)}) = U_k′(V′)U_{k+1}" (RG I p.265) (d2-0b-r2 m-E; the earlier pointer "RG I p.256" is withdrawn);
    - (β) V^{(k)} in the support of the step-k indicators. CRE (2.17) tests U_{k,□}(V_k) on the cubes □ ⊂ Ω_k, and p.268 bounds U_{k+1} only on Ω_{k+1}. So the plaquette test at V_k = V^{(k)} needs the comparison of U_{k,□}(V^{(k)}) with U_{k+1} on □^∼ (d2-0b-r2 m-C). *Repaired after d2-0c-r3 m10 (derived from Bałaban's own steps, between 17:28 and 17:36, 22 Oct, date).* Let □ ⊂ Ω_{k+1}^∼ with □ ⊂ □′ ⊂ R_{k+1}^c, □′ a cube of (3.16) (this holds on Λ_{k+1}^∼, CRE (3.20), p.269). By (3.19), p.268, |V^{(k)}(b)(V^{(k)}_{□′}(b))^{−1} − 1| < δ_k for b ∈ (□′^{∼2})^{(k)*}, so V_k = V^{(k)} passes (3.3)'s test (threshold 2δ_k). The argument of 𝐇_{k,□} in (3.6) is then bounded by 2δ_k ≤ 4δ_k off the boundary layer, since |(1/i)log x| ≤ 2|x − 1| for |x − 1| ≤ 1/2 (d2-0c-r4 m6(c)), as p.266 requires, and U_{k+1,□′} passes (3.2)'s test since □′ ⊂ P_{k+1}^c. So (3.7) and (3.8) hold at V_k = V^{(k)} as printed, and χ_k(□) = 1 there (p.266: "Thus χ_k(□) = 1 for □ ⊂ Ω_{k+1}^∼"). (3.8) uses the test of (3.2) at threshold ε_{k+1}(L^{−1}η)², not p.268's regularity at 2B₃ε_{k+1}(L^{−1}η)², and needs only 2(1+β₀)L^{−2} + 4/10 < 1. The earlier route through 2B₃(1+β₀) ≤ L² is withdrawn: B₃ depends on L (VAR Thm 1, p.279: "a₀, a₁, B₃, depend on d and L only"), so that condition cannot be met by the choice of L. *The cubes with □′ ⊂ R_{k+1} (d2-0c-r4 m6(b), checked against p.268 and CMP98 pp.33, 42).* p.268 derives (3.19) from (3.17), the regularity of U_{k+1} on Ω_{k+1} (which bounds 𝐇_{k+1,□′} on □′^{∼2}), (3.18), and CMP98 (106)–(108), (159)–(163). (106)–(108) are identities for the gauge transformation u of CMP98 (p.33 does not name the gauge; d2-0c-r5 m7), and (159)–(163) bound Ū^k(Ū_0^k)^{−1} for U = U′U_0 from |(1/i)log \bar{\bar U}′^j| < 2α₁L^jη (the doubly barred Ū′ as printed in (161)) (CMP98 (161), from its Proposition 4); here U′ is the factor exp iL^{−1}η𝐇_{k+1,□′} of (3.17). (3.19) bounds V^{(k)}, a function not of the fluctuation A_k (p.268: "the characteristic functions χ_k′ depend now on the field V^{(k)}, hence on the new field variables V_{k+1}"), and no step of this derivation uses (3.16)'s test, which bears on the fluctuation A_k. So (3.19) holds for every cube □′ of (3.16), and the cubes □ ⊂ □′ ⊂ R_{k+1} are covered by the argument above. Still in D2.6's carrier item: the cubes □ ⊂ Ω_{k+1}^∼ in the band Ω_{k+1}^∼∖Ω_{k+1}^{∼−1}, which lie in no cube □′ of (3.16), since (3.16) partitions only Ω_{k+1}^{∼−1} (p.268; d2-0c-r4 m6(a)), and the cubes □ ⊂ Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1}^∼ whose localisation domain □^{∼4} (CRE (2.16)) meets Ω_{k+1}, which see V^{(k)} there too (d2-0c-r3 m10). Pages (d2-0c-r4 m6(d)): (3.2), (3.3), P_{k+1} and δ_k on p.265 (printed "δ_k = g_kA_1/A_0p_0(g_k)"; (3.19) uses ε_k = (A₀/A₁)δ_k); (3.16)–(3.19) on p.268; (3.20) on p.269; B₃ in VAR Thm 1, p.279;
    - (γ) the step-k neighbourhoods of the L old traversals lie in the step-(k+1) neighbourhood of n′ (D2.4, with the radii of (3));
    - (δ) ρ_k := C_ρg_k²(1+r_k)⁴ with r_k := A₁(log g_k⁻²)^{p₀}, as fixed in (3) (d2-0c-r3 m9);
    - (ε) the attached parts of the old insertions at U_{k+1}. Kept in D(k+1)(ii), the L old traversals of n′ would add them, a factor L per step (n′ parallel to the data), which is not controlled. So they leave (ii) at the end of step k+1's map and keep their species att(k, ·), bounded by (ii′) with j = k and paid per component in (f) (corrected after d2-0c-r5 m1: as att(k+1, Z) they would sit in D(k+1)(ii), the factor-L situation excluded here) (d2-0c-r4 m5; r3 S1(b) showed that they cannot be scalar local activities); D2.6. (The earlier "rescaling gain" for the large-field term came from a shift −1 that D(k)(ii) did not carry; it is withdrawn, d2-0a-r2 S1.) The wrap term stays: it grows by at most L per step and is bounded by CL^{K−j}ρ̂_je^{−cN^{(j)}_min/M} at step K (d2-0b-r2 m-D; the factor Cρ̂_j restored, d2-0c-r3 m11). With N^{(j)}_min ≍ T_phL^{K−j} ((g)) and x := L^{K−j} ≥ 1 this is Cρ̂_jxe^{−c′T_phx/M}, and xe^{−ax} ≤ e^{−a} for x ≥ 1 once a ≥ 1; so each term is at most Cρ̂_je^{−c′T_ph/M}. That alone gives K terms of this size, which is not uniform in K (d2-0c-r4 m8). With a := c′T_ph/M ≥ 1 and L ≥ 2, L^ie^{−aL^i} = e^{−a}L^ie^{−a(L^i−1)} ≤ e^{−a}L^ie^{−(L^i−1)}, and Σ_{j≤K}L^{K−j}e^{−aL^{K−j}} ≤ e^{−a}Σ_{i≥0}L^ie^{−(L^i−1)} ≤ 2e^{−a}: the terms are 1, ≤ 0.74, ≤ 0.20, ≤ 0.01, …, their values at L = 2, since xe^{−(x−1)} decreases for x ≥ 1. So the sum over j is at most 2C(sup_jρ̂_j)e^{−c′T_ph/M}, small under the condition T_ph ≥ C·M added to (g). At j near K the condition T_ph ≫ ℓ_C alone would not do;
    - (ζ) the plaquettes of Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1}, where the maximum of δ*^{(k)} is not matched by δ*^{(k+1)}: the split of (3)(c) (d2-0a-r2 m1), a D2.4 item.
- **(d) Fluctuation parts of the old insertions. Needs D2.1–D2.5** (d2-0b m6). Y(U_k) − Y(U_{k+1}) is of order ρ_kg_k, with zero mean at first order in the step-(k+1) fluctuation.
  - Its second-order mean is O(ρ_kg_k²), and it is perimeter-proportional (P_kρ_kg_k² → ∞). At flat U_{k+1} the mean is scalar, by invariance under constant gauge transformations and Schur's lemma; that part goes to (a). The rest is O(ρ_kg_k²·curvature) and stays in the class, as for the pair contractions below (d2-0b m4).
  - Its pair contractions are of order ρ_k²g_k² per pair with another old insertion, and ρ_kg_k² per pair with the fluctuation of the leading word, which is O(g_k) per step-k bond. They decay over one new block, since the step-(k+1) covariance decays at the scale-(k+1) block scale (the analogue of μ_H = γ_L/(8Lm_L) in fine units; IT-1 note (D0)). So there are O(L²) pairs per new traversal, with a constant that depends on L through the decay rate (μ_H, not supplied here).
  - Gauge covariance makes the leading part of each pair contraction scalar. Convention (d2-0b m5; d2-0b-r2 m-G): t_a is an orthonormal basis of 𝔤 for the invariant metric, T^a := π_*(t_a), C₂(π) := Σ_aT^aT^a (positive for 𝔤 realised by Hermitian matrices, U = exp iA as in CRE), Ad(X)t_b = Σ_aAd(X)^{ab}t_a, and X is the transport between the two insertion points in the orientation of the word. If the covariance of the fluctuations at n and n″ is c(n,n″)·Ad(X_{n→n″}), then XT^bX^{−1} = Σ_cAd(X)^{cb}T^c gives Σ_{a,b}T^aXT^bAd(X)^{ab} = Σ_{a,c}T^aT^cX(Ad(X)Ad(X)ᵀ)^{ac} = (Σ_aT^aT^a)X = C₂(π)X, since Ad(X) is orthogonal and Σ_aT^aT^a is scalar by Schur. (With the transposed convention the identity fails, e.g. for SU(2).) For covariant families M^a, N^a (π(g)M^aπ(g)^{−1} = Σ_cAd(g)^{ca}M^c and likewise for N^a, for all g ∈ G; d2-0b-r2 m-G), the same computation gives Σ_{a,b}M^aXN^bAd(X)^{ab} = (Σ_aM^aN^a)X, and Σ_aM^aN^a commutes with π(G), hence is scalar; this covers the pairs of old insertions. The scalar part goes to (a). The non-scalar part is the deviation of the covariance from the transported form, which is of the order of the background curvature between n and n″.
  - Within one new block the curvature between n and n″ is summed over at most L² step-k plaquettes, so it is O(L²δ*^{(k)}) = O(δ*^{(k+1)}). So the non-scalar remainder per new traversal is O(L²ρ_kg_k²δ*^{(k+1)}). Against the class bound ρ_{k+1}δ*^{(k+1)} this is the factor L²g_k²ρ_k/ρ_{k+1} ≤ (1+β₀)⁸L²g_K², small for g_K ≤ g(L) (g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K, CRE (2.6); d2-0b m6). With D2.4(5)'s ρ̃ the old dev bound is ρ̃_kδ*, and against ρ̃_{k+1}δ*^{(k+1)} the factor is L²g_k²ρ̃_k/ρ̃_{k+1} ≤ (1+β₀)^{10}L²g_K², which is D2.4(6)'s C_dL²(1+β₀)²g_K²·(1+β₀)⁸ (d2-4-r1 m11).
- **(e) Fresh insertions.** These come from integrating the step-(k+1) fluctuations of the leading word f_k. They are of order C_newρ_{k+1}δ*^{(k+1)} per new traversal: V3's §3a deviation form at step k+1, with D2.1's form of f_k. **Needs D2.1–D2.2.**
- **(f) Large-field attached parts and exposed segments.**
  - Count: P_k·p_k, with p_k the probability that a step-k bond lies near a large-field region. p_k ≤ e^{−c(log g_k⁻²)^{2p₀}} is proved; a lower bound of the same order is expected (IT-1 note §3d) and not proved (d2-0b m7). The available bound P_kp_k = 4ℓ_CL^{K−k}e^{−c(log g_k⁻²)^{2p₀}} diverges as K − k → ∞, since (log(K−k))^{2p₀} = o(K−k), so the available estimates cannot bound these terms: **carry them as local activities attached to the large-field regions**, as V3's E_bdry cannot be bounded (iteration audit §4(5)).
  - *Payment, per component and over its lifetime (rewritten after d2-0b S1).* LFR II p.381 gives the fundamental factor exp(−½γ₀A₁²p₀²(g_j)(d_j′(Z_j^{(i)})+1) − 2p₀(g_j)) for each large-field region Z_j^{(i)} *created at step j* (IT-1 note R-d″(ii) gives the step-1 form, with ½c₄ϑ²A₁²p₀²(g₁)). By p.384 the factor of a component Z of Z_j is exp(−κ_j(Z) − 2p₀(g_{j(Z)})), and it "controls K renormalization steps" through (1.80). The attached and exposed costs near Z recur at every one of these steps, so the payment must cover all of them.
  - *Per-step cost.* The contour has at most c_C·MR_n scale-n bonds per MR_n-cube, c_C depending only on the shape of C (c_C = 4 for a rectangle: each straight side meets a cube of side MR_n in at most MR_n bonds). So at step n the costs e^{Cτ_n²} per segment cost at most e^{Cc_CMR_nτ_n²} per MR_n-cube of the current region, S^{n−j}(Z) if no large fields are created near Z.
  - *Lifetime sum.* D_j(Z) := Σ_{n=j}^{j+K_Z}Cc_CMR_nτ_n²·#cubes_n(S^{n−j}(Z)), with K_Z the lifetime of Z (p.384's "K renormalization steps"; d2-0b-r2 m-H). Bałaban's chain on p.384–385 bounds exactly this volume count: #cubes_n(S^{n−j}(Z)) = (MR_n)^{−d}|S^{n−j}(Z)| ≤ 63^d(MR_n)^{−d}|Z^{(n−j)}| ≤ 3(126)^d2^{−(n−j)}d_j′(Z) for n ≤ n₀, and S^{n−j}(Z) lies in a cube of size 64MR_n, so #cubes_n ≤ 64^d, for the at most R_j steps after n₀ (K_Z ≤ n₀ − j + R_j, p.385). With R_n ≤ LR_j (CRE (2.9)), τ_n² ≤ τ̄² := sup_{n≤K}τ_n² ≤ Cg_K²(log g_K⁻²)^{6p₀} (τ = C[g(1+r)³ + e^{−cr²}] is increasing in g for small g, and g_n ≤ (1+β₀)g_K by CRE (2.6)), and the step n = j itself (#cubes_j(Z) ≤ 3·2^{d−1}d_j′(Z) for d_j′(Z) > 0 by p.384's inequality at n = j, and ≤ 2^d otherwise), this is (1.81) with O(1)M^dR_n^{d+1} replaced by Cc_CMR_nτ_n², and d_n′(S^{n−j}(Z)) by #cubes_n, bounded by the same middle member (d2-0b-r2 m-H):
