    D_j(Z) ≤ C_dc_CMLR_j²τ̄²(d_j′(Z)+1), C_d := C(3(126)^d + 64^d + 3·2^{d−1}).
  - *The attached species (d2-0c-r4 m5).* D_j(Z) covers only the per-segment costs. The species att(j, Z), att(j, coll) (per component through B_j(Z)) and lw(Z) are carried without growth (D(k)(ii′): the carry does not increase the left sides; by the regrouping, while their stability under the evaluation at U_{k+1}, under the fluctuation parts and under the multi-parent insertions is the D2.6 item of D(k)(ii′), d2-0c-r5 m12), so they are paid once, not at every step of Z's lifetime. Their totals, at the scales of D(k)(ii′), are
    A_j(Z) := Cρ̂_jN_j(Z) + Cρ_{t,j}[Σ_{Δ→Z}𝔪̂^{(j)}_ΔN_j(Δ) + C′_χg_jΣ_{b→Z}h(m^{(j)}_b)N_j(b)] + [j = 1]Σ_{i→Z}‖π(T̂_iT_i^{−1}) − 1‖,
    with ρ̂_j of order g_j²(log g_j^{−2})^{4p₀} and ρ_{t,j} of order g_j(log g_j^{−2})^{p₀}. The cost to be paid by Z's fundamental factor is D_j(Z) + A_j(Z). The bound of A_j(Z) by a multiple of d_j′(Z) + 1 (counting the step-j traversals within distance O(M) of Z, the marks and the inputs assigned to Z) and the size of the lw term are part of the D2.6 rerun.
  - *The condition (stated for j = 1 after d2-0b-r2 m-B).* At creation at step 1 the cost is paid if C_dc_CMLR_1²τ̄² ≤ ¼c₄(434)^{−d}ϑ²A₁²(log g₁⁻²)^{2p₀} − O(1)2(64)^dM^dL^{d+1}R_1^{d+2}: p.385's j = 1 condition, with ¼γ₀(14)^{−d} replaced as in IT-1 note §3f(2) Minor 4, and its right side at most doubled once C_dc_Cτ̄² ≤ O(1)2(64)^dM^{d−1}L^dR_1^d. The left side is g_K² times a polylog, so this holds for small g, by the same "p₀ large, g small" as p.385. For the small domains of p.386, add D ≤ C·100^dc_CMLR_{j+1}²τ̄² ≤ ½(log g_j⁻²)^{p₀} for small g. The comparison at general j, with (1.85)'s "γ₀A₁²p₀²(g_{j+1})(d′+1)", is part of the D2.6 rerun.
  - *What is left (D2.6; gaps block).* Bałaban's induction pp.384–389 with the summand of (1.80) enlarged by Cc_CMR_nτ_n²#cubes_n(S^{n−j}(Z)). In the merging step (1.86) the new summand needs no margin, since #cubes is subadditive under Z ⊂ X ∪ Y (Bałaban's d′ needs the +2d of p.386). The future sums of Z against those of X and Y are on p.387, not read here; that is the check.
  - *Localisation (d2-0b m8).* §3i(7) gives ∗-connected sets X meeting K_j and Dᶜ, which may meet several components. So the payment is per polymer, a union of components linked by such sets, with the weight e^{κ#boxes(X)}; the per-cube comparison of §3i(7) (*Against (0)*) is a D2.6 item.
- **(g) Torus and corner–corner parts of E°.** At step k these are Cτ_k²(n_k e^{−cι_{T,k}} + (n_k+1)e^{−cn_k}) with n_k ≍ ℓ_CL^{K−k} and the step-k quantity ι_{T,k} := max(0, N_min^{(k)}/4 − R_0) (d2-0b m9). With a torus of physical side T_ph ≫ ℓ_C, N_min^{(k)} ≍ T_phL^{K−k}, and the terms are dominated by the last O(1) steps and **summable** (iteration audit §4(3)). With a fixed ι_T the torus sum would not be summable. The wrap terms of (c)(ε) need in addition T_ph ≥ C·M, with M the decay length of D(k)(ii) (d2-0c-r3 m11). Both are lower bounds on the periods, which may be taken as large as needed.
- **(h) The norm of the dressed loop.** |𝒲_k| ≤ |κ^{(k)}|e^{|𝓔_k|}∏_n(1+ν_n) is at most |κ^{(k)}|exp(CP_k(ρ_kε_k + τ_k²)); this bound is exponentially large in L^{K−k}. D(k) never uses it, and the step map may use only local bounds: Cauchy estimates on polydiscs in bounded sets of variables (IT-1 note §6(f4)) and cluster expansions with local activities. At k = K, P_K = 4ℓ_C, so |𝒲_K| ≤ |κ^{(K)}|e^{Cℓ_C} (d2-0b m13).

**Result of the count check.** Counting does not exclude D(k) with constants uniform in k and K, under four conditions:
- every perimeter-proportional scalar part is absorbed exactly ((a));
- corner remainders are O(τ_k²g_k^δ), and summable ((b): needs the coupling upper bound, printed for SU(2));
- the deviation part contracts at L^{−1} per step ((c));
- every large-field-attached term is carried as a local activity and paid by its own component over its lifetime ((f)).
Their inputs (d2-0b m10): C_new uniform in k ((e)); the (d) remainder at most (1+β₀)⁸L²g_K² times the class bound against ρ_{k+1}, or (1+β₀)^{10}L²g_K² against ρ̃_{k+1} after D2.4(5) (d2-4-r1 m11); only local bounds in the step map ((h)); the torus condition of (g).

Each is a D2 item. Items (4)–(7) of the iteration audit, fatal as bounds at step 1, are carried by D(k) instead of bounded (d2-0b m11): (4) and (5) as local terms and activities attached to large-field regions ((f); a translation-invariant part of (4), if any, in (a)); (6) as the deviation part ((c)) plus attached parts ((f)); (7) by the per-traversal class without a global bound ((c), (e), (h)). The fluctuation parts (d) have no step-1 counterpart and feed (a) and (c).

### (6) Order of work and reviews

- D2.1 first, since D2.2–D2.5 take its form of the leading word as input; then D2.2 with D2.5 and D2.6's carrier item, then D2.3 and D2.4, then the rest of D2.6, then D2.7. D2.2–D2.6 are first done for k ≤ N, before D2.7 (d2-0b m12).
- A review round follows D2.0, and each of D2.1, …, D2.7, one subsection per packet, in app sessions on claude-opus-5-5.

## D2.1 The leading word at step k+1 (written 13:58–14:01, 22 Oct; reviewed it1-d2-1-20261022-r1, re-reviewed it1-d2-1-20261022-r2: no fatal or serious finding; V-1 to V-10 applied by 15:52, 22 Oct, date)

### (0) What is needed

D2.2 integrates the step-(k+1) fluctuation field with the dressed loop as the observable. Its input from the leading word is f_k(U_k), written through the step-(k+1) background U_{k+1} and the fluctuation variable of CRE (3.15), in the class form: f_{k+1} plus insertions that are local along C, analytic, and small with the fluctuation. That this is the weakest form D2.2 can use is a target, to be confirmed when D2.2 states what it consumes (d2-1 M13). The fluctuation integral is done by a cluster expansion, so every non-leading part must be a local activity, and D2.0(5)(h) excludes any bound on the whole word. The old insertions, words and scalar exponent of 𝒲_k are D2.2's, after D2.5.

### (1) Quoted

*Notation (d2-1 M11).* RG2's constant κ₁ is written κ_s in this section, to keep it apart from D2.0(3)'s step-1 perimeter coefficient κ₁; the threshold of (4) is ε_H, to keep it apart from CRE's ε₀ = g₀p₀(g₀). RG2's ε₂, ε₃ keep their names; RG2's ε₁ is written ε₁^{RG2} and VAR's ε₁ is written ε₁^{VAR}, to keep both apart from CRE's ε_k = g_kp₀(g_k) at k = 1 (d2-1-r2 V-10(a)). CRE's "[I]" is RG I, CMP 109, 249–301 (CRE reference list, p.285; d2-1 M15).

- *The step-(k+1) variables.*
  - CRE (3.10)–(3.11), p.266: V^{(k)} = M^k(U_{k+1}) on Ω_{k+1}^{(k)}, and V_k′ = V_k(V^{(k)})^{−1} on B(Γ_{k+1}).
  - CRE (3.14), p.267: |V_k′ − 1| < O(1)ε_k on Ω_{k+1}, and A_k′ = (1/i)log V_k′.
  - CRE p.267, after (3.14): "The next steps are exactly the same as in Sect. C [16], and in fact the same as in Sect. 2 [I]. … only the symbols represent different operators, determined by the sequence {Ω_j}."
  - CRE uses the symbol A_k′ twice (d2-1-r2 V-3): first A_k′ = (1/i)log V_k′ ((3.14)), then the scaling A_k′ = g_kA_k (p.267), which defines the integration variable A_k. Below, A_k is always this scaled variable. After the scaling, the integrand of (3.15) contains U_k(exp i[g_kCA_k − hD̃(g_kCA_k)]V^{(k)}), and U_{k+1} where A_k = 0. So the step-k fine field is U_k(e^{iB′}V^{(k)}), with B′ := g_kCA_k − hD̃(g_kCA_k) on B(Γ_{k+1}) (RG2 (1.19), where A_k is called B). Off B(Γ_{k+1}), V_k is a pending variable and B′ := 0.
  - RG2 (1.20): |B′| ≤ O(1)g_k|A_k| + 4C₂(O(1)g_k|A_k|)² ≤ C₁g_k|A_k| < C₁ε₁^{RG2} for g_k|A_k| < ε₁^{RG2}. RG2 p.6, after (1.20): 𝐇_k is analytic in A_k for g_k|A_k| < ε₁^{RG2}, so this is a complex domain; C₁ε₁^{RG2} ≤ ε₃ (RG2 p.7) puts B′ in the domain of (1.18). RG1 p.267: D̃ is analytic and "has an expansion beginning with quadratic terms".
  - *Derived (d2-1 M2; constant by d2-1-r2 V-8).* Fix A_k with g_k|A_k| < ε₁^{RG2} and put f(t) := −hD̃(tg_kCA_k) = B′(tA_k) − tg_kCA_k for |t| < T := ε₁^{RG2}/(g_k|A_k|). The map t ↦ B′(tA_k) is analytic with B′(0) = 0 and derivative g_kCA_k at t = 0 (D̃ starts with quadratic terms), and |B′(tA_k)| < C₁ε₁^{RG2} by (1.20); Cauchy's estimate at t = 0 gives |g_kCA_k| ≤ C₁ε₁^{RG2}T^{−1} = C₁g_k|A_k|. So f is analytic with f(0) = f′(0) = 0 and |f(t)| ≤ 2C₁ε₁^{RG2}. The Schwarz lemma for a double zero gives |f(1)| ≤ 2C₁ε₁^{RG2}T^{−2}, so |B′ − g_kCA_k| ≤ 2C₁(ε₁^{RG2})^{−1}(g_k|A_k|)². No bound on C beyond (1.20) is used.
- *The composition.*
  - RG1 p.265 (RG1's [15] is VAR, RG1 reference list p.299; d2-1 M3): (2.3) "V^{(k)} = Ū^k_{k+1} = M^k(U_{k+1})"; "As in Sect. G [15] we write U_k(V′V^{(k)}) = U_k′(V′)U_{k+1}"; after a transformation to the axial gauge and the gauge transformation u_k to the Landau gauge, "U_k′^{u_k^{−1}} = exp iη𝐇_k(B′)".
  - RG1 (2.6), p.266: "Using the gauge invariance of the action … A(U_k(V′V^{(k)})) = A(exp iη𝐇_k(B′)U_{k+1})".
  - VAR p.305, Sect. G: with U₀ = U_k(V₀), "fix the Landau gauge for the configuration U_k(V′V₀)U₀^{−1} = U₁, and we have U₁ = exp iη𝓗(B)".
  - VAR p.307: 𝓗(B) "is an analytic function of 𝔤^c-valued configurations B defined on 𝔅_k and satisfying (172)". The transformation back to the axial gauge "is an analytic function of 𝓗, hence of B". (181) extends to G^c-valued gauge transformations.
- *The form of 𝐇_k.*
  - RG2 (1.2)–(1.4), p.2: 𝐇_k(B′) = H₀B′ + 𝐀₀ − HD(H₀B′ + 𝐀₀), with D(A′) = C(A′ − HD(A′)) and 𝐀₀ + G̃(δV/δA′)(H₀B′ + 𝐀₀) = 0.
  - RG2 (1.14), (1.16): |D(H(s),A′)| ≤ 4C₂|A′|² and |𝐀₀| ≤ 4C₄(B₀e^{16κ_s})³|B′|². So the first-order part of 𝐇_k is H₀B′.
- *The multi-domain form (d2-1 M4, M9).*
  - CRE (3.6), p.266: U_{k,□} = (exp iη𝐇_{k,□}((1/i)log[M·(Q_k^{s*}V_k)(M·(U_{k+1,□′}))^{−1}])U_{k+1,□′})^{u_{k,□}^{−1}}; CRE (3.17), p.268, the same at step k+1. The gauge transformation acts on the product e^{iη𝐇}U_{k+1}.
  - CRE (3.7), p.266: |𝐇_{k,□}|, |∇^η_{U_{k+1,□′}}𝐇_{k,□}| < (1/10)ε_k on □^∼, for A₁/A₀ and γ small.
  - CRE p.267, continuing the quote above: "only the symbols represent different operators, determined by the sequence {Ω_j}. Their properties are essentially the same as before, because of the localization of the field A_k." On the pages read this is the only transfer to the multi-domain operators. It names RG1 Sect. 2 and Sect. C [16]. RG2 Sect. 1's construction reaches CRE's operators through this sentence together with RG2 p.3, "we can replace the propagators by arbitrary operators having the same regularity properties and satisfying the same bounds", and (1.5). It is accepted as Bałaban's statement. It is used quantitatively in case (A) of (4), for RG2's constants on CRE's operators, which are k-independent, and qualitatively in case (B) (d2-1-r2 V-1).
- *Decoupling, RG2 pp.3–6.*
  - (1.6) and p.3: the propagators are random-walk sums. Each walk term is localised in X̃₀⁵ ∪ ⋯ ∪ X̃_n⁵, and its kernel vanishes within ⅓M₁ of ∂X_j.
  - p.3: the cubes Δ of a partition σ_k carry parameters s(Δ) (the print's σ_k has cubes of side R₁M₁ ≤ M; its example, and (4) below, use cubes of side M; d2-1 M12). Each walk term is multiplied by ∏s(Δ) over the cubes meeting its localisation domain; this defines H(s), G̃(s), H₀(s), and "They coincide with the original ones for s = 1".
  - (1.5): D and 𝐀₀ are functionals of the operators.
  - p.4, at s(σ^c) = 0: the kernels "vanish, unless both arguments are in the interior of one component of Y(σ), in fact with distances to the boundary of this component bigger than M₁". 𝐀₀ "is equal to 0 on the neighborhood of Y(σ)^c", and on a component it depends on the propagators and B′ restricted to that component.
  - (1.11): for |s(Δ)| ≤ e^{κ_s} and δ₁M ≥ κ_s, H(s)X is analytic in s and bounded by B₀e^{16κ_s}|X|.
  - (1.17)–(1.18), p.6: 𝐇_k(s(Y₀),B′) is analytic in s(Y₀) and B′, with |𝐇_k(s(Y₀),B′)| ≤ 4B₀e^{16κ_s}|B′| "and the same bound for all admissible norms". This holds for |B′| < ε₃, with 4C₂B₀e^{16κ_s}ε₂ ≤ 1 (p.5), 4C₄B₀²e^{32κ_s}ε₃ ≤ 1 and 2B₀e^{16κ_s}ε₃ ≤ ε₂ (pp.5–6), uniformly for (𝐔,𝐉) in a complex domain given by I.(i)–(iii) (p.6, after (1.18)).
  - RG2's example decouples only the cubes disjoint from the interior of □̃⁴ (p.3). Below, all cubes carry parameters.

### (2) The leading word as a function of the fluctuation (derived from (1), with reading R-g)

- *Reading R-g (notation).* In RG1's "U_k′^{u_k^{−1}}", the gauge transformations act on U_k(V′V^{(k)}) with U_{k+1} fixed, as in VAR's "fix the Landau gauge for the configuration U_k(V′V₀)U₀^{−1}". CRE (3.6) and (3.17) print this form for the multi-domain functions: the gauge transformation acts on the product e^{iη𝐇}U_{k+1} (d2-1 M4). It is also the reading under which RG1 (2.6) "Using the gauge invariance of the action" applies as written: if the transformation acted on the factor U_k′ alone, U_k′U_{k+1} and (U_k′)^{u}U_{k+1} would not be gauge equivalent in general. So: for real A_k on the support, there is a G-valued lattice gauge transformation u with U_k(e^{iB′}V^{(k)})^u = e^{iη𝐇_k(B′)}U_{k+1} bondwise.
- *The identity.* χ_π∘Hol_C is invariant under lattice gauge transformations, since Hol_C(U^u) = u(x₀)Hol_C(U)u(x₀)^{−1} for the base point x₀ of C. So for real A_k on the support,
  f_k(U_k(e^{iB′}V^{(k)})) = dim(π)^{−1}χ_π(Hol_C(e^{iη𝐇_k(B′)}U_{k+1})).
- *Complex A_k and complex backgrounds* (for D(k+1)(iii); d2-1 M6, d2-1-r2 V-5). Run the identity theorem jointly in (A_k, a′), with 𝐔 = e^{iηa′}U_{k+1} as in (4) and a′ 𝔤^c-valued (CRE (2.39) complexifies the configuration, U′ = exp iξA′, not the variables that define U_{k+1}). Take a polydisc centred at a real point (real A_k, a′ = 0) that lies inside both the analyticity domain of the left side (VAR (172), VAR p.307 and (181), Prop. 9 [15] for the minimiser, D(k)(iii) for f_k) and RG2's domain I.(i)–(iii) (p.6), on which the right side is analytic by RG2 (1.18). The two sides agree at the real points of the polydisc, a uniqueness set for functions analytic on a polydisc centred at a real point, so they agree on the polydisc, and by the identity theorem on the connected component of the intersection of the two domains that contains it. The gauge transformation is not needed here.
- *At A_k = 0.* 𝐇_k(0) = 0 by (1.18), so f_k(U_k(V^{(k)})) = f_{k+1} := dim(π)^{−1}χ_π(Hol_C(U_{k+1})). This is the leading word of D2.0(3) at step k+1.
  - Where C runs outside Ω_{k+1}, U_{k+1} is a function of the pending variables. CRE p.266: U_{k+1} "is determined by the sequence of domains {Ω_{k+1},Ω_k,…}, and the corresponding sequence of fields {V_{k+1}|Γ_{k+1},V_k|Γ_k,…}". These variables enter f_{k+1} as outer variables, as in D(k).
  - The composition formulas on the pages read are RG1's single-domain (2.3)–(2.6) and CRE's cube-local (3.6) and (3.17). The identity above rests on CRE p.267's transfer of RG1 Sect. 2 to the multi-domain operators in every case, since 𝐇_k is non-local ((1), accepted as Bałaban's statement; d2-1 S1, d2-1-r2 V-1).

### (3) Transported form and the first-order term (derived)

- *Transport identity.* Let b_1, …, b_P be the fine bonds of C in order from x₀, U a configuration and X a 𝔤^c-valued function of the bonds. Then
  Hol_C(e^{iX}U) = (∏_{i=1}^{P} exp(i Ad(Γ_i)X(b_i)))·Hol_C(U), with Γ_i := U(b_1)⋯U(b_{i−1}) and the product ordered with i increasing to the right.
  - Proof: e^{iX₁}U₁e^{iX₂}U₂⋯ = e^{iX₁}(U₁e^{iX₂}U₁^{−1})U₁U₂⋯, and induction.
  - Orientation (d2-1 M8). The b_i are oriented along C. If C traverses a lattice bond b backwards, b_i = b̄ with U(b̄) = U(b)^{−1}, and (e^{iX}U)(b̄) = U(b)^{−1}e^{−iX(b)} = e^{iX(b̄)}U(b̄) with X(b̄) := −Ad(U(b)^{−1})X(b). With this convention the identity holds as stated.
- *The first-order term.* Take X = η𝐇_k(B′), with first-order part ηH₀B′ by (1), and B′ = g_kCA_k + O(C₁(ε₁^{RG2})^{−1}(g_k|A_k|)²) by (1) (d2-1 M2). Then the part of f_k(U_k(e^{iB′}V^{(k)})) linear in A_k is
  iηg_k Σ_i dim(π)^{−1}tr[π_*(Ad(Γ_i)(H₀CA_k)(b_i))·π(Hol_C(U_{k+1}))], with Γ_i the U_{k+1}-transports.
  - It is odd in A_k. It is the leading word's first-order fluctuation of D2.0(5)(d).
  - Size (d2-1 M9). Cauchy's estimate in t for t ↦ 𝐇_k(tB′), |t| < ε₃/|B′|, and (1.18) give |H₀B′| ≤ 4B₀e^{16κ_s}|B′| in the sup norm. With |g_kCA_k| ≤ C₁g_k|A_k| (Cauchy, (1)) and L^kη = 1, the term is at most c_π·4B₀e^{16κ_s}C₁g_k|A_k|_∞ per step-k bond of C, where |A_k|_∞ is the sup norm. This uses (1.18) at s = 1 for CRE's operators (CRE p.267). With RG2's constants it holds at bonds of C in Ω_k, the scale-k set of VAR (190) ((4), *Two cases*), where (190) has j = k; at bonds in older regions (190) carries L^{k−j}, a D2.6 item (d2-1-r2 V-6). CRE (3.7) prints a consistent sup bound < ε_k/10 for the cube-local functions 𝐇_{k,□} on □^∼.
  - Its locality in A_k is that of (4). In older large-field regions (y ∈ Λ_j, j < k), VAR (190) carries the prefactor (L^jη)^{−1} = L^{k−j}; that is case (B) of (4), whose sizes belong to D2.6 (the margin comparison is D2.2(4)(f), (6)(e), under R-dec; d2-2e-r2 S1(d)).

### (4) Localisation along C (derived from (1))

*Setting.* Let σ_k be a partition of the torus into cubes of side M, as in RG2's example (d2-1 M12). Every cube Δ ∈ σ_k carries a parameter s(Δ). H(s), G̃(s), H₀(s) are defined by RG2 p.3's rule, and 𝐇(s,B′) by (1.2)–(1.4) with these operators ((1.5)). For σ ⊆ σ_k let Y(σ) := ∪_{Δ∈σ}Δ, with RG2's connectedness (p.4: a common (d−1)-dimensional wall). Since no cube is exempt here, RG2's second case of connectedness, the one involving □̃⁴, does not occur.

- **(a)** 𝐇(1,B′) = 𝐇_k(B′) (p.3). Also 𝐇(0,B′) = 0: every walk term meets a cube, so H(0) = G̃(0) = H₀(0) = 0; then 𝐀₀ = 0 by (1.4), and 𝐇 = 0 by (1.2).
- **(b)** Let s(σ^c) = 0. The argument of RG2 p.4 uses the walk localisation and the multiplication rule of p.3, and the locality of C in (1.3) ("D(A′) = C(A′) on the neighborhood of Y(σ)^c"), of V₀, and of B′ in B (d2-1-r2 V-10(c)). By it, 𝐇(s,B′) vanishes on a neighbourhood of Y(σ)^c, and on a component Y of Y(σ) it depends only on s|_Y, B′|_Y and (𝐔,𝐉)|_Y. Call it 𝐇_Y(s|_Y,B′|_Y).
- **(c)** The proofs of (1.11) and (1.18) use (1.7), |s(Δ)| ≤ e^{κ_s}, δ₁M ≥ κ_s, the count of at most 2⁴ cubes met by a short walk (p.5), the contraction bound (1.13), Prop. 4 [15] (p.5, with ε₂ for ε₃), and the conditions on ε₂, ε₃ quoted in (1) (d2-1 M14). None of these uses the exempt cubes, so they hold with all cubes carrying parameters. 𝐇_Y is analytic for |s(Δ)| ≤ e^{κ_s} and |B′| < ε₃, and applying (1.18) to B′1_Y gives |𝐇_Y(s,B′)| ≤ 4B₀e^{16κ_s}|B′|_Y, where |B′|_Y is the supremum over Y. These are RG2's single-scale constants; where they apply is the case split of the bounds below.

*Expansion.* Let Φ(s) := Hol_C(e^{iη𝐇(s,B′)}U_{k+1}), as π-matrices at x₀. The torus has finitely many cubes, so the fundamental theorem of calculus in each s(Δ) gives
Φ(1) = Σ_{σ⊆σ_k} ∏_{Δ∈σ}∫₀¹ds(Δ)∂_{s(Δ)}Φ(s)|_{s(σ^c)=0}.
- σ = ∅ gives Hol_C(U_{k+1}), by (a).
- At s(σ^c) = 0, by (b), the factor on a fine bond b of C is e^{iη𝐇_Y(b)}U_{k+1}(b) if b lies in a component Y, and U_{k+1}(b) otherwise. A component that contains no fine bond of C gives zero, since Φ does not depend on its parameters. So only the σ whose components all contain bonds of C remain.
- *Arcs and groups.*
  - For such a component Y, let S(Y) be the set of fine bonds of C in Y. Let I(Y) be the arc of C obtained by removing the longest maximal run of bonds not in S(Y); ties go to the run whose first bond comes first from x₀, and I(Y) = C if S(Y) = C.
  - Components whose arcs share a bond are joined into *groups* G: the connected components of the overlap graph. The arc of a group is I(G) := ∪_{Y∈G}I(Y), and distinct groups have disjoint arcs.
- *Insertions.* For a group G whose arc runs from x_G to x_G′, let
  Y_G := [∏_{Y∈G}∏_{Δ⊂Y}∫₀¹ds(Δ)∂_{s(Δ)}] Hol_{I(G)}(e^{iη𝐇(s,B′)}U_{k+1}) at s(Δ) = 0 for every cube Δ outside the components of G (d2-1 M7),
  a linear map from the fibre at x_G′ to the fibre at x_G, as a transport under 𝐔^u(b) = u(b_−)𝐔(b)u(b_+)^{−1} (orientation of D2.2(4)(a), d2-2b m1). Write #(Y) for the number of cubes of Y and #(G) := Σ_{Y∈G}#(Y).
  - Inside a family, the other components' parameters do not change Y_G: a bond of C in a component Y′ of another group G′ lies in S(Y′) ⊆ I(G′), which is disjoint from I(G), and by (b) the factors on the bonds of I(G) depend only on the parameters of the components that contain them.
- **Result.** Put the groups in cyclic order along C, and let U_{(i)} be the U_{k+1}-transport along C from the end of I(G_i) to the start of I(G_{i+1}). Then
  f_k(U_k(e^{iB′}V^{(k)})) = f_{k+1} + Σ_{𝔊≠∅ compatible} dim(π)^{−1}tr(Y_{G_1}U_{(1)}⋯Y_{G_n}U_{(n)}).
  - A family 𝔊 is *compatible* if (0) each of its components is a connected union of cubes containing a fine bond of C, (i) its groups have disjoint arcs, (ii) its components are pairwise disjoint with no common wall, and (iii) each group is connected by arc overlaps.
  - The map σ ↦ (components of Y(σ)) ↦ 𝔊 is a bijection onto the compatible families. Given 𝔊, σ is the set of cubes of its components, and the components of Y(σ) are exactly these, since they are connected and pairwise wall-disjoint.
  - If one group has arc C, the family consists of that group alone and the term is dim(π)^{−1}tr Y_G.
  - This is the class form of D2.0(3) with Q ≡ 1 (hard-core interactions only).
- **Bounds** (case split after d2-1 S1).
  - *Two cases (region pinned after d2-1-r2 V-2).* (A) every component of G lies in Ω_k, where (190) has j = j′ = k and RG2's constants of (c) apply; (B) some component of G meets Ω_kᶜ. A component can meet Ω_kᶜ while C lies in Ω_k, and large-field crossings are the main case of the IT-1 note's D1.1, so (B) is generic.
    - *Why Ω_k is the scale-k set of VAR (190).* (190) holds "for x ∈ Δ(y), … y ∈ Λ_j, y′ ∈ Λ_{j′}" (VAR p.308), with VAR's sets defined on p.277: "Λ_j = Ω_j^{(j)}∖Ω_{j+1}^{(j)}, Ω_{k+1} = ∅, or B^j(Λ_j) = Ω_j∖Ω_{j+1}", and 𝔅_k = ∪_{j≤k}Λ_j. So VAR's scale-k set is B^k(Λ_k) = Ω_k. RG1 p.265 applies VAR Sect. G at the step k → k+1, and CRE p.267 runs it on CRE's sequence {Ω_j} ((1)), so VAR's Ω_j are CRE's.
    - VAR's Λ_j are not CRE's small-field sets Λ_j of (3.20), which lie inside Ω_j; V3 L122's "the large-fluctuation region R_1, which with Ω_1 determines the filled region Λ_1" is about CRE's Λ_1, and the round-2 objection came from the shared name. Below, VAR's sets are written Λ_j^{VAR} where they occur.
  - *Case (B).* The identity of (2) and the expansion above hold, since they need only the qualitative analyticity of 𝐇(s,B′) in (s,B′), which is CRE p.267 together with RG2 p.3: (1.5), the walk multiplication rule, and the walk expansion of the propagators ((3.107) [13] for RG1's operators; the multi-domain version through CRE p.267, not on the pages read otherwise; d2-1-r2 V-1). The size of Y_G is not derived here: at points of a scale-j region, j < k, the kernels carry the prefactor (L^jη)^{−1} = L^{k−j} of VAR (190), and neither the per-bond bound of (c) nor (1.13)–(1.16) is on the pages read with k-independent constants. It is a D2.6 item: bound L^{k−j} against the walk decay e^{−δ₀d/8}, with d the distance from supp B′ ⊆ B(Γ_{k+1}). (Reconciled after d2-2e-r2 S1(d): the comparison of the older-layer deformation with the analyticity margins is made in D2.2(4)(f) and (6)(e) under reading R-dec; the sizes of the case (B) insertions stay with D2.6.)
  - *Constants.* Fix c_π with ‖π(e^{iX})‖ ≤ e^{c_π|X|} for X ∈ 𝔤^c; c_CM bounds the length of C in a cube of side M (D2.0(5)(f)). Fix ε_H < ε₃ with c_πc_CM·4B₀e^{16κ_s}ε_H ≤ 1. ε_H depends on π, M, κ_s, Bałaban's constants, and on C only through c_C (d2-1 M5).
  - *Conditions used* (d2-1 M10; completed after d2-1-r2 V-4), all checkable:
    - 4C₂B₀e^{16κ_s}ε₂ ≤ 1 (RG2 p.5), 4C₄B₀²e^{32κ_s}ε₃ ≤ 1 and 2B₀e^{16κ_s}ε₃ ≤ ε₂ (pp.5–6), C₁ε₁^{RG2} ≤ ε₃ (RG2 p.7), ε_H < ε₃, δ₁M ≥ κ_s;
    - κ_s ≥ 1 (for the Cauchy radius e^{κ_s} − 1 ≥ e^{κ_s−1}), and κ_s > 3 for decay at complex backgrounds;
    - O(1)ε_k < ε₁^{RG2} (for (1.20)) and O(1)ε_k ≤ ε_H for every k ≤ K, with the O(1) of CRE (3.14) (times C₁ of (1.20) in the second); these are smallness conditions on g_K through ε_k = ε(g_k) and g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K (for a fixed g₀ they restrict K); the general-G form of CRE (2.6) is the coupling item of the D2 gaps block (reading R-cpl);
    - g_k|A_k| ≤ ε_H/C₁ on the complex A_k-domain ((4), *The hypothesis*), fixed with D2.2 and D2.4;
    - (𝐔,𝐉) in RG2's domain I.(i)–(iii) (p.6), including a bound on ∇a′ as in CRE (2.39);
    - VAR (172) for the configurations B at which 𝓗(B) is used;
    - 4c_πM‖a′‖_∞ ≤ 1 at complex backgrounds: a condition on the radius of D(k+1)(iii), fixed with D2.4;
    - every torus period at least twice the side of C (for the arc-length step of the proof; at step 1 V3 L124's "every coarse period K_μ … satisfies K_μ ≥ 3(n+2)" gives it for C_n of side nL);
    - |𝐇_k| < a₁ (RG1 p.266, for the analyticity of u_k in 𝐇_k), which follows from (1.18) if 4B₀e^{16κ_s}ε₃ ≤ a₁;
    - VAR (7), |(∂V)(p′) − 1| < ε₁^{VAR} on 𝔅_k, for V^{(k)}. This is part of Bałaban's construction as presented: RG1 p.265 applies VAR Sect. G with V₀ = V^{(k)} ("As in Sect. G [15] we write U_k(V′V^{(k)}) = U_k′(V′)U_{k+1}"), VAR Sect. G assumes "V₀ satisfies the condition (7)", and CRE p.267 transfers this to the multi-domain step. CRE p.268's regularity of U_{k+1} on Ω_{k+1} (D2.0(2)) alone does not give it on 𝔅_k.
  - *The hypothesis |B′|_Y ≤ ε_H (d2-1-r2 V-3).*
    - At real configurations. By CRE (3.11) and (3.15), V_k′ = e^{iB′} on B(Γ_{k+1}), so (3.14) gives |B′| < O(1)ε_k there directly; under O(1)ε_k ≤ ε_H this is the hypothesis. No (1.20) is needed.
    - The range of (1.20). g_k|A_k| < ε₁^{RG2} is part of what CRE p.267 transfers ("we perform the scaling transformation A_k′ = g_kA_k. All the applied formulas are the same as in Sect. 2 [I]"); RG2's domain for those formulas is g_k|B| < ε₁^{RG2}. The bound g_k|A_k| < O(1)ε_k for the scaled variable is not on the pages read: RG1 p.267 makes the change of variables B′ = B − hD̃(B) and then the scaling B = g_kB′ without stating it. It is used only in M2's second-order bound and in the complex A_k-domain of D2.2, where the radius g_k|A_k| ≤ min(ε₁^{RG2}, ε_H/C₁) is part of the domain's definition; the claim below uses |B′| directly.
    - At complex A_k. (1.20) on its complex domain gives |B′| ≤ C₁g_k|A_k|, so the hypothesis holds for g_k|A_k| ≤ ε_H/C₁. This is a radius condition of the complex A_k-domain, fixed with D2.2 and D2.4. The uniformity of (1.20) in (𝐔,𝐉) is RG2's "Gathering together the above statements", read as including the uniformity sentence just before it.
  - *Claim (case (A)).* Let G be a group of case (A), and suppose |B′|_Y ≤ ε_H for every Y ∈ G. Let the background be 𝐔 = e^{iηa′}U_{k+1} with a′ 𝔤^c-valued, as in CRE (2.39) (d2-1 M1), and let C be a square or a′ = 0 (d2-1-r2 V-10(b)). Then
    ‖Y_G‖ ≤ ∏_{Y∈G}(|B′|_Y/ε_H)e^{−(κ_s−2−4c_πM‖a′‖_∞)#(Y)}, where ‖a′‖_∞ is the sup over the bonds of I(G).
    - At real backgrounds, a′ = 0 and the exponent is −(κ_s−2)#(Y). For C a square (the target's C, D2.0(5)), the condition 4c_πM‖a′‖_∞ ≤ 1 gives e^{−(κ_s−3)#(Y)}. For general contours the arc length is not controlled by #(Y), and the complex-background bound is not derived.
  - Proof.
    - Replace B′|_Y by t_YB′|_Y for each Y ∈ G. By (c), the integrand is analytic in the s(Δ) for |s(Δ)| ≤ e^{κ_s}, and in the t_Y for |t_Y| ≤ T_Y := ε_H/|B′|_Y, where T_Y ≥ 1.
    - At t_Y = 0, 𝐇_Y = 0 on S(Y) for every s. So the arc product does not depend on s|_Y, and the s|_Y-derivatives vanish. Since this holds for each Y separately, the Schwarz lemma in each t_Y gives the factor ∏_YT_Y^{−1}.
    - Cauchy's estimate on circles of radius e^{κ_s} − 1 ≥ e^{κ_s−1} (κ_s ≥ 1) around s(Δ) ∈ [0,1] gives e^{−(κ_s−1)} per cube.
    - The arc product over the polydisc. Write the factor on a bond b of I(G) as e^{iη𝐇_Y(b)}e^{iηa′(b)}U_{k+1}(b) (no 𝐇-factor on bonds in no component) with U_{k+1}(b) unitary. Its norm is at most exp(c_πη(|𝐇_Y(b)| + |a′(b)|)). Summing, the supremum is at most exp(c_πηΣ_{Y∈G}Σ_{b∈S(Y)}|𝐇_Y(s,t_YB′)(b)|)·exp(c_π‖a′‖_∞·length(I(G))). The first factor is ≤ exp(c_πc_CM·4B₀e^{16κ_s}ε_H·#(G)) ≤ e^{#(G)}, since η#S(Y) is the length of C in Y and is at most c_CM#(Y).
    - Arc length for a square C. I(Y) is the smallest arc of C containing S(Y), and Y is a connected union of #(Y) cubes of side M, so S(Y) has ℓ∞-diameter D ≤ M#(Y) (distances on the torus; every period is at least twice the side of C). If S(Y) lies on one side, it lies in a segment of length ≤ D, so length(I(Y)) ≤ D (d2-1-r2 V-7). If D is less than the side of C, S(Y) meets no two opposite sides, so at most two adjacent sides, and every point of it on one of them is within D of their common corner (the ℓ∞-distance of points on two perpendicular sides at distances a, b from the corner is max(a,b)); so length(I(Y)) ≤ 2D. Otherwise length(I(Y)) ≤ length(C) = 4·side ≤ 4D. Hence length(I(G)) ≤ Σ_{Y∈G}length(I(Y)) ≤ 4M#(G).
    - Multiplying the factors gives the claim.
  - Locality. Y_G depends on B′ and (𝐔,𝐉) on the components of G, and on U_{k+1} along I(G).
  - First order. A group with two or more components has no part linear in B′, since each component's factor vanishes at t_Y = 0. For G = {Y}, the linear part is the s|_Y-derivative of iηΣ_{b∈S(Y)}Hol_{I(Y),<b}(U_{k+1})·π_*((H₀(s)B′)(b))·Hol_{I(Y),≥b}(U_{k+1}), which is odd in B′.

### (5) What D2.1 gives, and what it leaves

- *Gives.* The leading word after the step-(k+1) substitution, exactly: f_{k+1} plus compatible families of local insertions, for every family (cases (A) and (B) of (4)).
  - Each insertion is analytic in A_k and in the background, on RG2's domain I.(i)–(iii); in case (B) by CRE p.267's transfer.
  - An insertion of case (A), all components in Ω_k = B^k(Λ_k^{VAR}), has size ∏(|B′|_Y/ε_H)e^{−(κ_s−2−4c_πM‖a′‖_∞)#(Y)}, for the square C, at real and complex backgrounds.
  - The first-order parts are odd.
  - The constants are independent of k, K, the contour size and the torus, and depend on C only through c_C (D(k+1)(iv); d2-1 M5).
  - No bound on the whole word is used.
- *Leaves to D2.2.*
  - (i) The old insertions, words and scalar exponent of 𝒲_k, composed with U_k(e^{iB′}V^{(k)}). The same expansion applies to each closed product once its factors are G-covariant (D2.5).
  - (ii) The parameters s of (4) and the decoupling of the fluctuation covariance (RG2 Sect. 2; IT-1 note §3i(2)) must be merged into one cluster expansion.
  - (iii) The integration itself.
- *Leaves to D2.6* (by components, d2-1 S1). The size of the insertions of case (B): groups with a component meeting Ω_kᶜ, the older scale sets B^j(Λ_j^{VAR}) = Ω_j∖Ω_{j+1}, j < k (d2-1-r2 V-2), whether or not C runs there. At points of a scale-j region the kernels carry L^{k−j} (VAR (190)), to be bounded against the walk decay e^{−δ₀d/8} from supp B′ ⊆ B(Γ_{k+1}).
  - *Decay distance (d2-1-r2 V-9).* A component Y with B′|_Y = 0 contributes zero: 𝐇_Y(s, 0) = 0 by (4)(c), so the arc product does not depend on s|_Y and the s|_Y-derivatives vanish. So every surviving case-(B) component meets both B(Γ_{k+1}) and Ω_kᶜ, and M#(Y) is at least the ℓ∞-distance between them. This is the decay distance D2.6 bounds L^{k−j} against. Where C runs outside Ω_{k+1}, the identity of (2) rests on CRE p.267's transfer of RG1 Sect. 2.
- *Leaves to D2.4.* The radius condition 4c_πM‖a′‖_∞ ≤ 1 of (4), together with the complex-background factor of the transports U_{(i)} between groups, which is the product form of D1(iii)′ (IT-1 note, e^{P(C)ρ₁} with the perimeter).

## D2.2 The small-field map at step k+1 (from 14:56, 22 Oct; (0)–(4) reviewed in round 9, it1-d2-2a/2b-20261022-r1, repairs applied 17:03–17:25, 22 Oct, date)

### (0) What is needed

D(k) → D(k+1) on the small-field side, for k ≤ N − 1, where no R-operation acts (D2.0(3), reading R-h; d2-2a m9). Given 𝒲_k on a labelled term of ρ_k, D2.2 has to produce the dressed loop of step k+1 on each term of ρ_{k+1} and show that it has the class form of D2.0(3) at step k+1. Its parts:
- (2) the numerator identity, which defines 𝒲_{k+1};
- (3) the carrier at step k+1, with D2.6's carrier item;
- (4) the composition of 𝒲_k with the step-(k+1) variables: D2.1 for the leading word, the same expansion for the old insertions once they are G-covariant (D2.5);
- (5) one cluster expansion for the localisation of the observable and for the fluctuation measure;
- (6) the integration, the analogue of V3 Lemmas c5-5-2 and c5-5-3, with its output in class D(k+1).

### (1) Quoted (CRE pp.267–271)

- *The step-(k+1) integrand* at fixed Ω_{k+1}, pp.267–268: χ_{k+1}(Ω_{k+1})∫dV_k|_{Ω_{k+1}ᶜ}δ(V̄_kV_{k+1}^{−1})ζ(Ω_{k+1}ᶜ)·T_kexp[A_k(1/g_k², U_{k+1}) + log g_kd(𝔤)|B(Γ_{k+1})*| + log σ₀|B(Γ_{k+1})*| − log z(L⁴−1)|Γ_{k+1}|]·z^{(k)}∫dA_k|_{Ω_{k+1}}χ_k′(Ω_{k+1})exp[−½⟨A_k, C*Δ^{(k)}CA_k⟩ + 𝐏^{(k)}(g_k, U_{k+1}, A_k) + {(𝐄_k+𝐑_k+𝐁_k)(U_k(exp i[g_kCA_k − hD̃(g_kCA_k)]V^{(k)})) − (𝐄_k+𝐑_k+𝐁_k)(U_{k+1}) + A(1/g_k²(·) − 1/g_k², U_k(…)) − A(1/g_k²(·) − 1/g_k², U_{k+1})}] ((3.15)).
- *(3.16), p.268:* on Ω_{k+1}^{∼−1}, 1 = Σ_{R_{k+1}}χ^{(k)}(R_{k+1}ᶜ)χ^{(k)c}(R_{k+1}), with the tests sup_{b∈(□′^{∼2})^{(k)*}}|A_k(b)| < δ_k, resp. ≥ δ_k, on L²M₂R_{k+1}-cubes. On cubes of R_{k+1}ᶜ the small-field function of (3.3) "is equal to 1, because of the bound on A_k introduced by the second" ((3.17)–(3.19), pp.268–269).
- *(3.20)–(3.21), p.269:* Λ_{k+1} = Ω_{k+1}^{∼−2} ∩ (R′^∼_{k+1})ᶜ and S_{k+1} = (Ω_{k+1}∖Ω_{k+1}^{∼−1}) ∪ R_{k+1}; Λ_{k+1} is modified "in the same way as in Sect. 1" (components of Λ_{k+1}ᶜ inside a cube of size 100LMR_{k+1} are replaced by the smallest rectangular parallelepiped containing them); χ(Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ, S_{k+1}) = χ^{(k)}(R_{k+1}ᶜ∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ)χ^{(k)c}(R_{k+1})χ_k′(S_{k+1}).
- *The change of variables, p.269:* on the bonds b ∈ S_{k+1}^{(k)*}, (1.22) with 𝐇^{(k)}(b) for 𝐇_{1,Ax}(b) on (R_{k+1}^{∼2}∩S_{k+1})^{(k)*}, the simpler change elsewhere. It turns χ_k′(S_{k+1}) into the same function with V^{(k)} replaced by V^{(k)}_{Λ_{k+1}ᶜ∩Λ_k}; "The other characteristic functions are unchanged"; the action gains V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}, A_k, 𝐇^{(k)}).
- *(3.23)–(3.25), p.270:* T^{(k+1)} := Σ_{S_{k+1}}∫dV_k|_{Ω_{k+1}ᶜ}δ(V̄_kV_{k+1}^{−1})ζ(Ω_{k+1}ᶜ)z^{(k)}∫dA_k|_{Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ}χ(Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ, S_{k+1})exp[−½⟨A_k, C*Δ^{(k)}CA_k⟩ + ½⟨A_k, C*Δ^{(k)}CC^{(k)}(Λ_{k+1})C*Δ^{(k)}CA_k⟩], T_{k+1} = T^{(k+1)}T_k, and
  C^{(k)}(Λ_{k+1}) is the inverse of the Λ_{k+1}-block of C*Δ^{(k)}C. This is the step-(k+1) form of CRE (1.26), p.253 (checked in the transcription, lines 6–11; not in the d2-2a-r3 packet, m8), where dμ_{C^{(0)}(Λ₁)} is the normalised Gaussian measure of ½⟨Λ₁A, C*Δ₁CΛ₁A⟩, as the completed square ½⟨Λ₁ᶜA, C*Δ₁CC^{(0)}(Λ₁)C*Δ₁CΛ₁ᶜA⟩ there shows (d2-2a m5).
  (Tρ_k)(V_{k+1}) = Σχ_{k+1}(Ω_{k+1})T_{k+1}exp[A_k(1/g_k², U_{k+1}) + E_0^{(k)}]·z^{(k)}∫dA|_{Λ_{k+1}}χ^{(k)}(Λ_{k+1})exp[−½⟨A, C*Δ^{(k)}CA⟩ − ⟨A, C*Δ^{(k)}CA_k⟩ − ½⟨A_k, C*Δ^{(k)}CC^{(k)}(Λ_{k+1})C*Δ^{(k)}CA_k⟩ + 𝐏^{(k)}(g_k, (A_k, A)) + {…} + V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}, (A_k, A))].
  "Here A_k denotes the fluctuation field on Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ, and A denotes this field on Λ_{k+1}."
- *(3.26)–(3.27), p.271:* the logarithm of the last integral is written by interpolation in s (multiplying 𝐑_k″) and t (multiplying 𝐁_k, the A(1/g_k²(·) − 1/g_k², ·) terms and V^{(k)}), with ⟨·⟩_{s,t} "the expectation value with respect to the probabilistic measure" Z_{s,t}^{−1}dμ_{C^{(k)}(Λ_{k+1})}(A)χ^{(k)}exp[−⟨A, C*Δ^{(k)}CA_k⟩ + 𝐏^{(k)} + {…}_{s,t} + tV^{(k)}(S_{k+1})]. The print has χ_k and S_k in (3.27), read as χ^{(k)} and S_{k+1} as in the display before it; and the display called "(3.26)" on pp.275, 277, 279 carries no printed number (transcriber's notes, p.271; d2-2a m4).

### (2) The numerator identity at step k+1 (derived, k ≤ N − 1; repaired after d2-2a S2, 17:12–17:18, 22 Oct, date)

- *Linearity.* From (3.1) to (3.25), every operation of CRE pp.264–270 acts on the integrand of a fixed term in one of seven ways (pp.264–267 read in full for this list):
  - (i) inserting a decomposition of unity: (3.2) for P_{k+1} and (3.3) for Q_{k+1}, p.265, and (3.16) for R_{k+1}, p.268;
  - (ii) a pointwise identity on the support of the characteristic functions: (3.8)–(3.9), p.266, "χ_k(□) = 1 for □ ⊂ Ω_{k+1}^∼" and "χ_Ax((Ω_{k+1}^∼)^{(k)}) = 1"; and (3.17)–(3.19), pp.268–269, the small-field function of (3.3) is 1 on the cubes of R_{k+1}ᶜ;
  - (iii) a change of integration variables on the bonds of S_{k+1} (p.269), a bijection ψ_{k+1} of the A_k-variables there;
  - (iv) regrouping the sums: at fixed Ω_{k+1} the resummation over P_{k+1}, Q_{k+1} into ζ(Ω_{k+1}ᶜ), p.267, which "applies only to the characteristic functions and the gauge fixing terms localized in Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1}"; then the reindexing R_{k+1} ↦ (Λ_{k+1}, S_{k+1}) of p.270, a bijection since R_{k+1} = S_{k+1}∖(Ω_{k+1}∖Ω_{k+1}^{∼−1}); also the filling of Λ_{k+1}, a function of (Ω_{k+1}, R_{k+1}), and the pointwise regroupings of factors on pp.267, 269, 270 (d2-2a-r2 n10);
  - (v) splitting the A_k-integral into the conditional integral over A = A_k|_{Λ_{k+1}} and the outer integral in T^{(k+1)}, with the block form of the quadratic form ((3.23)–(3.25)): the exponent of (3.23) plus that of the last integral of (3.25) is −½⟨A_k + A, C*Δ^{(k)}C(A_k + A)⟩, the two terms with C^{(k)}(Λ_{k+1}) cancelling;
  - (vi) the change of variables of pp.266–267 on B(Γ_{k+1}): the fluctuation field V_k′ = V_k(V^{(k)})^{−1} ((3.11)), A_k′ = (1/i)log V_k′, the expansion "with respect to A_k′", the linearisation of the δ-functions, their removal "using the operator C", and the scaling A_k′ = g_kA_k. Together these give V_k = e^{iB′(A_k)}V^{(k)} with B′ = g_kCA_k − hD̃(g_kCA_k), and the Jacobian is part of the first exponential of (3.15) and of 𝐏^{(k)} (reading R-x, its 𝐏^{(k)} half, (iii)(b); d2-2a-r3 m2);
  - (vii) the axial gauge fixing of p.265, "as in (1.5), with V_k, ε_k, g_k instead of U, ε_0, g_0", in (P′^∼_{k+1} ∪ Z′^{∼5}_k)ᶜ, followed by the Faddeev–Popov procedure (p.265: "We get an expansion of the form (1.6)"; the procedure itself is on p.247, not reread here; d2-2a-r2 n7).
- *Each commutes with the insertion.* Multiply the integrand by a bounded function F of the integration variables.
  - (i), (ii), (v): directly; (v) by Fubini on the finite torus.
  - (iv): F does not depend on P_{k+1}, Q_{k+1}, and the p.270 regrouping is a reindexing, so F stays inside each term.
  - (vi): F is defined as composed with it, F = 𝒲_k(U_k(e^{iB′}V^{(k)})).
  - (iii): with F composed with ψ_{k+1}. This rests on three inputs. (a) ψ_{k+1} is a bijection between the supports (below). (b) *Reading R-x (exact changes, d2-2a m1).* p.269 prints: "The action is changed by terms corresponding to (1.24), and by terms obtained by the first order expansion of the action with respect to the perturbative parts of the changes of variables … We denote this new part of the action by V^{(k)}(S_{k+1},A_k,𝐇^{(k)})" ((1.24) not reread here; d2-2a-r2 n5). R-x reads V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}, A_k, 𝐇^{(k)}) as the exact change of the action under ψ_{k+1}, with its Jacobian (first-order Taylor formula with integral remainder), and 𝐏^{(k)} as the exact remainder of the p.267 expansion in A_k′. The 𝐏^{(k)} half is also what (vi)'s "=" uses. This is the reading under which CRE's "=" in (3.25) holds as printed, and the one the IT-1 note adopts at step 1 (gaps block of the IT-1 note). (c) p.269's "The other characteristic functions are unchanged" and "These changes of variables transform the function χ_k′(S_{k+1}) into the same function, but with V^{(k)} replaced by V^{(k)}_{Λ_{k+1}ᶜ∩Λ_k}", accepted as Bałaban's statements (m3). The identity itself does not depend on (c), since ∫G·F = ∫(G∘ψ)(F∘ψ)|det Dψ|; (c) only identifies the result with CRE's printed (3.25) and (3.27) (d2-2a-r2 n11). Whether the (3.16) tests of cubes next to S_{k+1} are unchanged under the "simpler change" is not visible from the text read; no counterexample is known.
  - (vii): the Faddeev–Popov step needs the integrand to be invariant under the gauge transformations u of V_k used there, which are 1 at the centres of the scale-(k+1) blocks and act in (P′^∼_{k+1} ∪ Z′^{∼5}_k)ᶜ ⊂ Λ_k. Without F this is CRE p.250's "invariance of all the expressions determining the density with respect to the gauge transformations of the new field" (quote completed from the transcription, d2-2a-r3 m8). For F, put ũ(x) := u(y) for x ∈ B^k(y). The block averages are covariant (RG1 (0.6), "M({u𝐔_jv}) = uM({𝐔_j})v"; REG p.78, "(Ū^u)^j_b = u(b_−)(Ū^j)_bu^{−1}(b_+) for b ⊂ T^{(j)}") and the action is invariant, so U_k(V_k)^ũ minimises under the constraint of V_k^u, as U_k(V_k^u) does. By the uniqueness of the minimiser in its orbit (Bałaban's; RG1 p.265 states it for V^{(k)}: "there exists the exactly one critical point, which is obtained by taking the critical orbit … and choosing the element of the orbit satisfying the axial gauge conditions"), U_k(V_k^u) = (U_k(V_k)^ũ)^r, with r in REG's subgroup (1.14) (r(y) = 1 for y ∈ 𝔅_k) restoring the gauge condition. *Repaired after d2-2a-r3 S1:* the earlier "agree up to the residual subgroup of (4)(d), compensated as there" is withdrawn, since that compensation rotates the slot values and changes F, and r = 1 on the blocks that matter is derived instead.
    - *r = 1 where ũ = 1 (derived from REG pp.78–79 and RG1 pp.252–253).* REG (1.19), "∏_{b⊂Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n}}R(Ū_0^n(Γ_{x_{n+1},b_−}))Ũ′^n_b = 1" with Ũ′^n = (overline{U′U_0})^n(Ū_0^n)^{−1} (1.20), telescopes to Ū^n(Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n}) = Ū_0^n(Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n}) for U = U′U_0 (for two bonds b₁, b₂: Ū_{b₁}Ū_{0,b₁}^{−1}·Ū_{0,b₁}Ū_{b₂}Ū_{0,b₂}^{−1}Ū_{0,b₁}^{−1} = Ū_{b₁}Ū_{b₂}(Ū_{0,b₁}Ū_{0,b₂})^{−1}). The absolute form (1.15) is the case U_0 = 1. By the covariance, for U^r the condition reads r(x_{n+1})Ū^n(Γ)r(x_n)^{−1} = Ū_0^n(Γ), so r(x_n) = Ū_0^n(Γ)^{−1}r(x_{n+1})Ū^n(Γ): r is fixed down each chain x_j, x_{j−1}, …, x₀ from r(x_j) = 1. The path Γ_{x_{n+1},x_n} joins x_{n+1} to x_n ∈ B(x_{n+1}), and the averages on its bonds use the contours 𝐆(y,x), "shortest contours with the initial point at y, and the final point at x" for x ∈ B(y) (RG1 p.252), inside the two blocks of each bond (RG1 p.253's M(𝐔,c)). So the conditions on a block B^k(y) involve the configuration on B^k(y) only. On a block where ũ = 1, U_k(V_k)^ũ = U_k(V_k) there, which satisfies them, so r = 1 on that block. This holds for any background U_0 that the transformation u leaves unchanged; U_0 = U_{k+1}(V_{k+1}) is unchanged, since u = 1 at the centres of the scale-(k+1) blocks and the averages are covariant. Which gauge condition the step's representative uses is the OPEN representative item (D2.2(4)(d)); the argument covers both (1.15) and (1.19).
    - Hence ũr = 1 on every block outside the gauge-fixing region, and R(ũr)ω = ω below. ũ = 1 on every block outside the gauge-fixing region, in particular near Z_k = Λ_kᶜ, which carries the outer fluctuation fields ω: they live on Ω_{j+1}∩Λ_{j+1}ᶜ ⊆ Λ_kᶜ, j < k, since Λ_k ⊆ Ω_k ⊆ Λ_{k−1} (pp.264–265), and at k = 1 on the bonds I∖D(Λ) and J of V3's exterior (IT-1 note §3d(2)(d)). So R(ũr)ω = ω, and Cov(k) gives F(U_k^{ũr}, ω) = F(U_k, ω) for the closed words, that is F(V_k^u) = F(V_k). CRE p.250 prints the same block-constant extension at step 1, "u_0(x)=v(y) for x ∈ B(y), and the field U_0 constructed in (1.6) transforms by u_0" (read for d2-2a-r3 m8).
- *The bijection ψ_{k+1} (d2-2a m2; restated after d2-2a-r2 n4).* ψ_{k+1} is a bijection between the supports by (6)(a), the step-(k+1) form of V3 Lemma boundary-bijection (V3 L548). Its inputs are Inputs 1–4 of (6)(a), Input 3 being |Z_b − 1| < C_Zδ_k from (3.13). Its conditions are e^{R_k} ≥ 40·O(1)A₀/A₁ and δ_k ≤ δ_G(C_Z), both uniform in k for g_K small by g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K (coupling item, reading R-cpl₀; relabelled after d2-3 N8). It rests on five readings of the gaps block, as (6)(a) lists them: the (k)* convention, R-g_k, and Input 4's three (the k-independent O(1) of p.269, R-cpl₀, and the bound on S_{k+1} for bonds leaving S_{k+1}) (d2-2a-r3 m1).
- The nonlinear step, the treatment of the logarithm of the A-integral from the unnumbered display of p.271 on, concerns the normalisation only.
- *The identity.* Apply (i)–(vii) to each term of (3.1) for the numerator N_k^C = Σχ_kT_k[𝒲_kexp A_k] (D2.0(3)) with F := 𝒲_k(U_k(e^{iB′}V^{(k)}))∘ψ_{k+1}, B′ = g_kCA_k − hD̃(g_kCA_k) (D2.1(1)). Then
  T N_k^C = Σχ_{k+1}(Ω_{k+1})T_{k+1}[𝒲_{k+1}exp A_{k+1}], with 𝒲_{k+1} := ⟨F⟩_{1,1},
  where exp A_{k+1} is CRE's integrand of (3.25) (A_{k+1} the logarithm, unchanged) and ⟨·⟩_{1,1} is the expectation of (3.27) at s = t = 1: at s = t = 1, (3.27) is the normalised integrand of the last integral of (3.25), with the constant −½⟨A_k, …A_k⟩ cancelled by the normalisation.
  - Z_{1,1} ≠ 0: the last integral of (3.25) equals z^{(k)}·(Gaussian normalisation)·e^{−½⟨A_k,…A_k⟩}·Z_{1,1}, and CRE takes its logarithm in the unnumbered display of p.271; p.279 states that the resulting terms "satisfy all the conditions of the inductive assumption" (accepted with CRE's construction; d2-2a m6).
  - F is finite on the support of the characteristic functions: |𝒲_k| ≤ |κ^{(k)}|e^{|𝓔_k|}∏_n(1+ν_n^{(k)}) < ∞ on the finite torus, from the class form of D2.0(3). The identity uses no other bound on F (m8).
- *What 𝒲_{k+1} depends on.* V_{k+1} (through U_{k+1}); the outer variables of T_{k+1}, namely those of T_k, A_k on Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ and V_k on Ω_{k+1}ᶜ; and the labels. These are D2.0(3)'s outer variables at step k+1 (CRE (2.20)–(2.21)).
- So D2.2's task is to write ⟨F⟩_{1,1} in the class form of D2.0(3) at step k+1, with D(k+1)(ii)–(v) (d2-2a m7).

### (3) The carrier at step k+1 (D2.6's carrier item)

- *(a) The carrier is CRE's Λ_{k+1}.* The observable F is integrated in A = A_k|_{Λ_{k+1}} under ⟨·⟩_{1,1}; A_k on Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ is outer. D2.2 adds no label at step k+1. At step 1, V3's labels Λ = Λ₁^fin refine CRE's Λ₁ (IT-1 note D1.0(P)); whether the integration of (6) needs a refinement of Λ_{k+1} is decided in (6). What (6) can use without one: on Λ_{k+1}^∼ the small-field tests of (3.16), and on all of Ω_{k+1} the bound |A_k′| < O(1)ε_k of CRE (3.14), which gives D2.1's condition |B′| ≤ ε_H under O(1)ε_k ≤ ε_H (D2.1(4); d2-2a m10).
- *(b) The step-1 domain Λ = Λ₁^fin at the later steps (repaired after d2-2a S1, m11–m14; every line of CRE pp.255–284 containing Λ_j, Z_j or φ_j read in its context, pp.264–267 in full, 17:18–17:25, 22 Oct, date).*
  - *The data.* With (P), the step-1 data of a labelled term are (Ω₁, Λ₁, Λ, R₁, S₁). Ω₁, Λ₁, R₁, S₁ are CRE's, Λ₁ stays an outer label, and Λ = Λ₁^fin ⊆ Λ₁ is made by V3's retreat and plays CRE's Λ₁ at the later steps, with Z₁ = Λᶜ (IT-1 note §3c(4⁗), §3f(2) M5; m11).
  - *The printed conditions on the domains, at Λ.* p.255: "if a component of Z_j is contained in a cube of the size 100MR_j (in the L^{−j}-lattice), then it is a rectangular parallelepiped". p.256: Ω_j, Λ_j, "which are determined by he j-th renormalization transformation, but not by the R-operation, are unions of MR_j-cubes in the lattice T_{L^{−j}}. They satisfy also other conditions, e.g., the distance between their boundaries is at least equal to 2MR_j, which will be determined inductively by the operations in the k+1-st step" (m12: two conditions printed, others announced without a list; print "he" for "the", clause restored after d2-2a-r3 m10). At Λ: Λ is a union of ℓ-cubes, the MR₁-cubes of T_{L^{−1}} (ℓ = LMR₁ fine, D2.0(1)); Λ ⊆ Λ₁ ⊆ Ω₁^{∼−2}, so the distance condition holds (IT-1 note §3c(4′)); and Λ is filled, since the retreat ends with Fill, the iteration of CRE's p.251 modification until nothing changes (V3 L2407, L2410, L2419). P.251 (transcription, not in the r3 packet; d2-2a-r3 m8): components of Λ₁ᶜ "which are contained in cubes of sizes smaller than 100LMR_1" are replaced "by the smallest rectangular parallelepipeds containing them". Filling can merge components; the label indicator still factorises over the ∗-components of Λᶜ (IT-1 note §3c(4⁗)(d), which treats Fill_1 explicitly).
  - *The uses of Λ_j, j ≤ k, on pp.262–284, each with the property it needs and its status at j = 1:*
    1. *𝐄^{(j)}(Λ_j, U_k, z)* with (2.26)–(2.27): z ∈ Λ_j⁰, the sum over X ∈ 𝐃_j with X ⊂ Λ_j, properties (i)–(iv) of p.259. Used in (2.43) (p.263, Ω ⊂ B^j(Λ_j⁰)), on p.276 ("For the term 𝐄^{(j)} it is in fact restricted to the domain Λ_j"), and in the proof of Theorem 2 and the renormalisation, pp.279–284 (cases (I.3.5) for X ⊂ Λ_j; comparison with "(2.26) for Λ_j = L^{−j}Z^d", p.282). Needs: the representation at Λ, and that the terms with X ⊂ Λ do not depend on Λ, the step-1 form of p.278's remark ("do not depend on Λ_{k+1}, and coincide with the corresponding terms arising from" the whole space). At Λ: built by CRE's step-1 construction at Λ (D1.0(P)(i), IT-1 note §3c(4″)), under reading R-c.
    2. *The coupling function.* (2.24), p.259: 1/g_{j−1}²(x) = 1/g_j²(x) + β_j(g_{j−1})φ_j(x), φ_j ∈ C_0^∞(Λ_j), φ_j = 1 on Λ_j^{∼−1}. Used on p.263 (Ω = B^j(Λ_j⁰), φ = φ_j), in the terms A(1/g_k²(·) − 1/g_k², ·) of (3.15) and the unnumbered display of p.271, in p.279's update by (2.24), and on p.283 ("For z ∈ Λ_j⁰ we have h_zφ_j = h_z"). At Λ: φ₁ ∈ C_0^∞(Λ), φ₁ = 1 on Λ^{∼−1}, by CRE (1.27) at Λ (p.253, checked in the transcription, not in the r3 packet, d2-2a-r3 m8: "We renormalize it in the region Λ₁ … φ₁ ∈ C_0^∞(Λ₁), φ₁ = 1 on Λ₁^{∼−1}"), which (P) applies with Λ (IT-1 note §3c(4), "A₁^Λ given by CRE's (1.26)–(1.30) with Λ₁ replaced by Λ"). h_zφ₁ = h_z for z ∈ Λ⁰ needs only that Λ is a union of MR₁-cubes. The j = 1 piece β₁(g₀)(1 − φ₁) of 1/g_k²(·) − 1/g_k² vanishes on Λ^{∼−1}, which contains every later Ω_{k+1} ⊆ Ω₂ ⊆ (Z₁′^{∼6})ᶜ (p.265, (3.5)), as for CRE's Λ₁.
    3. *E_k.* p.262: "The constant E_k (depending on {Ω_j}, {Λ_j} also) is obtained by subtracting one-step vacuum energy expressions, generated in small field regions, from the initial constant E". Λ enters through the step-1 subtraction only, the values at U₁ = 1 in Λ (p.253: "We renormalize it in the region Λ₁ subtracting the values at U₁=1", checked in the transcription, d2-2a-r3 m8). At Λ: by (1.26)–(1.30) at Λ, under (P). Its bound (p.264: "E_k, except the terms logarithmic in coupling constants, has the same bound as above, only with a different constant") holds at Λ (d2-2a-r2 n3, n6): at k = 1, Λ^{(1)} ⊆ Ω₁^{(1)} = Γ₁; for k ≥ 2 the bound is CRE's for every Λ₁ satisfying the printed conditions, under reading R-c (D1.0(P)(i)), as in (b)'s conclusion, and Λ satisfies them (second bullet of (b); d2-2a-r3 m3).
    4. *The 𝐁-bound (2.47)–(2.48), p.264 (d2-2a-r2 n2).* Λ_k⁰ enters (2.47) in the terms j = n ("this inequality with |Γ_n∖Λ_k⁰| for j = n"). Since Γ_n ∩ Λ_k = ∅ for n < k (Λ_k ⊆ Ω_k ⊆ Ω_{n+1}), (2.48) keeps it only for n = k. So Λ enters only at k = 1: |𝐁₁| ≤ 2B₁|Ω₁^{(1)}∖Λ⁰| (IT-1 note §3c(4⁗)(a), under R-c).
    5. *The label sums* over {Ω_j}, {Λ_j}, {S_j} in (3.1), (3.15) and on p.270. The operations act term by term ((2)). Each step-1 term has the form (2.18) at k = 1 with Λ (D1.0(P)), and the label sum is IT-1 note §3c(4⁗)(d)'s.
    6. *Z_k = Λ_kᶜ, pp.264–265 (m14).* It is the input of Z_k′, "the union of all LMR_{k+1}-cubes intersecting the region Z_k", and of the step-(k+1) restrictions built around Z_k′^{∼4}, …, Z_k′^{∼6}. The construction takes any union of cubes, so at k = 1 it applies to Z₁ = Λᶜ.
    7. *p.269, j = k.* The determining set 𝐁(Λ_{k+1}ᶜ∩Λ_k) ∪ 𝐁_{k+1} of (2.14), with 𝐁_k(Λ_k)|_{Ω_{k+1}ᶜ}, the minimiser U_{k+1,Λ_{k+1}ᶜ∩Λ_k}, V^{(k)}_{Λ_{k+1}ᶜ∩Λ_k}, 𝐇^{(k)}, and with them ψ_{k+1} and V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}). At k = 1 they use Λ through 𝐁₁(Λ)|_{Ω₂ᶜ}, which needs Λ to be a union of M₁-cubes (p.261's construction). This holds: CRE's cube partitions are nested, so each ℓ-cube is a union of M₁-cubes (V3 L548, on pp.245–246).
    8. *(3.38)–(3.39), p.274:* Euclidean covariance of the whole-space construction (Ω_j = Λ_j = T_η); no Λ_j.
    9. *p.277 (d2-2a-r2 n1).* The last expression in the curly bracket of (3.27), or (3.15), A(1/g_k²(·) − 1/g_k², U_k(…)) − A(1/g_k²(·) − 1/g_k², U_{k+1}), is localised by "supp(g_k^{−2}(·)−g_k^{−2}) ⊂ Z_k" (d2-2a-r3 m4). By (2.24) and the constant recursion of (I.0.20), cited on p.259 ("Here the coupling constants g_{j−1} are defined as in (I.0.20)"; (I.0.20) is RG1's and not reread here; (1.27) at j = 1), with 1/g_0²(·) = 1/g_0², g_k^{−2}(·) − g_k^{−2} = Σ_{j≤k}β_j(g_{j−1})(1 − φ_j); p.259 prints the case j = k as "Thus g_j²(x)=g_j² on the last domain" (d2-2a-r3 m5). The j-th summand vanishes on Λ_j^{∼−1}. Λ_k ⊆ Ω_{j+1} ⊆ Λ_j^{∼−1} for j < k (item 2's inclusion, Ω_{j+1} ⊆ (Z_j′^{∼6})ᶜ, p.265). So the support lies in (Λ_k^{∼−1})ᶜ, which is what the localisation uses, since Ω_{k+1} ⊆ (Z_k′^{∼6})ᶜ ⊆ Λ_k^{∼−1} (item 2's inclusion at j = k), so (Λ_k^{∼−1})ᶜ ⊆ Ω_{k+1}ᶜ ⊆ Λ_{k+1}ᶜ, and the terms are localised away from Λ_{k+1}, as "contribute to the boundary terms only" requires; the print's "⊂ Z_k" is not needed (d2-2a-r3 m6). At j = 1 the inclusion is item 2's Ω₂ ⊆ Λ^{∼−1}.
       - *The inclusion (Z_j′^{∼6})ᶜ ⊆ Λ_j^{∼−1} (d2-2a-r3 m7).* By (2.9), second line at n − m = 1, R_j ≤ L(1+g_{j+1}²β′)^{β₀}R_{j+1} < L²R_{j+1} for g small, and the R's are powers of L, so R_j ≤ LR_{j+1}. So an LMR_{j+1}-cube of p.264's partition contains every MR_j-cube it meets (the partitions are compatible, p.264), and (Λ_j^{∼−1})ᶜ ⊆ Z_j′^{∼1}. The same holds in item 2.
    10. *The inductive form on pp.257–261 at j = 1 (d2-2a-r2 n9).* Each use is at Λ by the inductive form at k = 1, through (P) (D1.0(P)(i), IT-1 note §3c(4); §3c(4) is not in the review packet, d2-2a-r3 m11):
       - (2.19)–(2.22): T_k(Z_k)'s factorisation and C^{(0)}(Λ), with Z₁ = Λᶜ;
       - (2.25): the subtractions;
       - (2.41)–(2.42): the 𝐁^{(1)} localisation X∩Ω₁ ≠ ∅, X∩(Λᶜ)^∼ ≠ ∅, analyticity on Ũ₁ᶜ and the bound B₀e^{−κd₁(X)};
       - p.262's alternative with d_j(X∖Z_j), which is not used.
    - Pages 271–273, 275 and 278 use only Λ_{k+1}, the new step's own domain; p.274 is item 8 and p.277 item 9; pp.280–281 contain no Λ_j, Z_j or φ_j.
  - *𝐑^{(j)}* (p.260 (2.30)–(2.31): X ⊂ Λ_j^{∼−1}, unions of MR_j-cubes; (2.44), p.263) arise from the R-operations, so they go with D2.7.
  - *Conclusion (small-field objects).* Every use of Λ_j, j ≤ k, on CRE pp.262–284 needs either a geometric property that Λ has (unions of MR₁- and M₁-cubes, the 2MR₁ distance, filling) or a step-1 object that (P) builds at Λ by CRE's own formulas, under reading R-c. So the small-field part of CRE Sect. 3 accepts Λ at every later step. The large-field objects (D1.0(P)(ii), whose j = 1 factor is R-d″(ii)) and the 𝐑-terms are not covered here (until D2.7).
- *(c) Not covered by (b).* The large-field parts of the later steps (LFR I, LFR II: the bounds of the T-operation and Bałaban's induction pp.384–389, with the geometry of (1.76)), the R-operations and 𝐑^{(j)}, and p.256's announced "other conditions", which are not listed. On pp.262–284 those conditions enter only through the properties listed in (b); their content stays with D2.6 (large fields) and D2.7 (the R-operation) (d2-2a-r2 n8; d2-2a-r3 m9). If (6) needs no refinement of Λ_{k+1}, no further retreat occurs, and the enlarged (1.84) of IT-1 note §3c(4⁗) is needed for the j = 1 factor only (m13).

### (4) Composition of 𝒲_k with the step-(k+1) variables, with D2.5 (gauge covariance) (written 15:17–, 22 Oct)

*(a) The covariance property Cov(k) (definition).*
- *Gauge transformations* are G-valued functions u on the sites of the fine lattice. They act on fine configurations by 𝐔^u(b) = u(b_−)𝐔(b)u(b_+)^{−1}, and on the outer fluctuation fields ω (the A_j among D2.0(3)'s outer variables) by R(u), (R(u)A)(b) = u(b_−)A(b)u(b_−)^{−1}, as in CRE (1.17), p.250, and (2.41)(iii), p.261 ("the variables 𝐉, A are transformed by the adjoint representation"). The outer block variables V_j enter only through the background: CRE p.266, U_{k+1} "is determined by the sequence of domains {Ω_{k+1},Ω_k,…}, and the corresponding sequence of fields {V_{k+1}|Γ_{k+1},V_k|Γ_k,…}".
- *Cov(k).* Every insertion of 𝒲_k is the value at 𝐔 = U_k of a function Y_W(𝐔, ω) of a fine configuration 𝐔 and of ω, both restricted to a domain X_W containing the interval of W. For k ≥ 2 it is the value at (𝐔, 𝐉) = (U_k, J_k) of a function Y_W(𝐔, 𝐉, ω) (D2.0(3), amendment of D(k)(iii)), and the law below reads Y_W(𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉, R(u)ω) = π(u(x_W))Y_W(𝐔, 𝐉, ω)π(u(x′_W))^{−1}; Cov(1) has no 𝐉 (d2-2d-r2 S-new-1). It is analytic on the domain of D(k)(iii), jointly in 𝐔 and ω (D(k)(iii), *Outer fluctuation fields*), and
  Y_W(𝐔^u, R(u)ω) = π(u(x_W)) Y_W(𝐔, ω) π(u(x′_W))^{−1} for every u,
  where x_W is the initial and x′_W the final endpoint of the interval in the orientation of C, as in V3 (c4-11-5) (d2-2b m1). Under 𝐔^u(b) = u(b_−)𝐔(b)u(b_+)^{−1} a transport along an arc maps the fibre at its final point to the fibre at its initial point, and Y_W is read the same way. This is the matrix analogue of CRE (2.27)(i)–(iii) and (2.41)(i)–(iii); for closed words it reduces to CRE's invariance (m2).
- *Consequence.* Every closed word dim(π)^{−1}tr(Y_{W₁}U_{(1)}⋯Y_{W_m}U_{(m)}), with 𝐔-transports U_{(i)} and the leading word as the case m = 0, is invariant under (𝐔, ω) ↦ (𝐔^u, R(u)ω): the conjugations cancel at each endpoint, and tr is conjugation invariant.
- *The scalar parts.* At step 1 the interactions and the local terms are numbers on each labelled term (IT-1 note §4(iii): "φ and e_X are numbers on each labelled term"). D(k) is read with the same clause: Q_k(𝔅) and 𝓔_k do not depend on V_k, so they pass through the substitution unchanged. This is an output requirement on (6) at step k+1: the field-dependent parts go into the insertions, and the reference and Schur scalars go to D2.3. It is D2.0(3)'s D(k)(v) (added 15:5x, 22 Oct).

*(b) Cov(1) (from V3 and the IT-1 note).*
- The step-1 insertions are Y_W = X_W·(transport of the interval), with X_W = Υ¹_W + a^Λ_W·id (IT-1 note §4). Their extension to 𝐔 = e^{ia′}U₁ is covariant, with a′ ↦ Ad_u a′, on every labelled term (IT-1 note §3e(2)(b): "The formula of each factor is covariant identically in 𝐔"; §3e(4)).
- The coefficient form is V3 (c4-11-5), c_W(U^g; g(o)hg(o)^{−1}) = c_W(U;h). Burnside (V3 Lemma c5-5-7(a)) turns it into the matrix form: for irreducible π the π(h), h ∈ G, span End(V_π), so tr(Xπ(h)) = 0 for all h forces X = 0. This holds for every compact simple G.
- *The exterior inputs.* On uncovered terms the rows of a group that is not good consult V₀, A_e and a_old (IT-1 note §3d(4)). V₀ consists of fine links (the unintegrated original field on Ω₁ᶜ), and A_e, a_old are fluctuation fields. V3's formulas use them through the same operations as the free variables: plaquette actions, the block mean (covariant by RG1 (0.6), V3 L5031), the chart logarithm (conjugation covariant, V3 L88(ii)), Haar measure, and the cross term s⟨a, H_{ξ,IJ}A_e⟩ of (c4-11-1). With U ↦ U^g on links and R_g on fluctuations, the intertwinings of §c4-11-2 (Q_{U^g}R_g = R^{(1)}_gQ_U, h_{U^g} = R_gh_U(R^{(1)}_g)^{−1}) apply term by term. So Cov(1) holds with ω = (A_e, a_old). V₀, the original field on Ω₁ᶜ, is the background there and is part of 𝐔 (d2-2b m3).
- *Not yet in the packets (d2-2b m11).* The quoted sentence of IT-1 note §3e(2)(b), the exterior-input claim of §3d(4) and the printed formula Y_W = X_W·transport of §4 go into the next packet of this part.

*(c) The substitution identity (derived from Cov(k) and reading R-g).* For real A_k on the support, let w be the gauge transformation of R-g, U_k(e^{iB′}V^{(k)})^w = e^{iη𝐇_k(B′)}U_{k+1}. By Cov(k),
F = 𝒲_k(e^{iη𝐇_k(B′)}U_{k+1}, R(w)ω)∘ψ_{k+1}.
- *Factors without ω-dependence:* w drops out, as for the leading word (D2.1(2)). On covered terms this covers every factor.
- *Factors with ω-dependence:* w enters through R(w)ω. Its locality is (d).
- *ψ_{k+1}* changes only the A_k on S_{k+1} ⊆ Λ_{k+1}ᶜ (p.269). These are outer variables at step k+1, so ψ_{k+1} does not affect the dependence on the integrated field A = A_k|_{Λ_{k+1}}.
- *Complex A_k and complex backgrounds (after d2-2b S2).* The identity theorem applies jointly, as in D2.1(2). The right side is analytic:
  - in the background by D(k)(iii), and in ω by D(k)(iii)'s *Outer fluctuation fields* clause;
  - in w, since with the representative of (d) w = u_k^{−1}, which is analytic in 𝐇_k for |𝐇_k| < a₁ (RG1 p.266).
  - R(w)ω stays in the ω-domain. (R(w)ω)(b) = Ad_{w(b_−)}ω(b), so |R(w)ω − ω| ≤ (e^{2c_G|log w|} − 1)|ω| bond by bond, with c_G := sup_{X∈𝔤^c}‖ad_X‖/(2|X|), the operator norm on 𝔤^c of the Hermitian extension of the Ad-invariant norm; this holds for complex w and ω (d2-2b-r2 R5; for a product w = ∏e^{Z_j} with real conjugating transports, with Σ|Z_j| for |log w|). So, on the layer of ω_j, dist(R(w)ω, real support) < r^{(k)}_{ω,j} per bond when dist(ω, real support) + (e^{2c_G|log w|} − 1)|ω| < r^{(k)}_{ω,j} (the neighbourhood form of D(k)(iii) with its per-layer radii, d2-2b-r2 N6; d2-2b-r3 m3).
  - At k = 1 the real inputs satisfy |ω| ≤ Cr, C absolute ((6)(e), j = 0; d2-2b-r3 m2), in B-units on the support and r_ω^{(1)} = 1 (D(k)(iii)), so this holds for real ω and |𝐇_1| = O(ε_1). For k ≥ 2 the bound on |ω| on the support, the radii r^{(k)}_{ω,j} per component and the form |log w| ≤ C_wθ^{k−j−1}h_k of |log w| on older layers are derived in (6)(e) (d2-2b-r2 N8).

*(d) w is block-local in 𝐇_k (derived from the quoted pages; repaired after d2-2b S3, m5, 17:03–17:12, 22 Oct, date).*
- RG1 p.265 builds w from two transformations. The first takes U_k′(V′) "into the axial gauge Ax_k(T^{(k)},U_{k+1})" and is "equal to 1 on T^{(k)}". The second, u_k, changes "the axial gauge into the Landau gauge" and "can be expressed in terms of the configuration 𝐇_k(B′) by the formula (106) in [12] with U_1 = exp iη𝐇_k". Here [12] is "Averaging operations for lattice gauge theories", CMP 98 (RG1 p.299; not yet in a packet, m11).
- *The representative (after d2-2b S3).*
  - VAR p.305: "U_0=U_k(V_0), and if we fix a gauge condition for U_k(V′V_0)U_0^{−1}, then it is an analytic function of B=1/i log V′". RG1 p.265 takes V_0 = V^{(k)}: (2.3) "V^{(k)}=Ū^k_{k+1}=M^k(U_{k+1})", and "U_k(V′V^{(k)})=U_k′(V′)U_{k+1}". So the reference is U_0 = U_k(V^{(k)}) = U_{k+1}.
  - It does not depend on B′: U_{k+1} is determined by {V_{k+1}|Γ_{k+1}, V_k|Γ_k, …} (CRE p.266), B′ lives on B(Γ_{k+1}) (CRE (3.11)), and Γ_k = Ω_k^{(k)}∖Ω_{k+1}^{(k)} (CRE (2.2) at step k+1) lies outside Ω_{k+1} (d2-2b-r2 N12). Reading RG1's single-domain T^{(k)} as 𝔅_k is CRE p.267's transfer ("only the symbols represent different operators, determined by the sequence {Ω_j}"; d2-2b-r2 m5).
  - *Which representative (after d2-2b-r3 S2, 22:46–22:59, 22 Oct, date; replaces the argument that the choice is free).* There are two representatives in play: ρ_k(V_k), at which D(k)'s identity holds with the slot values of the step-k integration, and the step map's representative of U_k(e^{iB′}V^{(k)}) in Ax_k(𝔅_k, U_{k+1}). They are related by RG1 p.265's first transformation v₁, which depends on B′, because the configuration being gauge-fixed varies with B′. RG1's reason that v₁ is harmless, "This does not change the expressions in (2.1) because this gauge transformation is equal to 1 on T^{(k)}", covers Bałaban's expressions, not the ω-dependent factors, and (6)(b7)'s rotation covers only B′-independent changes. So the earlier sentence "the choice is free, and D(k) need not fix a representative" was wrong (d2-2b-r3 S2).
    - *Route (i): state D(k) at VAR Sect. G's axial configuration.* VAR p.305 (Sect. G, "Analyticity and an Expansion of U_k"; [15] of RG1 is VAR): "We take U_0=U_k(V_0) and we consider the pair of configurations U_k(V′V_0), U_0. We repeat the whole procedure of Sects. A–E for this pair. At first we fix the Landau gauge for the configuration U_k(V′V_0)U_0^{−1}=U_1". VAR p.307: "We make the gauge transformation inverse to the one applied previously, i.e. we transform the configuration in the Landau gauge back to the axial gauge. … we obtain a gauge field configuration in the axial gauge, which we denote also by U_k(V′V_0). It is an analytic function of V′ … and for V′ with values in G it coincides with the minimal configuration constructed in the previous sections". VAR p.305 calls U_k(V) "a (multi-valued) function of the average variables V", so this fixes a branch.
    - Put ρ_k^{ax}(V_k) := this U_k(V′V₀), with V₀ = V^{(k)} = M^k(U_{k+1}) and U₀ = U_k(V₀) = U_{k+1} (RG1 (2.3)). Its axial gauge is that of the pair (U_k(V′V₀), U₀), i.e. Ax_k(𝔅_k, U_{k+1}) (REG (1.19) with reference U_{k+1}; multi-domain by CRE p.267's transfer, as above). REG p.79: "The conditions (1.19) determine uniquely an element in each orbit given by the subgroup (1.14)", so ρ_k^{ax} is the step map's representative, and nothing is left to choose within Ax_k(𝔅_k, U_{k+1}) (the earlier "Two representatives … differ by an element u of REG (1.14)'s subgroup" is withdrawn). If D(k)'s identity holds at ρ_k^{ax}, with the slot values read in that frame, then v₁ = 1 and (d) stands as written.
    - *What route (i) needs: OPEN.* It needs the step-(k−1) map to deliver D(k) at ρ_k^{ax}. The sources do not give this. RG1 p.265 transforms U_k′(V′) into Ax_k(T^{(k)}, U_{k+1}) "at first", so its U_k(V′V^{(k)}) is not in that gauge in general. Its reference is fixed by "choosing the element of the orbit satisfying the axial gauge conditions 𝐆(V)=0" (p.265), which is not shown to be the axial configuration relative to the next reference. At k = 1, V4's U₁ is "in its axial mean gauge" of CRE p.248 (V4 L2148, L4672), not in Ax_1(T^{(1)}, U₂). Re-representing D(k) afterwards by a gauge transformation that depends on V_k is B′-dependent at step k, which is the problem itself.
    - *Route (ii): OPEN.* Put w := u_k^{−1}v₁ in (c), and prove for v₁ the three properties of (d): block locality in (𝐇_k, U_{k+1}), analyticity, and a B′-independent value at 𝐇_k = 0. Then build (e)'s w(s) from both factors; (6)(b7)'s rotation absorbs only the B′-independent part. The axial construction should give these: REG (1.19), and CMP 98 (64)–(72) (REG p.79: "They were introduced in [3], (64--72) by the conditions"), which are not read (not in the packets).
    - Until one route is closed, (c)–(e) hold at the step map's representative, and the identification with D(k)'s representative is OPEN (gaps block, d2-2b-r3 S2).
  - No change of integration variables is made. (The earlier argument by a change of the outer integration variables with CRE p.250's invariance of the rest of the integrand was withdrawn after d2-2b-r2 N1: p.250 excepts χ_0′(Ω_1) from the invariance under (1.17), and its invariance of χ_0′ is under block-constant transformations, not REG (1.14)'s subgroup. The argument that the choice of representative is free was withdrawn after d2-2b-r3 S2.)
  - At the step map's representative ρ_k^{ax}, U_k′(V′) is already in Ax_k(T^{(k)}, U_{k+1}), which is REG (1.19) with reference U_{k+1}. So the first transformation is 1 there; at D(k)'s representative it is v₁, and the bullets above say what is open. RG1 p.265 prints "U_k′^{u_k^{−1}} = exp iη𝐇_k(B′)", so in R-g's notation w = u_k^{−1} (d2-2b-r2 N5); locality, w₀ = 1, |log w| = |log u_k| and analyticity are those of u_k.
- *u_k on a block B^k(y) ⊂ Ω_k.* CMP 98 (106), p.33, gives u(x) for x ∈ B^k(y), y ∈ Ω^{(k)}, along the chain x = x_0 ∈ B(x_1), …, x_k = y. It is a product over j = k−1, …, 0 of the inverse transports R(U_0(Γ^{(j+1)}_{x_{j+1},x}))^{−1} and of the brackets [(average)^{−1}(R̄^j_{0,x_{j+1}}Ū̄^j_1)(Γ_{x_{j+1},x_j})], with the averages (105) over the blocks B(x_{j+1}).
  - *Locality.* Every contour and every average (105) in this product lies in B^k(y), and "the definition of (Ū̄_1^k)_c, c ⊂ Ω^{(k)}, involves only the gauge fields U_{1,b}, U_{0,b} at bonds b ⊂ B^k(c_−) ∪ B^k(c_+)" (p.34). For an order-j average at a scale-j bond c ⊂ Γ_{x_{j+1},x_j}, j < k, p.31's general remark gives the fine bonds of B^j(c_±) ⊂ B^{j+1}(x_{j+1}) ⊂ B^k(y) (d2-2b-r2 N12). So u_k on B^k(y) depends only on (𝐇_k, U_{k+1}) on the bonds inside B^k(y).
  - *u_k = 1 there when 𝐇_k vanishes on those bonds.* Then U_1 = 1 there and R(U_0)1 = 1. The one-step average (89), p.31, of V_1 = 1 is 1, since every logarithm in the point averages vanishes and (V̄₁V₀)_c(V̄₀)_c^{−1} = 1. So Ū̄_1^j = 1 by (90)–(91), every bracket of (106) is 1 by (105), and R(·)1 = 1.
  - *Size.* Proposition 4, p.38: Q_j(U_0, ηA) = (1/i)log Ū̄_1^j is analytic in A, and the display on p.38 gives |(L^jη)^{−1}Q_j(U_0, ηA)| ≤ 2α_1 for |A| < α_1 and small parameters. A contour Γ_{x_{j+1},x_j} in B(x_{j+1}) has O(L) scale-j bonds. So the j-th bracket has |log| ≤ O(L)L^jη sup|𝐇_k| = O(1)L^{j+1−k}sup|𝐇_k|, the real transports R(U_0(·))^{−1} preserve |·|, and the sum over j ≤ k−1 is geometric: |log w| ≤ O(1)sup|𝐇_k|. The same holds at complex 𝐇 with |𝐇| < min(a₁, c₄), by the analyticity of Proposition 4, whose own range is c₄ (d2-2b-r3 m4).
  - *Proposition 4's hypotheses (d2-2b-r2 N10).* It needs α₀, α₁ ≤ c₄ for U_0 = U_{k+1}: plaquette regularity of U_{k+1} on every block used, which CRE (3.12) gives on Ω_{k+1}; on Ω_j∖Ω_{j+1}, j ≤ k, U_{k+1} "coincides with a U_j-type configuration, it has the same regularity properties and bounds" (CRE p.275); and a size condition on 𝐇. At real B′, on Ω_k, |𝐇| ≤ 4B₀e^{16κ_s}|B′| = O(ε_k). On (f)'s polydisc the size is h_k = O(α_{1,k}) ((6)(e)), so the condition is h_k < min(a₁, c₄), which holds for small couplings (d2-2b-r3 m4).
- *Blocks of the older layers.* On a block B^j(y), y ∈ VAR's Λ_j, j < k (VAR p.277: B^j(Λ_j) = Ω_j∖Ω_{j+1}; d2-2b-r2 N11), the axial conditions are REG (1.15), (1.19) with the chain of j steps, and REG p.81 uses CMP 98's formulas on this multi-domain lattice: "In [3] we have determined the gauge transformation u in terms of the configuration U_1. We refer the reader especially to the formulas (87), (99), (97), and (105) of that paper" ([3] is CMP 98, REG p.102). For bonds with both endpoints in Λ_j the left side of REG (1.30) "is equal to (Ū̄_1^j)_b by (92) of [3]"; the bonds that cross ∂Λ_j are treated by (97), (99), (87) of [3]. CMP 98 (97), p.32, is v_j(x) = (R_{0,x}U_1)‾(R̄_{0,x}Ū̄_1)‾⋯(R̄^{j−1}_{0,x}Ū̄_1^{j−1})‾, x ∈ Ω^{(j)}, the product of the averages of orders 0, …, j−1, and (99) is (R_{0,x}U_1)‾^{(j)} = v_j(x). REG states the multi-domain statement ("The rest of this paper is devoted to a proof of this statement", p.81) as Bałaban's result.
  - *Reading R-REG81 (labelled, d2-2b-r2 N2; for the user's review).* On B^j(y), y ∈ VAR's Λ_j, u is CMP 98's (106) with the chain of j steps, the product (97) truncated at order j. This gives the three properties of the k-blocks with k replaced by j: locality in B^j(y) (each factor of (97) is an average over blocks inside B^j(y)), u = 1 where 𝐇 vanishes on the block, and the size bound with L^{i+1−j} per order i < j. REG p.81 does not name (106); the reading is that its "determined … in terms of the configuration U_1" is (106) at order j. No counter-evidence: REG (1.29), "(R̄_0u)^j(y)=1 for y∈Λ_j", is the order-j analogue of CMP 98 (86). The bonds crossing ∂Λ_j are case (B) of D2.1(4) and go to D2.6 with it.
- *Result.* On each block B^j(y), j ≤ k, w is a function W_y of (𝐇_k, U_{k+1}) on the bonds inside the block, analytic in 𝐇_k for |𝐇_k| < a₁ (RG1 p.266), with |log W_y| ≤ O(1)sup|𝐇_k| for |𝐇_k| < min(a₁, c₄) (d2-2b-r3 m4), and W_y = 1 when 𝐇_k vanishes on the block (w₀ = 1).

*(e) The joint expansion (derived from (c), (d) and D2.1(4)).*
- *Interpolation.* With the parameters s and 𝐇(s,B′) of D2.1(4), put w(s) := (W_y(𝐇(s,B′), U_{k+1}))_y. For one old family 𝔅, let Φ_𝔅(s) be its closed word evaluated at (e^{iη𝐇(s,B′)}U_{k+1}, R(w(s))ω). Q_k(𝔅) is a constant by (a).
  - Φ_𝔅(1) is the word of (c), since 𝐇(1,B′) = 𝐇_k(B′) (D2.1(4)(a)).
  - Φ_𝔅(0) is the word at (U_{k+1}, ω), since 𝐇(0,B′) = 0 and w₀ = 1 ((d)).
  - W_y is given by (106) for every complex 𝐇 with |𝐇| < a₁ (RG1 p.266), and obeys (d)'s size bound for |𝐇| < min(a₁, c₄) (d2-2b-r3 m4), so w(s) is defined for every s, also where 𝐇(s,B′) is not the 𝐇 of a configuration. The identity of (c) is used at s = 1 only.
- *Locality at s(σᶜ) = 0.* 𝐇(s,B′) vanishes on a neighbourhood of Y(σ)ᶜ (D2.1(4)(b)). So w(s) = 1 on every block all of whose bonds lie in that neighbourhood, in particular on every block disjoint from Y(σ) (d2-2b m4). A factor then takes its s = 0 value unless its domain X_W, its interval or, for an ω-dependent factor, a block meeting X_W, meets a component of Y(σ). Blocks are the B^j(y), y ∈ VAR's Λ_j, j ≤ k, of (d) (m5; d2-2b-r2 N11).
- *Expansion.* The fundamental theorem of calculus in each s(Δ), as in D2.1(4), gives Φ_𝔅(1) = Σ_σ ∏_{Δ∈σ}∫₀¹ds(Δ)∂_{s(Δ)}Φ_𝔅(s)|_{s(σᶜ)=0}. A component on which Φ_𝔅 does not depend gives zero.
  - Call a component Y *active* for 𝔅 if it contains a fine bond of C, or meets X_W for some W ∈ 𝔅, or meets a block meeting X_W for an ω-dependent W ∈ 𝔅 (d2-2b m9).
  - Only the σ whose components are all active remain.
- *Arcs and new groups.* For an active Y let S′(Y) be the fine bonds of C in Y together with the intervals of the W ∈ 𝔅 for which Y meets X_W (or, ω-dependent W, a block meeting X_W). Let I(Y) be the smallest arc containing S′(Y) by D2.1(4)'s rule, then enlarged at each end to the end of any old interval that it cuts. Old intervals are pairwise disjoint (old compatibility), so the enlargement stops after at most one interval at each end, and every old interval lies inside I(Y) or is disjoint from it.
  - The new groups G are the connected components of the overlap graph of the arcs I(Y), with I(G) := ∪_{Y∈G}I(Y).
  - The new insertion of G is Y_G := [∏_{Y∈G}∏_{Δ⊂Y}∫₀¹ds(Δ)∂_{s(Δ)}] of the ordered product along I(G) of the old insertions with intervals in I(G) and the transports of e^{iη𝐇(s,B′)}U_{k+1} along the pieces of C between them, at s = 0 off the components of G. Its endpoints are on C, at the ends of I(G).
  - An old insertion whose interval lies in no new arc stays, evaluated at (U_{k+1}, ω). Since w₀ = 1, it is an insertion of step k+1 with the same group, domain and interval (d2-2b m10).
- *Result.* For real A_k on the support,
  F = κ^{(k)}e^{𝓔_k}[f_{k+1} + Σ_{𝔊≠∅}dim(π)^{−1}tr(Y_{G₁}U_{(1)}⋯) + Σ_{𝔅≠∅}Q_k(𝔅)Σ_{𝔊 compatible with 𝔅}dim(π)^{−1}tr(word of (𝔅,𝔊))]∘ψ_{k+1}.
  - The first sum is D2.1(4)'s.
  - In the second, the word of (𝔅,𝔊) is the product along C, in cyclic order, of the new insertions Y_G, the untouched old insertions and the U_{k+1}-transports between them.
  - 𝔊 is *compatible with 𝔅* if each of its components is a connected union of cubes (D2.1(4)'s clause (0), d2-2b m9), its components are active for 𝔅 and pairwise disjoint without a common wall, its arcs are disjoint, and each group is connected by arc overlaps.
  - The map σ ↦ 𝔊 is a bijection onto the families compatible with 𝔅, as in D2.1(4).
  - For complex A_k and backgrounds the same formula holds, by (c).
- *Locality.* Y_G depends on B′ and (𝐔,𝐉) on the components of G, on ω on the domains of its old insertions, on U_{k+1} along I(G), and on the blocks of (d) meeting these domains. This is D2.2(0)'s "localisation of the observable", in the form (5) needs.
- *Gauge covariance of the new pieces (closed in (6)(b), not re-reviewed; d2-2b-r3 m5).* B′ transforms by R(v) for gauge transformations v of V_{k+1} (RG1 p.265: "A gauge transformation v of V induces the gauge transformation v of V^{(k)} and the transformation B′ → R(v)B′"). Each factor of Y_G is then covariant in (U_{k+1}, ω) if 𝐇(s,·) is built from covariant operators. That RG2's walk expansion has this property is not printed on the pages read (RG2 pp.3–4; d2-2b e11, m11). It is checked with Cov(k+1) in (6); (g) lists what it needs.

*(f) The analyticity domain of the old factors (with reading R-m).*
- The bounds of (e), by the Schwarz lemma and Cauchy's estimate as in D2.1(4), need every old factor to be analytic at e^{iη𝐇(s,tB′)}e^{iηa′}U_{k+1} for |s(Δ)| ≤ e^{κ_s}, |t_Y| ≤ T_Y and a′ in the radius of D(k+1)(iii), and, for ω-dependent factors, with ω replaced by R(w(s,t))ω. That stays in the ω-domain by (c) with sup|𝐇(s,tB′)| ≤ min(β,1)α_{1,k}/2 in place of sup|𝐇_k| on Ω_k, and on older layers with (6)(e)'s decaying bound (d2-2b-r3 S1); at k = 1 since α_{1,1}r → 0, and for k ≥ 2 by (6)(e)'s propagation (d2-2b-r2 N7). The leading word needs no condition, since a character of a holonomy is entire in the background.
- Write the point as e^{iηA″}X₁ with the real centre X₁ := e^{iη𝐇_k(B′)}U_{k+1} = U_k(e^{iB′}V^{(k)})^w, which lies in the support up to the gauge transformation w. By BCH and (1.18), |A″| ≤ 4B₀e^{16κ_s}(T_Y|B′|_Y + sup|B′|) + |a′| + O(·)², with sup|B′| for the term 𝐇_k(B′), since 𝐇_k is not local (d2-2b m6).
- D(k)(iii) asks |A″| below the step-k radius of CRE (2.39), and the step-(k+1) radius is smaller:
  - on Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1}, n < k, by β2^{−(k+1−n)}α_{1,n}L^{k−n} (in units of |A″|; the phase margin per fine bond is this times η = L^{−k});
  - on Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1} by (β/2)α_{1,k};
  - on Ω_{k+1} by α_{1,k} − α_{1,k+1}/L (the step-(k+1) radius there is that of n = k+1). This is ≥ α_{1,k}/2 when α_{1,k+1} ≤ (L/2)α_{1,k}, and that holds (d2-2b m7). CRE (2.6), first line, with n = k+1, m = k (reading R-cpl₀, d2-2b-r3 m7), gives g_{k+1} ≤ (1+β₀)g_k. If g_{k+1} ≥ g_k, then α_{1,k+1}/α_{1,k} ≤ g_{k+1}/g_k ≤ 1+β₀ ≤ L/2 for L ≥ 2(1+β₀), since (log g^{−2})^{q₁} decreases as g grows. If g_{k+1} < g_k, then α_{1,k+1} < α_{1,k}, since g(log g^{−2})^{q₁} increases in g once log g^{−2} > 2q₁.
  So the condition is 4B₀e^{16κ_s}(T_Y|B′|_Y + sup|B′|) < margin, for a′ within the step-(k+1) radius: the margin is already the step-k radius minus the step-(k+1) radius, and |a′| is below the latter (d2-2b-r2 N3). B′ vanishes off B(Γ_{k+1}) and |B′| < O(1)ε_k there at real configurations (CRE (3.11), (3.14), (3.15); D2.1(4), *The hypothesis*). On Ω_k, with T_Y ≥ 1, it holds when 8B₀e^{16κ_s}T_YO(1)ε_k < min(β, 1)α_{1,k}/2.
- *Reading R-m.* CRE p.277 states the same requirement for the boundary terms 𝐁_k and asserts it: "we have to use a part of the analyticity domains of terms in 𝐁_k for the function 𝐇_k. This is the reason for putting the powers of 1/2 in the conditions (2.36)–(2.39). The analyticity domains become smaller after each step, but the difference is very small and exponentially decreasing in the number of steps. The localization of the fluctuation field, and the exponential decay of the minimizing function 𝐇_k imply that this difference is still much greater than bounds on this function" (the middle sentences restored after d2-2b-r3 m10; the older layers use the decay, (*The older regions*) below). Accepted as Bałaban's statement. It applies to the old factors because D(k)(iii) is posed on the same domains (D2.0(3), complex part of (2.39)).
  - *What R-m entails (after d2-2b S1).* On Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1} the margin (β/2)α_{1,k} is the power-of-1/2 difference, and there is no decay to use there. So R-m's "much greater than bounds on this function" is the inequality 8B₀e^{16κ_s}O(1)ε_k < (β/2)α_{1,k}.
  - On Ω_{k+1} R-m does not apply, since the margin α_{1,k} − α_{1,k+1}/L is not a power-of-1/2 difference. But that margin is ≥ α_{1,k}/2 ≥ (β/2)α_{1,k}, as β ≤ 1, so the same inequality closes it.
  - So the expansion of (e) needs, and R-m gives, 8B₀e^{16κ_s}O(1)ε_k < min(β,1)α_{1,k}/2. Two points are part of the reading (d2-2b-r3 m8): "much greater than bounds on this function" is read as at least a factor 2 (the "/2"); and when q₁ = p₀, (6)(e)'s complex-data form with the constant 32C₁^{RG2} below is read into R-m too. For q₁ > p₀ both forms hold for g_K small without the reading. Without it already Φ_𝔅(0), and the Cauchy estimates in s(Δ) at t_Y = 1, would leave the domain.
