  - With ε_k = g_kA₀(log g_k^{−2})^{p₀} (CRE (2.4), p.255) and α_{1,k} = g_kC₁(log g_k^{−2})^{q₁} ((2.28), p.259: "q_0, q_1 are integers greater than 1, C_0, C_1 are sufficiently large positive numbers"), the inequality is q₁ > p₀ for g_K small (g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K for k ≤ K, CRE (2.6) with reading R-cpl₀, d2-2b-r3 m7), or q₁ = p₀ with C₁/A₀ > 16B₀e^{16κ_s}O(1)/min(β,1) (d2-2b-r2 N4). (6)(e)'s complex data need the stronger C₁/A₀ > 32C₁^{RG2}B₀e^{16κ_s}O(1)/min(β,1) in the equality case (d2-2e; d2-2b-r2 N8). Recorded with R-m: V3 L3968's "p_0 ≥ 5𝔯" (line corrected after d2-2d-r2 m11) (at CRE p.246) and CRE p.259's "C_0, C_1 are sufficiently large positive numbers".
  - No printed comparison of q₁ with p₀ was found: q₁ occurs only on CRE p.259 and LFR II p.368, and p₀ is not fixed on CRE p.255 (search of the CRE, RG1, RG2, LFR I, LFR II and UV3 transcriptions, 17:03–17:12, 22 Oct, date). The entailment is recorded with R-m for the user's review (gaps block). A printed q₁ < p₀ would contradict R-m and would be reported as a counterexample.
  - *T_Y.* T_Y is taken as large as the margin allows. By the inequality T_Y ≥ 1, and 1/T_Y = max(|B′|_Y/ε_H, 16B₀e^{16κ_s}|B′|_Y/(min(β,1)α_{1,k})) ≤ 1. This is the factor per component in D2.4's count.
- *The older regions (corrected after d2-2b-r2 N9; repaired after d2-2b-r3 S1, by 22:45, 22 Oct, date).* On Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1}, n < k, the margin in the units of |A″| is β2^{−(k+1−n)}α_{1,n}L^{k−n} = (β/2)α_{1,n}(L/2)^{k−n}. CRE (2.6), first line, gives g_k ≤ (1+β₀)(k−n)^{1/2}g_n, and (2.7), first line (both with reading R-cpl₀, d2-2b-r3 m7), (log g_k^{−2})^{q₁} ≤ (1+β₀)(log g_n^{−2})^{q₁}; so α_{1,n} ≥ α_{1,k}/((1+β₀)²(k−n)^{1/2}), and the margin is ≥ (β/2)α_{1,k}(L/2)^m/((1+β₀)²√m), m = k−n ≥ 1.
  - The deformation there is not bounded by the Ω_k inequality: the inequality of (f) bounds it on Ω_k only. The earlier sentence "the same inequality closes the older layers in the sup norm, without decay" was wrong (d2-2b-r3 S1); the file's own D2.1(3), D2.1(4) case (B) and (6)(e) say so.
  - The decay closes it. On Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1}, sup|𝐇(s,tB′)| ≤ O(1)(Lθ)^{m}h_k with θ := e^{−δ₀RM₁/32}, by VAR (190), PRT2's weighted distance with VAR (1), and, on the polydisc, reading R-dec (labelled; it replaces R-adm's "decay form", which misread RG2 (1.18), d2-2e-r2 S1), as derived in (6)(e) (*Older layers*: the layer of ω_j is Ω_{j+1}∖Ω_{j+2}, so k−j−1 = m). Against the margin it suffices that O(1)(1+β₀)²√m(2θ)^mh_k < (β/2)α_{1,k}. Since h_k ≤ min(β,1)α_{1,k}/2 by R-m's inequality in its complex-data form ((6)(e)), and √m x^m ≤ x for 0 < x ≤ 1/2, m ≥ 1, this holds for all m ≥ 1 once 2θ ≤ 1/2 and 2θ·O(1)(1+β₀)² < 1. That is so for g_K small, since R ~ R_j is large uniformly in j ((6)(e)).
  - So the older-layer margin rests on the decay, as CRE p.277 says (the sentence on the exponential decay of 𝐇_k, restored in R-m's quote above, d2-2b-r3 m10), and on reading R-dec. No condition L ≥ 3 or (1+β₀)² ≤ 3/2 is needed for it. The gradient part follows R-dec and R-adm as in (6)(e); the curvature and 𝐉 parts on the older layers are in (6)(e), *The curvature conditions*, *Older layers* (d2-2e-r2 S2). Case (B) of D2.1(4) goes to D2.6 for the sizes of its insertions; for this margin see (6)(e).
  - The bounds sup|𝐇(s,tB′)| ≤ min(β,1)α_{1,k}/2 of (f) and |𝐇| ≤ 4B₀e^{16κ_s}|B′| of (d) are on Ω_k (d2-2b-r3 S1, matching updates).
- *Carrying 𝐉 (k ≥ 2; after d2-2d S1, by 19:54, 22 Oct, date).* For k ≥ 2 the old factors are functions Y_W(𝐔, 𝐉, ω) (D2.0(3), amendment of D(k)(iii)). At the deformed background the 𝐉-argument is carried as Bałaban carries it for 𝐄^{(j)}:
  - *Not in the packets yet (d2-2b-r3 m9).* RG1 (3.10)–(3.13), p.272, RG1 p.273, RG2 p.2 and PBG (3.42), p.397, quoted below, were NOT SUPPLIED in packet it1-d2-2b-r3; they go into the next packet of this part.
  - RG1 (3.10)–(3.13), p.272. 𝐉′ = D^{ξ*}_{e^{iξ𝐀}𝐔}ξ^{−2}π Im∂e^{iξ𝐀}𝐔 is expanded by (3.11). For 𝐀 in the Landau gauge, the operator D*D is replaced by Δ_𝐔^ξ − P₁ + (lower order, local operator) (p.272: "We use the fact that 𝐇_j satisfies the Landau gauge condition RD*𝐇_j=0"). The old term becomes the function (3.13) of (𝐔, 𝐉, 𝐀), with second argument (L^{j−1}η)³𝐉 + Δ_𝐔^ξ𝐀 − P₁𝐀 + 𝐅₁(𝐔,𝐀). It is analytic for 𝐀 satisfying (3.14): |𝐀|, |P₁𝐀|, |∇_𝐔^ξ𝐀|, |Δ_𝐔^ξ𝐀| < α₂ on X.
  - RG2 p.2 states this structure for every old term: "there exists a function 𝐄(X,𝐔,𝐉,𝐀) analytic and localized to X in 𝐔, 𝐉, 𝐀, such that a given term is obtained substituting a proper nonlocal expression in the place of 𝐀". So Ỹ_W(𝐔, 𝐉, 𝐀, ω) := Y_W at the (3.13)-substituted arguments is local in 𝐀 on X_W's neighbourhood. At 𝐀 = 𝐇_k(B′), which is in the Landau gauge, it is the old factor at the true configuration ((3.11)–(3.12)). At 𝐀 = 0 it is Y_W(𝐔, 𝐉, ω).
  - The s-expansion of (e) substitutes 𝐀 = 𝐇(s, tB′), with (𝐔, 𝐉) in D(k+1)(iii)'s domain. At s ≠ 1, 𝐇(s, tB′) need not be in the Landau gauge, and nothing requires it: Φ_𝔅(s) is a value of the analytic function Ỹ_W, equal to the true word at s = 1 and to the word at (𝐔, 𝐉, ω) at s = 0.
  - The condition. (3.14) for 𝐀 = 𝐇(s, tB′), with α₂ replaced by the 𝐉-margin β2^{−(k+1−n)}α_{0,n}(L^nξ)^{−3} between the step-k and step-(k+1) 𝐉-bounds of CRE (2.36), in the units of (3.13). At s = 1 RG1 p.273 asserts it: "We assume also that ε₁ is so small that 𝐇_j(B(t)) satisfies (3.14) with 1/3α₂ on the right-hand side". For the s-localised 𝐇(s, ·) the |P₁𝐀| and |Δ_𝐔𝐀| bounds are RG2 (1.18)'s "same bound for all admissible norms" with reading R-adm: PBG Theorem 3.1, (3.42), p.397, lists |(Δ_UG′(U)λ)(x)| among its norms, with weight 1 against (L^jη)² for |G′λ|. RG1 p.272 promises such bounds: "for terms in this expansion we will have good bounds, including bounds for the covariant Laplace operator". So R-adm is extended to the Laplacian and P₁ norms (gaps block). The resulting smallness condition on ε_k is of the kind of (f)'s, and its sizes go to D2.4.

*(g) What (4) gives, and what it leaves.*
- *Gives.* F is written exactly, on every labelled term, as f_{k+1} plus compatible families of local insertions at step k+1. These are new groups, built from s-components and the old insertions they touch, together with the untouched old insertions. The scalar parts are unchanged. Covered and uncovered terms are treated alike, and the gauge transformation is made explicit and block-local (w = u_k^{−1}, w₀ = 1). D2.5's composition part, for all compact simple G: Cov(1) holds; Cov(k) ⇒ (c)–(e) for the factors without ω-dependence; for the ω-dependent factors with D(k)(iii)'s ω-clause, the representative of (d), and the bound on |ω| of (c), whose propagation is (6)'s (d2-2b g1).
- *Leaves to (5).* The localised pieces of (e) and the fluctuation measure (3.27) must go into one cluster expansion, with the s of (e) and the covariance decoupling of RG2 Sect. 2 (IT-1 note §3i(2)).
- *Leaves to (6).*
  - Cov(k+1): the integrated insertions must be values at (𝐔, 𝐉) = (U_{k+1}, J_{k+1}) of covariant functions of (𝐔, 𝐉, ω′), where ω′ is ω together with the slot variable σ_k of A_k on Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ. The construction is RG1 p.272 after (3.12), "we replace J_{k+1} by the variable 𝐉, and in the remaining expressions we replace U_{k+1} by the new variable 𝐔", and p.273 for the operators.
  - This needs every ingredient of (6) to be built covariantly from (𝐔, 𝐉): the measure (3.27), the s-localised operators, and the transports. Done in (6)(b), after d2-2d.
  - The scalar parts of the output must not depend on V_{k+1} ((a)).
- *Leaves to D2.4.* The sizes of the new insertions: the factor per component 1/T_Y of (f) from the Schwarz lemma, times the old insertion's own weight, against the count. Also the radius condition 4c_πM‖a′‖_∞ ≤ 1 of D2.1(4), now along the arcs I(G) that contain old intervals; after the merging of (6)(d) the constant is 20 (d2-2e-r2 m1).
- *Leaves to D2.6.* Case (B): components meeting Ω_kᶜ, the bonds crossing ∂Λ_j in (d), and the sizes of the ω-dependent factors. For these R(w)ω is complex for complex B′, with |R(w)ω − ω| ≤ (e^{2c_G|log w|} − 1)|ω| ((c)); the bound on |ω| on the support at k ≥ 2 is derived in (6)(e) (d2-2b-r2 N8, N9). The older-layer margin of (f) rests on (6)(e)'s decaying bound on 𝐇(s,tB′) (d2-2b-r3 S1).

### (5) The joint cluster expansion (derived from RG2 (2.1)–(2.12), written 16:00–16:03, 22 Oct, date)

*(a) Reduction to tensor expectations.* By (4)(e), F is a finite sum (finite torus) of terms c·T, with c = κ^{(k)}e^{𝓔_k}Q_k(𝔅) a number (D(k)(v)) and T = dim(π)^{−1}tr(X₁U₁X₂U₂⋯X_mU_m) a closed word.
- The X_i are the new insertions Y_G and the untouched old insertions; the U_i are U_{k+1}-transports. Only the Y_G depend on the integrated field A = A_k|_{Λ_{k+1}}, through B′. Let J be the set of these slots. Y_{G_i} depends on A only on D_i := the union of the components of G_i (D2.1(4)(b), (4)(e) *Locality*).
- In matrix entries, T = dim(π)^{−1}Σ_{α,β}∏_i(X_i)_{α_iβ_i}(U_i)_{β_iα_{i+1}} (α_{m+1} := α₁) is multilinear in the X_i. So ⟨T⟩_{1,1} is the contraction, with the A-independent factors, of the tensor expectation ⟨𝒳⟩_{1,1}, 𝒳 := ⊗_{i∈J}X_i, a function of A with values in ⊗_{i∈J}End(V_π). If J = ∅ (the leading word f_{k+1} and the words of untouched old insertions only), ⟨T⟩ = T.
- ⟨𝒳⟩_{1,1} = I(𝒳)/I(1), with I(·) the last integral of CRE (3.25) with the observable inserted, at fixed outer variables ((2)).

*(b) Source of the expansion (repaired after d2-2c S1, m2, by 20:16, 22 Oct, date).* RG2 Sect. 2 (pp.11–14) expands the fluctuation integral of RG1 (2.13) ("Thus we have prepared the integral in (I.2.12), or (I.2.13), to the last step in our construction", p.11), with the action localised as Σ_{Y∈𝐃_k}𝐕_k(Y,B) (RG2 Lemma 2, (1.41)). For the measure of CRE (3.27) Bałaban's own statement is CRE pp.275–279 (read in full in the transcription; p.275 was NOT SUPPLIED in packet d2-2c-r2 and goes into the next packet, d2-2c-r2 m6):
- p.275: "Let us consider now the expectation values in the formulas (3.26), (3.28), (3.37). To construct their cluster expansions we repeat all the considerations of Sect. 3.7 [I]" (so printed), with the multi-scale cover {□}, "which is a cover of Ω_1" (p.275), built from "cubes □, which are unions of 2^d neighboring cubes from π_j", and its partition of unity.
- p.276 localises "the actions defining the measures (3.27), (3.29)": "The action in (3.29) differs only by the additional dependence on the field tA_k, but this was already taken into acount [so printed] in the above considerations. The action in (3.27) contains more expressions" (d2-2c-r2 m7): 𝐑_k as 𝐄_k; 𝐁_k by (I.3.6)–(3.7), localised over Y₀ ∈ 𝐃_k meeting Λ_{k+1}. p.277: these terms "can be estimated as in (2.42), but with the additional factor O(ε_k) arising from the bound of the fluctuation field"; the A(1/g_k²(·) − 1/g_k², ·) terms are localised in Z_k; V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}) likewise.
- p.277: the normaliser of the last expectation value in (3.37) "is expanded in exactly the same way as in Sect. 7 [I]" and "represented as exp Ẽ^{(k+1)}(Λ_{k+1})", given by (I.7.12) with terms (I.7.13). The numerator with an inserted localised term is (3.44), with one marked domain Z₀; "for n>0 the factors H(Z_i) are the same as in the expansion of the denominator". p.278: the sum over the polymers not meeting Z₀ along a cube or a wall "is exponentiated, and the exponent is represented by (I.7.12), with the additional restriction on the polymers Z_i" ((3.45)–(3.46)).
- p.279: "The other expectation values in (3.26), (3.28) are treated in exactly the same way. We have discussed already the localized expansions for the actions defining the corresponding measures, and for the integrated expressions. Having these expansions we proceed with the cluster expansions, and with the operations described above in (3.44)–(3.47)."
- The first expectation value of the display called (3.26) is ⟨·⟩_{s=1,t}, t ∈ [0,1], under (3.27), whose source term −⟨A, C*Δ^{(k)}CA_k⟩ carries no t ((1)). At t = 1 it is ⟨·⟩_{1,1}. So the localised action of (3.27) at s = t = 1, the exponentiation of Z_{1,1} and the exponentiation of the restricted gases are Bałaban's construction as presented, accepted as such.
- *Reading R-vac (labelled, d2-2c S1, m4).* (i) "treated in exactly the same way" (p.279) includes the convergence of the cluster expansions of Z_{1,t} and of the gases restricted to polymers compatible with given domains, for every t ∈ [0,1] and with the source term at full strength. On the supplied pp.276–279 Bałaban's statements on that term are p.276's sentence "The action in (3.29) differs only by the additional dependence on the field tA_k, but this was already taken into acount [so printed] in the above considerations", with the analyticity space (3.43), and p.279's "Similarly, the first expectation value in (3.26) is included into this boundary term, too", which names ⟨·⟩_{s=1,t} under (3.27) directly (d2-2c-r2 m4). The field A_k there can be of size O(ε_k/g_k) (p.267: |A_k′| < O(1)ε_k, A_k′ = g_kA_k). (The earlier clause on the support of C*Δ^{(k)}CA_k and the label "d2-2c N6" are dropped: no supplied page gives that locality, and no step uses it; d2-2c-r2 m5.) No counterexample is known, so the statement is used as Bałaban's. In (i), "convergence" means absolute convergence (d2-2c-r2 m3). (ii) Bałaban's "sufficiently small" (RG2 (2.12)) is used in the Kotecký–Preiss form Σ_{Z′≁Z}|H(Z′)|e^{a(Z′)+d(Z′)} ≤ a(Z) for every polymer Z and every single cube Z = {□} (d2-2c-r2 m2), with a(Z) = c|Z| and d(Z) = 9 log2·|Z|, |Z| the number of cubes; then Σ_{𝒞≁X}|φ^T(𝒞)|e^{d(𝒞)} ≤ c|X| for every union X of cubes, d(𝒞) := Σ_jd(Z_j) (a cluster ≁ X has a member ≁ {□} for a cube □ of X, since a wall in a union of closed cubes is a face of one of them). The weights d are needed for the bounds of (d) and in (6)(c). (ii) is derived in D2.4(2), (e)–(g), with c = (9/64)e^{−4κ} for κ ≥ κ_vac, and is no longer a reading of its own. The derivation needs (i) in a bound form, reading R-vac(i′) of D2.4(2): the activities of the gas of (3.27) with the source term satisfy (2.38) with Bałaban's constants (d2-4-r1 S4); and reading R-L3 of D2.4(1).
- *Reading R-I7 (labelled, d2-2c m2): "Sect. 7 [I]" is RG2 Sect. 2.* CRE's [I] is RG1 (CMP 109) "and references therein" (CRE p.285). CRE's "Sect. 7 [I]" and "(I.7.1)"–"(I.7.13)" match RG2 Sect. 2 under (7.n) ↔ RG2 (2.n); for "Sect. 6 [I]" only (I.6.41) ↔ RG2 (1.41) is evidenced on the supplied pages. The lemma numbers do not follow the equation rule: "Lemmas 5, 6 [I]" are read as RG2's lemmas of Sect. 1, of which only Lemma 2 is supplied (d2-2c-r2 m9; the earlier "whose sections are 0–5" is dropped as NOT SUPPLIED). The evidence: RG2 (2.1) is the Mayer expansion, (2.11)–(2.13) the exponentiated expansion, (1.41) the localisation of Lemma 2, and RG2 p.15 prints "|(7.14)|" for the bound of (2.14) (transcriber's note there). RG2's introduction: "This expansion is described in Sect. 2". The identity is not printed as such.
- So (c)–(d) run RG2 (2.1)–(2.12) on the measure (3.27) at s = t = 1, with CRE's localised action (pp.276–277) for Σ𝐕_k(Y,B), CRE's cover (pp.275–276) and χ^{(k)} of CRE (3.16), p.268 (d2-2c-r2 m10; p.268 goes into the next packet).

*(c) The expansion with marked supports (derived).* Run RG2 (2.1)–(2.11) on I(𝒳):
- (2.1) Mayer expansion of exp Σ_Y𝐕_k(Y): unchanged, with 𝒳 multiplying the integrand.
- (2.2) Y₀ := ∪_{Y∈𝐃}Y ∪ D_J^# (after d2-2c m1). Here D_J^# is the smallest union of cubes of the partition π_k (CRE p.278: "Z̄_0^c is the union of all cubes from π_k, such that they do not intersect Z_0, or intersect it along a two-dimensional edge at most") that contains in its interior every bond on which some Y_{G_i}, i ∈ J, depends. It is unique, being the intersection of all such unions (d2-2c-r2 m1: p.276's cover {□} overlaps and has several sizes, so a smallest union of its cubes need not be unique). This is the convention RG2 p.12 uses for P ("bonds of P have to be contained in the interior of Z_0"). Z₀ is the smallest localisation domain containing Y₀ and P. D_J^# is a union of such cubes, so Z₀ is formed as in RG2. The enlargement from ∪D_i to D_J^# costs a bounded factor per cube in D2.4's count.
  - D2.4(2) counts "π_{k+1}-cubes" of RG2's 𝐃_{k+1}. *CRE p.278's π_k (checked 00:02–00:05, 23 Oct, date, after the d2-2c-r2 triage; transcriptions read).* The families are different: π_j is the cubes "of a size M" in the scale of T_{L^{−j}} (RG1 p.257), of side ML^jη in CRE's η-scale (CRE p.275), and a π_{k+1}-cube is "of the size LM in the scale corresponding to the lattice T_η" (RG2 p.13), the union of L⁴ cubes of π_k (RG2 p.19 sums over "(L+2)^4 cubes □′ from π_k"). P.278 uses π_k only to formulate the condition that "the domains Z_i do not intersect Z_0 along a cube, or a wall of a cube" as Z_i ⊂ Z̄_0^c. Z_0, …, Z_n "satisfy all the conditions described in Sect. 7 [I] in connection with the formula (7.11)" (p.277), so under R-I7 they are unions of π_{k+1}-cubes. For such unions the two formulations agree. The π_k-lattice refines the π_{k+1}-lattice (both have side a power of L and nested centres), so Z_i ⊂ Z̄_0^c if and only if every π_k-subcube q of every π_{k+1}-cube Q of Z_i meets Z_0 in a set of dimension ≤ 2. Q ∩ Z_0 is Q or a union of faces of Q, since Q and Z_0 are made of cubes of one lattice, and each face of Q of dimension ≥ 3 is a union of faces of subcubes of the same dimension. So this holds if and only if Q ∩ Z_0 has dimension ≤ 2, which is the condition stated with π_{k+1}-cubes. So the index does not change the condition, and D2.4(2)'s count in π_{k+1}-cubes is the count of RG2's polymers. D_J^#, a union of π_k-cubes, enters only through Z₀, its π_{k+1}-hull with P.
- (2.3) the decomposition of χ on Y₀^{c*} (after d2-2c m8). CRE's χ^{(k)} is a product of sup-tests at δ_k over cubes (p.268, (3.16): χ({sup_{b∈(□′^{∼2})^{(k)*}}|A_k(b)| < δ_k})), and each test is on single-bond values |A_k(b)|, so it is a product of per-bond indicators. (2.3)'s decomposition then applies bond by bond, with CRE's thresholds in place of RG2's ε_1/g_k.
- (2.4)–(2.6) conditioning on Z₀ᶜ: the integrand F(Z₀,B)𝒳 is localised in Z₀ with respect to B, which is all that (2.5) uses. 𝒳 is a function of the conditioned Z₀-variable B, since D_J^# ⊂ Z₀, and the X-variables enter only through Γ_k. So (2.5)–(2.6) hold with 𝒳 inside G (d2-2c m9).
- (2.8) s-localisation of C^{(k)}(Z₀), (C^{(k)})^{1/2}, Δ_k: unchanged. 𝒳 carries no s-parameter, so H(Z,Z₀) now also contains 𝒳, and its external-field dependence is localised in Z together with the arcs and blocks on which the Y_{G_i} depend ((4)(e) *Locality*).
- (2.9)–(2.10) factorisation. RG2 states (2.10) for the components of Z ("From the definition of H(Z) it is also clear"). With 𝒳 it holds over *linked components*: call two components of Z linked if some D_i meets both (a group with several components gives a disconnected D_i). The Mayer factors, the χ-factors, the conditioned Gaussian integrals at s(σᶜ) = 0 and the factors of 𝒳 all split over linked components, by the same argument. A linked component with no slot of J has the vacuum activity H(Z) of (2.11). One with slot set J(M) ≠ ∅ has a *marked activity* H̃(M) with values in ⊗_{i∈J(M)}End(V_π).
- (2.11) Hence I(𝒳) = Σ_𝔐 (⊗_{M∈𝔐}H̃(M))·Ξ(𝔐), where 𝔐 runs over the families of pairwise compatible (ζ = 1) marked linked polymers whose slot sets partition J, and Ξ(𝔐) := Σ ∏H(Z) over the families of vacuum polymers compatible with each other and with every M ∈ 𝔐. The tensor product is over slots, so the order of the factors does not matter. I(1) = Ξ := Ξ(∅).

*(d) The ratio (Bałaban's exponentiation formula, RG2 (2.12); repaired after d2-2c S2, by 20:16, 22 Oct, date).* Under R-vac, which gives the smallness that RG2 (2.12) uses ("If the activities H(Z) … are sufficiently small, then the polymer expansion can be exponentiated according to the well-known formula"),
Ξ(𝔐)/Ξ = exp(−Σ_{𝒞≁𝔐}φ^T(𝒞)), φ^T(Z₁,…,Z_n) := (1/n!)ρ^T(Z₁,…,Z_n)H(Z₁)⋯H(Z_n),
the sum over the ordered vacuum clusters 𝒞 = (Z₁,…,Z_n) of which some Z_j is incompatible with some M ∈ 𝔐. This is the same formula, applied to Ξ and to the gas of vacuum polymers compatible with 𝔐, whose cluster sum is that of Ξ minus the clusters incompatible with 𝔐. The restricted gas has the activities H(Z)·1[Z compatible with 𝔐], so the Kotecký–Preiss condition of R-vac(ii) passes to it; for one marked domain this is CRE p.278's restricted exponentiation.
- *Per-polymer form (exact).* For a cluster 𝒞 let S_𝔐(𝒞) := {M ∈ 𝔐 : 𝒞 ≁ M} and s := |S_𝔐(𝒞)|. Put
  - Ψ(M) := Σ_{𝒞≁M}φ^T(𝒞), all clusters incompatible with M;
  - Ψ_≥(T) := Σ_{𝒞≁M for every M∈T}φ^T(𝒞), for T ⊆ 𝔐;
  - Q_{k+1}(𝔐) := exp(Σ_{T⊆𝔐,|T|≥2}(−1)^{|T|}Ψ_≥(T)).
  Then Ξ(𝔐)/Ξ = ∏_{M∈𝔐}e^{−Ψ(M)}·Q_{k+1}(𝔐). Proof: a cluster with s ≥ 1 appears s times in Σ_MΨ(M) and once in Σ_{𝒞≁𝔐}, and Σ_{T⊆S_𝔐(𝒞),|T|≥2}(−1)^{|T|} = (1−1)^s − 1 + s = s − 1. So both sides have the exponent −Σ_{𝒞≁𝔐}φ^T, and Q_{k+1}(𝔐) = exp(Σ_𝒞(s−1)_+φ^T(𝒞)). The rearrangement is absolutely convergent: a cluster lies in 2^s − 1 − s of the Ψ_≥(T) with |T| ≥ 2, and s ≤ 9|supp 𝒞| ≤ 9Σ_j|Z_j|, because the M are pairwise compatible and each M ∈ S_𝔐(𝒞) contains a cube of supp 𝒞 or one of its wall-neighbours, at most 9 per cube in d = 4 (ζ = 0 for a shared wall, RG2 p.14), the cubes and walls being those of π_k ((c)). On the finite torus s ≤ |𝔐| < ∞, so the rearrangement converges absolutely whenever Σ_{𝒞≁𝔐}|φ^T(𝒞)| < ∞, which is R-vac(i): the identity uses R-vac(i) only. R-vac(ii), with 2^s ≤ e^{d(𝒞)}, gives the bounds uniform in 𝔐 (d2-2c-r2 m3).
  - Ψ(M) depends on M alone, and Ψ_≥(T) on T alone (with the fields). So Q_{k+1} is a product of genuine |T|-body factors, which is the form (6)(c) needs. (The earlier grouping by "exactly S" made the self-energy of M depend on the other members of 𝔐; d2-2c S2.)
- The self-energy e^{−Ψ(M)} is put into the marked activity: Ĥ(M) := e^{−Ψ(M)}H̃(M).

*(e) Result (k ≤ N, real outer variables independent of the integrated field A; d2-2c m7).*
⟨𝒳⟩_{1,1} = Σ_𝔐 Q_{k+1}(𝔐)·⊗_{M∈𝔐}Ĥ(M), so ⟨T⟩_{1,1} = Σ_𝔐 Q_{k+1}(𝔐)·dim(π)^{−1}(contraction of ⊗_MĤ(M) with the A-independent factors of T).
- Each M is a new *group* at step k+1, possibly with several intervals of C (one per slot in J(M)); its insertion Ĥ(M) is a tensor over its slots and depends on M alone. The hard core is ζ, the soft interaction is Q_{k+1}. This is the class form of D2.0(3) with these changes, settled in (6) (after d2-2c m3):
  - insertions may be multi-interval tensors: (6)(d);
  - Q_{k+1}, the self-energies e^{−Ψ(M)} and the H̃(M) depend on U_{k+1} through the vacuum activities, and on the outer fluctuation field A_k on Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ, through −⟨A, C*Δ^{(k)}CA_k⟩, 𝐏^{(k)}(g_k, (A_k, A)) and V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}, (A_k, A)) in (3.27). In the slot variables this is the dependence on σ_k ((6)(b6)), and the reference extraction of (6)(c) is taken at ω′ = 0;
  - the marked polymers have no hard core with the supports of the untouched old insertions, which take no part in the expansion: (6)(d)'s second rule merges each untouched old insertion whose domain is incompatible with a constituent of a group into that group, an exact regrouping.
- |Q_{k+1}(𝔐)| ≤ exp(Σ_𝒞(s−1)_+|φ^T(𝒞)|) ≤ ∏_{M}exp(Σ_{𝒞≁M}|φ^T(𝒞)|) and |Ψ(M)| ≤ Σ_{𝒞≁M}|φ^T(𝒞)|. Under R-vac(ii) at the single-cube test sets the exponent is at most c|M| ((b); D2.4(2)(g); the earlier a(M⁺) ≤ 9c|M| of d2-2c m4 stays valid but is weaker, d2-2c-r2 m2). Whether c|M| ≤ νσ(M)/16, the form of D2.0(3)'s norms, is D2.4's count.
- *Exactness.* (c) is an identity on the finite torus. (d) uses the absolute convergence of the vacuum cluster expansion: Bałaban's construction as presented (CRE pp.275–279), under R-vac(i); R-vac(ii) is used for the bounds only (d2-2c-r2 m3). No bound on 𝒳 is used beyond boundedness on the support (as in (2)).

*(f) What (5) leaves (completed after d2-2c m5, m6).*
- *To (6):* (i) D(k+1)(v) needs the scalars as numbers given the labels, but Q_{k+1}(𝔐) and e^{−Ψ(M)} are functions of U_{k+1} and of the outer field. Either a reference extraction at a flat background (V3's route at step 1, with the difference as a field-dependent insertion of deviation size), or a revision of D(k)(v) to gauge-invariant local functions, composed at the next step as Bałaban composes 𝐄^{(k+1)}(X). Done in (6)(c) by the extraction. (ii) Multi-interval tensor insertions: either D2.0(3) is widened to tensors over several intervals (the class form allows it if words are contractions), or the slots of one M are merged along C. Done in (6)(d) by merging, with the hard-core rule for untouched old insertions. (iii) Cov(k+1) for Ĥ(M), Ψ_≥ and Q_{k+1}. RG2 Lemma 2's last statement gives the gauge invariance of the potentials 𝐕_k(Y) only (it concerns "the functions 𝐕_k(Y,𝐔,𝐉,B), and both terms in (1.42)"). The invariance of the activities H(Z), and so of φ^T, Ψ, Ψ_≥ and Q_{k+1}, needs in addition the covariance of C^{(k)}(Z₀,s), (C^{(k)})^{1/2}(s), Δ_k(s) and of the cutoffs, and with the outer field the transformation acts on σ_k too. Done in (6)(b1)–(b6). (iv) The extension to complex outer variables, which the class needs for analyticity and Cov, while (e) is stated for real ones: (6)(e).
- *To D2.4:* the sizes of Ĥ(M): the D2.1/D2.2(4) factor per component, times the conditioned Gaussian expectation, against the count; the enlargement of D_J to D_J^# ((c)); c|M| ≤ νσ(M)/16 (d2-2c-r2 m2); and that Bałaban's bounds give R-vac(ii) with the weight d(Z) = 9 log2·|Z| (derived in D2.4(2)).
- *To D2.6:* marked polymers that meet Λ_{k+1}ᶜ or older regions, and vacuum polymers that do, where the conditioning domain and the propagators are those of CRE's multi-domain construction.
- *Readings:* R-vac and R-I7 ((b)), to be raised with the user.

### (6) The integration: output in class D(k+1) (written from 17:52, 22 Oct, date)

*(0) What (6) does.* Input: the identity of (2), 𝒲_{k+1} = ⟨F⟩_{1,1} with F composed with ψ_{k+1}, and (5)(e), ⟨T⟩_{1,1} = Σ_𝔐Q_{k+1}(𝔐)·dim(π)^{−1}(contraction of ⊗_MĤ(M)). Output: the class form of D2.0(3) at step k+1 on every labelled term of ρ_{k+1}. (6) writes the structural parts: (a) the bijection ψ_{k+1} used in (2)(iii); (b) Cov(k+1); (c) the scalar parts, D(k+1)(v); (d) multi-interval insertions; (e) D(k+1)(iii), with the ω-clause. The sizes, D(k+1)(ii), (ii′) and the norms, are D2.4's count and D2.6's.

*(a) The bijection ψ_{k+1} (derived, 17:52–17:59, 22 Oct, date; the step-(k+1) form of V3 Lemma boundary-bijection, V3 L548).*
- *The map.* For b ∈ S_{k+1}^{(k)*}, ψ_b is p.269's change of variables: g_kA_k(b) = log(e^{ig_kA_k^{new}(b)}e^{−iω_b𝐇^{(k)}(b)}), with ω_b := g(A_k^{new}(b)) for b ∈ (R_{k+1}^{∼2}∩S_{k+1})^{(k)*} and ω_b := 1 on the other bonds of S_{k+1}^{(k)*} (p.269: "For bonds in (R_{k+1}^{∼2}∩S_{k+1})^{(k)*} we take the change of variables given by the formula (1.22), with 𝐇^{(k)}(b) instead of 𝐇_{1,Ax}(b). For the remaining bonds we take the simpler change of variables with the function g(A_k′(b)) replaced by 1"). log is the chart logarithm. g is CRE p.252's function at (g_k, δ_k): 0 ≤ g ≤ 1, g = 0 on |A| ≤ (4/3)g_k^{−1}δ_k, g = 1 where |e^{ig_kA} − 1| ≥ (5/3)δ_k. One such g with |Dg| ≤ 6g_k/δ_k is fixed by V3's construction (V3 L542) with (g₀, δ₀) replaced by (g_k, δ_k).
  - *Reading R-g_k (d2-2d minor 3).* p.269 says only "(1.22), with 𝐇^{(k)}(b) instead of 𝐇_{1,Ax}(b)", and does not restate g's parameters. (g_k, δ_k) is the reading. It is used only through |Dg| ≤ 6g_k/δ_k and the two support conditions.
- *The star sets.* X^{(k)*} is CRE p.247's X* = X∖{b₀(c) : c ∈ X^{(1)}} at step k+1: the bonds of the scale-k lattice with at least one endpoint in X, minus the bonds b₀(c) for c with at least one endpoint in X^{(k+1)} (p.247: "with at least one end-point belonging to □′^{∼2}, except the bonds b₀(c) for c, such that at least one end-point belongs to (□′^{∼2})^{(1)}"). CRE Sect. 3 writes (k)* in (3.3) and (3.16) without restating the definition. RG1 pp.267–268 nearly force the reading: "We perform the same operation as in the first step, namely using the δ-functions δ(Q̃B′) we eliminate the variables B′(b₀(c)), c ∈ T^{(k+1)}" (quoted as printed after d2-2d-r2 m1), so there is no variable A_k(b₀(c)) for p.269's "each bond variable A_k(b), b ∈ S_{k+1}^{(k)*}" to act on. p.269's statement that the changes "transform the function χ_k′(S_{k+1}) into the same function" presupposes it too: on a bond b₀(c) the test would involve B′(b₀(c)), a function of all the free variables of the corridor, and no bondwise change could act on it in this way. This is a reading, recorded with reading (c) of (2)(iii).
- *Input 1, the bond form (derived).* For b ∈ S_{k+1}^{(k)*}, V_k(b) = e^{ig_kA_k(b)}V^{(k)}(b).
  - The step-k field is e^{iB′}V^{(k)} on B(Γ_{k+1}), with B′ = g_kCA_k − hD̃(g_kCA_k) (D2.1(1)).
  - RG1 p.267: "The function hB is equal to 0 everywhere, except the set {b₀(c): c ∈ T^{(k+1)}}". RG1 pp.267–268: "namely using the δ-functions δ(Q̃B′) we eliminate the variables B′(b₀(c)), c ∈ T^{(k+1)}. Denoting the remaining variables by B we have B′ = CB". So (CA_k)(b) = A_k(b) and (hD̃)(b) = 0 off the bonds b₀(c). CRE p.267 transfers this to the multi-domain operators ("exactly the same as … Sect. 2 [I]", D2.1(1)).
  - A bond b of S_{k+1}^{(k)*} is not a b₀(c). If b = b₀(c), b lies in the corridor {b : b_− ∈ B(c_−), b_+ ∈ B(c_+)} (RG1 p.267), so its endpoint in S_{k+1} lies in the block of an endpoint of c. That block lies in S_{k+1}, since the cube partitions are nested (p.265: the "partition is compatible with all the other partitions"; V3 L548 at step 1). So c has an endpoint in S_{k+1}^{(k+1)}, which b ∈ S_{k+1}^{(k)*} excludes.
  - *Which bonds carry A_k (d2-2d minor 6).* p.267 integrates dA_k|_{Ω_{k+1}} and dV_k|_{Ω_{k+1}ᶜ} separately. Input 1 and ψ_b are claimed for the bonds of S_{k+1}^{(k)*} that carry an A_k variable; on the others the variable is V_k, which p.269's change ("each bond variable A_k(b)") does not touch.
- *Input 2, the tests (quoted).* For b ∈ S_{k+1}^{(k)*}, let □′(b) ⊂ S_{k+1} be an L²M₂R_{k+1}-cube containing an endpoint of b. It exists: S_{k+1} = (Ω_{k+1}∖Ω_{k+1}^{∼−1}) ∪ R_{k+1} is a union of such cubes, the layer by the nesting and R_{k+1} by (3.16). Then b ⊂ □′(b)^∼ ⊆ □′(b)^{∼2}. On the support, the factor χ_k′(□′(b)) of χ_k′(S_{k+1}) in (3.21) is 1. By (3.3), p.265, that is |V_k(b′)(V^{(k)}_{□′(b)}(b′))^{−1} − 1| < 2δ_k for b′ ∈ (□′(b)^{∼2})^{(k)*}. At b′ = b, with Input 1, this reads |e^{ig_kA_k(b)}Z_b − 1| < 2δ_k, where Z_b := V^{(k)}(b)(V^{(k)}_{□′(b)}(b))^{−1}.
- *Input 3, the size of Z_b (quoted).* (3.13), p.267, is stated for every cube □′ ⊂ Ω_{k+1}: |V^{(k)} − V^{(k)}_{□′}| < (4dL)²(1+β₀)B₃ε_k on □′^∼. Hence |Z_b − 1| < C_Zδ_k, with C_Z := (4dL)²(1+β₀)B₃A₀/A₁, since δ_k = (A₁/A₀)ε_k (p.265; confirmed by (3.19), p.268, "O(1)44d²B₃²(1+β₀)ε_k exp(−R_k) = O(1)44d²B₃²(1+β₀)(A₀/A₁)exp(−R_k)δ_k") and the factors are unitary.
  - At step 1, V3 had |Z_b − 1| < 2δ₀ from (B1). The input here is weaker. C_Z exceeds 2 when A₁/A₀ is small. C_Z depends on d, L, B₃, β₀ and A₀/A₁ only, so it is fixed before the coupling.
- *Input 4, the size of 𝐇^{(k)} (quoted, with the reading of (2)).* p.269: 𝐇^{(k)} is "bounded on the set S_{k+1} by O(1)ε_k exp(−R_k)". So |𝐇^{(k)}(b)| ≤ O(1)(A₀/A₁)e^{−R_k}δ_k ≤ δ_k/40 once e^{R_k} ≥ 40·O(1)A₀/A₁. This holds for small g_k, uniformly in k by the coupling item (g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K), with this O(1) independent of k.
  - Three readings enter here (d2-2d minor 5). The k-independence of the O(1), supported by the explicit constant of (3.19) and p.269's "with similar bounds". The coupling item g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K, reading R-cpl₀ of the gaps block (not CRE text; relabelled after d2-3 N8). And "bounded on the set S_{k+1}" applied to the bonds of S_{k+1}^{(k)*} with an endpoint outside S_{k+1}, as V3 L570 reads p.251 at step 1.
- *The constants.* r_y := (π/2)(2 + C_Z), r_K := r_y + 1 and r_F := (π/2)(r_K + 1/40). The condition on the coupling is δ_k ≤ δ_G(C_Z) := min(log(3/2)/(√N r_F), r_G/(√N(r_K + 1/40))), with r_G and N as in V3's lemma. It holds for g_K small, since δ_k → 0 uniformly in k (coupling item). At C_Z = 2 these constants are V3's up to rounding (r_y = 2π ≈ 6.28 for V3's 6.3).
- *The lemma.* Put 𝒯_b := {A ∈ 𝔤 : h(g_kA) < ϱ_G, |e^{ig_kA}Z_b − 1| < 2δ_k}. Under Inputs 1–4 and δ_k ≤ δ_G(C_Z), V3's statements (a)–(c) hold for every b ∈ S_{k+1}^{(k)*}. So Ψ_{k+1} maps the domain of the new variables bijectively onto that of the old ones, DΨ_{k+1} is invertible there, and the Jacobian is ∏_b|det Dψ_b|. The domain of the new variables is defined as at V3 L567: the preimage of the old domain, with the new variables restricted to ∏_b𝒲_b ("the inclusion ⊆ being the definition").
- *The proof is V3's, with these changes.*
  - (a) Use Input 2 at the actual A_k, and the chart condition from (3.14), p.267: |V_k′ − 1| < 2δ_k + (4dL)²(1+β₀)B₃ε_k = (2 + C_Z)δ_k ≤ r_G/√N, so V_k′ lies in the chart domain and |A_k′(b)| ≤ r_yδ_k < ϱ_G, under δ_k ≤ δ_G(C_Z) (A_k′ = g_kA_k on free bonds, by Input 1). No further coupling condition enters (d2-2d minor 2).
  - (b) |e^{iy} − 1| < (2 + C_Z)δ_k, so |y| ≤ r_yδ_k. Take K := {|x| ≤ r_Kδ_k}.
    - K lies in the chart ball and e^{ix}W(x) in the chart domain, since (r_K + 1/40)δ_k ≤ r_G/√N.
    - |F(x)| ≤ r_Fδ_k, and ‖DR‖ ≤ ½ on {|X| ≤ r_Fδ_k}, since e^{√N r_Fδ_k} − 1 ≤ ½. So |F(x) − x| ≤ 2|𝐇^{(k)}(b)| ≤ δ_k/20.
    - Hence T(x) := x − F(x) + y maps K into itself, |T(x)| ≤ (r_y + 1/20)δ_k < r_Kδ_k. Brouwer's theorem gives a fixed point.
  - (c) |e^{ix} − 1| < (2 + C_Z + 1/40)δ_k, so |x| ≤ (π/2)(2 + C_Z + 1/40)δ_k ≤ r_Kδ_k, and both points lie in K.
    - The injectivity estimate |e^{ix} − e^{ix′}| ≤ (6/δ_k)|x − x′|·(δ_k/40) = (3/20)|x − x′| < ½|x − x′| does not involve C_Z.
    - The same holds for the differential.
  - V3's remark that the proof depends on the tolerance of Z_b only through these constants is what is used here.
- *What it closes.* Input (a) of (2)(iii), and the OPEN item "ψ_{k+1} bijection" of the gaps block. The readings it rests on are the (k)* convention above, R-g_k, and Input 4's three (the k-independent O(1), R-cpl₀, and the bound on S_{k+1} for bonds leaving S_{k+1}). They go with reading (c) of (2)(iii) to the user's review. The bijection uses only r_G, ϱ_G = π/√N and the √N comparisons of norms, so it holds for every compact simple G ⊂ U(N) (V3 L557).

*(b) Cov(k+1) (derived, 17:59–18:06, 22 Oct, date; repaired after d2-2d S1–S3 and minors 8–15, by 19:54, 22 Oct, date).*
- *Slot variables (after d2-2d S2).* The outer variables of step k+1 are ω′ := (ω, σ_k). Here σ_k is the old field A_k on the outer bonds of Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ, called the *slot variable* of ω_k. After (2)(iii) the integration variables on S_{k+1}^{(k)*} are the new variables A_k^{new}, and the slot variable receives σ_k(b) = ψ_b(A_k^{new}(b); 𝐇^{(k)}(b)), with ψ_b of (a). On the other outer bonds σ_k = A_k. Every insertion is a function of the slot variables; ψ_{k+1} only says which values they receive. So D(k+1)'s analyticity in the outer fields (D(k+1)(iii), *Outer fluctuation fields*) is analyticity in σ, and A_k^{new} is never complexified. g in ψ_b is not analytic in A_k^{new}, and this does not matter.
- *Statement (after d2-2d S1).* Every insertion Ĥ(M) of (5)(e) is the value at (𝐔, 𝐉) = (U_{k+1}, J_{k+1}) of a function Ĥ(M)(𝐔, 𝐉, ω′), analytic jointly. It transforms slot by slot: Ĥ(M)(𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉, R(u)ω′) = (⊗_{i∈J(M)}π(u(x_i)))Ĥ(M)(𝐔, 𝐉, ω′)(⊗_{i∈J(M)}π(u(x′_i)))^{−1}, with x_i, x′_i the endpoints of the arc of slot i. Q_{k+1}(𝔐) and the self-energies e^{−Ψ(M)} are invariant. Here u is any G-valued gauge transformation of the fine lattice. It acts on a field on the scale-j lattice through its values at the sites of that lattice, (R(u)A)(x,x′) = R(u(x))A(x,x′) (PBG (3.28)). So R(u) acts pointwise and is orthogonal.
  - At real configurations 𝐉 = J_{k+1} = J(U_{k+1}), and J(U^u) = R(u)J(U) (PBG p.395, after (3.30)). So nothing changes there. 𝐉 is a variable for RG1 p.272's reason: bounds for D*D "do not hold", "and this is the reason why we do not formulate second order regularity conditions, as in (1.9) [14], (2) [15], but we replace the expression J_j by the new variable 𝐉" (d2-2d-r2 m2).
  - ψ_{k+1}'s covariance is not used and is not claimed; for local u it is not available: 𝐇^{(k)} is built from the minimiser V^{(k)}_{Λ^c∩Λ_k}, which is gauge fixed (VAR p.277, "unique up to gauge transformations"; the axial gauge Ax). CRE p.250 keeps the invariance of the density under gauge transformations of the new field apart from the invariance of the effective action under (1.17), and CRE (3.17)–(3.18) show that the two minimisers differ by u_{k+1,□′} and a small field exp iL^{−1}η𝐇_{k+1,□′}, with 𝐇_{k+1,□′} "bounded by … ≤ 44d²B₃²(1+β₀)exp(−R_k)ε_k on □′^{∼2}" (CRE p.268, transcription read; d2-2d-r2 m3).
- *The functions of (𝐔, 𝐉) (after d2-2d N1).* RG1 p.272, after (3.12): "we replace J_{k+1} by the variable 𝐉, and in the remaining expressions we replace U_{k+1} by the new variable 𝐔". RG1 p.273, for the operators: "we replace the configurations U_{k+1}, J_{k+1} occurring in operators there by the configurations 𝐔, 𝐉". So every operator of (4)–(5) that depends on (U_{k+1}, J_{k+1}) is taken at (𝐔, 𝐉). The covariance is checked on these operators in (b1)–(b4).
- *(b1) The operators, and RG2's s-localised operators.*
  - PBG (3.30)–(3.34), pp.395–396, for every gauge transformation u: Δ^η(U^u) = R(u)Δ^η(U)R(u^{−1}), the same for Δ_U^η, G′, R, Δ_a and G; and (Q_j′(U^u)R(u)λ)(y) = R(u(y))(Q_j′(U)λ)(y). For the gauge-field averaging operators Q_j(U): "inspecting the definitions … we can see that the equalities (3.32) hold again" (p.396).
  - Restriction to a set (X ↦ ΩXΩ) and multiplication by a scalar function commute with R(u), since R(u) acts pointwise. So local inverses of these operators on cubes and domains are covariant too.
  - The walk terms. PBG p.412: "Each term in this expansion is a product of factors". Its examples are K(h′_□)G′_□h′_□, h_□C_□h_□ and □̃Q′[G′_{□₀}, h′²_{□₀}]G′_{□₀}Q′*h_□C_□h_□ (pp.412–413), the last with a leading characteristic function. Each factor is built from these local operators, the scalar partitions of unity h_□, h′_□ and characteristic functions. So every walk term T_ω satisfies T_ω(𝐔^u) = R(u)_{target}T_ω(𝐔)R(u)^{−1}_{source}, where target and source are the lattices on which T_ω takes and receives values ("act between different scales", p.413). So does every sum Σ_ωc_ωT_ω with scalar coefficients.
    - This is printed for PBG's expansion (3.98) (Theorem 3.9). For the other expansions, of G, G′, H, H₀, G̃, Δ_k, C^{(k)}(Z₀) and G̃₃(x) of RG2 p.13, the factor lists are Bałaban's and are not read here. They are used as Bałaban's, with RG2 p.11, "the operations in this section preserve the gauge invariance", and RG2 p.21, "By Lemma 2, and the transformation properties of the operators in (2.14) with respect to gauge transformations, e.g. see (3.28)–(3.34) [13], the expressions (2.14) are gauge invariant with respect to all G-valued transformations" (d2-2d N3; listed in (b8)). PBG p.408's "all the results of these theorems are gauge invariant" concerns the bounds of PBG Theorems 3.1–3.3 and is no longer cited for this (d2-2d-r2 m4).
  - The s-parameters. RG2 p.3: "for a random walk ω localized in X̃_0^5∪X̃_1^5∪…∪X̃_n^5 we take the {Δ_1,…,Δ_m} of all cubes from σ_0 which intersect this localization domain, and we multiply the term in (1.6) corresponding to ω by s(Δ_1)⋯s(Δ_m)"; p.13: "The parameters s(Δ), Δ∈σ_0, are introduced into the operators as before" (both read in the transcription, not in the r2 packet; d2-2d-r2 m10). (C^{(k)})^{1/2} is the integral (2.7) of the resolvents (xI + C*Δ_kC)^{−1} and the powers (C*Δ_kC)^n. The resolvent is expanded by (3.185) of [13], with the added term −½x‖χ*(QA + D̄μ(QA))‖². χ* is "the characteristic function of the set of bonds T_1^{(k)}∖{b₀(c): c ∈ T^{(k+1)}}" (p.13), a scalar multiplier on bonds. Q and D̄ are not identified on p.13; the transcription notes "printed with a bar, D̄; (1.19) prints D̃", and RG2 (1.19) is B′ = g_kCB − hD̃(g_kCB). If they are Q̃ and D̃ of RG1 p.267, they are covariant by the C/D̃ bullet below; their identification and covariance are listed in (b8) (d2-2d-r2 m5). μ is defined in [13] and is not read here; its covariance is used as Bałaban's ((b8)).
  - Hence, for every complex s, H(s), G̃(s), H₀(s), C^{(k)}(Z₀, s), (C^{(k)})^{1/2}(s) and Δ_k(s) are covariant, and Γ_k(Z₀, s), a scalar, is invariant. This is what RG2 p.21 calls "the transformation properties of the operators in (2.14) with respect to gauge transformations, e.g. see (3.28)–(3.34) [13]". It closes the OPEN item "covariance of RG2's s-localised operators" (d2-2b e11, m11).
  - *The operator C and B′.* RG1 p.268: "C is the operator determined by the configuration V^{(k)}". V^{(k)} = M^k(𝐔) transforms by u at the scale-k sites: M({uU_jv}) = uM({U_j})v for unitary site gauges with no smallness restriction (V3 L5031; V3 L84–92, Lemma (ii)). Q̃ is the linear part of the block constraint at V^{(k)}, and h is "an inverse of a coefficient at the variable B′(b₀(c)) in (Q̃B′)(c)" (RG1 p.267).
    - Q̃ and h (one line each, d2-2d minor 11). Differentiating the covariance of M at e^{iB′}V gives Q̃(V^u)R(u) = Ad(u(c_−))Q̃(V), with Q̃ based at c_− as in V3 L88(ii). Hence h_{V^u}(c) = Ad(u(b₀(c)_−))h_V(c)Ad(u(c_−))^{−1}.
    - C is fixed by Q̃CB = 0 with (CB)(b) = B(b) on the free bonds ((a), Input 1). b₀(c) enters only the constraint of c (V3 L84 (i)), and its coefficient there is invertible (RG1 p.267: "h is uniquely defined by these conditions"). R(u) preserves the free bonds, so R(u)C(𝐔)R(u^{−1}) satisfies the same two conditions at 𝐔^u, and uniqueness gives C(𝐔^u) = R(u)C(𝐔)R(u^{−1}).
    - D̃ (after d2-2d S3). Its equation is LQ̃B′ + C̃(B′) = LQ̃B (RG1 p.267), with C̃ the nonlinear part of the block constraint. C̃ is covariant as Q̃ is, so R(u)D̃ solves the equation at (V^u, R(u)B). The set in which RG1 p.267's "exactly one solution" holds is a ball in the sup norm of the pointwise matrix norm. RG1 p.267 proves it "following the proofs in the above mentioned papers", which are "Sect. C [15], Sect. E [14]" (p.267; [15] is VAR, [14] is REG, RG1 p.299). In VAR, (51), p.285, poses the fixed-point problem on |A′| < ε₃(L^jη)^{−1} on Ω_j, X = 0 on Λ₀ and |X| < ε₃/B₀ on 𝔅_k, and p.286 finds "exactly one fixed point". Every unitarily invariant matrix norm (the operator norm, or the tr XY norm of PBG pp.391–392) has |uXu^{−1}| = |X| for u ∈ U(N), also for complex X. So the ball is invariant, the transformed fixed-point map is R(u)TR(u)^{−1}, and uniqueness gives D̃(V^u, R(u)B) = R(u)D̃(V, B). The same follows from the power-series recursion, whose terms (RG1 p.267: "D̃(B) has an expansion beginning with quadratic terms, and D̃^{(2)}(B)=C̃^{(2)}(B)"; d2-2d-r2 m6) are covariant term by term. The data domain is invariant: RG1 p.263, "The spaces U_j^c(X,α_0,α_1) are, by the definition, gauge invariant also"; RG1 p.276's "so that the configurations after the transformations belong to proper spaces also" is a proviso on G^c-valued u and is not needed for G-valued u (d2-2d-r2 m7).
    - So B′(𝐔^u, R(u)A_k) = R(u)B′(𝐔, A_k).
    - Δ^{(k)}: RG1 (2.11) is ⟨H₁hC̃^{(2)}(B′), J⟩ − ½𝐆^{(2)}(B′) − ½⟨H₁B′, Δ₁H₁B′⟩ = −½⟨B′, Δ^{(k)}B′⟩, "see the definition (3.156) [13] with the δ-function gauge fixing term replaced by the exponential one". The first and third terms are invariant by PBG (3.30) (for J), by the average (for h and C̃^{(2)}) and by the walk terms above (for H₁ and Δ₁). 𝐆^{(2)} is defined in [13] and is not read here; its invariance is used as Bałaban's ((b8)). So C^{(k)}(𝐔) = (C*Δ^{(k)}C)^{−1}, its restrictions and C^{(k)}(Λ_{k+1}) are covariant.
- *(b2) 𝐇(s,·) (derived).*
  - RG2 (1.2)–(1.4): 𝐇_k(B′) = H₀B′ + 𝐀₀ − HD(H₀B′ + 𝐀₀), with D(A′) = C(A′ − HD(A′)) and 𝐀₀ + G̃(δV/δA′)(H₀B′ + 𝐀₀) = 0. These equations "are determined by local, analytic functions, and nonlocal propagators H, H₀, G̃" (RG2 p.2).
  - The local function C of (1.3) is the nonlinear part of the logarithmic average: VAR p.285, "Q_j(ηA) = L^jηQ_jA + C_j(L^jηA), |C_j(L^jηA)| ≤ C₂(L^jη)²|A|²", with C(A′) = C_j(L^jηA′) on Λ_j (VAR p.286). Q_j(U, ηA) = (1/i)log of the average (CMP98 Proposition 4, (4)(d)), and its linear part Q_j, are covariant by V3 L5031 and the conjugation equivariance of the chart logarithm (V3 L88(ii)). So C is covariant.
  - V is VAR (80), built from J, Δ_π, H, D and the higher-order part V₀ of the action. J is covariant by PBG (3.30). Δ_π is PBG (3.119), p.419: ⟨A, Δ_πA⟩ = ⟨A − DG′RD*A, Δ(A − DG′RD*A)⟩, covariant by PBG (3.30)–(3.34). The action is invariant by PBG (3.29). V₀ is the sum of the homogeneous orders ≥ 3 in A of the invariant action, and PBG p.395's expansion ("expanding both sides of the above equality in A we get a sequence of equalities between homogeneous polynomials of the same order") gives the invariance of each order, hence of V₀ (d2-2d-r2 m8). So V(𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉; R(u)A′) = V(𝐔, 𝐉; A′), and δV/δA′ is covariant.
  - *Uniqueness on invariant balls (after d2-2d S3).* RG2 (1.13)–(1.14) solve (1.3) by contraction on {X : |X| < 4C₂ε₂²} for |A′| < ε₂. RG2 (1.15)–(1.16) solve (1.4) on balls in max{|𝐀|, |∇𝐀|} for |B′| < ε₃. These are balls in sup norms of unitarily invariant pointwise norms, and |∇_{𝐔^u}R(u)𝐀| = |R(u)∇_𝐔𝐀| = |∇_𝐔𝐀| since ∇_{𝐔^u}R(u) = R(u)∇_𝐔. So the balls are invariant under R(u), also for complex 𝐀, and so is the data domain (RG1 p.263, the spaces U_j^c are "by the definition, gauge invariant"; d2-2d-r2 m7).
  - With (b1) at the same s, R(u)D and R(u)𝐀₀ solve the equations at (𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉, R(u)B′), and they lie in the invariant balls. The transformed fixed-point maps are R(u)TR(u)^{−1}, so uniqueness gives 𝐇(s; 𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉, R(u)B′) = R(u)𝐇(s; 𝐔, 𝐉, B′) for every s in RG2's domain. At s = 1 this is the covariance that RG1 p.276 uses ("in the function 𝐇_k(B′) the variables 𝐉, B are replaced by R(u_{k+1}^{−1})R(v^{−1})𝐉, R(u_{k+1}^{−1})R(v^{−1})B"); p.276 asserts the invariance of (3.25)–(3.26), not this covariance by itself.
- *(b3) w(s) (derived from CMP98 (85)–(106)).* W_y(R(u)𝐇, 𝐔^u) = u·W_y(𝐇, 𝐔)·u^{−1} pointwise, for every complex 𝐇 with |𝐇| < a₁.
  - Here U₀ = 𝐔 and U₁ = e^{iη𝐇}, so U₁(b) ↦ u(b_−)U₁(b)u(b_−)^{−1}: U₁ is based at the initial point of each bond.
  - The one-step average (89), p.31, (R̄_{0,c_−}V₁)^{−1}(V̄₁V₀)_c(V̄₀)_c^{−1}R̄_{0,c}(R̄_{0,c_+}V₁), is based at c_−. Under (V₀, V₁) ↦ (V₀^u, u(·_−)V₁u(·_−)^{−1}), (V̄₁V₀)_c(V̄₀)_c^{−1} is conjugated by u(c_−), since both averages are covariant (V3 L5031; V3 L84–92, Lemma (ii)). The point averages R̄_{0,x}V₁, averages over a block of the quantities transported to x ((85); (105) for the logarithmic mean), are conjugated by u(x), since the chart logarithm and exp are conjugation equivariant. The middle transport R̄_{0,c} is the adjoint action of (V̄₀)_c, and (V̄₀)_c ↦ u(c_−)(V̄₀)_cu(c_+)^{−1}, so it carries conjugation by u(c_+) of R̄_{0,c_+}V₁ to conjugation by u(c_−). So all four factors of (89) are conjugated by u(c_−).
  - By induction through (90)–(91), with V₀ = Ū₀^j covariant, every Ū̄₁^j is based at its initial point in the same way.
  - In (106) each bracket [(…)^{−1}(R̄^j_{0,x_{j+1}}Ū̄^j_1)(Γ_{x_{j+1},x_j})] is conjugated by u(x_{j+1}). R(U₀^u(Γ_{x_{j+1},x}))^{−1} carries conjugation by u(x_{j+1}) to conjugation by u(x). CMP98's definition of R is not read here; the property used is printed on PBG p.395 for the same notation, R(U^u(Γ_{y,x})) = R(u(y))R(U(Γ_{y,x}))R(u^{−1}(x)). So each factor of (106), and the product, is conjugated by u(x).
  - The same holds at the block centres, where CMP98 (87) gives the value w(y) = (R̄_{0,y}U₁)^{−1} (CMP98 writes u(y); renamed here to keep it apart from the gauge transformation u). It is a point average at y, so it is conjugated by u(y). So w need not be 1 there, and no such property is used.
  - *Scope.* (b3) covers the blocks on which (106), or its j-step form of (4)(d), gives w. The bonds crossing ∂Λ_j (VAR's Λ_j) are case (B) of D2.1(4); their covariance goes to D2.6 with them (d2-2d N3).
- *(b4) The new insertions before integration.* By (4)(e), Y_G is the ordered product along I(G) of old insertions at (e^{iη𝐇(s,B′)}𝐔, 𝐉′, R(w(s))ω) and of transports of e^{iη𝐇(s,B′)}𝐔, differentiated in s and taken at s = 0 off G. Here 𝐇(s, B′) = 𝐇(s; 𝐔, 𝐉, B′), and for k ≥ 2 𝐉′ = 𝐉′(𝐔, 𝐉, 𝐀) at 𝐀 = 𝐇(s, B′) is (4)(f)'s second argument of (3.13); at k = 1 there is no 𝐉′ (d2-2d-r2 S-new-1).
  - *The covariance of 𝐉′ (derived, after d2-2d-r2 S-new-1).*
    - RG1 p.272 (transcription read): (3.10) substitutes 𝐉′ = J̃(e^{iξ𝐀}U_{k+1}), with J̃(U) := D_U^{ξ*}ξ^{−2}π Im∂U; (3.11) is J̃(e^{iξ𝐀}U) = J̃(U) + D_U^{ξ*}D_U^ξ𝐀 + 𝐅(U, 𝐀) "for any regular configuration U", with 𝐅 "a local operator depending on U, ∂U, 𝐀, ∇_U^ξ𝐀 only"; then "we replace the operator D*D by D*D+DRD*=D*D+DD*−DPD*=Δ_U^ξ−P₁+(lower order, local operator)"; (3.12) is J̃(U_{k+1}) = (L^{j−1}η)³J_{k+1}; and J_{k+1} is replaced by 𝐉 and U_{k+1} by 𝐔.
    - So (3.13)'s second argument is (L^{j−1}η)³𝐉 + (D_𝐔^*D_𝐔 + D_𝐔R(𝐔)D_𝐔^*)𝐀 + 𝐅(𝐔, 𝐀), with 𝐅₁(𝐔, 𝐀) := 𝐅(𝐔, 𝐀) + (D*D + DRD* − Δ_𝐔^ξ + P₁)𝐀, the lower-order operator absorbed. 𝐅₁ is not defined in the print; this is the only reading under which (3.13) follows from (3.10)–(3.12) as printed (reading R-F₁, listed in (b8)). P₁ is not needed in this form.
    - J̃ is covariant. At G-valued U, J̃(U^u) = R(u)J̃(U) by (3.12) (an identity at every regular configuration of the step) and PBG (3.30), "J^u = R(u)J". J̃ is a rational function of the entries of the U(b) and U(b)^{−1} (on G, X* = X^{−1}), and (3.10) uses it at the complex configuration e^{iξ𝐀}U_{k+1}, so it is taken in this rational form. The law is a rational identity in the entries that holds on G-valued configurations, which are Zariski dense in the G^c-valued ones (a compact real form is Zariski dense in its complexification). So it holds at every G^c-valued configuration where J̃ is defined.
    - D_𝐔 and R(𝐔) are covariant: R by PBG (3.33), "R(U^u)=R(u)R(U)R(u^{−1})"; D_𝐔 because it is built from the transports, R(U^u(Γ_{y,x})) = R(u(y))R(U(Γ_{y,x}))R(u^{−1}(x)) (PBG p.395), so D_{𝐔^u}R(u) = R(u)D_𝐔, and D_𝐔^* likewise. They extend to complex 𝐔 by the same Zariski-density argument, since each is a rational function of the entries (R through the finite-volume inverse G′, by Cramer's rule). The same argument covers every operator of (b1) at complex 𝐔.
    - e^{iξR(u)𝐀}𝐔^u = (e^{iξ𝐀}𝐔)^u (as in the next bullet). So 𝐅(𝐔^u, R(u)𝐀) = J̃((e^{iξ𝐀}𝐔)^u) − J̃(𝐔^u) − D_{𝐔^u}^*D_{𝐔^u}R(u)𝐀 = R(u)𝐅(𝐔, 𝐀), and hence 𝐉′(𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉, R(u)𝐀) = R(u)𝐉′(𝐔, 𝐉, 𝐀) for every G-valued u.
  - By (b2), e^{iη𝐇(s; 𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉, R(u)B′)}𝐔^u = (e^{iη𝐇(s; 𝐔, 𝐉, B′)}𝐔)^u. By the bullet above at 𝐀 = 𝐇(s; 𝐔, 𝐉, B′), the 𝐉-argument of the old insertions becomes R(u)𝐉′. By (b3), R(w′(s))R(u)ω = R(u)R(w(s))ω, with w′(s) the transform at the transformed data.
  - Cov(k) for the old insertions and the transport law then give Y_G(𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉, R(u)B′, R(u)ω) = π(u(x_G))Y_G(𝐔, 𝐉, B′, ω)π(u(x′_G))^{−1}, for every s, so also after the s-integrals. Derivatives and integrals in s commute with a fixed conjugation. The untouched old insertions are Cov(k)'s.
  - The transformed arguments stay in Cov(k)'s domain: a G-valued u preserves |Im ω|, and the spaces of the background are gauge invariant, RG1 p.263: "The spaces U_j^c(X,α_0,α_1) are, by the definition, gauge invariant also" (unions of G^c-orbits, p.262; this replaces the appeal to RG1 p.276, whose "so that the configurations after the transformations belong to proper spaces also" is a proviso on G^c-valued u, d2-2d-r2 m7). |R(u)𝐉| = |𝐉| and |R(u)𝐉′| = |𝐉′|, since R(u) is orthogonal pointwise. For (3.14) at the transformed data: |R(u)𝐀| = |𝐀|, |∇_{𝐔^u}R(u)𝐀| = |∇_𝐔𝐀| ((b2)), |Δ_{𝐔^u}R(u)𝐀| = |Δ_𝐔𝐀| (PBG (3.31)), and |P₁(𝐔^u)R(u)𝐀| = |P₁(𝐔)𝐀| needs P₁ covariant. P₁ is not defined on RG1 p.272 (the printed identity suggests DPD* up to lower order); its covariance is used as Bałaban's ((b8)).
- *(b5) The integration (derived; the argument of RG2 p.21 with insertions).* A term of the marked activity H̃(M) is RG2's (2.14) with the factors of 𝒳 over J(M) inserted. In it make the changes of variables B ↦ R(u)B and X ↦ R(u)X in dμ_{C^{(k)}(Z₀,σ)}(B) and dμ₀(X). Their Jacobian is 1, since R(u) is orthogonal.
  - The Gaussian densities and Γ_k(Z₀, σ) are invariant by (b1); the pushforward of μ_{C(𝐔)} under R(u) is μ_{R(u)C(𝐔)R(u)^{−1}} = μ_{C(𝐔^u)}. The χ-factors are invariant, since they are bondwise functions of |B(b)| and |R(u)B| = |B| (the form of the χ in RG2 (2.14) is used as Bałaban's). The localised terms 𝐑_k, 𝐁_k and A(1/g_k²(·) − 1/g_k², ·) of CRE pp.276–277 are invariant ((b6), d2-2d-r2 m9). The 𝐕_k(Y) are invariant by RG2 Lemma 2's last statement (p.11: "The functions 𝐕_k(Y,𝐔,𝐉,B) … are gauge invariant with respect to the simultaneous gauge transformations (I.3.29)"). (I.3.29) is 𝐔 → 𝐔^u, 𝐉 → R(u)𝐉, B → R(u)B (RG1 p.276), so 𝐉 is transformed with the rest. 𝒳 is covariant slot by slot by (b4).
  - The Cauchy integrals in σ(Δ), τ(Y) do not involve u. So H̃(M) is covariant slot by slot, and the vacuum activities H(Z) are invariant, which is RG2 p.21's statement.
  - Ψ(M), Ψ_≥(T) and Q_{k+1}(𝔐) are functions of the H(Z) and of supports fixed by the labels ((5)(d)), so they are invariant (the argument applies verbatim to Ψ(M) and Ψ_≥(T); notation aligned after d2-2c-r2 m8). Ĥ(M) = e^{−Ψ(M)}H̃(M) is covariant.
- *(b6) The new outer field (after d2-2d S2; channel list repaired after d2-2d-r2 S-new-2).* The record of (3.25)–(3.27) above and (5)(e) have σ_k entering also through 𝐏^{(k)}(g_k, (A_k, A)) and V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}, (A_k, A), 𝐇^{(k)}), and V^{(k)} depends on A and on the non-covariant 𝐇^{(k)}. In the slot variables these are handled by reading R-x ((2)(iii)(b)): the exponent of (3.25) satisfies
  [old exponent at (A_k^{new}, A)] + V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}, (A_k^{new}, A), 𝐇^{(k)}) = [old exponent at (σ_k, A)] + log|det Dψ_{k+1}(A_k^{new})|,
  with "old exponent" the exponent before p.269's change of variables. So V^{(k)} is not a channel of its own, and σ_k enters through the old exponent at (σ_k, A), in three channels, each covariant under (𝐔, 𝐉, A, σ_k) ↦ (𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉, R(u)A, R(u)σ_k):
  - B′: through the configuration, and through 𝐑_k, 𝐁_k and A(1/g_k²(·) − 1/g_k², ·), which are gauge invariant (the Wilson action, PBG (3.29); CRE (2.27), (2.41), used as Bałaban's; CRE pp.276–277 localise them; d2-2d-r2 m9). B′ is covariant by (b1).
  - the cross term −⟨A, C*Δ^{(k)}Cσ_k⟩ of (3.27), invariant: ⟨R(u)A, R(u)C*Δ^{(k)}Cσ_k⟩ = ⟨A, C*Δ^{(k)}Cσ_k⟩ by (b1) (CRE (3.27) is used as printed in CRE and is not in the packets).
  - 𝐏^{(k)}(g_k, (σ_k, A)) + {…} = Σ_{Y∈𝐃_k}𝐕_k(Y, U_{k+1}, (σ_k, A)), RG2 (1.41) (Lemma 2, transcription read), with B = (σ_k, A) and CRE's localised action for Σ𝐕_k ((5)(b)). It is invariant by Lemma 2's last statement: "The functions 𝐕_k(Y,𝐔,𝐉,B), and both terms in (1.42), are gauge invariant with respect to the simultaneous gauge transformations (I.3.29)".
  - The Jacobian depends on A_k^{new} and 𝐇^{(k)} only, not on A (𝐇^{(k)} is built from the minimiser V^{(k)}_{Λ_{k+1}ᶜ∩Λ_k}). So it factors out of the A-integral, cancels in ⟨·⟩_{1,1}, and stays in Bałaban's density of the outer variables; it is part of no insertion.
  - ψ_{k+1} gives the values of σ_k and is not part of the function. So R(u) acts on ω′ = (ω, σ_k) jointly, as Cov(k+1) states. (6)(e)'s *V^{(j)}* bullet, which treats V^{(j)} as a σ_j-dependence at frozen θ, is the same exponent written in σ_j, and is unchanged (S-new-2's proposed (6)(e) edit is already superseded by the d2-2e-r2 S3 repair there).
  - The slot values on the support. σ_k = A_k, the old variable, so its bound on the support is CRE (3.14) directly ((e)). At later steps the background on the layer of σ_j can be complex, and then σ_j(b) = ψ_b(A_j^{new}(b); 𝐇^{(j)}(b)) is complex through 𝐇^{(j)}, by at most 2|𝐇^{(j)}(b)|/g_j in the sense of (a)'s proof step (b). CRE p.269 bounds 𝐇^{(j)} by O(1)ε_je^{−R_j} on S_{j+1}, as an analytic function of the background. Its size and its change at the complex backgrounds of later steps join D2.4's complex-data sizes, and (e)'s radius propagation must include it (OPEN, D2.4).
- *(b7) Representatives (after d2-2d S2; replaces the appeal to CRE p.250).* Two representatives of U_{k+1}(e^{iB′}V^{(k+1)}) in Ax_{k+1}(𝔅_{k+1}, U_{k+2}) differ by a G-valued u fixing every block variable ((4)(d)). By Cov(k+1), each closed word satisfies word(𝐔^u, R(u)𝐉, σ) = word(𝐔, 𝐉, R(u)^{−1}σ). So a change of representative rotates the slot values by the real R(u)^{−1}. R(u) is real and orthogonal, so |Re σ| and |Im σ| are kept bond by bond, and the rotated values satisfy the same support bounds. D(k+1) states its clauses for slot values satisfying those bounds, so it holds for every representative. No integration variable is changed, so no Jacobian and no invariance of the rest of the integrand is used. The w of (4)(c) at the next step acts on the slot values in the same way, R(w)σ, with the radius bound of (4)(c) and (e) for complex w. *Scope (after d2-2b-r3 S2).* This covers changes of representative by a u that does not depend on the next step's integration variable. RG1 p.265's first transformation v₁ depends on B′, so it is not covered; see (4)(d), *Which representative* (OPEN). At the next step the same holds with B″: the rotation R(v₁(B″))^{−1}σ depends on the integration variable, and neither this pointwise argument nor (4)(e)'s interpolation of R(w(s))σ covers it (d2-2d-r2 S-new-3; the route through w_tot = u_k^{−1}v₁ and what is still open are in the gaps block's representative entry).
- *(b8) What it closes.* (4)(e)'s "Gauge covariance of the new pieces (OPEN until (6))" and (5)(f)(iii), in the slot variables. The inputs are quoted (PBG, RG1, RG2, CMP98, VAR) or derived above. Used as Bałaban's printed results, with no proof printed or read here (d2-2d minor 15):
  - RG1 p.267's "exactly one solution" for D̃, proved there by reference to VAR Sect. C and REG Sect. E; the invariance of its ball is derived above from VAR (51);
  - PBG p.396's "inspecting the definitions … the equalities (3.32) hold again", for the gauge-field averages;
  - the contraction and uniqueness of RG2 (1.13)–(1.16);
  - RG2 p.13's representation via (3.185) of [13], with G̃₃(x), which "has the same properties as G̃₂", and the covariance of μ there;
  - the walk-term structure of the expansions other than PBG (3.98), with RG2 p.11's "the operations in this section preserve the gauge invariance" and RG2 p.21 (d2-2d-r2 m4);
  - the invariance of 𝐆^{(2)} ([13] (3.156)) and the bondwise form of the χ in RG2 (2.14);
  - RG2 Lemma 2's last statement, for 𝐕_k;
  - after d2-2d-r2: reading R-F₁ ((b4), 𝐅₁ of RG1 (3.13) is 𝐅 of (3.11) plus the absorbed lower-order operator) and the covariance of P₁ (RG1 p.272, not defined there), for the |P₁𝐀| clause of (3.14) (S-new-1); reading R-x, D2.2(2)(iii)(b), for (b6)'s exponent (S-new-2); the invariance of 𝐑_k, 𝐁_k and A(1/g_k²(·) − 1/g_k², ·), CRE (2.27), (2.41) and the Wilson action, as Bałaban's (m9); Q, D̄ and μ of RG2 p.13's added term in [13]'s (3.185), not identified or read here (m5).
  - Not used any more: CRE p.250's invariance of the rest of the integrand, and the gauge covariance of the minimiser V^{(k)}_{Λ^c∩Λ_k}.

*(c) The scalar parts, D(k+1)(v) (decided: reference extraction; (c) and (d) derived 18:06–18:10, 22 Oct, date).*
- *Where field dependence enters.* κ^{(k)}, 𝓔_k and Q_k(𝔅) are numbers by D(k)(v), and they pass through (2)–(5) unchanged. ⟨·⟩_{1,1} is normalised, and its normaliser is CRE's ((2)), so (2)–(5) create no new scalar exponent. The self-energies e^{−Ψ(M)} are inside Ĥ(M), and insertions may depend on the field. So the only field-dependent scalar is Q_{k+1}(𝔐) = exp(Σ_{T⊆𝔐,|T|≥2}(−1)^{|T|}Ψ_≥(T; 𝐔, 𝐉, ω′)) at (𝐔, 𝐉) = (U_{k+1}, J_{k+1}) (RG2's activities are functions of (𝐔, 𝐉), p.15; d2-2e c3). This is the per-polymer form of (5)(d), after d2-2c S2 (rewritten by 20:16, 22 Oct, date).
- *Decision.* Reference extraction at the flat reference (𝐔, ω′) = (1, 0), with the labels of the term. This is V3's route at step 1 (q°, E° at U°₁ = id, V3 L986). D(k)(v) then stands as written, and (4)–(5) stay as they are. The alternative, field-dependent scalars composed at the next step as Bałaban composes 𝐄^{(k+1)}(X), would put Mayer expansions of the old interactions into (4)–(5) at every step, and D2.3 would still need numbers for δm and z. So it is not taken.
- *The extraction (exact).* Put Ψ°_≥(T) := Ψ_≥(T; 𝐔 = 1, 𝐉 = 0, ω′ = 0) and δΨ_≥(T) := Ψ_≥(T; 𝐔, 𝐉, ω′) − Ψ°_≥(T), and likewise Ψ°(M), δΨ(M). 𝐉 = 0 is the current of the flat background, J(1) = 0 (d2-2e c6). The vacuum expansion of (5)(d) converges at (1, 0, 0): (𝐔, 𝐉) = (1, 0) satisfies (2.34)–(2.39) trivially, Bałaban's bounds are uniform on the space (RG2 p.6: "all the bounds above are uniform on the domain"; p.15, the activity estimated as an analytic function on U^c_{k+1}(X, α₀, α₁); d2-2e c5), and ω′ = 0 removes the coupling term of (3.27). R-vac(ii) is applied at the reference point (1, 0, 0); by RG2 p.6's uniformity this is the same count as at the true background, derived in D2.4(2) (d2-2e-r2 m5). So Ψ°_≥(T) is defined, and it is a number given the labels and the supports of the M ∈ T; it depends on T alone.
  - The self-energies are split the same way (d2-2e c15): e^{−Ψ(M)} = e^{−Ψ°(M)}e^{−δΨ(M)}, with e^{−Ψ°(M)} a number given the labels and the support of M. So Ĥ(M) = e^{−Ψ°(M)}Ĥ′(M), Ĥ′(M) := (1 + (e^{−δΨ(M)} − 1))H̃(M); e^{−Ψ°(M)} goes to D2.3 and Ĥ′(M) carries e^{−δΨ(M)} (d2-2e-r2 m7).
  - Then, with ε_T := (−1)^{|T|}, Q_{k+1}(𝔐) = exp(Σ_Tε_TΨ°_≥(T))·∏_{T}(1 + (e^{ε_TδΨ_≥(T)} − 1)) = exp(Σ_Tε_TΨ°_≥(T))·Σ_𝒮∏_{T∈𝒮}(e^{ε_TδΨ_≥(T)} − 1), over the sets 𝒮 of subsets T ⊆ 𝔐 with |T| ≥ 2. The product is finite on the finite torus.
  - For fixed 𝒮, call two M linked if some T ∈ 𝒮 contains both. The classes of the generated equivalence relation are the *scalar-linked groups* 𝔊(𝒮). The factor ∏_{T∈𝒮}(e^{ε_TδΨ_≥(T)} − 1) splits over them, each factor depending on its T alone.
  - exp(Σ_Tε_TΨ°_≥(T)) splits into ∏_{G∈𝔊(𝒮)}exp(Σ_{T⊆G}ε_TΨ°_≥(T)), a number per group that depends on G alone, times Q°(𝔊(𝒮)) := exp(Σ_{T not inside one group}ε_TΨ°_≥(T)), a number given the labels.
  - So ⟨𝒳⟩_{1,1} = Σ_𝔐Σ_𝒮 Q°(𝔊(𝒮))·⊗_{G∈𝔊(𝒮)}Ĥ(G), with Ĥ(G) := exp(Σ_{T⊆G}ε_TΨ°_≥(T))·∏_{T∈𝒮,T⊆G}(e^{ε_TδΨ_≥(T)} − 1)·⊗_{M∈G}Ĥ(M), a tensor over the slots of G that depends on G alone.
- *Properties.* Q°(𝔊) is a number given the labels, so D(k+1)(v) holds. δΨ_≥(T) and δΨ(M) are invariant by (b5), so Ĥ(G) is covariant slot by slot, and Cov(k+1) is kept. δΨ_≥(T) is analytic where the vacuum expansion converges locally uniformly (Weierstrass), and it vanishes at (1, 0, 0).
- *Left to D2.4 and D2.6.*
  - The multiplicity (after d2-2c S2): a cluster 𝒞 enters 2^s − 1 − s of the Ψ_≥(T), s = |S_𝔐(𝒞)| ≤ 9|supp 𝒞| ((5)(d)). So Σ_{|T|≥2}|Ψ°_≥(T)| and Σ_{|T|≥2}|δΨ_≥(T)| are bounded with the weight 2^s ≤ e^{d(𝒞)} of R-vac(ii).
  - The sizes: |e^{ε_TδΨ_≥(T)} − 1| through |δΨ_≥(T)| ≤ Σ_{𝒞≁every M∈T}|φ^T(𝒞; 𝐔, 𝐉, ω′) − φ^T(𝒞; 1, 0, 0)|, and |δΨ(M)| likewise over 𝒞 ≁ M. Each term must carry a deviation factor, since the species dev is bounded by C_χρ_kδ*_n (D2.0(3)), and that holds for every cluster size (after d2-2e c12, m10). Route, for a cluster of linear size ℓ on its support:
    - Choose a gauge on the support in which the field of 𝐔 is at most a constant times ℓδ, with δ the local curvature size (the form of CRE (2.38)). Interpolate along the real path from 1 to 𝐔 in that gauge, with 𝐉 along it, and use Cauchy's estimate in the transverse complex direction. The radius is the analyticity radius α over the path velocity, so |φ^T(𝐔) − φ^T(1)| ≤ C(ℓδ/α)sup|φ^T| while ℓδ ≤ cα.
    - For ℓδ > cα, use |φ^T(𝐔)| + |φ^T(1)| ≤ 2sup|φ^T| with sup|φ^T| ≤ Ce^{−κℓ} and e^{−κℓ} ≤ (2δ/(κcα))e^{−κℓ/2}.
    - Both bounds carry the factor δ. With q₁ = p₀ the threshold α/ε is a constant, so the second case includes clusters of fixed size, and the second bound is needed. The gauge on the support is CRE (2.38)'s local gauge; that it exists at the needed size on the cluster support is part of D2.4. That item also covers (d2-2e-r2 m8): the real points e^{ixA}, x ∈ [0,1], of the path must lie in the real part of the space ((2.34)–(2.35) and (2.38)), with 𝐉 = J(e^{ixA}) obeying (2.36)–(2.37); and the gauge must bound the scale-weighted gradient (L^nξ)²|∇A| too, since (2.39)'s gradient condition limits the transverse radius.
  - The bound |Q°(𝔊)| ≤ ∏_Ge^{θ(G)}, θ(G) := Σ_{M∈G}Σ_{𝒞≁M}e^{d(𝒞)}|φ^T(𝒞; 1, 0, 0)|, from the multiplicity bound; under R-vac(ii) θ(G) ≤ c|G|, as in (5)(e) (was 9c|G|; d2-2c-r2 m2), with |G| := Σ_{M∈G}|M|, the number of cubes (d2-2e-r2 m5).
  - The species: dev when the clusters lie in the small-field region and depend on 𝐔 only; att(k+1, ·) when they meet Λ_{k+1}ᶜ or depend on ω′ (D2.6).
  - D2.3 extracts δm_{k+1} and z_{k+1} from the flat parts exp(Σ_{T⊆G}ε_TΨ°_≥(T)) and e^{−Ψ°(M)}, split off above.

*(d) Multi-slot insertions: merging along C (decided; derived).*
- *The issue.* Ĥ(G) is a tensor over the slots of G, one per group of (4)(e) that G contains (d2-2e m14). D(k)'s words have one insertion in End(V_π) per group, on one interval (IT-1 note §4: "insertions Y_W ∈ End(V_π), one per group"). Two ways out were listed in (5)(f)(ii). Merging is taken, since it keeps the class of D2.0(3) unchanged.
- *The arc hull.* For a slot i, let I_i be the arc of its group in (4)(e). For a group G of (c), let I(G) be the smallest arc of C containing ∪_{i∈J(G)}I_i, by D2.1(4)'s rule, enlarged at each end to the end of any interval that it cuts, as in (4)(e). Merge the groups of 𝔊(𝒮) whose arcs I(G) overlap, and repeat until the arcs are pairwise disjoint. The untouched old insertions whose intervals lie inside an arc join the group of that arc.
  - *Second rule (after d2-2e S2).* Also merge into a group every untouched old insertion W whose domain X_W is incompatible with a constituent domain of the group. A marked polymer's support contains Z₀ ⊇ Y₀ and Z ⊇ Z̃₀ ((5)), which are larger than the s-components, and (4)(e) only guarantees that no s-component meets X_W. Merging is a union of classes: a W already in a group by the arc rule carries its group with it, so the two groups merge (d2-2e-r2 m2). After each merge, recompute the arc hull by D2.1(4)'s rule with the enlargement, and repeat both rules to a fixed point. The process is monotone and stops on the finite torus. The words are unchanged, since it regroups an ordered product along C.
  - Call the results the *step-(k+1) groups* 𝒢.
- *The insertion.* For 𝒢 with arc I(𝒢) = [x, x′], let Y_𝒢 ∈ End(V_π) be the contraction along I(𝒢), in the order of C, of the tensors Ĥ(G), G ⊂ 𝒢, with the untouched old insertions in I(𝒢) and the U_{k+1}-transports between consecutive slots. By multilinearity, dim(π)^{−1}(contraction of ⊗Ĥ(M) with the A-independent factors of T) = dim(π)^{−1}tr(Y_{𝒢₁}U_{(1)}⋯Y_{𝒢_r}U_{(r)}), with the 𝒢's in cyclic order and U_{(i)} the transports between their arcs. This is exact.
- *Class form.* The families {𝒢} have disjoint intervals, and their constituent domains are pairwise compatible: the M are compatible by (5)(c), the old insertions by D(k), and a new constituent with an untouched old insertion by the second rule. A touched old insertion has X_W ⊂ D_J^# ⊂ Z₀, so (5)(c) covers it (d2-2e-r2 m2). Inside one 𝒢 the constituents are incompatible by construction, so the condition of IT-1 note §4 is read across distinct 𝒢's, with the union of its constituent domains as the domain of 𝒢. So they are compatible families in the sense of IT-1 note §4 (V3 L967, quoted in the IT-1 note: "all their constituent domains … mutually compatible and their intervals are disjoint"). Cov(k+1) holds for Y_𝒢 with the endpoints x, x′, by (b) and the transport law. The interaction between distinct 𝒢's is the part of Q° and of Q_k(𝔅) between them; the parts inside one 𝒢 are numbers and go into Y_𝒢 (d2-2e-r2 m3; D2.3(3)).
  - *Amended by D2.3(3) (after d2-3 S1, 22:3x, 22 Oct, date; not reviewed).* A third merging rule is added: Q° and the old interactions Q_k(𝔅) are expanded in cluster occurrences, the members hit by one occurrence join one group, and the arc hull is taken over the arcs J_Γ of the old occurrences as well. Then distinct step-(k+1) groups interact only by the hard core of D2.3(3), and the sentences above on Q° between distinct 𝒢's are superseded.
- *Cost (D2.4; after d2-2e m2, m3).*
  - Norms. At real backgrounds, in the projective norm on ⊗End(V_π), contraction with unitary transports does not raise the norm. In the operator norm on V_π^{⊗m} a contraction can cost up to dim(π)^{m−1}: the swap Σ_{ij}E_{ij}⊗E_{ji} has norm 1 and contracts to dim(π)·1. So D2.4 uses the projective norm. At complex backgrounds the transports along I(𝒢) add exp(c_π‖a′‖_∞·length(I(𝒢))), which the chord–arc bound and 20c_πM‖a′‖_∞ ≤ 1 (after d2-2e-r2 m1; see *Arcs*) turn into e^{#cubes}.
  - Arcs. The merging enlarges arcs: the arc of 𝒢 can exceed the spatial diameter of its supports. For the squares C_n the shorter arc between two points of C is at most 2 times their distance in the maximum norm, with every period at least twice the side (D2.1(4)). For the hull of a set the constant is 4: length(I) ≤ 4·(ℓ∞-diameter of the supports), as in D2.1(4)'s proof, and the four midpoints of the sides attain 3. (Corrected after d2-2e-r2 m1: a merged arc is not at most the sum of its constituents' arcs, since a second-rule merge also covers the gap between W's interval and I(𝒢).) After the fixed point, length(I(𝒢)) ≤ 4·diam_ℓ∞(S(𝒢)), S(𝒢) the union of the constituent intervals. With each interval in its constituent's support, a shortest chain of linked constituents has links of length ≤ 2diam_i + 2diam_j (overlapping arcs) or ≤ 1 (incompatible domains), so diam S(𝒢) ≤ 5Σ_i(diam supp_i + 1) and length(I(𝒢)) ≤ 20Σ_i(diam supp_i + 1). D2.4's count pays for this with the decay of the activities, and the radius condition becomes 20c_πM‖a′‖_∞ ≤ 1 in place of 4c_πM‖a′‖_∞ ≤ 1. For other contours this chord–arc constant enters.
- *What it closes.* (5)(f)(ii), with D(k+1)'s groups, intervals and insertions in the form of D2.0(3).

*(e) D(k+1)(iii): the background domain and the ω-clause (derived 18:11–18:26, 22 Oct, date; with reading R-adm).*
- *The background domain must change at k + 1 ≥ 2.*
  - Ĥ(M) contains the covariances and propagators of (5) at the complex background 𝐔. RG2 p.15 gives their analyticity only "on the space of configurations (𝐔,𝐉) satisfying the conditions I.(i)–(iii) on the domain Z, with constants α₀′, α₁′ much bigger than α₀, α₁". Those conditions bound gradients as well.
  - D(k)(iii)'s sup-only domain does not control gradients. On Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1} it allows |A′| < αL^{k−n} in step-k field units, hence |∇^ξA′| ≤ 2ξ^{−1}|A′| < 2αL^{2k−n}, so (L^nξ)²|∇^ξA′| < 2L^nα. That exceeds CRE's bound α by the factor 2L^n, which is not bounded in n.
  - So D(k+1)(iii), k + 1 ≥ 2, is posed on CRE's domain at 𝐔 = e^{iηa′}U_{k+1} (after d2-2e S4): the full complex part of Ũ^c_{k+1}, i.e. (2.34)–(2.35) for 𝐔 and its averaged configurations, (2.36)–(2.37) for 𝐉, and (2.39), with both the sup condition and the gradient condition (L^nξ)²|∇^ξ_UA′| < (1 − β(1 − 2^{−(k+1−n)}))α_{1,n}. The conditions are posed on Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1} for n = 1, …, k and on Ω_{k+1} for n = k+1; CRE poses none on Ω₀∖Ω₁ (d2-2e m17). RG2 p.15's domain then contains it by definition.
    - The reason (2.39) alone does not suffice: for 𝐔 = e^{iξA′}U, |∂𝐔 − 1| ≤ |∂U − 1| + C(ξ²|∇_UA′| + ξ²|A′|²), so (2.39) at radius α_{1,n} allows curvature up to Cα_{1,n}ξ²(L^nξ)^{−2}, within (2.34)'s α_{0,n}ξ²(L^nξ)^{−2} only if α_{1,n} ≲ α_{0,n}, and CRE (2.28) fixes no relation between (q₀, C₀) and (q₁, C₁).
    - The real background. U_{k+1} on the support lies in the real part of the space: on Ω_{k+1} by CRE p.268 ("The field U_{k+1} satisfies the regularity condition |∂U_{k+1} − 1| < 2B₃ε_{k+1}(L⁻¹η)² on Ω_{k+1}"), and on Ω_{k+1}ᶜ by the conditions of the earlier steps (CRE p.277: "on the domain Ω_{k+1}^c there is no regularity improvement").
      - *Derived instead of assumed (after d2-2e-r2 m4).* On Ω_{k+1}, (2.34) at j = n = k+1 has the factor 1: |∂U − 1| < α_{0,k+1}ξ²(L^{k+1}ξ)^{−2} (p.261). p.268 gives |∂U_{k+1} − 1| < 2B₃ε_{k+1}(L^{−1}η)². Both weights are the scale-(k+1) plaquette weight. *Unit check (00:05–00:08, 23 Oct, date; transcriptions read).* CRE p.276: "In all bounds connected with such a cube we have to replace η=L^{−k} by L^{−n}", so η = L^{−k} and (L^{−1}η)² = L^{−2(k+1)}. (2.34) for X ∈ 𝐃_{k+1} is in the scale ξ = L^{−(k+1)} (p.256: "the lattice T_ξ, ξ=L^{−j}"), and at n = k+1, ξ²(L^{k+1}ξ)^{−2} = ξ² = L^{−2(k+1)}. The weights are equal, so membership needs 2B₃ε_{k+1} < α_{0,k+1}, i.e. (with ε_{k+1} = g_{k+1}A₀(log g_{k+1}^{−2})^{p₀}, α_{0,k+1} = g_{k+1}C₀(log g_{k+1}^{−2})^{q₀}) q₀ > p₀ for g_K small, or q₀ = p₀ with C₀/A₀ > 2B₃; this is the same kind of entailment as q₀ ≥ p₀ below, which it now bases. Condition (2.38) for U_{k+1} on Ω_{k+1} follows from a local gauge on cubes of size CM: a D2.4 item, with (LPG). On Ω_{k+1}ᶜ the conditions are those of the earlier steps, with R-m (p.277); p.277 names (2.36)–(2.39), not (2.34)–(2.35), so on Ω_{k+1}ᶜ the curvature conditions rest on the earlier steps' (2.34)–(2.35) at their own margins.
  - This is weaker than the sup-only form, which stays at k = 1 (D1(iii)′, from V3). D1(iii)′ implies the new form at k = 1, since its domain contains CRE's.
  - *The consumer still gets what it needs.* (4)(f) at the next step evaluates the old factors at A″ = 𝐇(s,tB″) + a″, with a″ in the new domain. So (4)(f)'s margin inequality is needed in the gradient norm too.
    - RG2 (1.18) bounds 𝐇_k(s(Y₀),B′) by 4B₀e^{16κ_s}|B′| "and the same bound for all admissible norms". RG2 p.5 bounds the operator H(s(Y₀)) of the same formula "in all norms of Theorems 3.1.-3.10 [13]" ([13] is read as PBG; RG2's reference list was NOT SUPPLIED in packet d2-2e-r2, m6). PBG Theorem 3.1, (3.42), p.397, bounds |G′λ| and |∇_UG′λ| by B₀(L^jη)² and B₀L^jη on Δ(y), y ∈ Λ_j. That is the weighting of CRE (2.39), (L^nξ)² for the gradient against L^nξ for the field.
    - *Reading R-adm (for the user's review).* The "admissible norms" of RG2 (1.18) are the norms of PBG Theorems 3.1–3.10. They include the scale-weighted gradient norm of (2.39). RG2 does not define the term; the identification rests on p.5's wording for H and on (1.15). (1.15) holds on the ball max{|𝐀|, |∇𝐀|} < 4C₄(B₀e^{16κ₁})³ε₃², and "holds for all admissible norms" keeps the map in that ball. Its ∇ is unweighted (PBG (3.39)), and H's weighted-gradient bounds sit in PBG Theorems 3.2–3.10, which are not read here (d2-2e m5).
    - With R-adm, the gradient part of A″ obeys the same bound as its sup part, up to the change of the covariant derivative between the centres U_{k+1} and X₁ of (4)(f). That change is (Ad(e^{iξ𝐇}) − 1)(·)/ξ, of size O(|𝐇||a″|), so (L^nξ)² times it is ≤ (L^nξ|𝐇|)(L^nξ|a″|), and it joins the O(·)² of (4)(f). So (4)(f)'s condition carries over unchanged to the gradient norm. D2.0(3)'s D(k)(iii) is amended accordingly (note added there).
    - *The curvature conditions (2.34)–(2.35) (after d2-2e S4).* At the evaluation points e^{iηA″}X₁ the change of curvature against e^{iηa″}U_{k+1} is ≤ C(L^nξ)^{−2}ξ²[(L^nξ)²|∇𝐇| + (L^nξ|𝐇|)² + (L^nξ|𝐇|)(L^nξ|a″|)], with 𝐇 = 𝐇(s, tB′). It must stay below the curvature margin β2^{−(k+1−n)}α_{0,n}ξ²(L^nξ)^{−2}. At t = 1 this is an inequality of R-m's type with α_{0,n} in place of α_{1,n}: 8B₀e^{16κ_s}C·O(1)ε_k < (β/2)α_{0,k}, i.e. q₀ > p₀, or q₀ = p₀ with C₀/A₀ large. CRE's powers of 1/2 appear in (2.34)–(2.37) as in (2.39), and R-m's statement on p.277 covers the 𝐉-bounds (2.36)–(2.37), which carry α_{0,n}. On the polydisc T_Y is taken so small that the deformation also respects this margin: 1/T_Y ≥ C′B₀e^{16κ_s}|B′|_Y/(min(β,1)α_{0,k}) is added to the maximum of (4)(f). The entailment q₀ ≥ p₀ joins R-m's (gaps block).
      - *Older layers (added after d2-2e-r2 S2, 23:07–, 22 Oct, date).* The check above is at n = k. On Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1}, n < k, m := k−n ≥ 1, the bound on the deformation in the units of |A″| is O(1)L^mθ^m times its Ω_k-size, by R-dec in the sup and the scale-weighted gradient norm. With h_k^{(0)} := 4min(β,1)α_{0,k}/C′ + 8C₁^{RG2}B₀e^{16κ_s}O(1)ε_k, the bound on the polydisc under the T_Y condition above, the curvature part needs O(1)(2θ)^m h_k^{(0)} < (β/2)α_{0,n}. CRE (2.6)–(2.7), first lines (for every p; reading R-cpl₀), give α_{0,n} ≥ α_{0,k}/((1+β₀)²√m). The n = k inequality in its complex-data form gives h_k^{(0)} ≤ min(β,1)α_{0,k}/2 for C′ ≥ 16. With √m x^m ≤ x for x ≤ 1/2, the condition holds for all m ≥ 1 once 2θ ≤ 1/2 and 2θ·O(1)(1+β₀)² < 1, as for the sup part in (4)(f). The 𝐉-bounds (2.36)–(2.37) are treated the same way at the 𝐉-margin of (4)(f), *Carrying 𝐉*, with R-dec covering its Laplacian and P₁ parts (the extension of R-adm there is part of R-dec). The basis on the older layers is CRE p.277 (R-m) with R-dec.
- *Analyticity of the new insertions (derived from (4)(f) and RG2 p.15).* For real U_{k+1} on the support and a′ in the new domain at step k+1:
  - the old factors are analytic at e^{iη(𝐇(s,tB′) + a′)}U_{k+1} by (4)(f), in the sup and the gradient norm;
  - the transports and the leading word are entire in the background;
  - the Gaussian parts and the 𝐕_k(Y) are analytic on Bałaban's domain by RG2 p.15, which contains the new one by the definition above;
  - the characteristic functions do not depend on a′. They depend on the fluctuation field (CRE p.268: "We want to have characteristic functions depending on the fluctuation field only", (3.16)) or on the real step-(k+1) configuration (the (3.3) factors that remain), and the latter are among the term's indicators, kept as factors.
  - Ĥ(M), and with it Y_𝒢 of (d), is then analytic in a′ by Morera's theorem in each variable under the integrals, and jointly by Hartogs' theorem. This needs (d2-2e m4): an integrable majorant for the parameter integrals, locally uniform in a′ (RG2 p.15's second measure is complex, and the large-field B is unbounded, so a uniform bound in B is not available); locally uniform convergence of the cluster series inside Ĥ(M), Ψ(M) and the vacuum sums, with Weierstrass' theorem. Both are part of D2.4's sizes at complex data (D(k+1)(ii) with δ*^{(k+1),30} + a*^{(k+1)}). Ĥ(M) is G-covariant by (b).
- *The ω-clause: where ω lives (quoted).* At step k the components of ω are ω₀, V3's exterior fields on the bonds I∖D(Λ) and J (IT-1 note L786–790; the earlier pin L598 for I∖D(Λ) was wrong, d2-2e-r2 m6) of Ω₁∩Λᶜ, and the slot variables ω_j := σ_j, the old A_j on Ω_{j+1}∩Λ_{j+1}ᶜ ((b)), for 1 ≤ j ≤ k−1. Since Ω_{j+2} ⊆ Λ_{j+1} (CRE pp.264–265), ω_j lies in the layer Ω_{j+1}∖Ω_{j+2}. That layer is a union of scale-(j+1) blocks: VAR p.277, "B^j(Λ_j)=Ω_j∖Ω_{j+1}", with VAR's Λ_j := Ω_j^{(j)}∖Ω_{j+1}^{(j)}, not CRE's. The new component ω_k := A_k on Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ lies in the layer Ω_{k+1}∖Ω_{k+2}.
- *|ω| on the support (derived; closes the OPEN item "|ω| bound at k ≥ 2").*
  - At step j+1, CRE (3.14), p.267, gives |A_j′| < O(1)ε_j on Ω_{j+1} on the support of the step-(j+1) characteristic functions of (3.3) ("|V_k′−1|<O(1)ε_k on Ω_{k+1}… |A_k′|<O(1)ε_k also"). These are kept as factors in T_{j+1}. CRE p.268 removes a (3.3) factor only on cubes □′ ⊂ R_{j+1}ᶜ, where it "is equal to 1, because of the bound on A_k introduced by the second", the (3.16) factor. So the support is unchanged.
  - By (a), Input 1, A_j′ = g_jA_j on the free bonds, so |A_j| < O(1)ε_j/g_j = O(1)A₀(log g_j^{−2})^{p₀}. This bounds the slot variable σ_j = A_j of (b) directly, which is the bound the ω-clause uses.
