After ψ_{j+1} on S_{j+1}, |g_jA_j^{new} − g_jA_j| ≤ 2|𝐇^{(j)}| ≤ δ_j/20 by (a)'s proof step (b), and δ_j = (A₁/A₀)ε_j (Input 4). So also |A_j^{new}| ≤ O(1)ε_j/g_j on the support.
  - At j = 0 (after d2-2e m13). IT-1 note §3d(2)(d) gives |a_old| < r on I∖D(Λ) and |A_e(b)| < r on J for exterior data in the support of the collar indicators, with r = A₁(log g₀^{−2})^{p₀} (V3 L122, L3966–3973; IT-1 note L1149 does not print r, d2-2e-r2 m6). ω₀ is bounded on all of Ω₁∩Λᶜ by the step-1 small-field test on Ω₁ (the earlier sentence that bad collar tests give no upper bound is deleted: it contradicts the IT-1 gaps block's "(ϑr ≤ |A| < r)", and the argument by the step-1 test suffices; d2-2e-r2 m6): |e^{ig₀A(b)}Z_b − 1| < 2δ₀ with |Z_b − 1| < 2δ₀ from (B1) (V3 L559, L570) gives |e^{ig₀A(b)} − 1| < 4δ₀, so |g₀A(b)| ≤ 2πδ₀. Since δ₀/g₀ = r, this is |B(b)| ≤ Cr with an absolute C, with the conversion between phases and B-units of IT-1 note §3d(2)(d). The j = 0 condition below holds with C in place of 2.
- *The size of w on the layer of ω_j (derived from (4)(d)'s computation; repaired after d2-2e S1).* On a block B^n(y), n ≤ k, the chain of (106) has n steps. By (4)(d), *Size*, the j′-th bracket has |log| ≤ O(L)L^{j′}η sup|𝐇| with η = L^{−k}, and the real transports preserve |·|. The sum over j′ < n gives |log W_y| ≤ O(1)L^nη·sup_{B^n(y)}|𝐇| = O(1)L^{n−k}sup_{B^n(y)}|𝐇|, with 𝐇 in fine units as in (4)(d).
  - *The complex data.* The ω-dependent old factors are evaluated at R(w(s,t))ω on the whole polydisc of (4)(e)–(f), |s(Δ)| ≤ e^{κ_s}, |t_Y| ≤ T_Y, not only at t = 1. There sup|𝐇(s,tB′)| ≤ 4B₀e^{16κ_s}(max_YT_Y|B′|_Y + sup|B′|) ≤ min(β,1)α_{1,k}/4 + 8C₁^{RG2}B₀e^{16κ_s}O(1)ε_k =: h_k, by 1/T_Y ≥ 16B₀e^{16κ_s}|B′|_Y/(min(β,1)α_{1,k}) ((4)(f)). By R-m's inequality h_k ≤ α_{1,k}/2. So the size is O(α_{1,k}), not O(ε_k).
  - *The adjacent layer, j = k−1.* There n = k, the layer Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1} is in the scale-k region, and RG2 (1.18) bounds 𝐇 by h_k directly. So |log w| ≤ C_wh_k.
  - *Older layers, j ≤ k−2.* The sites lie in Ω_{j+1}∖Ω_{j+2} ⊆ Ω_kᶜ, where no k-independent sup bound of 𝐇 holds: VAR (190), p.308, bounds δ𝓗_μ(B,x)/δB_ν(y′) by O(1)(L^jη)^{−1}(L^{j′}η)^{−d}exp(−⅛δ₀d(y,y′)) for x ∈ Δ(y), y ∈ Λ_j, y′ ∈ Λ_{j′}, and the prefactor (L^{j+1}η)^{−1} = L^{k−j−1} cancels the chain's gain L^{j+1−k}. The decay replaces it:
    - d is PRT2's weighted distance (2.46), p.231. PBG cites it as "(2.36) in [4]", [4] = PRT2; the equation numbers differ, and which printed display PBG means is to be resolved against PRT2 in the next packet (d2-2e-r2 m6): d(y,y′) = inf_Γ Σ_m(L^mη)^{−1}|Γ∩B^m(Λ_m)|, each layer's length in its own block units.
    - VAR (1), p.277: (L^mη)^{−1}dist(Ω_mᶜ, Ω_{m+1}) > RM₁. A contour from B(Γ_{k+1}) ⊆ Ω_{k+1} to the layer Ω_{j+1}∖Ω_{j+2} crosses Ω_m∖Ω_{m+1} for m = j+2, …, k, each with weighted length > RM₁. So d > (k−j−1)RM₁.
    - Integrating the derivative along tB′ (𝓗(0) = 0) and keeping half the decay for the sum over y′ gives, at s = 1, sup_{B^{j+1}(y)}|𝐇| ≤ O(1)L^{k−j−1}e^{−δ₀(k−j−1)RM₁/16}h_k.
    - *At the complex data (repaired after d2-2e-r2 S1, 23:04–23:07, 22 Oct, date).* VAR (190) is printed for VAR's 𝓗 at s = 1. The earlier sentence, that for 𝐇(s, tB′) on the polydisc the same kernel bound is RG2 (1.18)'s "same bound for all admissible norms" under R-adm with PBG (3.42)'s decay, was a misreading: RG2 (1.18) and p.5's bound "B_0e^{16κ_1}|X| in all norms of Theorems 3.1.-3.10 [13]" are norm bounds obtained by spending the walk decay against e^{mκ₁} at complex s, and PBG (3.42)'s decay is an operator bound, not one of p.397's norms (d2-2e-r2 S1).
      - *Single scale (derived from RG2 p.5).* RG2 bounds H(s(Y₀))X by (1.11), "B_0|X|sup_ω M_1^{−1/2|ω|}exp(−δ_0d(ω))e^{mκ_1}", and for m > 2⁴, "δ_0d(ω) ≥ δ_1mM". If δ₁M ≥ 2κ₁ (twice RG2's δ₁M ≥ κ₁; a condition on M, fixed before the coupling), then e^{−δ₀d(ω)}e^{mκ₁} ≤ e^{−δ₀d(ω)/2} for m > 2⁴, and for m ≤ 2⁴ the factor is at most e^{16κ₁}e^{−δ₀d(ω)}. So RG2's single-scale H(s) keeps half the decay of the walks at complex s. The walk bound (1.7) behind (1.11) is not read.
      - *Reading R-dec (labelled; for the user's review; replaces R-adm's "decay form").* On CRE's multi-domain lattice, 𝐇(s, tB′) on the polydisc obeys VAR (190)'s kernel bound with the rate halved, in the sup and in the scale-weighted gradient norm. The basis is RG2 p.5's argument above for one scale, CRE p.267 ("Their properties are essentially the same as before, because of the localization of the field A_k"), VAR (190) (reading R-v), and CRE p.277, where Bałaban asserts the comparison made here: "The localization of the fluctuation field, and the exponential decay of the minimizing function 𝐇_k imply that this difference is still much greater than bounds on this function". The gradient part rests on R-adm as well. No counterexample is known.
      - With R-dec, sup_{B^{j+1}(y)}|𝐇(s,tB′)| ≤ O(1)L^{k−j−1}θ^{k−j−1}h_k on the polydisc, with θ := e^{−δ₀RM₁/32}. So |log w| ≤ C_wθ^{k−j−1}h_k on ω_j's layer.
    - CRE's domains satisfy VAR (1) with R of the size of R_{j}: the layers are built from a fixed number of layers of LMR_{j+1}-cubes around the large-field regions (CRE p.265: "four layers of the LMR_{k+1}-cubes, i.e., we take the domain Z_k′^{∼4}", then further single layers and "two layers of such cubes"; "four or five" undercounted, d2-2e-r2 m6), and R_j ≥ (log g_j^{−2})^r (CRE (2.5)) is large uniformly in j for g_K small.
  - So |log w| ≤ C_wθ^{k−j−1}h_k at the sites of ω_j, j ≤ k−1, with θ := e^{−δ₀RM₁/32} and C_w independent of j, k; for j ≤ k−2 this rests on reading R-dec (d2-2e-r2 S1). Without R-dec the supplied pages give it only for j = k−1.
- *The radii.* Fix r_{ω,j} when ω_j is created: r_{ω,0} := 1 in V3's B-units (D(1)(iii)), and r_{ω,j} := c_ωε_j/g_j for j ≥ 1.
  - In slot variables (b), the insertions depend on ω_j = σ_j, the old A_j, and not on A_j^{new}. So the cutoff g in ψ_{j+1} never enters their analyticity (d2-2e S3 is met by d2-2d S2's slot form: ψ only gives the values).
  - At step j+1, σ_j enters through the configuration U_j(exp i[g_jCσ_j − hD̃(g_jCσ_j)]V^{(j)}) of CRE (3.15) (the old factors and the terms 𝐄, 𝐑, 𝐁, A(1/g_j²(·) − 1/g_j², ·)), through the quadratic form ⟨A_j, C*Δ^{(j)}CA_j⟩ of (3.15), and through 𝐏^{(j)}(g_j, U_{j+1}, A_j) of (3.15) and (3.27) (d2-2e m7). The cross terms of the quadratic form between A_j|_{Λ_{j+1}} and σ_j are linear in σ_j, hence entire. 𝐏^{(j)} is analytic for g_j|A_j| small, which the radius below respects.
    - *V^{(j)} (repaired after d2-2e-r2 S3; the m7 sentence that it does not depend on the integrated field was wrong).* p.269: the change of variables on S_{j+1} changes the action "by terms corresponding to (1.24), and by terms obtained by the first order expansion of the action with respect to the perturbative parts of the changes of variables. We denote this new part of the action by V^{(k)}(S_{k+1},A_k,𝐇^{(k)}). All terms of the previous action contribute to it", and p.271 puts V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}) inside ⟨·⟩_{s=1,t} and tV^{(k)}(S_k) into the measure (3.27). So V^{(j)} depends on the integrated field, and its dependence on σ_j (the S_{j+1}-bonds are bonds of σ_j) enters Ĥ(M) through the measure.
    - On (R_{j+1}^{∼2}∩S_{j+1})^{(j)*} the change of variables is (1.22), "A(b) = (1/ig_0)log exp ig_0A′(b)exp(−ig(A′(b))𝐇_{1,Ax}(b))" (p.251) with 𝐇^{(j)}(b) in place of 𝐇_{1,Ax}(b), and the Jacobian is (1.24) (p.252). Both carry the cutoff values g(A_j′(b)), and (1.24) also their derivatives. These are frozen as real factors θ_b := g(A_j′(b)), θ′_b := g′(A_j′(b)) at the real configuration and kept with the indicators, the device of d2-2e S3. At frozen θ, A_j′(b) = (1/ig_j)log(exp(ig_jσ_j(b))exp(iθ_b𝐇^{(j)}(b))) is analytic in σ_j for g_j|σ_j| small, and V^{(j)} is the first-order term of the action in the perturbation together with the (1.24) terms. So V^{(j)} is analytic in σ_j for g_j|σ_j| < 2O(1)ε_j, as 𝐏^{(j)} is, on the domain where the action is analytic. Its size on S_{j+1} is O(|𝐇^{(j)}|) ≤ O(1)ε_je^{−R_j} (p.269: 𝐇^{(k)} is "bounded on the set S_{k+1} by O(1)ε_kexp(−R_k)"). V^{(j)} is restored to the list of the σ_j-dependences.
  - In the first, B′ = g_jCσ_j − hD̃(g_jCσ_j) is RG2 (1.19), with |B′| ≤ C₁^{RG2}g_j|B| for g_j|B| < ε₁^{RG2} (RG2 (1.20): "where C_1 is an absolute constant", written C₁^{RG2} here to keep it apart from CRE's C₁ in α_{1,k}). Fix c_ω ≤ O(1), the O(1) of CRE (3.14). For |σ_j − σ_{j,0}| < r_{ω,j} around a real σ_{j,0} in the support, g_j|B| < 2O(1)ε_j, so |B′| ≤ 2C₁^{RG2}O(1)ε_j. So D2.1(4)'s conditions, (4)(f)'s margin condition and R-m's entailment hold with O(1) replaced by 2C₁^{RG2}O(1) (d2-2e m1). These are smallness conditions on g_K, and R-m's alternative q₁ = p₀ becomes C₁/A₀ > 32C₁^{RG2}B₀e^{16κ_s}O(1)/min(β,1).
  - D(m)(iii)'s ω-clause, m ≥ j+1 (polydiscs, after d2-2e m6): joint analyticity in σ_j on the polydiscs |σ_j − σ_{j,0}| < r^{(m)}_{ω,j} := r_{ω,j}(1/2 + 2^{−(m−j)}) per bond, around real σ_{j,0} in the support. A tube |Im σ_j| < r would not control the real shift of R(w)σ off the real support. At k = 1 this matches the row domain, the polydisc 𝒰_c. So r^{(j+1)}_{ω,j} = r_{ω,j} at creation (r^{(1)}_{ω,0} = 1 = r_ω^{(1)}), decreasing to r_{ω,j}/2. These are the powers of 1/2 of CRE (2.36)–(2.39), used in the same way.
- *Propagation (repaired after d2-2e S1).* At the step k → k+1, k ≥ j+1, (4)(c) needs |σ_j − σ_{j,0}| + (e^{2c_G|log w|} − 1)(|σ_{j,0}| + |σ_j − σ_{j,0}|) < r^{(k)}_{ω,j} whenever |σ_j − σ_{j,0}| < r^{(k+1)}_{ω,j}. The two radii differ by r_{ω,j}2^{−(k+1−j)}. With e^{x} − 1 ≤ 2x for x ≤ 1 and |log w| ≤ C_wθ^{k−j−1}h_k, it suffices that 4c_GC_wθ^{k−j−1}h_k(|σ_{j,0}|/r_{ω,j} + 1) ≤ 2^{−(k+1−j)}, that is
  16c_GC_w(2θ)^{k−j−1}h_k(|σ_{j,0}|/r_{ω,j} + 1) ≤ 1.
  - θ = e^{−δ₀RM₁/32} ≤ 1/4 once RM₁ ≥ 64 log 2/δ₀ (d2-2e-r2 S1(c)). This holds for g_K small, since the layer widths are of the size of R_j and R_j ≥ (log g_j^{−2})^r (above). Then (2θ)^{k−j−1} ≤ 2^{−(k−j−1)}.
  - *j ≥ 1.* |σ_{j,0}|/r_{ω,j} ≤ O(1)/c_ω on the support. The condition follows from h_k ≤ α_{1,k}/2 ≤ c(c_ω), which holds for g_K small, uniformly in k and K, by g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K and the monotonicity of g(log g^{−2})^{q₁} for small g (coupling item).
  - *j = 0.* |σ_{0,0}|/r_{ω,0} ≤ Cr = CA₁(log g₀^{−2})^{p₀}, and r is not bounded by a power of log g_k^{−2} alone. CRE (2.6) with n = k, m = 0 gives 1/g₀² ≤ 1/g_k² + β′k, so log g₀^{−2} ≤ log g_k^{−2} + log(1 + β′k) for g_k ≤ 1. Hence α_{1,k}r2^{−(k−1)} ≤ 2^{p₀}C₁A₁g_k(log g_k^{−2})^{q₁}[(log g_k^{−2})^{p₀} + sup_{k≥1}2^{−(k−1)}(log(1+β′k))^{p₀}]. The sup is finite, and the bracket times g_k(log g_k^{−2})^{q₁} is small for g_K small, uniformly in k and K. So the j = 0 condition holds.
  - The layer decay θ^{k−j−1} carries the propagation; the chain's gain L^{j+1−k} is cancelled on older layers by VAR (190)'s prefactor. With the decay, L ≥ 3 is no longer needed here. D2 keeps L ≥ 3, which V3's L > 11 odd (V3 L3968) implies anyway (d2-2e m15).
  - *Constraint for D2.4 (d2-2e S1(ii)).* If D2.4 uses D2.1(4)'s complex A_k-domain g_k|A_k| ≤ ε_H/C₁ while ω-dependent factors are present, sup|𝐇| reaches 4B₀e^{16κ_s}ε_H, a fixed constant, and the j = 0 condition fails (ε_H r → ∞ as g₀ → 0). That radius must then be cut to g_k|A_k| ≤ cα_{1,k}/C₁^{RG2}.
  - *Not yet included (OPEN, D2.4; after d2-2d S2).* At the complex backgrounds of later steps the slot values σ_j(b) = ψ_b(A_j^{new}(b); 𝐇^{(j)}(b)) move with 𝐇^{(j)}, which is analytic in the background on Λ_{j+1}ᶜ∩Λ_j (CRE p.269). Their imaginary parts must be added to the left side of the propagation condition above, step by step. Needed: the change of 𝐇^{(j)} at the step-k complex data on ω_j's layer, against the radius difference r_{ω,j}2^{−(k+1−j)}. What closes it: a Cauchy estimate for 𝐇^{(j)} on its analyticity domain, with the size of the step-k deformation on that layer.
- *Bonds crossing the layer boundaries.* The bonds of (4)(d)'s blocks that cross ∂Λ_j (VAR's Λ_j) are case (B) of D2.1(4) and stay with D2.6, as in (4)(d).
- *What it closes.* (4)(c)'s bound on |ω| for k ≥ 2 and (4)(g)'s ω-propagation item, except the slot-value item above (OPEN, D2.4). It amends D(k)(iii)'s domain for k ≥ 2 to CRE's full complex space. It adds reading R-adm, reading R-dec for the older layers (after d2-2e-r2 S1; without R-dec the propagation is closed for j = k−1 only), and the entailment q₀ ≥ p₀ of the curvature margin. The sizes at complex data are D2.4's.

*(f) What (6) leaves (18:26, 22 Oct, date; updated after round 10).*
- *To D2.4:* the sizes of the new groups, D(k+1)(ii) and (ii′), with the norms at complex data of (e), in the projective norm (d); the per-component factor T_Y of (4)(f), with the curvature-margin term of (e); the chord–arc cost of the merging in (d), constant 4; the majorants and locally uniform convergence that Morera's and Hartogs' theorems need in (e); the deviation factor for every cluster size in (c), with the local gauge on the cluster support; the slot values at complex backgrounds (OPEN, (e)); the cut of D2.1(4)'s complex radius when ω-dependent factors are present ((e)).
- *To D2.6:* the species of the new insertions, and the marked polymers that meet Λ_{k+1}ᶜ ((5)(f)); case (B) of D2.1(4), the bonds that cross the layer boundaries.
- *To D2.3:* the extraction of δm and z from the flat parts Ψ° and the reference scalars of (c).
- *Λ_{k+1}.* (6) uses CRE's Λ_{k+1} (p.269) as printed, with ω_k on Ω_{k+1}∩Λ_{k+1}ᶜ. No refinement of Λ_{k+1} is needed for (a)–(e): (a) works bond by bond on S_{k+1} ⊆ Λ_{k+1}ᶜ, (b)–(d) are identities, and (e) uses only Ω_{j+2} ⊆ Λ_{j+1} (CRE pp.264–265) and VAR (1) (d2-2e m16).
- *L.* D2.2(4)(f) needs L ≥ 2(1+β₀). D2 takes L ≥ 3, which V3's L > 11 odd (V3 L3968, d2-2d-r2 m11) implies. With V3's L ≥ 13 the first condition holds for β₀ ≤ 11/2; at L = 3 it would need β₀ ≤ 1/2, which CRE allows (d2-2e m15). D2.2(4)(f)'s older-layer margin no longer needs (1+β₀)² ≤ 3/2: it rests on (6)(e)'s decay, with the condition 2θ·O(1)(1+β₀)² < 1, which holds for g_K small (d2-2b-r3 S1; the earlier β₀ ≤ 0.22 of d2-2b-r2 N9 is withdrawn). D2.4(6) needs (1+β₀)⁸ < L, i.e. β₀ < 3^{1/8} − 1 ≈ 0.147 at L = 3 and β₀ < 13^{1/8} − 1 ≈ 0.378 at V3's L ≥ 13 (d2-4-r1 m11).

## D2.3 Exact absorption of δm_{k+1} and z_{k+1} (written from 19:02, 22 Oct, date; reviewed it1-d2-3-20261022-r1: no fatal, serious S1, S2, minor M1–M11; repairs applied from 22:28, 22 Oct, date; not re-reviewed)

### (0) What is needed

- *Input.* D2.2's output on a labelled term ℓ of ρ_{k+1}: ⟨𝒲_k⟩_{1,1} = κ^{(k)}e^{𝓔_k}·Σ_𝔉 Q(𝔉)·dim(π)^{−1}tr(Y_{𝒢₁}U_{(1)}⋯Y_{𝒢_r}U_{(r)}), the sum over the families 𝔉 of step-(k+1) groups of (6)(d), with f_{k+1} for 𝔉 = ∅. The insertions are Y_𝒢(𝐔, ω′) at 𝐔 = U_{k+1}, covariant by (6)(b), with flat scalars Ψ° by (6)(c).
- *Output.* The form of D(k+1):
  - (i) κ^{(k+1)} = κ^{(k)}κ_{k+1}, κ_{k+1} = e^{−δm_{k+1}P_k(C)}z_{k+1}⁴, with δm_{k+1} and z_{k+1} independent of the contour size, the torus and ℓ;
  - (v) the scalar parts are numbers given ℓ;
  - the insertions that remain vanish at the flat reference, or are attached to ℓ's data (D2.6).
- *Constraint (D2.0(5)(a), (b)).* No remainder proportional to P_k(C) may be left. The corner remainders must be summable over k uniformly in K.

### (1) Quoted

- *Step 1 (V4, WILSON_FIRST_RG_STEP_V4.tex at hash d463c554b76f, 19:15, 22 Oct, date; every passage below has the same text in V3 at a520a59f363d, typography aside).*
  - *Reference data,* L1050: the same regions, cubes and cutoffs, with U°₁ = id, V°₀ = id, A°_e = 0; "These data need not be a branch of the history sum." The lemmas used "apply to U = id directly".
  - *Schur,* Lemma schur, L1055–1064. At the identity background a constant gauge transformation fixes the data. So the group matrix Υ = w°(W)∫π(P°_W(a))d(⊗ν̂°)(a) satisfies Υ = π(u)Υπ(u)^{−1} for all u ∈ G, hence Υ = a_W·𝟙, and S°_{ξ,C} = Σ_{𝔅 compatible}∏_{W∈𝔅}a_W (eq:part1-reference-gas).
  - *Joint extraction,* Prop. joint-extraction, L1070–1075. With the joint defect R_ξ(𝔅) := Σ_{𝒮⊆𝔅}(−1)^{|𝔅∖𝒮|}(∏_{W∈𝔅∖𝒮}a_W)A_ξ(𝒮), F_ξ(0) = S°_{ξ,C}f_ξ(0) + Σ_{𝔅≠∅}S°^{[𝔅]}_{ξ,C}R_ξ(𝔅). At the reference data every nonempty defect vanishes (derived; the supplied lines end at the statement, d2-3 M5): by Lemma schur A°(𝒮) = ∏_{W∈𝒮}a_W, so R°(𝔅) = ∏_{W∈𝔅}a_W·Σ_{𝒮⊆𝔅}(−1)^{|𝔅∖𝒮|} = 0 for 𝔅 ≠ ∅.
  - *Perimeter,* eq:marginal-full-perimeter (L1515): κ_v = Σ_{Γ: 0∈J_Γ}Φ_v(Γ)/|J_Γ| on the infinite straight line, δm_v = −κ_v/(Lε), with |κ_v| ≤ Cτ² from the rooted tail eq:marginal-cluster-tail (L1494). eq:marginal-line-endpoints (L1532) is the exact endpoint identity for a finite segment.
  - *Corners,* eq:corner-local-extraction (L1622): ζ_{v,w} = Σ_{j∈ℤ}(a_{v,w}(j) − k_{v,w}(j)) on the infinite bent path, with |a_{v,w}(j) − k_{v,w}(j)| ≤ Cτ²e^{−c|j|} (eq:corner-localisation-bound, L1630). eq:corner-mixed-cluster-formula (L1640) shows that ζ contains the straight-edge endpoint deficits.
  - *Square,* eq:corner-square-extraction-bound (L1683): |F_{□,∞}(n) − nΣ_vκ_v − Σ_{(v,w)∈𝒞_□}ζ_{v,w}| ≤ Cτ²(n+1)e^{−cn}, with z_c := exp(¼Σζ_{v,w}) (eq:corner-square-factor, L1692).
  - *Iteration,* Conjecture iteration, L3532, and Remark next-step (3)(e). There the torus and corner part E°_{T,n} "at most Cτ²(2+1/c)" is "one source of the term (k+2)τ²". Cτ²(2+1/c) bounds the torus part, by 4ne^{−cι_T} ≤ 1 from (H3), and the corner part, by (n+1)e^{−cn} ≤ 1+1/c (V4 L210); the decay in n is not used (d2-3 M6).
- *The partitions (Bałaban).*
  - RG2 p.3: "a regular partition σ_k of the space T, in the scale corresponding to the lattice T_η, into cubes Δ of the size R₁M₁. The number R₁ is a power of L".
  - RG2 p.13: "π_{k+1}, i.e. cubes of the size LM in the scale corresponding to the lattice T_η".
  - CRE p.245: "a partition into cubes of the size MR₀, where R₀ is the smallest power of L such that R₀ ≥ (log g₀^{−2})^r, r ≥ 2, and M = L^m is sufficiently large. The second is a partition into cubes of the size LM₂R₀, where M₂ = L^{m₂}".
  - PBG p.408: "We take these numbers as powers of L" (R, M).
  - CRE p.256: the domains Ω_j, Λ_j "which are determined by he j-th renormalization transformation, but not by the R-operation, are unions of MR_j-cubes in the lattice T_{L^{−j}}" (print "he" for "the"). At step k+1 the small-field tests of CRE (3.16) are on L²M₂R_{k+1}-cubes (D2.2(1)) (d2-3 M1).
  - *Reading R-tr (translation covariance; d2-3 N3).* Every operation of Bałaban's step (averages, minimisers, propagators, cutoffs, s-localisation, activities) is defined from the lattice, the partitions and the fields alone, so a lattice translation that preserves all the partitions maps each object to the object of the translated data. The prints define the operations this way; no page states the covariance. Labelled here, for the user's review.
  - Nesting: each cube of a coarser partition is a union of cubes of each finer one, the L-blocks included (V4 L612, V3 L548 as used in D2.2(6)(a)).
  - CRE (2.9), p.256, for n > m: R_n ≤ LR_m and R_m ≤ L(1+g_n²β′(n−m))^{β₀}R_n ≤ (L+1)(n−m)^{β₀}R_n.

### (2) The flat value of a group is a scalar (derived, 19:02–, 22 Oct, date)

- *The reference point.* On a labelled term ℓ it is (𝐔, 𝐉, ω′) = (1, 0, 0) with ℓ's regions, as in (6)(c) ("𝐔 = 1, 𝐉 = 0, ω′ = 0") and V4 L1050 (d2-3 M3). Below, (1, 0) abbreviates it. Write ℓ₀ for the trivial-label term: Ω_j = Λ_j = T for every j ≤ k+1, no large-field regions, hence no outer variables. On ℓ₀ the reference point is a real configuration in the support of every indicator, so D2.2's identities hold there. On other terms it need not lie in the support, and the insertions are taken there through the reference clause.
- *Reference clause (derived; added to D(k)(iii)).* The background-dependent factors of D(k) on ℓ are defined and analytic also at 𝐔 = e^{iη_kA′}·1, A′ in D(k)(iii)'s domain, jointly with ω in its radii around ω = 0.
  - At k = 1 this is V4 L1050: the step-1 lemmas "apply to U = id directly", on every branch.
  - At step k+1 it is (6)(e)'s analyticity argument with U_{k+1} = 1:
    - (4)(f) at U_{k+1} = 1 uses the clause at step k;
    - the transports and the leading word are entire;
    - the Gaussian parts and 𝐕_k(Y) are analytic on Bałaban's domain, given by the conditions I.(i)–(iii) (RG2 pp.6, 15; "I" is RG1). RG1 p.262 states them: (i) 𝐔 = U′U with |∂U − 1| < α₀ξ² (1.11) and, on cubes of size O(1)LM, a gauge with U^u = exp iξA, |A|, |∇^ξA| < O(1)LMBα₀ (1.12); (ii) U′ = exp iξA′, |A′|, |∇_U^ξA′| < α₁ (1.13); (iii) |∂𝐔 − 1| < α₀ξ², |𝐉| < γ₀ (1.14). At (𝐔, 𝐉) = (1, 0) take U = U′ = 1, u = 1, A = A′ = 0: every inequality holds strictly, and it holds for e^{iη_kA′}·1 with A′ in D(k)(iii)'s domain, whose bounds are those of (1.13) with smaller constants (derived; d2-3 M10);
    - the large-field objects in the insertions on ℓ ≠ ℓ₀ are defined at the flat point (derived; d2-3 M10): RG1 p.260, for regular V "there exists exactly one regular, critical orbit" of the action, "and it is a minimal orbit". At V = 1 the configuration 1 is a minimum (the action is ≥ 0 and vanishes there) and satisfies the constraint, so the orbit is that of 1. Hence the minimisers V^{(k)}_{Λᶜ∩Λ_k} are 1 in the axial gauge, 𝐇^{(k)} = 0, and the slot values ψ_{k+1}(A_j^{new}; 𝐇^{(j)}) are taken at 𝐇^{(j)} = 0 and ω′ = 0. That these are defined there is the statement; their sizes are D2.4's;
    - the characteristic functions do not depend on a′.
  - The bounds at the reference point are part of D2.4's sizes at complex data.
- *Schur.* Let 𝒢 be a step-(k+1) group with arc I(𝒢) from x to x′. Take u ≡ g constant in Cov(k+1) ((6)(b), (d)). Then 1^g = 1 and R(g)0 = 0, and the labels are gauge invariant. So Y_𝒢(1, 0) = π(g)Y_𝒢(1, 0)π(g)^{−1} for all g ∈ G.
  - π is irreducible (V3 (H1): χ = dim(π)^{−1}tr π). By Schur's lemma, Y_𝒢(1, 0) = c_𝒢𝟙 with c_𝒢 = c_𝒢(ℓ) ∈ ℂ, a number given ℓ.
  - This is V3's Lemma schur at step k+1. V3 applies Schur to the integrated reference group matrix; here the integral is already inside Y_𝒢 ((5)(c)).
  - Old groups that stand alone in 𝔉 (the untouched old insertions of (5)(a)) are covered by Cov(k) in the same way. For a whole-contour group, x = x′ is the base point and the argument is the same.
- *The split.* Let T_𝐔(I) := π of the ordered product of the 𝐔-transports along the arc I, in the orientation of C. Put
  Y^d_𝒢(𝐔, ω′) := Y_𝒢(𝐔, ω′) − c_𝒢T_𝐔(I(𝒢)).
  - T_𝐔(I(𝒢)) transforms as π(u(x))·π(u(x′))^{−1}, so Y^d_𝒢 is covariant with the endpoints of Y_𝒢. T_𝐔 is entire, so Y^d_𝒢 is analytic on Y_𝒢's domain. T_1 = 𝟙, so Y^d_𝒢(1, 0) = 0.
  - The split is made in the formulas, as D2.0(3)'s *Species* requires, not by the sizes.
  - The flat parts of D2.0(5)(d) (the scalar parts of pair contractions of old insertions at flat U_{k+1}) are in c_𝒢. Their background-dependent remainder, of deviation size, is in Y^d_𝒢. This is the division that D2.0(5)(a) asks for.

### (3) The exact factorisation (derived)

- *The interactions (repaired after d2-3 S1, 22:3x, 22 Oct, date).* The earlier subset form Φ(S) is withdrawn: from step 2 on its terms with S inside one 𝒢 depend on C outside I(𝒢), since a cluster of the step-k reference gas that hits an old group can contain groups anywhere on C (d2-3 S1). Both interactions are kept in cluster form and expanded into the groups, as V4 does with its vacuum clusters at step 1 (eq:c-cluster-expansion, eq:part1-group-weight, L1017).
  - *Old part.* By D(k)'s interaction clause in cluster form (note in D2.0(3)), Q_k(𝔅) = exp(−Σ_{Γ hits 𝔅}φ_k(Γ)), over the clusters Γ of the step-k reference group gas that are incompatible with some member of 𝔅. The φ_k(Γ) are numbers given ℓ, and they depend on C only through J_Γ, the traversals in the arcs of Γ's groups. At k = 1 this is IT-1 note §4's Q(𝔅) = exp(−Σ_{Γ hits 𝔅}φ(Γ)). At step k+1 it is derived in the last bullet of this (3).
  - *Vacuum part.* Q° = Q_{k+1}(𝔐) of D2.2(5)(d) at the reference point, exp(Σ_𝒞(s_𝔐(𝒞) − 1)_+φ^T(𝒞)), over the vacuum clusters 𝒞 that are incompatible with at least two marked polymers. It does not involve C.
  - *Expansion in occurrences.* Expand the old part as the exponential series Σ_{m≥0}((−1)^m/m!)Σ_{Γ₁,…,Γ_m each hits 𝔅}∏_jφ_k(Γ_j), and the vacuum part as ∏_𝒞(1 + (e^{(s_𝔐(𝒞)−1)φ^T(𝒞)} − 1)), over the 𝒞 with s_𝔐(𝒞) ≥ 2. Both converge absolutely: Σ_{Γ hits W}|φ_k(Γ)| ≤ θ(W) by D(k)'s norms, Σ_{𝒞≁M}|φ^T(𝒞)| ≤ 9c#M by D2.4(2)(g), and |e^x − 1| ≤ |x|e^{|x|}. On the finite torus these are infinite sums, since clusters may repeat polymers, so the rearrangements below need this absolute convergence (d2-3 M2).
  - *Third merging rule (amends D2.2(6)(d)).* In each term, the old groups hit by one old occurrence, and the marked polymers hit by one vacuum occurrence with factor e^{(s−1)φ^T} − 1, join one group. The arc hull is taken over the arcs of the members and over J_Γ for every old occurrence Γ; vacuum occurrences add no arc. Then D2.2(6)(d)'s first two rules are repeated to a fixed point. Each occurrence lies in exactly one group 𝒢. Its factor goes into Y_𝒢 as a number, with (−1)^{m_𝒢}/m_𝒢! for the m_𝒢 old occurrences of 𝒢: the m! of the series splits into the m!/∏_𝒢m_𝒢! interleavings, as in eq:part1-group-weight.
  - *Hard core.* Two groups are compatible when their arcs are disjoint, their constituent domains are compatible, and no occurrence of one hits a member of the other. The terms of the expansion are then in bijection with the families of pairwise compatible groups with their occurrences. So the step-(k+1) reference group gas is a pure hard-core gas: Q(𝔉) = 1, and every interaction is inside a group.
- *Multilinear expansion.* Put Y_𝒢 = c_𝒢T_𝐔(I(𝒢)) + Y^d_𝒢 into each word and expand:
  dim(π)^{−1}tr(Y_{𝒢₁}U_{(1)}⋯Y_{𝒢_r}U_{(r)}) = Σ_{D⊆𝔉}(∏_{𝒢∈𝔉∖D}c_𝒢)·w^d(D).
  - w^d(D) is dim(π)^{−1}tr of the word with Y^d_𝒢 on the arcs of D and the 𝐔-transports elsewhere, and w^d(∅) = f_{k+1}. The transports of the scalar terms join the neighbouring U_{(i)}, so w^d(D) is the class word of the family D.
  - This is V3's joint defect. Boolean inversion of A(𝔉) = Σ_{D⊆𝔉}(∏_{𝔉∖D}c_𝒢)R(D) has the unique solution R(D) = w^d(D). So at step k+1 the joint defect is the word of the deviation parts. As in V3, it vanishes at the reference point for D ≠ ∅.
- *Resummation.* Σ_𝔉Q(𝔉)Σ_{D⊆𝔉}(∏_{𝔉∖D}c_𝒢)w^d(D) = Σ_Dw^d(D)·S°^{[D]}(ℓ), where
  S°^{[D]}(ℓ) := Σ_𝔈 Q(D ∪ 𝔈)∏_{𝒢∈𝔈}c_𝒢 = Σ_𝔈∏_{𝒢∈𝔈}c_𝒢,
  the sum over the families 𝔈 disjoint from D for which D ∪ 𝔈 is a family (pairwise compatible groups), with Q = 1 by the hard core above. Put S°(ℓ) := S°^{[∅]}(ℓ).
  - The interchange needs Σ_𝔉∏_{𝒢∈𝔉}(|c_𝒢| + ‖Y^d_𝒢‖) < ∞ at the evaluation point, over the families with their occurrences. Without the occurrences the family sum is finite on the finite torus; with them it is an infinite sum, and the absolute convergence is D2.4's (d2-3 M2), as V3's proof of Prop. joint-extraction does with its β_W.
- *Result (exact).* Where S°(ℓ) ≠ 0,
  ⟨𝒲_k⟩_{1,1} = κ^{(k)}e^{𝓔_k}S°(ℓ)·[f_{k+1} + Σ_{D≠∅}Q^{out}_{k+1}(D)·w^d(D)], Q^{out}_{k+1}(D) := S°^{[D]}(ℓ)/S°(ℓ).
  - The name Q^{out} keeps it apart from D2.2(5)(d)'s vacuum interaction Q_{k+1}(𝔐) (d2-3 M3). Q^{out}_{k+1} is D(k+1)'s Q_{k+1} in D2.0(3)'s display.
  - Q^{out}_{k+1}(D) is a number given ℓ, so D(k+1)(v) holds for it.
  - The insertions at step k+1 are the Y^d_𝒢. They vanish at the reference point.
- *S°(ℓ₀) is the flat reference integral.* At (1, 0) every Y^d_𝒢 vanishes and f_{k+1} = 1, so the bracket is 1 and ⟨𝒲_k⟩_{1,1}(1, 0) = κ^{(k)}e^{𝓔_k}S°(ℓ). On ℓ₀ the reference point is a configuration of the term. So
  𝒲°_{k+1}(C; T) := S°(ℓ₀) = ⟨f_k + Σ_{𝔅≠∅}Q_k(𝔅)word_k(𝔅)⟩_{1,1} at U_{k+1} = 1,
  the expectation of D(k)'s bracket under the flat step-(k+1) fluctuation measure of CRE (3.25), with its small-field cutoffs. It is the step-(k+1) analogue of V3's 𝒲°_T(C) = N^σ_{C,T}/D^σ_T (eq:marginal-literal-ratio, V4 L1282), the bare loop W_χ(C; ·) under the flat first-step measure.
- *The gas and its logarithm.* S°(ℓ) is the partition function of the *reference group gas*: polymers 𝒢 (with their occurrences) with activities c_𝒢 and the hard core of the *Hard core* bullet. There are no other interactions (the earlier |S| = 1 step was vacuous, d2-3 M4, and is withdrawn with the subset form).
  - Under D2.4's Kotecký–Preiss condition its cluster series converges. Then S°(ℓ) = exp(Σ_ΓΦ°(Γ; ℓ)) ≠ 0, the sum over the clusters Γ of groups, Φ°(Γ) the Ursell function times ∏c_𝒢 (FV (5.11)). log S° denotes this series.
  - Every group has an arc on C (its slots, or the old group's arc), and the vacuum clusters were divided out in (5)(d). So every cluster Γ has a nonempty set J_Γ of traversals of C in the arcs of its groups.
- *The interactions at step k+1 have the cluster form (after d2-3 S1).* S°^{[D]} is the partition function of the gas with the activities c_𝒢·1[𝒢 compatible with every member of D], so the Kotecký–Preiss condition passes to it (as in D2.2(5)(d)), and its cluster series is that of S° without the clusters incompatible with D. Hence
  Q^{out}_{k+1}(D) = exp(−Σ_{Γ hits D}Φ°(Γ; ℓ)),
  the sum over the clusters incompatible with some member of D. This is D(k+1)'s interaction clause in the cluster form of (3)'s *Old part*, with φ_{k+1} := Φ°, and the old occurrences of the next step are these clusters. The bound Σ_{Γ hits 𝒢}|Φ°(Γ)| ≤ θ_{k+1}(𝒢) is FV (5.11) under D2.4's condition (D2.4(3), θ_ref).

### (4) Periodicity, contour locality and the infinite lattice (derived, except (Z))

- *The period (list completed after d2-3 M1).* Let Π_{k+1} be the largest cube side, in step-k bonds, of the partitions and cube families used at the steps 1, …, k+1: those of (1), the MR_j-cubes of which the step-j domains are unions (CRE p.256), and the L²M₂R_j-cubes of the tests (3.16).
  - All of them are powers of L, by (1). By the nesting, every one is invariant under the translations by Π_{k+1} step-k bonds along each axis. The torus periods are multiples of Π_{k+1}, since the partitions are regular partitions of T.
  - *Size.* At step j+1 the cubes are, in step-j units: R₁M₁ ≤ M (RG2 p.3); LM ≤ MR_j and LM₂R_j ≤ MR_j (M₂ < M, both powers of L, and R_j ≥ L for small g); the step-(j+1) domain cubes MR_{j+1} of scale j+1, which are LMR_{j+1} ≤ L²MR_j step-j bonds (R_{j+1} ≤ LR_j, CRE (2.9)); and L²M₂R_{j+1} ≤ LMR_{j+1} ≤ L²MR_j. In step-k units the largest is at most L^{j−k}L²MR_j. By CRE (2.9) with n = k, m = j, this is ≤ (L+1)(k−j)^{β₀}L^{j−k}L²MR_k ≤ L²MR_k for k > j, since (L+1)m^{β₀}L^{−m} ≤ 1 + 1/L for m ≥ 1, β₀ ≤ 1, and the side is a power of L; for j = k it is L²MR_k. So Π_{k+1} ≤ L²MR_k.
- *Invariance on ℓ₀ (derived from reading R-tr).* On ℓ₀ every object of the steps 1, …, k+1 is defined from the lattice, the partitions, the flat field and, for the insertions, their groups and occurrences, the arcs of C. A translation τ by a multiple of Π_{k+1} step-k bonds preserves the first three, and by R-tr ((1)) each object is carried to the object of the translated data. So c_{τ𝒢} = c_𝒢 and Φ°(τΓ) = Φ°(Γ) whenever τ maps the arcs of 𝒢 (of the groups of Γ) onto arcs of C of the same shape, by the next bullet.
- *Contour locality (derived from D2.1(4), D2.2(4)(e), (5)(c) and (3); repaired after d2-3 S1).* For a group 𝒢 with a short arc (fewer than n_C/2 traversals), Y_𝒢 depends on C only through the bonds of C in I(𝒢).
  - Y_𝒢 is the contraction along I(𝒢) of the Ĥ(G), whose slots lie in I(𝒢), with the old insertions in I(𝒢) and the transports along I(𝒢) ((6)(d)), times the factors of its occurrences ((3)). The vacuum activities, the vacuum occurrences and Ψ° do not involve C. An old occurrence Γ has weight φ_k(Γ), which depends on C only through J_Γ ⊆ I(𝒢) (the third merging rule). The old insertions and the old cluster weights satisfy the same statement, by induction from step 1: there V3's group words are products along their intervals, the contour enters through the contact sets (eq:part1-contact-set, V4 L1017), and V3's cluster weights φ(Γ) are Ursell functions times activities of groups with arcs in J_Γ. The hard core between groups depends on C only through their arcs.
  - The earlier statement without the occurrences was false from step 2 on (d2-3 S1); with the subset form Φ(S), Y_𝒢 contained cluster terms from all of C.
  - The hull rule depends on the length of C, so whole-contour groups are excluded. They have spatial extent at least n_C/2 in contour distance and are paid by D2.4's tail bound.
- *Placement of C.* C is placed with a corner at the common origin of the partitions. The bare expectation is translation invariant, so this is no restriction. C is a union of step-K bonds, with side s_k := ℓ_CL^{K−k} in step-k bonds.
  - Call step k+1 *commensurate* for C if Π_{k+1} divides s_k. Then the four corners lie on the period lattice and each side is a union of n_k := s_k/Π_{k+1} period cells.
  - Since both are powers of L times ℓ_C's factor, step k+1 is commensurate whenever Π_{k+1} ≤ L^{K−k}. By Π_{k+1} ≤ L²MR_k and CRE (2.9) with n = K−1, m = k, R_k ≤ (L+1)(K−1−k)^{β₀}R_{K−1}. So a step that is not commensurate has L^{K−k} < L²(L+1)M(K−k)^{β₀}R_{K−1}, that is K − k ≤ a*, with a* depending on L, M, β₀ and R_{K−1} only (d2-3 M1). R_{K−1} is fixed by g_{K−1}, which CRE (2.6) compares with g_K. So at most a* steps are not commensurate, and a* does not grow with K.
  - *The admissible sides (adopted after d2-3 S2; for the user's review).* Let L^{q*} be the least power of L with L^{q*} ≥ sup_{k<K}Π_{k+1}L^{k−K}, the largest period in step-K units. By Π_{k+1} ≤ L²MR_k and CRE (2.9), Π_{k+1}L^{k−K} ≤ LMR_{K−1} at k = K−1 and ≤ (L+1)MR_{K−1} for k < K−1, so L^{q*} ≤ L²MR_{K−1}. ℓ_C is restricted to the multiples of L^{q*}. Then Π_{k+1} divides s_k = ℓ_CL^{K−k} at every step (both are powers of L times ℓ_C's factor), every step is commensurate, and (6)(b) is void.
    - This is the kind of restriction D2.0(1) already makes (C a union of step-K bonds, d2-0a m8). It is not a restriction of generality when the scale at which the steps stop may depend on the loop. A square on the L-adic lattices has side pL^{−q} in units of a scale u. Stopped at uL^{−j}, its side is ℓ_C = pL^{j−q}, a multiple of L^{q*} once j ≥ q + q*. Here q* is fixed by the coupling at the stopping scale through R_{K−1} ≲ L(1+g_K²β′)^{β₀}(log g_K^{−2})^r (CRE (2.5), (2.7)), and log g^{−2} grows like log j along the scales. So q* grows like r·log_L log j, and j ≥ q + q*(j) has solutions. With the stopping scale held fixed, it does restrict the sides to multiples of L^{q*}. That D2's consumers allow a loop-dependent stopping scale is a check for the final-scale step (gaps block).
    - Without the restriction, the steps with Π_{k+1} ∤ s_k leave a remainder of order τ_k²s_k from the two sides off the origin (d2-3 S2; (6)(b)).
- *(Z) Torus consistency (D2.4 item; added to D(k) as clause (vi), at flat data on ℓ₀).* For a cluster Γ of the reference gas on ℓ₀ whose groups have short arcs on a straight line or on a bent path, the weight Φ°(Γ; T) is defined on every torus T of the sequence (H3) in which Γ embeds. The item: these weights have limits Φ°^∞(Γ) as the periods grow, at the rate Σ_{Γ: b∈J_Γ}|Φ°(Γ; T) − Φ°^∞(Γ)| ≤ C_Tτ_k²e^{−cι_{T,k+1}}, the sum over the clusters rooted at a traversal b that embed in T.
  - At step 1 this is V4's periodisation argument (L1656, with the image bound eq:bulk-image-error, L3813). The rate is V4's |F_{C,T} − F_{C,∞}(n)| ≤ Cτ²(4n)e^{−cι_T} (eq:regional-square-periodic-comparison, L2118), one of the three parts of E°_{T,n} listed at L210.
  - At step k+1 it needs the same for Bałaban's flat activities H(Z), which the s-parameters localise (RG2 p.13: σ₀ is the family of cubes of π_{k+1} "disjoint with the interior of Z̃₀"), and for the step-k objects by (vi) at step k.
  - The coefficients below are defined on ℤ⁴ through these limits. V4 does the same at step 1 (L1505: "Use the infinite-lattice covariance constructed from the same periodic block grid").

### (5) The coefficients (definitions; convergence from D2.4's tail bound)

- *D2.4's tail bound (T).* For the reference gas on ℓ₀ (on T, and on ℤ⁴ through (Z)), for the straight line, the bent path and the square:
  Σ_{Γ: b∈J_Γ}|Φ°(Γ)|e^{η′ℓ(Γ)} ≤ C_Tτ_k² for every traversal b,
  uniformly in k. Here ℓ(Γ) is an extent with V3's network property: the network of supports and arcs of Γ has length at most C_netℓ(Γ) in step-k bonds (V4 L1501, C_net). This is the step-(k+1) form of eq:marginal-cluster-tail and eq:corner-bent-tail.
  - On the bent path (T) is needed with C_netℓ(Γ) ≥ |b| − R_* for a cluster rooted at b with arcs on both rays, with C_net itself; V4 L1630 uses a bent-path constant of up to 2C_net (d2-3 M8). With 2C_net, c_E and C_ζ below change by that factor and nothing else changes.
  - The groups now carry old occurrences ((3)), so the extent and the network include the arcs J_Γ and the supports of the old occurrences, and (T) must pay for them (D2.4).
- *Perimeter.* For v ∈ 𝒱_□ let ℓ_v ⊂ ℤ⁴ be the straight line through the origin in orientation v, with its step-k traversals indexed by b ∈ ℤ. The reference gas on ℓ_v has the groups with short arcs on ℓ_v. By contour locality, their activities and interactions are the values on any contour that contains their arcs, taken on ℤ⁴ by (Z). With J_Γ the traversals in the arcs of Γ's groups, put
  a_v(b) := Σ_{Γ: b∈J_Γ}Φ°^∞_v(Γ)/|J_Γ|, κ^{(k+1)}_v := Σ_{b=0}^{Π_{k+1}−1}a_v(b), δm_{k+1} := −(4Π_{k+1})^{−1}Σ_{v∈𝒱_□}κ^{(k+1)}_v.
  - a_v is Π_{k+1}-periodic by the invariance. By (T), |a_v(b)| ≤ C_Tτ_k², so |δm_{k+1}| ≤ C_Tτ_k².
  - δm_{k+1} is per step-k bond, D2.0(3)'s convention (δm_1 := δm^σP(C_n)/P_0(C_n); V3's eq:marginal-full-perimeter is per unit length, with the factor 1/(Lε)). At a commensurate step, δm_{k+1}P_k(C) = −n_kΣ_vκ^{(k+1)}_v, since P_k(C) = 4s_k = 4n_kΠ_{k+1}.
- *Corners.* For (v, w) ∈ 𝒞_□ (eq:corner-square-pairs), take the bent path of eq:corner-bent-path at the scale of the step-k traversals, with the bend at the origin, a period point. Let a_{v,w}(b) be its rooted density, and k_{v,w}(b) := a_v(b) for b < 0 and a_w(b) for b ≥ 0. Put
  ζ^{(k+1)}_{v,w} := Σ_{b∈ℤ}(a_{v,w}(b) − k_{v,w}(b)), ζ_{k+1} := ¼Σ_{(v,w)∈𝒞_□}ζ^{(k+1)}_{v,w}, z_{k+1} := e^{ζ_{k+1}}.
  - A cluster whose arcs all lie on one ray has the weight of the same cluster on that ray's line. Its groups see C only through their arcs (contour locality), the flat construction on ℤ⁴ does not involve C, and the bend is at a period point. This is V3's argument in the paragraph that leads to eq:corner-localisation-bound (d2-3 M11), including "Vacuum carriers may cross the bend".
  - So only clusters with arcs on both rays survive in a_{v,w}(b) − k_{v,w}(b). Their network reaches the bend from b, so C_netℓ(Γ) ≥ |b| − R_*, and (T) gives |a_{v,w}(b) − k_{v,w}(b)| ≤ 2C_Tτ_k²e^{−η′(|b|−R_*)_+/C_net}. Here R_* is the range of D2.4's network bound. Hence |ζ_{k+1}| ≤ C_ζτ_k², with C_ζ := 2C_Te^{η′R_*/C_net}(1 + 2/(e^{η′/C_net} − 1)).
- *Independence.* δm_{k+1} and ζ_{k+1} are ℤ⁴ quantities of a straight line and a bent path at flat trivial-label data. They do not depend on the size of C, the torus or ℓ. They depend on k through the couplings and the old objects, and on L, G, χ, σ and the prescriptions.

### (6) The square: the remainder E°_{k+1} (derived from (T) and (Z), as V4 L1683)

Put F°_{k+1}(C; T) := log 𝒲°_{k+1}(C; T) = Σ_ΓΦ°(Γ; T) and
E°_{k+1}(C, T) := F°_{k+1}(C; T) + δm_{k+1}P_k(C) − 4ζ_{k+1}.
The torus satisfies (5)(g)'s condition T_ph ≫ ℓ_C, so N^{(k)}_min ≫ s_k.

- *(a) Commensurate steps.* |E°_{k+1}| ≤ C_Eτ_k²[(s_k+1)e^{−c_Es_k} + s_ke^{−cι_{T,k+1}}].
  - Allocate by |J_Γ|^{−1}: F°_{k+1} = Σ_ba_□(b; T) over the 4s_k traversals of C, with a_□(b; T) := Σ_{Γ: b∈J_Γ}Φ°(Γ; T)/|J_Γ|.
  - Give each corner the last ⌊s_k/2⌋ traversals of its incoming side and the first s_k − ⌊s_k/2⌋ of its outgoing side (V3's half-sides). Translate the corner to the origin. It is a period point, since the step is commensurate. At the base corner, where the traversals are numbered from, V4 L1683 first uses the cyclicity of the trace; here F° is a scalar cluster series and each Y_𝒢 is the product ordered along its own arc (D2.2(6)(d)), so a group across the base point is treated like any other and no cyclicity step is needed (d2-3 M8).
  - Let b lie in the half-sides of the corner (v, w). A cluster Γ ∋ b with C_netℓ(Γ) < s_k/2 − 2R_* stays more than R_* away from the other corners. It does not wrap the torus, since N^{(k)}_min ≫ s_k. Its groups have short arcs. By contour locality and the invariance, it is a cluster of the bent path, with the same weight on T. The other clusters at b cost at most C_Tτ_k²e^{−η′(s_k/2−2R_*)/C_net}, by (T).
  - By (Z), the bent-path weights on T differ from those on ℤ⁴ by at most C_Tτ_k²e^{−cι_{T,k+1}} per root. So |a_□(b; T) − a_{v,w}(b)| ≤ C_Tτ_k²(2e^{−η′(s_k/2−2R_*)/C_net} + e^{−cι_{T,k+1}}), the factor 2 for the same truncation of a_{v,w}.
  - The sum of a_{v,w}(b) over the half-sides is the sum of k_{v,w}(b) over the half-sides, plus ζ^{(k+1)}_{v,w}, minus the part of ζ^{(k+1)}_{v,w} from |b| ≥ s_k/2. By (5) that part is at most C_ζτ_k²e^{−η′s_k/(2C_net)}.
  - Over the four corners, the half-sides make up the four sides. Each side is n_k whole period cells, starting at a period point, so its sum of a_v(b) is n_kκ^{(k+1)}_v exactly. The total is n_kΣ_vκ^{(k+1)}_v = −δm_{k+1}P_k(C).
  - Adding up: |E°_{k+1}| ≤ 4s_k·C_Tτ_k²(2e^{−η′(s_k/2−2R_*)/C_net} + e^{−cι_{T,k+1}}) + 4C_ζτ_k²e^{−η′s_k/(2C_net)}. This is the bound, with c_E := η′/(2C_net) and C_E := 8e^{2η′R_*/C_net}C_T + 4C_ζ.
- *(b) The other steps (corrected after d2-3 S2).* With the admissible sides of (4) there are none, and (b) is void. Without the restriction there are at most a* of them, and the bound is
  |E°_{k+1}| ≤ C_E′τ_k²[Π_{k+1} + 1 + s_ke^{−cι_{T,k+1}}] + 4C_Tτ_k²s_k.
  - Compare a_□(b; T) with the density of the line that carries b's side. For b at distance d from the nearest corner the comparison costs C_Tτ_k²(2e^{−η′(d−R_*)_+/C_net} + e^{−cι_{T,k+1}}), by the argument of (a) with the line in place of the bent path. Summed over b this is at most 8C_Tτ_k²e^{η′R_*/C_net}(1 + 2/(e^{η′/C_net} − 1)) + 4s_kC_Tτ_k²e^{−cι_{T,k+1}}.
  - The two sides through the origin lie on ℓ_{e₁}, ℓ_{e₂} (or their reverses). A side of s_k traversals need not be a union of cells; its sum of a_v(b) differs from (s_k/Π_{k+1})κ^{(k+1)}_v by at most 2Π_{k+1}C_Tτ_k².
  - The two far sides lie on x₁ = s_k and x₂ = s_k, the lines ℓ_v + t·e_⊥ with t = s_k mod Π_{k+1} ≠ 0, which are not period translates of ℓ_v. Their period sums κ_v^{[t]} have no relation to κ^{(k+1)}_v beyond |κ_v^{[t]}|, |κ^{(k+1)}_v| ≤ Π_{k+1}C_Tτ_k², so each far side leaves (s_k/Π_{k+1})|κ_v^{[t]} − κ^{(k+1)}_v| ≤ 2C_Tτ_k²s_k. The earlier bound omitted these two terms (d2-3 S2).
  - With |4ζ_{k+1}| ≤ 4C_ζτ_k², this gives the bound.

### (7) The sum over the steps (derived)

- Put τ̄² := sup_{k<K}τ_k². τ = C[g(1+r)³ + e^{−cr²}] increases in g for small g, and g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K (the last inequality of CRE (2.6); reading R-cpl₀ at general G). So τ̄² ≤ Cg_K²(log g_K^{−2})^{6p₀}, as in D2.0(5)(f).
- With m := K − k ≥ 1 and s_k = ℓ_CL^m, (6) gives
  Σ_{k<K}|E°_{k+1}| ≤ C_Eτ̄²Σ_{m≥1}2ℓ_CL^me^{−c_Eℓ_CL^m} + C_Eτ̄²Σ_{m≥1}ℓ_CL^me^{−cι_{T,K−m+1}}
  for the admissible sides of (4), where every step is commensurate.
  - Without the restriction, the at most a* steps of (6)(b) add C_E′τ̄²a*(Π* + 1) + 8C_Tτ̄²ℓ_CL^{a*} + C_E′τ̄²Σ_mℓ_CL^me^{−cι_{T,K−m+1}}, with Π* := L²M(L+1)(a*)^{β₀}R_{K−1} bounding Π_{k+1} there, by (4) (d2-3 M1). The term 8C_Tτ̄²ℓ_CL^{a*} is uniform in K but linear in ℓ_C (d2-3 S2).
  - The first sum is at most a constant C(c_E, L), for every ℓ_C ≥ 1 and every K: its terms are xe^{−c_Ex} at x = ℓ_CL^m, which grow at most geometrically up to x ≍ 1/c_E and then decay faster than geometrically.
  - The third is D2.0(5)(g)'s torus sum. With N^{(k)}_min ≍ T_phL^{K−k} it is at most Cℓ_Ce^{−c′T_ph}, small for T_ph large.
- So, for the admissible sides, the remainders of all steps sum to at most C_Στ̄², with C_Σ depending on L, c_E and C_E. It does not depend on K or ℓ_C. C_Στ̄² ≤ Cg_K² times a power of log g_K^{−2}, small for g_K small. Without the restriction C_Σ also depends on M, β₀, C_E′ and, through a* and R_{K−1}, on g_K, and the bound gains the ℓ_C-linear term above.
- *Consequence for D2.0(5)(b) (stated with its inputs after d2-3 M9).* At a commensurate step the corner remainder is at most C_Eτ_k²(s_k+1)e^{−c_Es_k}, not of order τ_k²g_k^δ. Its sum over the steps is O(τ̄²) uniformly in K, using g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_K only. This closes (b) without an upper bound on the couplings once D2.4 proves (T), (Z) and the Kotecký–Preiss condition with constants independent of k. Until then (b) stays open, with this as its closing route. (b) was the only user of the upper bound (D2.0(5), *The couplings*), so on this route D2 does not use reading R-cpl (RG I (0.31), Thm 2, SU(2)). R-cpl₀ is still used, here for τ̄².
- *Consequence for V3's Conjecture iteration.* Remark next-step (3)(e) counts the torus and corner part as Cτ²(2+1/c) per step, one source of (k+2)τ². With the exact coefficients of every step kept in κ^{(K)}, this source contributes at most C_Στ̄² in total. The other source of (k+2)τ², replacing every z_j by 1 (Remark (2)), does not arise.
- *D(k)'s clause on E_const (corrected after d2-3 M7).* The remainder of step j carries τ_{j−1}², since it is E°_j of the step j−1 → j. So |E_const,k| ≤ Σ_{j≤k}Cτ_{j−1}²(n_je^{−cι_{T,j}} + (n_j+1)e^{−c_En_j}), with n_j := s_{j−1}, at the admissible sides. The clause is kept with τ_{j−1}²; a conversion to τ_j² would need CRE (2.6)–(2.7) for both parts of τ, which is not done here. Without the restriction, each of the at most a* other steps adds Cτ_{j−1}²(Π_j + 1 + 4s_{j−1}). The clause is amended so (note in D2.0(3)).

### (8) D(k+1)(i) and (v): the assembly (derived)

On every labelled term ℓ, (3) gives ⟨𝒲_k⟩_{1,1} = κ^{(k)}e^{𝓔_k}S°(ℓ)[f_{k+1} + Σ_{D≠∅}Q^{out}_{k+1}(D)w^d(D)]. Put
- κ^{(k+1)} := κ^{(k)}κ_{k+1}, κ_{k+1} := e^{−δm_{k+1}P_k(C)}z_{k+1}⁴, with δm_{k+1} and z_{k+1} of (5);
- E^∂_{k+1}(ℓ) := log S°(ℓ) − log S°(ℓ₀), the difference of the two cluster series;
- 𝓔_{k+1} := 𝓔_k + E^∂_{k+1}(ℓ) + E°_{k+1}(C, T), and E_const,k+1 := E_const,k + E°_{k+1}(C, T).

Since log S°(ℓ₀) = F°_{k+1} = −δm_{k+1}P_k(C) + 4ζ_{k+1} + E°_{k+1}, this gives κ^{(k)}e^{𝓔_k}S°(ℓ) = κ^{(k+1)}e^{𝓔_{k+1}} exactly.
- D(k+1)(i) holds, with coefficients independent of the size of C, the torus and ℓ ((5)).
- D(k+1)(v) holds: E^∂_{k+1}(ℓ), E°_{k+1}(C, T) and Q^{out}_{k+1}(D) are numbers given ℓ.
- E^∂_{k+1}(ℓ) is the step-(k+1) form of V3's E^∂_ξ, |E^∂_ξ| ≤ Cτ²Σ_je^{−cd_j} (eq:regional-square-boundary-comparison, cited at V4 L210). It is the sum of the cluster terms that see ℓ's data: the clusters of ℓ₀ that ℓ does not have (they meet ℓ's large-field regions or their boundaries), the clusters that only ℓ has, and the changes of the activities near ℓ's data. Its form as a local sum Σ_Xe^{(k+1)}_X with D(k)(ii)'s anchored bound is D2.6's, as IT-1 note §3b and §3i do at step 1. It must not be bounded globally (D2.0(5)(f)).

### (9) What D2.3 leaves

- *To D2.4:*
  - (T), with C_T, η′, C_net and R_* uniform in k;
  - (Z), at the rate e^{−cι_{T,k+1}};
  - the Kotecký–Preiss condition that gives S°(ℓ) ≠ 0 and the cluster series of S°^{[D]}, on every ℓ;
  - the absolute convergence used in (3), now over the families with their occurrences;
  - Σ_{Γ hits 𝒢}|Φ°(Γ)| ≤ θ_{k+1}(𝒢) for the cluster form of Q^{out}_{k+1}, which gives |Q^{out}_{k+1}(D)| ≤ ∏e^{θ};
  - the payment, in (T) and in D2.4's size statement (S), for the occurrences merged into the groups by the third rule of (3): their arcs J_Γ, their supports and the entropy of the occurrence tuples (d2-3 S1, cost);
  - the sizes of the Y^d_𝒢, D(k+1)(ii) for the deviation parts, including at the reference point (reference clause).
- *To D2.6:* E^∂_{k+1}(ℓ) as attached local terms; the species of the Y^d_𝒢 near ℓ's data; the reference clause's sizes on labelled terms.
- *To the final-scale step:* that the stopping scale may depend on the loop, which makes the admissible sides of (4) no restriction of generality.
- *Amendments of D(k) (notes in D2.0(3)):* the reference clause in (iii); clause (vi), (Z); the cluster form of the interaction clause; the E_const bound with τ_{j−1}², and the non-commensurate term if ℓ_C is not restricted.
- *Amendment of D2.2(6)(d):* the third merging rule and the expansion of Q° ((3); note in D2.2(6)(d)).
- *Readings.* R-tr (translation covariance, (1)), new, for the user's review. The other Bałaban inputs are the partition sizes of (1), CRE p.256 and CRE (2.9), quoted, and RG1 pp.260, 262, quoted in (2).
- *For the user's review:* the restriction of ℓ_C to multiples of L^{q*} ((4)).

## D2.4 The contraction count (written from 21:49, 22 Oct, date; not reviewed)

### (0) What is needed

- *From D2.3(9):*
  - the Kotecký–Preiss condition for the reference gas on every ℓ, with the absolute convergence used in D2.3(3);
  - (T), with C_T, η′, C_net and R_* uniform in k;
  - (Z);
  - |Q_{k+1}(D)| ≤ ∏e^{θ}, and the decay of Φ_{k+1}(S);
  - the sizes of the Y^d_𝒢: D(k+1)(ii) for the deviation parts, including at the reference point.
- *From D2.2(5)(f):* R-vac(ii) from Bałaban's bounds, with d(Z) = 9 log2·|Z|; c|M| ≤ νσ(M)/16 (was 9c|M|; d2-2c-r2 m2); the sizes of Ĥ(M); the enlargement of D_J to D_J^#.
- *From D2.0(3)(c) and (5)(c):* the matching (γ) of old and new neighbourhoods and radii, the split (ζ) of the maximum, and the recursion for C_χ^{(k+1)}; the tolerance at complex data (d2-0c-r4 m9).
- *Output.* D(k+1)(ii) and the norms of D(k+1) for the insertions and interactions made at step k+1, with constants that satisfy D(k)(iv).

### (1) Quoted (Bałaban from the transcriptions Balaban\tex\<paper>\parts\pNNN.tex, 21:49–, 22 Oct, date)

- *RG1 p.257 (localization domains).* π_j is the family of closed cubes of size M. "Such a domain is a union of a connected, finite family of cubes from π_j. A connected family means that for every pair □, □′ of cubes from the family there exists a sequence … such that two consecutive cubes have a common wall, i.e. their intersection is a d−1-dimensional cube." The class is 𝐃_j. "Consider a class of tree graphs contained in X and intersecting all the cubes in X. A length of a shortest graph in this class, divided by M, is the linear size of X, and is denoted by d_j(X)."
- *RG2 p.14 (the vacuum gas).* (2.9) "(2.1) = Σ_ZH(Z)". (2.10): "if Z = Z₁ ∪ ⋯ ∪ Z_n, where Z_i is a connected component of Z, then H(Z) = H(Z₁)⋯H(Z_n)". (2.11) is the polymer expansion with ∏_{i<j}ζ(Z_i, Z_j), "where the function ζ(Z,Z′) is defined by the condition: ζ(Z,Z′) = 0 if Z∩Z′ contains a cube, or a wall of a cube, and ζ(Z,Z′) = 1 otherwise". (2.12)–(2.13) are the exponentiated series, with ρ^T(Z₁,…,Z_n) = Σ_{g∈C_n}∏_{{i,j}∈g}(ζ(Z_i,Z_j) − 1).
- *RG2 p.15 (domain).* "Thus the activities in (2.13), and the whole sum 𝐄^{(k+1)}(X), are analytic functions of (𝐔,𝐉), on the space 𝐔^c_{k+1}(X,α₀,α₁)." A term of H(Z) is (2.14), with contour integrals in complex σ(Δ), τ(Y) and real Gaussian integrals in X and B: "We consider it as an analytic function of (𝐔,𝐉) in the space 𝐔^c_{k+1}(X,α₀,α₁), and of the complex parameters σ(Z), τ. This complicates estimates of this expression, because the operators in it are not symmetric, and the second measure is complex." (2.15) bounds it.
- *RG2 pp.18–20 (the bound).*
  - (2.30), p.18: "(3·2³)^{−1}M^{−4}|Y| ≤ d_k(Y) ≤ M^{−4}|Y| − 1 holding for localization domains Y ∈ 𝐃_k".
  - p.19: "ε₂ = 2E₀ε₁C₁α₄^{−1}α₆^{−1}M^q exp C₂κ₁".
  - p.20: "Assuming that 2(L+2)⁴O(1)ε₂exp5κ ≤ 1, we obtain …"; "We define the constant C₃ = 2(L+2)⁴O(1)2E₀C₁α₄^{−1}α₆^{−1}M^q exp C₂κ₁."
  - Lemma 3, p.20: "Under all the above restrictions on the constants M, κ, κ₁, α₀, α₁, α₄, α₆, γ₂, γ, ε₁, the activity H(Z) for a localization domain Z ∈ 𝐃_{k+1} satisfies the inequality |H(Z)| ≤ C₃ε₁exp(−(1−8δ)½Lκd_{k+1}(Z))" (2.38).
  - p.21: (2.39)–(2.41) repeat "the considerations and bounds of the paper [26], for κ sufficiently large, and ε₁ sufficiently small", and give |𝐄^{(k+1)}(X)| ≤ O(1)C₃ε₁exp(−(1−10δ)½Lκd_{k+1}(X)). "At first we assume that (1−10δ)½L = 1, or δ = (1/10)(1−2L^{−1})." Also "we take M sufficiently large, so that δ₀M ≥ κ".
- *Check of (2.30), left side (derived).* (2.30), p.18, reads "(3·2³)^{−1}M^{−4}|Y| ≤ d_k(Y) ≤ M^{−4}|Y| − 1". With RG1's d_j, a single cube has d = 0: one point of the cube is a tree graph that meets every cube of the domain. So the left inequality fails for one cube (1/24 ≤ 0), and also for the 16 cubes around a common vertex. (2)(a) gives #(Y) ≤ 162(d(Y) + 1) in its place.
- *Its uses, and reading R-L3 (after d2-4-r1 m2; labelled, for the user's review).* The left side of (2.30), or its Section-1 form "M^{−4}|Y| ≤ 3·2³d_k(Y)", is used per domain in these steps:
  - p.20, in Lemma 3's proof: "We use the factor exp(−δ½Lκd_{k+1}(Z)), and the inequality (2.30), to bound the exponentials by 1". The exponentials are exp(A·#(Z)), A an absolute bound for O(1)(LM)⁴α₅ + exp(−½(κ₁−1)) (p.20). With (2)(a), exp(A#(Z) − δ½Lκd_{k+1}(Z)) ≤ e^{δ½Lκ} = e^{(L−2)κ/20} (p.21's δ) once δ½Lκ/162 ≥ A, i.e. κ ≥ 3240A/(L−2).
  - p.19, after (2.34): "The exponential on the right-hand side multiplied by exp(−δκd_k(Z_0)) can be estimated by 1". The exponent is exp(−(1/20)γ₂ε₁²/γ²)#(Z_0). The same argument costs e^{δκ} per component of Z_0, since e^{−4κ} ≤ δκ/162 for κ ≥ κ_vac, using p.18's "(1/20)γ₂ε₁²/γ² ≥ 4κ".
  - p.16, "Using (1.28)", and pp.8–9: (1.28) "M^{−4}|Y| ≤ 3·2³d_k(Y) ≤ exp(1/16)(κ₁−2)d_k(Y)" and p.9's "M^{−4}|Y| ≤ 3·2³d_k(Y) ≤ exp δκd_k(Y)" bound a sum over the cubes of Y. With (2)(a), M^{−4}|Y| ≤ 162(d_k(Y)+1) ≤ 162e^{d_k(Y)}, so each such step costs a factor 162 against the printed bound exp(cd_k(Y)) once (κ₁−2)/16 ≥ 1, resp. δκ ≥ 1.
  - p.21, after (2.40), uses it for 𝐄^{(k+1)}(X), which (2) does not use.
  - *R-L3.* Lemma 3 holds with C₃ replaced by C₃′ := C₃e^{(L−2)κ/20}, under κ ≥ 3240A/(L−2), with the per-domain factors e^{δκ} (p.19) and 162 (p.16) taken into the small factors those domains carry (ε₂ on p.19, (2.33); α₄ on p.16), and p.20's smallness assumption strengthened by 162e^{(L−2)κ/20 + δκ}. L ≥ 3 is needed, since δ = 0 at L = 2. Basis: the steps above are the uses found on pp.8–9 and 14–21, and Bałaban's order (κ, then M, then ε₁, p.21) allows the strengthening. The bookkeeping of the per-component factors through pp.19–20 is Bałaban's and is not redone here; no counterexample is known.
- *C₃′ε₁ (derived from the print, given R-L3).* C₃ε₁ = 2(L+2)⁴O(1)·ε₂, with the O(1) of the assumption on p.20 (C₃ is defined from that factor in the same paragraph). So p.20's assumption, strengthened as in R-L3, gives C₃′ε₁ ≤ e^{−5κ}.
- *FV Thm 5.4, p.224 (Friedli–Velenik).* Assume (5.2), |δ(γ,γ′)| ≤ 1, and that there is a: Γ → ℝ_{>0} with Σ_{γ′}|w(γ′)|e^{a(γ′)}|δ(γ,γ′)| ≤ a(γ) for each γ (5.10). Then for all γ₁, 1 + Σ_{k≥2}kΣ_{γ₂,…,γ_k}|φ(γ₁,…,γ_k)|∏_{j≥2}|w(γ_j)| ≤ e^{a(γ₁)} (5.11). Here φ(γ₁) = 1 and φ(γ₁,…,γ_m) = (1/m!)Σ_{G connected}∏_{{i,j}∈G}δ(γ_i,γ_j). For a hard-core gas 1 + δ is the compatibility indicator, so δ ∈ {−1, 0}, (5.2) holds, and (1/m!)ρ^T∏H is φ∏w. The polymer set is finite on the torus.
- *CRE (2.38), p.261.* For cubes □ of size CM in Ω_j there is a gauge transformation u on □∩X with "U^u = exp iξA, L^nξ|A|, (L^nξ)²|∇^ξA| < BCMα_{0,n}". This is condition (ii) of the definition of Ũ^c_j(X, α̃₀, α̃₁), p.261: a domain condition with α_{0,n}, not a bound proportional to the deviation (d2-4-r1 m1).
- *CRE pp.277–278.* "The factor H′(Z₀) is different; it is given by an integral with the integrand including, as one of the factors, a localized term from the expansion of the integrated function in the expectation value in (3.37). Although different, it satisfies the same bound as the other factors." And: "the terms 𝐄₀^{(k+1)}(Λ_{k+1},X,z) of this representation, for X ⊂ Λ_{k+1}, do not depend on Λ_{k+1}, and coincide with the corresponding terms arising from the expressions defined on the whole lattice, i.e. in the framework of [I]."
- *VAR (7), p.278, and Theorem 1, p.279.* (7): "|(∂V)(p′) − 1| < ε₁ for p′ ∈ 𝔅_k". Theorem 1: "for an arbitrary cube □ in the class described above, of a size 2ML^jη, M ≤ M(ε₁), there exists a gauge transformation u defined on a neighborhood of □ and such that on □, U^{u^{−1}} = e^{iηA}, |A| < B₃Mε₁(L^jη)^{−1}, |∇^ηA| < B₃Mε₁(L^jη)^{−2}". The bound is in the global ε₁ of (7).
- *VAR Proposition 9, p.309.* "The minimal configuration U_k(V) = U_k(V′V₀) in the axial gauge has an extension to an analytic function of G^c-valued small configurations V′ on 𝔅_k." "The function U_k(V′V₀)U_k(V₀)^{−1} transformed to the Landau gauge is, by the definition, equal to exp iη𝓗(B), where B = 1/i log V′." 𝓗 "is an analytic function of B, and also of the external gauge field configuration U", satisfies (19)–(21) with ε₂ = B₅ε₁, and its functional derivative (182) satisfies (190).

### (2) The Kotecký–Preiss condition for Bałaban's vacuum gas (derived given readings R-L3 and, on labelled terms, R-vac(i′); replaces reading R-vac(ii))

- *Setting (relabelled after d2-4-r1 S4).* The polymers are the Z ∈ 𝐃_{k+1} on T for ℓ₀, and the Z ⊂ Λ_{k+1} on a labelled term ℓ. On ℓ₀ the activities are RG2's, and Lemma 3 applies to them as printed (with R-L3 of (1)). On a labelled term they are the activities of the gas of CRE (3.27), with the source term, and the bounds below use:
  - *Reading R-vac(i′) (labelled, for the user's review).* For t ∈ [0,1] and with the source term at full strength, the activities H(Z), Z ⊂ Λ_{k+1}, of the gas of (3.27) satisfy (2.38) with Bałaban's constants. Basis: CRE p.277, "The differences, like the restriction of the integration to Λ_{k+1}, or the larger class of localization domains, do not matter", and p.279, "The other expectation values in (3.26), (3.28) are treated in exactly the same way". Point of doubt: neither sentence names the source term, and its coefficient can be of size O(ε_k/g_k) near ∂Λ_{k+1} (D2.2(5)(b)), which is where the polymers inside Λ_{k+1} near its boundary are affected. It is the bound form of R-vac(i) (D2.2(5)(b)), which speaks of convergence only.
  - R-I7 (CRE's "Sect. 7 [I]" is RG2 Sect. 2).
  - The polymers that meet Λ_{k+1}ᶜ are D2.6's. #(Z) := (LM)^{−4}|Z| is the number of π_{k+1}-cubes of Z. Lemma 3 is a bound for the analytic continuation on 𝐔^c_{k+1}(X,α₀,α₁) (p.15), so everything below holds uniformly on that domain.
- *The gas.* By (2.10), H of a domain is the product over its components, and distinct components share no cube and no wall (RG1's connectedness is through walls). So (2.11) is the hard-core gas of the connected Z ∈ 𝐃_{k+1}, with Z ≁ Z′ iff ζ(Z,Z′) = 0.
- *(a) Linear size (derived).* d_{k+1}(Z) ≥ #(Z)/162 − 1.
  - Rescale so that the cubes of π_{k+1} are unit cubes. Let 𝒯 be a shortest tree graph of RG1's class, of length ℓ = d_{k+1}(Z). A closed walk around 𝒯 runs along each edge twice and has length 2ℓ. Choose points p₀, …, p_N on it, consecutive ones at arc length ≤ 1, with N := ⌈2ℓ⌉ (one point if ℓ = 0).
  - Every point of 𝒯 is within arc length 1/2, hence within ℓ^∞-distance 1/2, of some p_i. Each cube of Z meets 𝒯, so it meets the closed ℓ^∞-ball B_i of radius 1/2 about some p_i.
  - B_i is a cube of side 1. In each coordinate a closed interval of length 1 meets at most 3 closed unit intervals of the partition, so B_i meets at most 3⁴ = 81 cubes. Hence #(Z) ≤ 81(N+1) ≤ 81(2ℓ+2).
- *(b) Activities.* With p.21's δ, (1−8δ)½L = L/10 + 4/5 ≥ 1 for L ≥ 2, and d ≥ 0, C₃′ε₁ ≤ e^{−5κ} ((1), R-L3; d2-4-r1 m2). So (2.38) with C₃′ and (a) give |H(Z)| ≤ e^{−5κ}e^{−κd_{k+1}(Z)} ≤ e^{−4κ}e^{−λ#(Z)}, λ := κ/162.
- *(c) Entropy.* At most 64^{n−1} connected Z with #(Z) = n contain a given cube. A closed depth-first walk from that cube along a spanning tree of the wall-adjacency graph of Z has 2(n−1) steps, each to one of the 8 wall-neighbours, and it determines Z.
- *(d) Incompatibility.* A wall lies in exactly two cubes. So Z′ ≁ Z iff Z′ contains a cube of Z⁺, the cubes of Z and their wall-neighbours, and |Z⁺| ≤ 9#(Z). The same holds for any union M of π_{k+1}-cubes in place of Z, with ≁ read as ζ = 0.
- *(e) The condition.* Let b ≥ 0, w̃(Z) := H(Z)e^{b#(Z)}, a(Z) := c#(Z). By (b)–(d),
  Σ_{Z′≁M}|w̃(Z′)|e^{a(Z′)} ≤ 9#(M)·e^{−4κ}Σ_{n≥1}64^{n−1}e^{−(λ−b−c)n}.
  If λ − b − c ≥ log 128, the series is at most 2e^{−(λ−b−c)} ≤ 1/64. So the left side is at most (9/64)e^{−4κ}#(M). With
  c := (9/64)e^{−4κ},
  this is (5.10) for w̃ and a, and the same bound holds for every union of cubes M. The requirement is κ/162 ≥ b + c + 7 log2.
- *(f) R-vac(ii) (derived, given R-L3 and, on labelled terms, R-vac(i′)).* Take b := 9 log2, the weight d(Z) = 9 log2·#(Z) of R-vac(ii) (D2.2(5)(b) writes |Z| for the cube count #(Z); d2-4-r1 m4). Since c ≤ 1, the requirement holds for
  κ ≥ κ_vac := 162(1 + 16 log2).
  Then Σ_{Z′≁Z}|H(Z′)|e^{a(Z′)+d(Z′)} ≤ a(Z) with a(Z) = c#(Z). This is R-vac(ii) with c fixed. It is derived from Lemma 3 on ℓ₀, and on every ℓ given R-vac(i′) (d2-4-r1 S4); both need R-L3. So (ii) is no longer a reading of its own; R-vac(i′) and R-L3 are.
- *(g) Consequence (derived from (5.11)).* For every union of cubes M,
  Σ_{𝒞≁M}|φ^T(𝒞)|e^{d(𝒞)} ≤ c#(M),
  the sum over the ordered clusters with some member ≁ M. By the symmetry of φ, a cluster with a member ≁ M is counted by putting that member first, which costs the factor k of (5.11). So the sum is at most Σ_{Z₁≁M}|w̃(Z₁)|(1 + Σ_{k≥2}kΣ|φ(Z₁,…,Z_k)|∏_{j≥2}|w̃(Z_j)|) ≤ Σ_{Z₁≁M}|w̃(Z₁)|e^{a(Z₁)} ≤ c#(M), by (5.11) and (e).
  - This is sharper than D2.2(5)(e)'s earlier a(M⁺) ≤ 9c|M|, which stays valid; (5)(e) now uses c|M| (d2-2c-r2 m2). The restricted gases (activities H(Z)·1[Z compatible with 𝔐]) satisfy (5.10) a fortiori, so D2.2(5)(d)'s ratio formula holds with these constants.
- *The condition on κ (for the user's review).* κ ≥ κ_vac is a lower bound on Bałaban's κ, of the kind RG2 p.21 imposes ("for κ sufficiently large"). RG2 fixes κ before M (δ₀M ≥ κ, p.21) and ε₁ (p.20's assumption, p.21's "ε₁ sufficiently small"), so the bound can be imposed in his order of choices. It is added to his list of restrictions, not read into his text.
  - The other conditions on κ used here (d2-4-r1 m5): κ ≥ 3240A/(L−2) of R-L3; p.20's "½(κ₁−1) ≥ 2Lκ"; p.18's "(1/20)γ₂ε₁²/γ² ≥ 4κ". All of them fit the order κ → κ₁, M → ε₁ → γ: each bounds κ by a constant, or a later constant by κ.

### (3) The size count σ_{k+1} and the weights θ_{k+1} (definitions; θ_vac derived)

- *Definition (new norm convention, for the user's review).* For a step-(k+1) group 𝒢 (D2.2(6)(d)) let n_{k+1}(𝒢) be the number of step-(k+1) traversals of C in I(𝒢), and #_{k+1}(𝒢) the number of π_{k+1}-cubes of the domains of 𝒢's constituents (marked vacuum domains, slot supports, the domains of the old groups), counted with multiplicity. Put
  σ_{k+1}(𝒢) := n_{k+1}(𝒢) + #_{k+1}(𝒢).
  - V3's σ(W) = n(W) + |ℋ(W)|/(LM) is linear in the hull. At step k+1 the vacuum weight of a group is proportional to the volume of its marked domains ((5)(d) of D2.2, (g) above), and these domains are 4-dimensional, so σ_{k+1} counts cubes.
  - The decay statements survive. Where the support of 𝒢 is wall-connected, two of its cubes are at step-(k+1) distance at most M·#_{k+1}(𝒢), so the envelope form σ ≥ dist/(C_FM) holds there. Where 𝒢 is linked along C, n_{k+1} gives it.
- *The weight.* θ_{k+1}(𝒢) := θ_vac(𝒢) + θ_ref(𝒢).
  - θ_vac(𝒢) := Σ_MΣ_{𝒞≁M}e^{d(𝒞)}|φ^T(𝒞)|, the sum over the marked domains M of 𝒢: D2.2(6)(c)'s θ(G), taken at every point of the domain. By (2)(g), θ_vac(𝒢) ≤ c#_{k+1}(𝒢) ≤ cσ_{k+1}(𝒢).
  - θ_ref(𝒢) is the reference-gas weight of (7): what |Q_{k+1}(D)| = |S°^{[D]}(ℓ)/S°(ℓ)| costs per group of D.
- *The requirement θ_{k+1} ≤ νσ_{k+1}/16, split as ν/32 + ν/32.*
  - θ_vac: c ≤ ν/32, i.e. (9/64)e^{−4κ} ≤ ν/32, i.e. κ ≥ ¼log(9/(2ν)). With (1)–(2): κ ≥ κ_D := max{κ_vac, ¼log(9/(2ν)), 3240A/(L−2)}.
  - *Which constants ν depends on (after d2-4-r1 m15).* V3 sets ν := η, "the reserve constant of Section c4-6-2" (V3 L2789). η is chosen "after the fixed geometry and forest decay constants" (V3 L4619, with 1000ηA_s < ϖ/8), and ϖ ≤ min(1/16, λ_*/(64C_g)) (V3 §c4-8-2, L4768ff.), λ_* the decay rate of V3's root weights m(Z) = ε_*e^{−λ_*ℓ₁(Z)}. So the bound fits Bałaban's order, κ before M and ε₁, if λ_*, A_s and C_g do not depend on M or ε₁ and λ_* does not decrease as κ grows.
    - *Checked against V3 (00:08–00:12, 23 Oct, date).* V3 L3839–3843: "Fix, in this order and depending on L and the group only: C_g=162 and δ=1/40; a decay rate κ≥3 with δκ ≥ 2C_g log128, λ′=(1−5δ)Lκ/2049, λ_*=λ′−2C_g ≥ 2C_g log128". So C_g is absolute, and λ_* depends on L and κ only and increases with κ (V3's κ is read as Bałaban's κ, as V3's λ′ = (1−5δ)Lκ/2049 converts it to the LM-scale). Then ϖ = min(1/16, λ_*/(64C_g)) of V3 (c4-8-3) equals 1/16 once λ_* ≥ 4C_g, and η can be taken as any number with 1000ηA_s < ϖ/8 (V3 L4619, L4790). A_s = 1 + A_h (V3 L4790), with A_h of V3 L4780, "|H_γ| ≤ A_h LMn_γ", which "follow[s] from the square geometry and the wall connectivity of a component's domains" (V3 L4798) and is "fixed before the coupling". So ν, hence κ_D's term ¼log(9/(2ν)), is a fixed number for κ large, with no circularity, provided A_h does not depend on κ or M. V3 measures |H_γ| in units of LM and derives A_h from the cube geometry, but does not state its dependence on M (the halo width enters through V3 L4615's index bound R_e). That last point is OPEN (small).
  - θ_ref ≤ νσ_{k+1}/32: (7), OPEN with the sizes.
- *The old weights do not accumulate.* The bound ν_n^{(k)} = Σ_{W∋n}e^{θ_k(W)}‖Y_W‖ of D(k)(ii) already contains e^{θ_k(W)}. So when an old insertion enters a new group, its factor e^{θ_k(W)}|Q_k|-share is paid inside the group's size by ν_n^{(k)} ((4), (7)). θ_{k+1} is the fresh weight of the step-(k+1) gas. It is not θ_k converted to step-(k+1) units, which would grow by a factor L per step.

### (4) The deviation factor (the Cauchy step derived; the local gauge OPEN)

The object is Y^d_𝒢 = Y_𝒢 − c_𝒢T_𝐔(I(𝒢)) of D2.3(2), for a step-(k+1) group 𝒢 with support X(𝒢): the union of its constituents' domains, the comparison boxes of its old insertions and its slots. D(k+1)(ii) needs a bound by the deviation of U_{k+1} near X(𝒢) (D2.0(5)(c), "Needs D2.4"). Here k+1 ≥ 2. Groups that meet Λ_{k+1}ᶜ or depend on ω′ are att(k+1, ·) and D2.6's, so ω′ = 0 below.

- *The local deviation (ℓ_X repaired after d2-4-r1 S1).* For X a union of π_{k+1}-cubes put ℓ_X := d_{k+1}(X) + 1, with RG1's linear size d_{k+1} ((1)), and let X⁺ be its step-(k+1) neighbourhood of width 1. Put Dev_X(U) := ℓ_X·η_{k+1}^{−2}max{δ_p(U) : p a fine plaquette in X⁺}, in the normalisation of δ*^{(k+1)} (D2.0(3)).
  - With ℓ_X := d_{k+1}(X), Dev_X would vanish for one cube and for the 16 cubes at a vertex ((1)), and (LPG) would force U_{k+1} to be pure gauge there. The +1 removes this.
  - *Bounds (derived).* ℓ_X ≥ 1, and ℓ_X ≥ #(X)/162 by (2)(a). If X is wall-connected, ℓ_X ≤ #(X): order its cubes so that each meets an earlier one in a wall. A point of the tree in that earlier cube is within distance 1 of the wall, so one segment of length ≤ 1 inside the two cubes adds the new cube, and d_{k+1}(X) ≤ #(X) − 1, as in (2.30)'s right side. If X is only connected as a closed set, the same argument through a shared vertex gives ℓ_X ≤ 2#(X).
  - In (S) and (6), ℓ is this ℓ_X. If (LPG) is proved by an axial gauge, 1 + diam_∞(X)/M is the sharper choice; which ℓ (LPG) holds with is part of (LPG).
- *(LPG), the local proportional gauge (OPEN).* For X ⊂ Ω_{k+1} in the support of the term's indicators, there are a gauge transformation u on a neighbourhood of X and a real 𝔤-valued A on X such that U_{k+1}^u = e^{iη_{k+1}A} on X, and, in the units of CRE (2.38)–(2.39) at n = k+1,
  L^{k+1}ξ|A|, (L^{k+1}ξ)²|∇^ξA| ≤ C_gDev_X(U_{k+1}), and |R(u)J_{k+1}| ≤ C_gDev_X(U_{k+1}) in the units of (2.36),
  with C_g independent of k, X and the term.
- *The Cauchy step (derived, given (LPG)).* Put ᾱ_{k+1} := min(α_{0,k+1}, α_{1,k+1}) and f(z) := Y_𝒢(e^{izη_{k+1}A}, zR(u)J_{k+1}, 0) − c_𝒢T_{e^{izη_{k+1}A}}(I(𝒢)).
  - f(0) = Y_𝒢(1, 0, 0) − c_𝒢𝟙 = 0, by Schur (D2.3(2)).
  - By covariance (D2.2(6)(b)) and since Y_𝒢 depends on 𝐔, 𝐉 only on X (D2.3(4), contour locality, and Bałaban's localisation), f(1) = π(u(x))Y^d_𝒢(U_{k+1}, J_{k+1}, 0)π(u(x′))^{−1}, with x, x′ the ends of I(𝒢). π(u) is unitary, so ‖f(1)‖ = ‖Y^d_𝒢(U_{k+1}, J_{k+1}, 0)‖.
  - For |z| < R_X := ᾱ_{k+1}/(C_gDev_X(U_{k+1})), (LPG) puts (zA, zR(u)J_{k+1}) in D(k+1)(iii)'s domain at the reference background 1. So f is analytic there, by the reference clause (D2.3(2)).
  - Let S_𝒢 be the supremum of ‖Y_𝒢‖ + |c_𝒢|‖T_𝐔(I(𝒢))‖ over D(k+1)(iii)'s domain about the real points of the support, together with the reference-clause domain. If R_X > 1, Schwarz's lemma gives ‖f(1)‖ ≤ S_𝒢/R_X. If R_X ≤ 1, then ‖f(1)‖ ≤ S_𝒢 ≤ S_𝒢/R_X. In both cases
    e^{θ_{k+1}(𝒢)}‖Y^d_𝒢(U_{k+1})‖ ≤ b_𝒢Dev_{X(𝒢)}(U_{k+1}), b_𝒢 := (C_g/ᾱ_{k+1})e^{θ_{k+1}(𝒢)}S_𝒢.
  - This is the per-insertion form of (5).
- *From the per-insertion form to D(k+1)(ii) (derived, given (LPG) and (7)).* Let t(𝒢) be the least t ≥ 1 with X(𝒢)⁺ within step-(k+1) distance R_t^{(k+1)} of n′. Then Dev_{X(𝒢)} ≤ ℓ_{X(𝒢)}η_{k+1}^{−2}Δ^{(k+1)}_{n′}(R^{(k+1)}_{t(𝒢)}), for X(𝒢)⁺ ⊂ Ω_{k+1}, since Δ^{(k+1)} takes only plaquettes of Ω_{k+1} (d2-4-r1 m7). The dev species and (LPG) are taken for such groups only. A group with X(𝒢)⁺ meeting Ω_{k+1}ᶜ is attached to a component of Ω_{k+1}ᶜ that X(𝒢)⁺ meets, as in D2.0(3)'s reclassification rule; its bound is D2.6's. If the sizes satisfy
  (S) Σ_{𝒢∋n′, t(𝒢)=t}e^{θ_{k+1}(𝒢)}S_𝒢ℓ_{X(𝒢)} ≤ C_Sρ_{k+1}w_t for every t,
  then Σ_{𝒢∋n′}e^{θ_{k+1}(𝒢)}‖Y^d_𝒢‖ ≤ C_gC_S(ρ_{k+1}/ᾱ_{k+1})δ*^{(k+1)}_{n′}(U_{k+1}). (S) is the size statement of (7). The decay of S_𝒢 at the rate e^{−κd_{k+1}} in cubes of side M against w_t = (t+1)³e^{−(31/8)η_Vt} with R_t = C_boxM(t+1) is the comparison it needs.
- *Why the quoted bounds do not give (LPG).* CRE (2.38) gives L^nξ|A|, (L^nξ)²|∇A| < BCMα_{0,n}: a domain condition (condition (ii) of Ũ^c_j, p.261; d2-4-r1 m1), not proportional to the deviation. VAR Theorem 1 gives |A| < B₃Mε₁(L^jη)^{−1}, |∇A| < B₃Mε₁(L^jη)^{−2}, in the global ε₁ of VAR (7). An axial gauge on X gives the sup bound from the plaquette deviations along its paths, but not the gradient bound. The gradient bound needs the smoothness of the minimiser in terms of its local data.
- *The route considered (not completed).* VAR Proposition 9 makes U_k(V′V₀)U_k(V₀)^{−1} = exp iη𝓗(B) analytic in small V′, with the derivative bounds (190). Flattening V_{k+1} annulus by annulus about X in a radial gauge changes V′ on each annulus by about C_boxM times the deviation there. By (190) the effect on X decays like e^{−δ₀R_t/8}, which matches w_t. The route stops at the last step: on the torus, the configuration that is flat on the largest ball has to be joined to the reference along a path of small V′, and its holonomies need not allow this. What would close it is the locality of the minimiser: its restriction to X depends on V_{k+1} outside a ball of radius R about X only up to e^{−cR} in the norms of (LPG).
- *At k = 1 (step 1 → 2)* the old dev bound is V3's own D1(ii), and the sup-only domain of D(1)(iii) needs no gradient bound. (LPG) is needed for the groups made at steps k+1 ≥ 2.

### (5) The amendment of D(k)(ii) (for the user's review)

- *The dev constant.* For k ≥ 2 the first term of D(k)(ii) becomes C_χρ̃_kδ*^{(k)}_n, with ρ̃_k := ρ_k/ᾱ_k, ᾱ_k := min(α_{0,k}, α_{1,k}), and ρ̃_1 := ρ_1. The factor 1/ᾱ_{k+1} of (4) is the Cauchy factor of the domain's radius, and S_𝒢 is of size ρ_{k+1} under (S) (OPEN, (7)). So the amendment is forced by the Cauchy route of (4), not by a choice (d2-4-r1 m6).
- *Sizes on the support are unchanged in order (derived).* On the support, δ*^{(k)}_n(U_k) ≤ 2c_σB₃ε_kΣ_tw_t (D2.0(3)(b)). So ρ̃_kδ*_n ≤ Cρ_kε_k/ᾱ_k, and ε_k/α_{i,k} = (A₀/C_i)(log g_k^{−2})^{p₀−q_i} ≤ A₀/C_i under R-m's entailments (q₁ > p₀, or q₁ = p₀ with C₁/A₀ large; q₀ > p₀, or q₀ = p₀ with C₀/A₀ large; gaps block). Hence ν_n^{(k)} = O(ρ_k) on the support, as before.
- *At complex data* (D(k)(iii)) the dev term carries δ*^{(k),30}_n + a*^{(k)}_n(A′), and ρ̃_ka*_n(A′) ≤ Cρ_kα_{1,k}/ᾱ_k = Cρ_k·max(1, (C₁/C₀)(log g_k^{−2})^{q₁−q₀}). So the complex-data size gains the factor 1 + (log g_k^{−2})^{(q₁−q₀)₊}, of the same kind as ρ^c_{ph,k}'s. This is the tolerance of d2-0c-r4 m9: it is derived here, and it is a power of log g_k^{−2}, which is the form D2.0(3)(iii) allows.
  - *Older layers (OPEN, after d2-4-r1 m8).* The bound above holds where the R_t^{(k)}-neighbourhoods of n stay in Ω_k. On Ω_m∖Ω_{m+1}, m < k, D(k)(iii)'s domain allows |η_kA′| < α_{1,m}L^{−m} per fine bond, so a*^{(k)}_n gets L^{k−m}α_{1,m} times the tail Σ_{t: R_t^{(k)} ≥ dist}(t+1)e^{−(30/8)η_Vt}, and j*^{(k)}_n likewise, since ϱ(b) = (1−β(1−2^{−(k−m)}))α_{1,m}L^{−m} there. CRE p.256 gives only dist(Ω_n, Ω_{n−1}ᶜ) ≥ L^nξM₁, so in step-k units an older layer can lie within about 2M₁ of n, and L^{k−m}α_{1,m}/ᾱ_k is not bounded in k − m. So the complex-data size of the dev term is not O(ρ_k(1 + (log g_k^{−2})^{(q₁−q₀)₊})) near older layers. What would close it: weight the bonds of Ω_m∖Ω_{m+1} in a* and j* by (cθ)^{k−m}, which needs the dev parts' dependence on A′ and 𝐉 there to decay at R-dec's rate per layer, as D2.2(6)(e) shows for 𝐇; then L^{k−m}α_{1,m}(cθ)^{k−m} ≤ α_{1,k} once cLθ(1+β₀)² ≤ 1. That needs D(k)(iii) restated and D2.2(6)(e)'s older-layer argument extended to the dev parts.
- *The per-insertion form.* Each dev insertion carries its own bound: e^{θ(W)}‖Y_W(𝐔)‖ ≤ b_W·Dev_{Q^#_W}(𝐔), with Q^#_W its comparison box. Dev_{Q^#_W} uses the plaquettes of (Q^#_W)⁺, and these are the comparison box of D2.0(3)'s reclassification rule (Q_W itself at step 1). The reclassification of (6)(ζ) sorts insertions by where Q^#_W lies. That needs a bound per insertion, which D(k)(ii)'s per-traversal sum does not give. At step 1 the form holds by V3's group bounds (IT-1 note §3a); at step k+1 it is (4)'s.
- *Normalisation (after d2-4-r1 S2).* Dev_{Q^#_W}, ℓ_W := ℓ_{Q^#_W} and b_W stay in the normalisation of the step j(W) at which W was made, as θ and σ do for carried att groups (D2.0(3), "Units of carried groups"). Only δ* is converted from step to step ((6)(γ)). Converting Dev would not contract: by the bounds of (4), ℓ^{(k)}_Q/ℓ^{(k+1)}_Q lies between L⁴/162 and 162L⁴ for wall-connected Q (a π_{k+1}-cube is L⁴ π_k-cubes), so the best statement is Dev^{(k)}_Q ≤ 162L²Dev^{(k+1)}_Q.
- *The tail clause (ii-t) (added to D(k)(ii) after d2-4-r1 S3).* Put β_W := b_Wℓ_Wη_{j(W)}^{−2}, so that b_WDev_{Q^#_W}(𝐔) = β_W·max{δ_p(𝐔) : p in (Q^#_W)⁺}. Let t^{(k)}(W) be the least t ≥ 1 with (Q^#_W)⁺ within step-k distance R_t^{(k)} of n, and W_t := Σ_{t′≥t}w_{t′}. The clause is
  (ii-t) Σ_{W∋n, t^{(k)}(W)≥t}β_W ≤ C_χρ̃_kη_k^{−2}W_t for every t ≥ 1 and every traversal n.
  - *It gives the sum form (derived).* For dev W, (Q^#_W)⁺ ⊂ Ω_k, so b_WDev_{Q^#_W} ≤ β_WΔ_t with Δ_t := Δ^{(k)}_n(R_t^{(k)}) at t = t^{(k)}(W), and Δ_t is nondecreasing in t. With B_t := Σ_{W∋n, t^{(k)}(W)≥t}β_W, Abel summation gives Σ_{W∋n}b_WDev_{Q^#_W} ≤ Σ_tΔ_t(B_t − B_{t+1}) = Δ_1B_1 + Σ_{t≥2}(Δ_t − Δ_{t−1})B_t ≤ C_χρ̃_kη_k^{−2}Σ_tΔ_t(W_t − W_{t+1}) = C_χρ̃_kδ*^{(k)}_n. So (ii-t) is the class statement and the sum form is its consequence.
  - *At step 1 (derived from IT-1 note §3a(6)).* For a good group, ‖Υ⁰_W‖ ≤ K_χ𝔡⁰_W with 𝔡⁰_W ≤ C_χ𝔰(W)³δ_{Q_W}(U₁)M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W) (§3a(3)), and e^{θ°(W)} ≤ e^{η𝔰/8} (η = η_V, the IT-1 note's notation). So β_W ≤ K_χC_χe^{η𝔰/8}𝔰³M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W). Q_W lies within C_boxLM𝔰 of n_f, so t(W) ≤ max(1, ⌊𝔰⌋). §3a(6)'s split by ⌊𝔰⌋, with 𝔰³e^{η𝔰/8} ≤ (j+1)³e^{η/8}e^{−(31/8)ηj}e^{4η𝔰} at j = ⌊𝔰⌋ and the contour pin Σ_{W∋n_f}M^{(ρ)}_Λe^{4η𝔰} ≤ Cρ, gives Σ_{W∋n_f, t(W)≥t}β_W ≤ Cρ·sup_{j≥t}w_j ≤ CρW_t for t ≥ 2, and Cρ·max_{j≥0}w_j ≤ C_ηρW_1 for t = 1. Summing over the L fine traversals of a step-1 traversal, with the conversions of D2.0(3)(a), gives (ii-t) at k = 1.
  - *It carries from k to k+1 (derived).* An old dev insertion with (Q^#_W)⁺ ⊂ Ω_{k+1} stays dev, with β_W unchanged. If (Q^#_W)⁺ lies within step-k distance R_t^{(k)} of n ⊂ n′, it lies within step-(k+1) distance R_t^{(k)}/L ≤ R_t^{(k+1)} of n′, so t^{(k+1)}(W) ≤ t^{(k)}(W). Hence Σ_{W∋n′, t^{(k+1)}(W)≥t}β_W ≤ Σ_{n⊂n′}Σ_{W∋n, t^{(k)}(W)≥t}β_W ≤ L·C_χ^{(k)}ρ̃_kη_k^{−2}W_t = L^{−1}(ρ̃_k/ρ̃_{k+1})C_χ^{(k)}ρ̃_{k+1}η_{k+1}^{−2}W_t, the coefficient of (6)'s recursion.
  - *New groups (derived, given (S)).* β_𝒢 = (C_g/ᾱ_{k+1})e^{θ_{k+1}(𝒢)}S_𝒢ℓ_{X(𝒢)}η_{k+1}^{−2}, so (S) summed over t′ ≥ t gives Σ_{𝒢∋n′, t(𝒢)≥t}β_𝒢 ≤ C_gC_Sρ̃_{k+1}η_{k+1}^{−2}W_t.
  - The other dev contributions of D2.0(5)(c)–(d) (fluctuation parts, pair contractions) enter the recursion through their sum bounds. Their tail form is to be stated with them, in D2.0(5)(d), which is marked "Needs D2.1–D2.5".

### (6) Matching, split and recursion (derived)

- *(γ) The matching.* Take R_t^{(k)} := C_boxM(t+1) in step-k units at every k, which is D2.0(3)'s R_t^{(1)} at k = 1. Let n be one of the L step-k traversals in the new traversal n′. Step-(k+1) distances are step-k distances divided by L, so a plaquette within step-k distance R_t^{(k)} of n lies within step-(k+1) distance R_t^{(k)}/L ≤ R_t^{(k+1)} of n′. On a common plaquette set, Δ^{(k)}_n(R_t^{(k)}) ≤ Δ^{(k+1)}_{n′}(R_t^{(k+1)}) for every t. With η_k^{−2} = L^{−2}η_{k+1}^{−2},
  δ*^{(k)}_n(U) ≤ L^{−2}δ*^{(k+1)}_{n′}(U) for every U, on the plaquettes of Ω_{k+1}.
  Dev is not converted (after d2-4-r1 S2, which found the earlier sentence "The same holds for Dev_{Q^#}" reversed). Each insertion keeps Dev, ℓ_W and b_W in the normalisation of its step j(W) ((5), *Normalisation*), and the per-traversal bound carries through (ii-t) and the inclusion t^{(k+1)}(W) ≤ t^{(k)}(W) ((5)).
- *(ζ) The split (derived from (ii-t), after d2-4-r1 S3 and m13).* An old dev insertion W with (Q^#_W)⁺ ⊂ Ω_{k+1} stays dev ((5), carrying (ii-t)). If (Q^#_W)⁺ meets Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1}, it becomes att(k+1, Z). By D2.0(3)'s reclassification rule, Z is the first component of Ω_{k+1}ᶜ that the box meets, so the box meets Z, and R^{(k)}_{t^{(k)}(W)} ≥ dist(n, Z).
  - *Its plaquettes.* (Q^#_W)⁺ ⊂ Ω_k for a dev insertion at step k. By membership (2.34) at j = k+1 (CRE p.261: |∂U − 1| < (1−β(1−2^{−(j−n)}))α_{0,n}ξ²(L^nξ)^{−2}), the fine plaquettes of Ω_k∖Ω_{k+1} (n = k) give η_k^{−2}δ_p ≤ c_σ(1−β/2)α_{0,k}, as in D2.0(3)(c). Those of Ω_{k+1} (n = k+1) give η_k^{−2}δ_p ≤ c_σL^{−2}α_{0,k+1} ≤ c_σ(1+β₀)²L^{−2}α_{0,k} ≤ c_σα_{0,k}. So b_WDev_{Q^#_W} ≤ β_Wη_k²c_σα_{0,k}.
  - *The bound.* Let t_Z be the least t with R_t^{(k)} ≥ dist(n, Z). By (ii-t),
    Σ_{W∋n, W ↦ att(k+1,Z)}b_WDev_{Q^#_W} ≤ c_σα_{0,k}η_k²Σ_{W∋n, t^{(k)}(W)≥t_Z}β_W ≤ C_χρ̃_kc_σα_{0,k}W_{t_Z} ≤ Cρ_k·max(1, α_{0,k}/α_{1,k})·e^{−cd_{k+1}^Z(n)/M},
    since ρ̃_kα_{0,k} = ρ_kα_{0,k}/ᾱ_k and W_t decays exponentially in t. This is per component, as D(k+1)(ii)'s att bound is (D2.0(3), "Per component").
  - D(k+1)(ii)'s att constant is Cρ̂_{k+1}, with ρ_k ≤ (1+β₀)⁶ρ_{k+1}. So the split needs α_{0,k}/α_{1,k} = (C₀/C₁)(log g_k^{−2})^{q₀−q₁} bounded, i.e. q₀ ≤ q₁ (*entailment q₀ ≤ q₁, for the user's review*). Otherwise the att(k+1, ·) constant is enlarged by (log g_k^{−2})^{(q₀−q₁)₊}, and D2.6's per-component payment must absorb that power of log (recorded for D2.6).
- *The recursion with ρ̃.* The old dev parts on Ω_{k+1} give, by (ii-t) carried ((5)) and its sum form, Σ_{n⊂n′}C_χ^{(k)}ρ̃_kδ*^{(k)}_n ≤ L^{−1}(ρ̃_k/ρ̃_{k+1})C_χ^{(k)}ρ̃_{k+1}δ*^{(k+1)}_{n′}.
  - By CRE (2.6), first and last lines with n − m = 1, g_{k+1} ≤ (1+β₀)g_k and g_k ≤ (1+β₀)g_{k+1}. By (2.7), first printed line and third (the continuation of the second, with (n−m)^{β₀} = 1 at n − m = 1; d2-4-r1 m14), (log g_{k+1}^{−2})^p ≤ (1+β₀)(log g_k^{−2})^p and (log g_k^{−2})^p ≤ (1+β₀)(log g_{k+1}^{−2})^p.
