  - So ρ_k/ρ_{k+1} ≤ (1+β₀)⁶ (D2.0(5)(c)) and α_{i,k+1}/α_{i,k} ≤ (1+β₀)², hence ᾱ_{k+1}/ᾱ_k ≤ (1+β₀)² and ρ̃_k/ρ̃_{k+1} ≤ (1+β₀)⁸.
  - The terms of D2.0(5)(c)–(d) that are linear in the old dev bound carry this ratio in place of (1+β₀)⁶. The quadratic term has two old dev bounds, and one of them is O(ρ_k) on the support by (5). So
    C_χ^{(k+1)} ≤ C_new + (L^{−1} + C_dL²(1+β₀)²g_K²)(1+β₀)⁸C_χ^{(k)} + C_d″ρ_k(C_χ^{(k)})²,
    with C_new := C_gC_S from (4). It is bounded uniformly in k if L^{−1}(1+β₀)⁸ < 1 and g_K is small.
  - *The condition (1+β₀)⁸ < L (for the user's review).* CRE p.255: "β₀ > 0 can be chosen arbitrarily small, if g is sufficiently small". So for every L ≥ 2 it holds once β₀ < L^{1/8} − 1. For V3's L ≥ 13 with β₀ ≤ 0.22 it holds: 1.22⁸ < 4.91. D2.0(5)(c)'s "L ≥ 2, β₀ small" is right only with "small" read as this bound. With the old exponent 6 it would have been (1+β₀)⁶ < L; at L = 3 and β₀ = 0.22 that fails too, since 1.22⁶ ≈ 3.297 > 3.
  - At the step 1 → 2 the ratio is ρ̃_1/ρ̃_2 = (ρ_1/ρ_2)ᾱ_2 ≤ (1+β₀)⁶ᾱ_2 < 1, so the first step costs nothing.

### (7) The sizes of the groups and the reference gas ((S) with the H′ bound, (T)'s uniformity, and (Z) OPEN)

- *Where the slot factors are evaluated (derived; answers the check left open in the plan).* In D2.2(5)(c) the slot factor 𝒳 is a function of the conditioned Z₀-variable B alone ((2.4)–(2.6) there). It does not depend on X or on the s-, σ-, τ-parameters. In RG2 (2.14) B is a real integration variable, and the complex quantities are (𝐔, 𝐉) ∈ 𝐔^c_{k+1}(X,α₀,α₁), σ and τ. (2.15) bounds (2.14) by an integral of absolute values. So 𝒳 enters (2.15) through sup‖𝒳‖ over real B in the support of the indicators and complex (𝐔, 𝐉) in the domain. That is D(k)(iii)'s domain at U_k(𝐔, B), which D2.2(4)(f) and (6)(e) supply.
- *The H′ bound for marked polymers (OPEN inside (S); relabelled after d2-4-r1 m12, was "reading R-H′").* CRE p.277 says of H′(Z₀), whose integrand contains one localized term of CRE's own integrated function, "Although different, it satisfies the same bound as the other factors". What (S) needs is a different bound for a different object, so it is not a reading of that sentence. It is a derivation still to be done: RG2's estimates of pp.15–20, run with a slot factor 𝒳_M as a bounded factor in (2.15) and with Z₀ ⊇ D_J^#, should give
  |H̃(M)| ≤ C₃′·sup‖𝒳_M‖·exp(−(1−8δ)½Lκ·d_{k+1}(M; D_J^#)),
  where d_{k+1}(M; D_J^#) is the linear size of M with the cubes of D_J^# contracted to one point.
  - C₃′ is not small. The term with no Mayer bond, no P and no s-derivative, the conditioned Gaussian expectation of 𝒳_M, is present for a marked polymer. Bałaban's smallness C₃ε₁ comes from the nontrivial structure of vacuum polymers, and it is not claimed for marked ones.
  - There is no decay across D_J^#: inside it the integrand need not contain a vacuum polymer. That decay has to come from 𝒳_M's own decay in the spread of its slots, that is, from the old norms and the slot sizes of D2.1.
  - CRE asserts the statement for one inserted term, its own. Our 𝒳_M is a product of loop slots and old insertions. Nothing established here rests on this bound.
- *(S), the size statement (OPEN).* Σ_{𝒢∋n′, t(𝒢)=t}e^{θ_{k+1}(𝒢)}S_𝒢ℓ_{X(𝒢)} ≤ C_Sρ_{k+1}w_t for every t ((4)), and the same with c_𝒢 in place of S_𝒢 for the reference gas, with ℓ_X = d_{k+1}(X) + 1 ((4); ℓ_{X(𝒢)} ≤ 2#(X(𝒢)) when X(𝒢) is connected). Summed over t′ ≥ t it gives (ii-t) for the new groups ((5)). Reduced to:
  - the H′ bound above;
  - the slot sizes per component (D2.1, D2.2(4)), with the old insertions entering through ν_n^{(k)}, which already contains e^{θ_k} ((3));
  - the entropy of the groups: linked families of marked polymers, old groups and slots, counted with FV Thm 5.4 on the gas of linked families, as D2.2(5)(d) does for one family;
  - the comparison of the decay rate (1−8δ)½Lκ/162 per cube with (31/8)η_V/(C_boxM) per step-(k+1) unit of R_t, plus the entropy.
- *(R), the reference gas (derived given (S), except the uniformity of (T)'s constants in k, OPEN below).* Its polymers are the linked families of D2.3(3): groups with activities c_𝒢 and the Mayer links e^{−Φ(S)} − 1, with |e^{−Φ} − 1| ≤ |Φ|e^{|Φ|}. If (S) holds with an extra factor e^{(ν/32 + η′C_net)σ_{k+1}} inside the sum, and the old Φ_k(S) satisfy the decay of (T) at step k (induction), then FV Thm 5.4 applies with a°(𝒢) := (ν/32)σ_{k+1}(𝒢), as in (2)(e)–(g). It gives:
  - S°(ℓ) ≠ 0 and its cluster series, hence the absolute convergence used in D2.3(3);
  - (T) at step k+1, with constants that depend on step k's. Their uniformity in k is OPEN (after d2-4-r1 S5, which found it asserted, not derived), in three parts:
    - (a) the recursion. The step-(k+1) clusters contain the old occurrences, bounded at step k by D(k)'s Σ_{Γ hits W}|φ_k(Γ)| ≤ θ(W) and (T) at step k. So the constants obey C_T^{(k+1)} ≤ F(C_S, ν, η′) + G·C_T^{(k)}, and uniformity needs G < 1, which should come from the small amplitude of the old occurrences;
    - (b) the network constant. C_net and R_* are V3/V4's constants for V3's clusters (V4 L1494–1505, "C_net = 8(1+R_*)(1+q_*+c_h)"). For clusters of linked families of step-(k+1) groups no network-length bound is derived yet. A linked family is connected through walls and arcs of C, so its extent should be at most Mσ_{k+1}, which would give a C_net at step k+1;
    - (c) the amplitude. (T) has amplitude C_Tτ_k², and the c-version of (S) gives activities of size ρ_{k+1} = C_ρg_{k+1}²(1+r_{k+1})⁴. Either (S) has to give ρ_{k+1} ≤ Cτ_{k+1}², or (T) has to be restated with ρ.
  - |Q_{k+1}(D)| = |S°^{[D]}/S°| ≤ ∏_{𝒢∈D}e^{θ_ref(𝒢)} with θ_ref(𝒢) ≤ a°(𝒢) = (ν/32)σ_{k+1}(𝒢), by the ratio formula of D2.2(5)(d) applied to the reference gas;
  - the decay of Φ_{k+1}(S) in the spread of S. Φ_{k+1}(S) is the Möbius inverse of the cluster sums F(D), so only clusters linked to every member of S contribute, and the weight e^{η′ℓ} bounds them.
  - *Superseded in part by D2.3(3) after d2-3 S1 (22:3x, 22 Oct, date).* The reference gas is now a pure hard-core gas of groups that carry their old and vacuum occurrences; there are no Mayer links e^{−Φ(S)} − 1, and Q^{out}_{k+1}(D) = exp(−Σ_{Γ hits D}Φ°(Γ)) replaces the subset form, so the last bullet is void and the third becomes Σ_{Γ hits 𝒢}|Φ°(Γ)| ≤ a°(𝒢), FV (5.11). (R) must be redone in this form: the polymer weight of a group includes ∏|φ_k(Γ_j)|/m_𝒢! of its old occurrences and the factors of its vacuum occurrences, and (S) must bound the sum over groups with their occurrences, with the arcs J_Γ in σ_{k+1}. The induction uses D(k)'s Σ_{Γ hits W}|φ_k(Γ)|e^{a(Γ)} ≤ θ(W) with a size weight a(Γ) that pays the arcs; that weighted form is part of (S).
- *(Z) (OPEN).* Two parts.
  - The activities made at step k+1. For polymers that do not wrap the torus, CRE p.278 says the terms "for X ⊂ Λ_{k+1}, do not depend on Λ_{k+1}, and coincide with the corresponding terms arising from the expressions defined on the whole lattice". Reading this as independence of the torus as well is not supported by the sentence, which is about Λ_{k+1} on a fixed lattice. The covariances on T and on ℤ⁴ differ by images of size e^{−cι_T}. A proof needs Bałaban's covariance bounds with the image sum, as V4 L3813 does at step 1.
  - The old insertions. They need torus consistency as functions of 𝐔, and D(k)(vi) states it only for the reference weights Φ°. So (vi) has to be strengthened to the insertions and the cluster weights φ_k(Γ) (the Φ_k(S) before d2-3 S1), and then proved at step 1 from V4 and carried.

### (8) Complex data (derived; the analyticity of the reference-gas sums given (S), d2-4-r1 m9)

- *Transports.* On Ω_{k+1}, |η_{k+1}A′| < α_{1,k+1}L^{−(k+1)} per fine bond, so ‖T_𝐔(I(𝒢))‖ ≤ e^{c_πα_{1,k+1}n_{k+1}(𝒢)}, c_π the constant of π. It is at most e^{νσ_{k+1}(𝒢)/64} once c_πα_{1,k+1} ≤ ν/64, which holds for g_{k+1} small, since α_{1,k} → 0 as g_k → 0. For an arc of at most 4M traversals the bound is e, under 4c_πMα_{1,k+1} ≤ 1. The older layers Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1}, n ≤ k, are att and D2.6's. Transports between groups are not needed: whole-word norms are never used (D2.0(5)(h)).
- *Analyticity of the sums (D2.2(6)(e), d2-2e m4).* Bałaban's bounds (2.38) and (2.15) hold at every point of the domain with the same constants. So the absolute-value integrand of (2.15), with the sup of 𝒳 over the domain, is a majorant, locally uniform in the complex parameters. Morera's theorem under the integrals and Hartogs' theorem then apply. The cluster series converge uniformly on the domain by (2) and (R), so their sums are analytic by Weierstrass. For the reference gas this uses (R), hence (S).
- *The enlargement D_J^#.* D_J^# is determined by J. It is not summed over, so it adds no entropy. Its cubes are counted in #_{k+1}(𝒢) and in #(M), so their cost is inside θ_vac ≤ c#_{k+1} and inside (S).
- *Slot values at complex backgrounds.* The bounds of the loop slots and old insertions at complex (𝐔, 𝐉) are D(k)(iii)'s at step k. At step k+1 they are part of (S) (OPEN, the existing item of D2.2(6)).

### (9) What D2.4 leaves

- *OPEN (list revised after d2-4-r1, m10):*
  - (LPG) of (4), the local proportional gauge for the minimiser at steps k+1 ≥ 2, with ℓ_X = d_{k+1}(X) + 1;
  - (S) of (7), the sizes, including the H′ bound for marked polymers (no longer a reading, m12), the payment for the occurrences merged into the groups, and the strengthened form with the extra factor e^{(ν/32 + η′C_net)σ_{k+1}} that (7)(R) needs; (R) is to be redone in the cluster form of D2.3(3);
  - the uniformity of (T)'s constants in k ((7)(R), S5): the recursion with G < 1, the network constant for linked families, and the amplitude ρ_{k+1} against τ²;
  - (Z) of (7), torus consistency of the new activities and of the old insertions as functions;
  - the slot values at complex backgrounds, inside (S);
